The previous note said 'a compiled-in extension honestly rejects with 400 where Jellyfin can uninstall any plugin'. The second half was false: `InstallationManager.UninstallPlugin` starts with `if (plugin.Instance?.CanUninstall == false) { LogWarning("Attempt to delete non removable plugin {PluginName}, ignoring request"); return; }` and `PluginsController` answers 204 anyway (v10.11.8). `BasePlugin.CanUninstall` is false for anything whose assembly sits in the server's own directory (BasePlugin.cs:54-55), which is every plugin compiled into Jellyfin's tree — so Jellyfin CANNOT uninstall its five in-tree providers either; it just says 204 and does nothing. Measured on the lane-3 pair before the fix: `DELETE /Plugins/{omdb}` F 400 / J 204. Ported in the F1 review pass and now verified by the journeys.py `j_plugin_uninstall` row (204 + still installed on each server's own read-back, `effect`), together with the version gate (`GetPlugin(id, version)`: 10.11.8.0 -> 204, 9.9.9.9 -> 404, 10.11.8 -> 404 because `Version.Equals` compares four components, notaversion -> 400 from the model binder). WHAT REMAINS DIVERGENT: a Ferrofin compiled-in EXTENSION reports `CanUninstall: true` — deliberately, because jellyfin-web gates its enable/disable toggle on that same flag — and then rejects the uninstall with 400, where upstream's true-flagged plugins really are deletable folders. A staged WASM plugin IS really removed (file + config + enabled override, restart-required). Admin-only.

A staged WASM plugin IS really removed (file + config + enabled override, restart-required). Admin-only.

Ferrofin no-ops with 204. The two contract-relevant statuses agree and are the status-pair j_livetv_admin measures: a registered service prefix → 204, an unknown prefix → 400 (`ArgumentException("Service not found.")`, mapped by Jellyfin.Api/Middleware/ExceptionMiddleware.cs:127).

The previous note ('no shared plugin id across the two servers, so no image to fetch on both sides') is false since batch F1. Measured on the shared ids: 404 on BOTH, for the upstream reason — an in-tree plugin's manifest has `ImagePath = null`, so `PluginsController.GetPluginImage` returns `NotFound()` (v10.11.8 PluginsController.cs:212-215). The assets.py `GET /Plugins/{pluginId}/{version}/Image` row now probes all five plus an unknown guid and is stamped `status-class`, NOT the body-diff headline: nothing is served, so the only thing that agreed is the refusal. The row would compare bytes if either server ever shipped one. Also fixed here: the path version is now honoured (`GetPlugin(id, version)`), so a wrong version is a 404 rather than the plugin's image.

--- ALSO ON THIS ROW (merged from worktree-dv-lane1) ---

instance (the 200 BODY only). No plugin on EITHER server reports HasImage — Ferrofin's registrations all pass `None`, and 10.11.8's five bundled plugins carry no Manifest.ImagePath — so the 200 branch is unreachable on both, and a shared image subject would need the SAME external plugin installed on both, which on the Jellyfin side needs .NET assembly loading. The STATUS paths are fully diffable and one of them diverged: the C# binds `[FromRoute, Required] Version version` and calls `GetPlugin(id, version)`, which matches with `p.Version.Equals(version)` (v10.11.8 Emby.Server.Implementations/Plugins/PluginManager.cs:293-311); Ferrofin bound the segment and DISCARDED it, so a malformed version was a 404 where Jellyfin gives 400, and — on the three sibling `{version}` routes — `9.9.9.9` and even `notaversion` returned 204 and really did enable/disable/uninstall the plugin. Fixed for all four routes via `plugin_at_version`, including .NET's absent-component-is-minus-one rule (a live 10.11.8 404s `10.11.8` against its installed `10.11.8.0`). The 200 path also gained the `Content-Disposition: attachment` header both trees set. Anonymous access is correct and stays: this is the ONE action upstream marks [AllowAnonymous] (PluginsController.cs:221).
- ▫ `POST /QuickConnect/Authorize` — security-fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1, cross-user authz): C# RequestHelpers.GetUserId (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Helpers/RequestHelpers.cs:67-85) has two halves: an absent/all-zero userId falls back to the caller, and a userId that is NOT the caller is honoured only for an ADMINISTRATOR, else SecurityException -> 403. Ferrofin ported only the first half here. Measured twice on the lane-3 pair (Ferrofin 18396 / Jellyfin 18397) with a second, non-admin account 's1probe' created on both: BEFORE, a non-admin passing ?userId=<admin> reached the manager on Ferrofin (404 for the unknown probe code - i.e. the user gate never ran) against 403 on Jellyfin. This one is a privilege escalation, not only a parity divergence: authorizing a pending request mints a session for the NAMED user on the pairing device, so any authenticated account could hand a device an administrator session. After the fix both servers return 403 for the same request, while an administrator naming another user's id and a non-administrator naming their own id still succeed on both (measured, twice). Regression tests in crates/ferrofin-api/tests/ cover all three cases per route. Fifth instance in this campaign of a C# authorization check Ferrofin served without one (A1 startup, B3 x29 Live TV routes, D2 get_lineups, F1 items); the campaign owes a dedicated audit of every [Authorize(Policy=...)] and RequestHelpers.GetUserId call site in the 10.11.8 controllers against Ferrofin's route guards.
- ▫ `DELETE /ScheduledTasks/Running/{taskId}` — jellyfin-bug: stopping a task that is no longer running is 500 on Jellyfin and 204 on Ferrofin. `ScheduledTaskWorker.Cancel()` (v10.11.8:377-385) opens with `if (State != TaskState.Running) throw new InvalidOperationException("Cannot cancel a Task unless it is in the Running state.")`, and `ScheduledTasksController.StopTask` calls `_taskManager.Cancel(task)` with no catch, so the exception reaches the error middleware. The action declares only 204 and 404 (`[ProducesResponseType(Status204NoContent)]`, `Status404NotFound`) and the vendored contract declares no 500 for this path, so the throw is unintended, not a documented behaviour — Ferrofin's `FerrofinTaskManager::stop_run` returns Ok for an already-idle task (404 only for an unknown id) and is kept correct (do not port Jellyfin bugs). MEASURED 2026-08-30 on the loop pair, deterministic, not a race: POST-then-DELETE on `ec2f221fd8e7706b3d3afd2c4591b4d7` (Audio Normalization) is (204, 500) four times running on Jellyfin and (204, 204) four times on Ferrofin, and a DELETE with nothing running at all is 500 vs 204. The journeys row therefore reads H=True J=False and only goes red on a WARM lab: the first Audio Normalization run does real work and is still Running when the DELETE lands (which is why an earlier journeys record has it green), while every later run finishes in well under a millisecond. Ferrofin's own leg passes on both.

## Empty-corpus — ∅ `empty-corpus` (NOT deep-verified)

_Both servers returned an EMPTY result set, so only the empty envelope's own zeros were compared — an unconditional-empty handler would pass identically. A real verification and a weaker one: never counted in the deep-verified number._

- ∅ `GET /Albums/{itemId}/InstantMix`
- ∅ `GET /Albums/{itemId}/Similar` — flat-provider methodology (SET-GATED, order not gateable). Seeds of this kind take the C# FLAT provider: one `InternalItemsQuery { Genres = item.Genres, Tags = item.Tags, IncludeItemTypes = [<kind>], EnableGroupByMetadataKey = false, EnableTotalRecordCount = true, OrderBy = [(ItemSortBy.Random, SortOrder.Ascending)] }` — identical in v10.11.8 (built inline at LibraryController.cs:791-801) and in master (SeriesSimilarItemsProvider.cs:49, MusicAlbumSimilarItemsProvider.cs:50, MusicArtistSimilarItemsProvider.cs:50, AudioSimilarItemsProvider.cs:50, LiveTvProgramSimilarItemsProvider.cs:89). Ferrofin runs the same query and, as of this pass, the same `Random` order. IT BRIEFLY DID NOT: batch A2 first shipped `SortName` here, which is not cosmetic — at limit < |candidates| a fixed order returns a DIFFERENT ITEM, measured on the loop pair as `/Items/{audio}/Similar?limit=1` answering 'Artist 01 Album 01 Track 02' on five consecutive Ferrofin calls while Jellyfin alternated Track 02 / Track 03. That divergence is now closed in the server (`similar_items_manager.rs::flat_similar`, unit test `the_flat_provider_orders_random_not_by_name`), not papered over. WHAT THIS LEDGER ROW DOES AND DOES NOT GATE: `Random` on both sides means the ORDER can never be diffed by any probe, and neither can a page narrower than the candidate pool — parity_diff aligns arrays by Path>Name>Id, so a reordered Items array returns 0 diffs and always will. The reads.py leg therefore pins `limit=1000`, far above the fixture's pool, where the answer is the whole candidate set and so fully determined; what is gated there is the SET, TotalRecordCount and every per-item DTO field. Anything order-sensitive is covered by unit tests instead, never by this row. GREEN BUT VACUOUS ON THE CURRENT FIXTURE — read this row as 'no divergence detected', not as 'verified'. `suite/perf/gen-fixtures.sh` gave each of the 3 albums a UNIQUE music genre, so the candidate universe is empty by construction on BOTH servers and the leg diffs `{"Items": [], "TotalRecordCount": 0}` against itself. The fixture is changed (two of the three artists now share a genre) but that only takes effect at the campaign's final wipe-and-provision; until that sweep runs, the album matching itself is covered by the `an_album_seed_matches_albums_sharing_a_genre_only` unit test (two albums sharing a genre, a same-genre track that must NOT come back because IncludeItemTypes is [MusicAlbum] alone, and the ExcludeArtistIds leg with a positive control). A real Ferrofin bug found behind this row IS fixed and verified live: album Genres/Studios/PremiereDate are inherited from the album's Audio children (C# AlbumMetadataService + MetadataService.UpdateGenres/UpdateStudios/UpdatePremiereDate); Ferrofin aggregated only album-artist and year, so every album had `Genres: []` and was unreachable by the Genres= query this endpoint issues. After the fix, F album Genres/PremiereDate/ProductionYear match J exactly (Jazz/Rock/Ambient, 2022-01-01T00:00:00.0000000Z, 2022).
- ∅ `GET /Artists/{itemId}/InstantMix`
- ∅ `GET /Auth/Keys`

Ferrofin ported only the first half here. Measured twice on the lane-3 pair (Ferrofin 18396 / Jellyfin 18397) with a second, non-admin account 's1probe' created on both: BEFORE, a non-admin passing ?userId=<admin> got 200 on Ferrofin against 403 on Jellyfin. The handler ignored userId entirely (no channel providers, so the body is empty either way) - but upstream runs the gate BEFORE the query, so an empty body is still an answer the caller was not entitled to. After the fix both servers return 403 for the same request, while an administrator naming another user's id and a non-administrator naming their own id still succeed on both (measured, twice). Regression tests in crates/ferrofin-api/tests/ cover all three cases per route. S1 spread that pre-existing defect to five more routes when it gave previously parameter-less handlers a `Query<T>`. Re-measured on the lane-3 pair BEFORE the fix: `?userId=` and `?userId=%20` were 400 on Ferrofin against 200 on Jellyfin (404/404 where the probe id does not exist). FIXED AS A CLASS, not as an instance: every `Option<Uuid>` field of a `Query<...>` struct in ferrofin-api now binds through `handlers::query_parse::empty_as_none_uuid`. AFTER, the four spellings (absent / all-zero / empty / whitespace) agree on both servers across /Devices, /Channels, /Channels/Items/Latest, /LiveTv/Recordings/Folders, /LiveTv/Recordings/{recordingId}, /Audio/{itemId}/universal AND the four routes that carried the defect before S1 ever ran (/Items, /UserViews, /Persons, /Years). Re-measured on the lane-3 pair BEFORE the fix: `?userId=` and `?userId=%20` were 400 on Ferrofin against 200 on Jellyfin (404/404 where the probe id does not exist). FIXED AS A CLASS, not as an instance: every `Option<Uuid>` field of a `Query<...>` struct in ferrofin-api now binds through `handlers::query_parse::empty_as_none_uuid`. AFTER, the four spellings (absent / all-zero / empty / whitespace) agree on both servers across /Devices, /Channels, /Channels/Items/Latest, /LiveTv/Recordings/Folders, /LiveTv/Recordings/{recordingId}, /Audio/{itemId}/universal AND the four routes that carried the defect before S1 ever ran (/Items, /UserViews, /Persons, /Years). Request BODIES keep serde's strict parse on purpose: System.Text.Json rejects `"UserId": ""` upstream too.

- ∅ `GET /LiveTv/Recordings/Folders` — security-fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1, cross-user authz): C# RequestHelpers.GetUserId (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Helpers/RequestHelpers.cs:67-85) has two halves: an absent/all-zero userId falls back to the caller, and a userId that is NOT the caller is honoured only for an ADMINISTRATOR, else SecurityException -> 403. Ferrofin ported only the first half here. Found by the S1 sweep. The handler took no userId at all, so the parameter was dropped on the floor rather than mis-handled — but upstream runs the gate before the query, so the answer was one the caller was not entitled to. Measured: 200 on Ferrofin against 403 on Jellyfin. Re-measured independently in the S1 review pass with a fresh non-admin account ('s1fixprobe', created and deleted on both servers): a non-administrator naming the administrator's id is 403 on BOTH servers, while the administrator naming another user and a non-administrator naming their own id still succeed on both. Recorded in the S1 review pass: the batch claimed these five as flipped but left no row for them, so the fix existed only in the code and the claim. | empty-guid binding fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1 review pass, reviewer finding F2): ASP.NET binds an EMPTY or whitespace-only value for a `Guid?` to null (SimpleTypeModelBinder), so upstream answers `?userId=` and `?userId=%20` exactly as it answers a request with no userId at all; serde's `Option<Uuid>` tried to parse "" and made both a 400. S1 spread that pre-existing defect to five more routes when it gave previously parameter-less handlers a `Query<T>`. Re-measured on the lane-3 pair BEFORE the fix: `?userId=` and `?userId=%20` were 400 on Ferrofin against 200 on Jellyfin (404/404 where the probe id does not exist). FIXED AS A CLASS, not as an instance: every `Option<Uuid>` field of a `Query<...>` struct in ferrofin-api now binds through `handlers::query_parse::empty_as_none_uuid`. AFTER, the four spellings (absent / all-zero / empty / whitespace) agree on both servers across /Devices, /Channels, /Channels/Items/Latest, /LiveTv/Recordings/Folders, /LiveTv/Recordings/{recordingId}, /Audio/{itemId}/universal AND the four routes that carried the defect before S1 ever ran (/Items, /UserViews, /Persons, /Years). Request BODIES keep serde's strict parse on purpose: System.Text.Json rejects `"UserId": ""` upstream too.
- ∅ `GET /LiveTv/Recordings/Groups`
- ∅ `GET /LiveTv/Recordings/Series`
- ∅ `GET /LiveTv/SeriesTimers`
- ∅ `GET /LiveTv/Timers`

- ∅ `GET /Shows/NextUp` — jellyfin-bug (nil-guid parameters), measured 2026-08-30 in the S1 review pass while verifying the empty-guid fix — recorded rather than left unmentioned. Jellyfin THROWS on an all-zero guid passed to a parameter that is not `userId`: `parentId=00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000` is 400 with the middleware's generic body `Error processing request.` (an unhandled ArgumentException from GetItemById/GetUserById — "Guid can't be empty" — not a designed 400), where Ferrofin treats the nil as absent and answers 200. `userId` is exempt on both sides because RequestHelpers.GetUserId maps a nil to the caller before anything looks it up. Measured on the lane-3 pair, admin token, all-zero value: GET /Items?parentId= (J400 H200), /Items?userId=&parentId= (J400 H200), /Items/Filters (J400 H200), /Items/Filters2 (J400 H200), /Years (J400 H200), /Shows/NextUp?seriesId=&parentId= (J400 H200), /Shows/{seriesId}/Episodes?seasonId=&adjacentTo= (J400 H404), /Trailers?parentId= (J500 H200 — an outright unhandled exception). Ferrofin is kept correct per the standing policy (do not port Jellyfin bugs); a 500 is never the right answer and neither is a leaked ArgumentException. NOT deferred and NOT accepted by an agent: this is a measured divergence awaiting the owner's call. UN-DEFER PATH if the owner wants byte parity on the refusal: reject a nil guid with 400 on the non-userId item-lookup parameters (parentId, seriesId, seasonId, adjacentTo, startItemId, appearsInItemId) in handlers::query_parse, leave `userId` alone, and add the eight probes above to the read layer as a status-class row.

- ∅ `GET /Videos/{itemId}/AdditionalParts`

## Classified divergence (accepted — not a bug)

_Settled decisions. An item here is a question that has been closed._

- ⚠️ `GET /Audio/{itemId}/RemoteSearch/Lyrics` — expected-extension: Jellyfin 10.11.8 ships NO ILyricProvider implementation — `git grep ": ILyricProvider" v10.11.8 -- '*.cs'` returns nothing (only the interface, LyricManager and the CoreAppHost DI scan), and that server's own GET /Libraries/AvailableOptions reports `LyricFetchers: []`. Ferrofin compiles LrcLib in the way it compiles TMDB in. On this fixture both answer `200 []` — Jellyfin structurally, Ferrofin because the synthetic titles ("Artist 01 Album 01 Track 01") match no lrclib record (egress verified: `curl https://lrclib.net/api/search` returns 200 from BOTH containers, so this is a corpus fact, not a network gap). The row therefore agrees on an empty list and compares nothing; only a real-track fixture would give it content, and then only Ferrofin could return any.
- ⚠️ `POST /Audio/{itemId}/RemoteSearch/Lyrics/{lyricId}` — expected-extension: the download half of the same asymmetry. With no ILyricProvider registered, `LyricManager.DownloadLyricsAsync` short-circuits on `GetProvider(lyricId.LeftPart('_')) is null` and the route can only ever 404 on Jellyfin 10.11.8, while Ferrofin's LrcLib really fetches. The only cross-server-comparable observation is the refusal, and it agrees: a bogus lyric id is 404/404 and a non-audio itemId is 404/404 (measured on the lab pair, twice). The success leg exists on one server only, so this row cannot earn a body diff — but the LyricDto it returns is the same parse that `GET /Audio/{itemId}/Lyrics` now body-diffs over identical uploaded bytes.

- ⚠️ `GET /Items/{itemId}/ExternalIdInfos` — accepted-divergence (provider lineup, 2 rows): Ferrofin offers two Identify-dialog id fields stock Jellyfin 10.11.8 does not — `TheTVDB Numerical`/`Tvdb`/`Series` on a Series, and `Comic Vine`/`ComicVine`/`Person` on a Person. Ferrofin ships TVDB compiled in; stock 10.11.8 does not. Evidence: `git grep -l ": IExternalId" v10.11.8` lists 19 classes (Imdb, ImdbPerson, Imvdb, AudioDb x4, MusicBrainz x7, Tmdb{BoxSet,Movie,Person,Series}, Zap2It) and NOT ONE Tvdb — upstream's TVDB support is the out-of-tree jellyfin-plugin-tvdb. Ferrofin advertises exactly ONE TVDB field, `TheTVDB Numerical`/`Tvdb`/`Series`, and it is BACKED: `resolve_tmdb_id` resolves a series through TMDB `/find?external_source=tvdb_id` (provider_manager.rs, gated on `TmdbKind::Series`) and the fanart.tv series lookup is keyed by TVDB id. The Season/Episode/Movie/Person TVDB fields Ferrofin used to advertise were HOLLOW — nothing read them — and were deleted in this batch rather than classified. `Comic Vine`/`ComicVine`/`Person` is a Ferrofin addition: 10.11.8 has no ComicVine IExternalId (same `git grep` evidence as above). It is backed — `external_ids.rs::book_urls` renders a Comic Vine "Links" entry for `Book | Person` items carrying the id — and Ferrofin ships a books provider (`ferrofin-providers/src/books.rs`, which also writes GoogleBooks/ISBN from OPF metadata). The Book-kind fields (ComicVine/GoogleBooks/ISBN) are the same divergence but are NOT measurable in this fixture, which has no Book items — see the residual note for batch A4. Measured live on the A4 pair (Ferrofin 18196 / Jellyfin 10.11.8 18197), same path-derived item id on both, twice: Movie, Season, Episode, MusicAlbum and Audio now diff byte-identical INCLUDING ORDER (the MusicBrainz descriptor block was registered out of order and is fixed); only Series and Person differ, by exactly the two rows above.
- ⚠️ `GET /Items/{itemId}/MetadataEditor` — accepted-divergence (provider lineup, 2 rows): the editor re-serves `ExternalIdInfos` verbatim (C# `ItemUpdateController.GetMetadataEditorInfo`), so it carries exactly the divergence recorded for `GET /Items/{itemId}/ExternalIdInfos` and nothing else. Ferrofin ships TVDB compiled in; stock 10.11.8 does not. Evidence: `git grep -l ": IExternalId" v10.11.8` lists 19 classes (Imdb, ImdbPerson, Imvdb, AudioDb x4, MusicBrainz x7, Tmdb{BoxSet,Movie,Person,Series}, Zap2It) and NOT ONE Tvdb — upstream's TVDB support is the out-of-tree jellyfin-plugin-tvdb. Ferrofin advertises exactly ONE TVDB field, `TheTVDB Numerical`/`Tvdb`/`Series`, and it is BACKED: `resolve_tmdb_id` resolves a series through TMDB `/find?external_source=tvdb_id` (provider_manager.rs, gated on `TmdbKind::Series`) and the fanart.tv series lookup is keyed by TVDB id. The Season/Episode/Movie/Person TVDB fields Ferrofin used to advertise were HOLLOW — nothing read them — and were deleted in this batch rather than classified. `Comic Vine`/`ComicVine`/`Person` is a Ferrofin addition: 10.11.8 has no ComicVine IExternalId (same `git grep` evidence as above). It is backed — `external_ids.rs::book_urls` renders a Comic Vine "Links" entry for `Book | Person` items carrying the id — and Ferrofin ships a books provider (`ferrofin-providers/src/books.rs`, which also writes GoogleBooks/ISBN from OPF metadata). The Book-kind fields (ComicVine/GoogleBooks/ISBN) are the same divergence but are NOT measurable in this fixture, which has no Book items — see the residual note for batch A4. Measured live on the A4 pair (Ferrofin 18196 / Jellyfin 10.11.8 18197), same path-derived item id on both, twice: Movie, Season, Episode, MusicAlbum and Audio now diff byte-identical INCLUDING ORDER (the MusicBrainz descriptor block was registered out of order and is fixed); only Series and Person differ, by exactly the two rows above. Every other field of the editor body (Countries, Cultures, ParentalRatingOptions, ContentTypeOptions) diffs clean.
- ⚠️ `GET /Items/{itemId}/RemoteImages` — accepted-divergence (provider lineup): two documented lineup facts, no server bug left. (1) `Providers` is built from `GetRemoteImageProviderInfo(item)` and narrowed ONLY by `type`, never by `providerName` (v10.11.8 RemoteImageController.cs:92-99) — so every leg, scoped or not, carries Ferrofin's extra `FanArt` entry (see `GET /Items/{itemId}/RemoteImages/Providers`). (2) On the UNSCOPED leg, fanart.tv adds its own images and Jellyfin's OMDb adds one Primary that Ferrofin cannot (upstream bakes an OMDb key at OmdbProvider.cs:257; Ferrofin's OMDb is inert without FERROFIN_OMDB_KEY — CLAUDE.md 'Current scope'). What this row now PROVES: on `providerName=TheMovieDb`, `Images` and `TotalRecordCount` are byte-identical INCLUDING ORDER on all four legs measured live on the A4 pair (default 138/138, includeAllLanguages=true 391/391, type=Logo 7/7, series 0/0). That is the fix this batch landed — Ferrofin was serving 497 images where 10.11.8 served 139, unfiltered and unsorted, because `ProviderManager.GetImages`' preferred-language filter and `OrderByLanguageDescending` were never ported (`RemoteImageQuery.include_all_languages` was read by nothing).
- ⚠️ `GET /Items/{itemId}/RemoteImages/Providers` — accepted-divergence (provider lineup, 1 entry): Ferrofin compiles fanart.tv in as a remote image provider and stock Jellyfin 10.11.8 does not — its ten concrete `IRemoteImageProvider` implementors are AudioDb{Album,Artist}, Omdb, StudioImages and Tmdb{BoxSet,Movie,Person,Episode,Season,Series} (`git grep -l IRemoteImageProvider v10.11.8`, minus the interface, the two Manager files and the two test files), with no fanart provider in tree — fanart is an out-of-tree plugin, and the only `fanart` strings in 10.11.8 are filename patterns and NFO tags. So a Movie, Series, MusicArtist or MusicAlbum lists one extra `FanArt` entry. Measured live on the A4 pair over seven path-correlated kinds: dropping that single entry makes the arrays equal field-for-field INCLUDING `SupportedImages` and order. The real bug that was hiding under this flag IS FIXED, not classified: Ferrofin had no `TmdbSeasonImageProvider` arm at all (a Season offered NO providers) and no `TmdbEpisodeImageProvider` (an Episode offered only OMDb, `Order = 90`); both are ported, ordered TMDB-first per `IHasOrder`, and Season/Episode/Audio/Person now diff clean.
- ⚠️ `POST /Jellyfin.Plugin.OpenSubtitles/ValidateLoginInfo` — expected-extension: compiled-in/extension route not on the stock Jellyfin surface the fixture exercises.
- ⚠️ `GET /Libraries/AvailableOptions` — expected-extension (SupportedImageTypes + the fetcher lists): Ferrofin compiles in TheTVDB, FanArt, Open Subtitles and IntroSkipper, which the lab's stock Jellyfin 10.11.8 does not ship (GET /Plugins there lists only AudioDB and MusicBrainz). They therefore appear as extra ImageFetchers/MetadataFetchers rows, and — because SupportedImageTypes is the flattened union of every compiled image provider's GetSupportedImages (ProviderManager.cs:706-714) — their image types (Art/Disc/Banner) widen that list beyond Jellyfin's. The 2026-08-11 'verified' text this replaces was false: sweep already recorded 15 extra / 39 mismatch / 2 missing paths and schema_valid:false on this row. Fixed in batch A3: DefaultImageOptions is now the verbatim TypeOptions.DefaultImageOptions table (order included, [] for unnamed types); SupportedImageTypes is derived from the providers instead of a hardcoded 11-element enum dump; isNewLibrary now drives DefaultEnabled; and SimilarItemProviders (post-10.11.8, additionalProperties:false) is no longer serialized. OPEN WORK, not accepted: upstream's 'Screen Grabber' (VideoImageProvider, Order 100, [Primary]) is still unported — Ferrofin owns the capability (MediaEncoder::extract_video_image) but does not register the provider, so it is deliberately absent from the list rather than advertised hollow.
- ⚠️ `POST /Library/VirtualFolders/Name` — accepted-divergence (Ferrofin converges eagerly where upstream defers). The row reads H=True J=False, measured twice on the loop pair 2026-08-30, and the FALSE is the ORACLE'S — but it is upstream's DOCUMENTED behaviour for the request this row actually issues, not a bug, which is why this entry is no longer categorised jellyfin-bug. WHAT THE PROBE ISSUES: `POST /Library/VirtualFolders/Name?name=&newName=` with NO refreshLibrary parameter, i.e. refreshLibrary=false. WHAT UPSTREAM DOES WITH THAT REQUEST: LibraryStructureController.RenameVirtualFolder (v10.11.8 and master byte-identical apart from three added ClearIgnoreRuleCache() calls, plus a [RegularExpression] master added to a DIFFERENT action's name) moves the directory and then, in its finally block, `if (refreshLibrary) { ValidateTopLibraryFolders(ct, true); ... } else { await Task.Delay(1000); _libraryMonitor.Start(); }`. So on refreshLibrary=false Jellyfin deliberately validates nothing and keeps the CollectionFolder row under the OLD name, pointing at a directory that no longer exists, and serves it from /UserViews until some later scan. THE ASSERTION: the journey asserts the ROOT VIEW converges — after the rename no CollectionFolder still carries the old name, the new name is materialized, and the root's CollectionFolder count is unchanged end to end — over /Items + /UserViews. It used to assert only that /Library/VirtualFolders lists the new name, which upstream builds by walking the user-views DIRECTORY (LibraryManager.GetVirtualFolders, LibraryManager.cs:1527-1572, identical in both trees: it matches each directory against GetUserRootFolder().Children by path and leaves ItemId NULL when nothing matches), so it is the one surface a stale DB row cannot corrupt — and both servers passed it while only one was right. WHY FERROFIN MUST NOT COPY IT: Ferrofin derives a CollectionFolder's id from its directory path and get_virtual_folders mints a row for every directory it sees, so leaving the vacated row behind yields TWO rows where upstream has one stale one — measured before the fix as a permanent phantom library ('Movies (synth) Renamed', 5730b9eb5c86a66014cedd6ea527295f) that survived restarts and POST /Library/Refresh and showed up in the client sidebar. FerrofinVirtualFolderManager::rename_virtual_folder now deletes the row the vacated directory backed and runs prune_orphan_collection_folders (the port of ValidateTopLibraryFolders' remove leg — 'If the user has somehow deleted the collection directory, remove the metadata from the database', LibraryManager.cs:1172-1190, identical in both trees — also called from remove_virtual_folder and at LibraryScanner::scan start). CONSEQUENCE, flagged for the owner: the API handler rescans the renamed library UNCONDITIONALLY, not only when refreshLibrary is set, because deleting the old row cascades its children away (FK_BaseItems_BaseItems_ParentId ON DELETE CASCADE) and skipping the refresh would leave the renamed library empty; upstream can skip it only because it keeps the stale row and its children. The rescan is scoped to the renamed library when library_id_by_name resolves its id, which is the normal case (get_virtual_folders mints the row before the lookup); if it does not resolve, the fallback is a FULL library scan — more than upstream queues for this request — and that arm now logs at WARN so an unrequested full pass is attributable. Dashboard-only op. SEPARATE FINDING, NOT WHAT THIS ROW SCORES: with refreshLibrary=TRUE, where the C# above promises ValidateTopLibraryFolders(ct, removeRoot: true), the 10.11.8 oracle still did not converge — renaming 'Music (synth)' to 'Music (synth) DiagRB' left BOTH names in the root for a full 120 s poll at 5 s intervals, i.e. it added the new row and never deleted the vacated one. Only an explicit DELETE /Library/VirtualFolders?refreshLibrary=true pruned it (which is how that measurement was cleaned up, in the same session). That is an upstream bug, recorded here so it is not lost, but it is deliberately NOT this row's category: the probe never sends refreshLibrary=true, and a category must describe the request that was scored. *** HISTORY, so the finding is not lost: batch S1 first measured this as an ORPHAN USER VIEW leak (rename, then rename back, left a phantom library in GET /UserViews that survived a container restart and a POST /Library/Refresh). Batch F3 then found the cause — ValidateTopLibraryFolders' remove leg was never ported, so rows minted from directories on read were never pruned when the directory vanished — and fixed it. The lane-3 branch still carried S1's pre-fix wording at merge time; this row supersedes it.
- ⚠️ `GET /MediaSegmentsApi` — expected-extension: MediaSegmentsApi is a compiled-in Ferrofin plugin; stock Jellyfin 10.11.8 lacks it (J=404 without the plugin installed). JSON API, not streaming.
- ⚠️ `POST /MediaSegmentsApi/{itemId}` — expected-extension: MediaSegmentsApi is a compiled-in Ferrofin plugin; stock Jellyfin 10.11.8 lacks it (J=404 without the plugin installed). JSON API, not streaming.
- ⚠️ `DELETE /MediaSegmentsApi/{segmentId}` — expected-extension: MediaSegmentsApi is a compiled-in Ferrofin plugin; stock Jellyfin 10.11.8 lacks it (J=404 without the plugin installed). JSON API, not streaming.
- ⚠️ `POST /MergeVersions/MergeEpisodes` — expected-extension: Ferrofin ships the MergeVersions controller compiled-in; stock Jellyfin 10.11.8 lacks it (H=2xx vs J=404). By design.
- ⚠️ `POST /MergeVersions/MergeMovies` — expected-extension: Ferrofin ships the MergeVersions controller (danieladov 'Merge Versions' plugin surface) compiled-in; stock Jellyfin 10.11.8 lacks it (H=2xx vs J=404). By design.
- ⚠️ `POST /MergeVersions/SplitEpisodes` — expected-extension: Ferrofin ships the MergeVersions controller compiled-in; stock Jellyfin 10.11.8 lacks it (H=2xx vs J=404). By design.

- ⚠️ `POST /Packages/Installed/{name}` — accepted-divergence: repository install of WASM plugins is real (2026-08-14: ABI gate → HTTPS-per-hop download → streamed size cap → sha256/md5 verify → component+identity validation → guid-squat refusal → atomic stage → restart-required; admin-only; end-to-end server test). Divergence vs Jellyfin: the install is SYNCHRONOUS — none of the PackageInstalling/Completed/Failed/Cancelled WebSocket messages are emitted (no dashboard progress; a slow repo holds the POST open), and the plugin appears in /Plugins only after the activating restart. Async install + Package* push is the recorded follow-up (WebSocket-push backlog).
- ⚠️ `DELETE /Packages/Installing/{packageId}` — accepted-divergence: always 404 — installs are synchronous, so no tracked installation exists to cancel (Jellyfin tracks async installs). Admin-only.
- ⚠️ `DELETE /Playlists/{playlistId}/Users/{userId}` — jellyfin-bug: Jellyfin's DELETE is a silent no-op on BOTH v10.11.8 and master. `PlaylistsController.RemoveUserFromPlaylist` (identical in both trees) resolves the share from one Playlist instance, then `PlaylistManager.RemoveUserFromShares` (v10.11.8 L641-648 / master L669-676, byte-identical) re-fetches a DIFFERENT instance via GetPlaylistForUser -> GetPlaylists -> LibraryManager.GetItemList (master L1656-1673, which returns _itemRepository.GetItemList directly, bypassing the BaseItem cache and materialising fresh objects) and calls `shares.Remove(share)`. `MediaBrowser.Model/Entities/PlaylistUserPermissions.cs:8` is a plain class with only UserId/CanEdit auto-properties — no Equals, no IEquatable, not a record — so `EqualityComparer<T>.Default` is REFERENCE equality, Remove returns false, the UNCHANGED list is written back, and the controller returns 204. Corroborated inside the same file: `AddUserToShares` removes an element it took from the SAME list, which is why POST works and DELETE does not. `git log v10.11.8..master` over both paths contains no change to RemoveUserFromShares — MASTER HAS NOT FIXED THIS. Ferrofin deletes by (playlist_id, user_id) in SQL (ferrofin-core/src/collection_manager.rs `remove_user_from_shares`), which is the semantic intent of the C#, so the share actually goes and a repeat DELETE correctly 404s — the controller's own `share is null -> NotFound("User permissions not found")` branch, which on Jellyfin is unreachable. Ferrofin is the correct side. Measured on the F6 pair 2026-08-30, twice: F 204 then GET Users [] then repeat DELETE 404; J 204 then GET Users STILL lists the user then repeat DELETE 204. Discriminating control on the SAME Jellyfin instance: `POST /Playlists/{id}` with `Users: []` (UpdatePlaylist assigns the list wholesale, no List.Remove) DOES clear and persist — so this is not container skew, fixture or a Shares-persistence problem, it is RemoveUserFromShares alone. Previously filed `instance` (instance noise / methodology), which was wrong: a deterministic, twice-reproduced upstream defect is not lab noise. The row cannot reach deep_verified while upstream is broken — journeys.py requires the write's effect on BOTH servers and Jellyfin's legitimately does not hold. That is the correct terminal state for a jellyfin-bug row, not a probe weakness. The second symptom is now recorded rather than asserted: `j_playlist_share` reports the repeat-DELETE status under `notes.playlist_share_repeat_delete_status` in journey-results.json (measured F=404 J=204, twice), deliberately kept OUT of the row's boolean so a future Ferrofin regression to a blind 204 cannot pass by matching Jellyfin.
- ⚠️ `GET /Plugins` — expected-extension (SET ONLY — the ORDER and the entry shape are now parity). The blanket 'the set necessarily differs from any stock Jellyfin instance' was hiding an unported rule: `PluginsController.GetPlugins` is `_pluginManager.Plugins.OrderBy(p => p.Name)` (v10.11.8 PluginsController.cs:55-57). Jellyfin returned AudioDB, MusicBrainz, OMDb, Studio Images, TMDb while Ferrofin returned registration order (OpenSubtitles, TMDb, Studio Images, OMDb, MusicBrainz, AudioDB) — measured on the lane-3 pair, fixed in the F1 review pass, re-measured identical apart from Ferrofin's extra entry. With the five in-tree provider plugins registered, the two lists now differ by exactly ONE row: Ferrofin's own compiled-in extension (OpenSubtitles), plus any staged WASM plugin. Every shared entry agrees field for field including `CanUninstall: false` and `Version: 10.11.8.0`. NOT compared: `Status` for a plugin whose enable/disable has been toggled since boot — see the classification on POST /Plugins/{pluginId}/{version}/Enable.
- ⚠️ `GET /Providers/Lyrics/{lyricId}` — expected-extension: undiffable by construction. Jellyfin 10.11.8 registers no lyric provider, so `InternalGetRemoteLyricsAsync` always hits `GetProvider(...) is null` and this route answers 404 for EVERY possible id, where Ferrofin's LrcLib returns a real 200. The ids are not portable either — Ferrofin's provider-local id is `{lrclib_id}_{synced|plain}`, a shape no Jellyfin provider would mint. What the two servers CAN agree on is the refusal, and they now do: a bogus id and an id with no variant suffix are both 404/404 (the latter used to be an off-contract 400 from LrcLibProvider::get_lyrics; the vendored contract declares only 200/404, and the C# has no 400 path). The parse behind the 200 is pinned by the GET /Audio/{itemId}/Lyrics body diff.
- ⚠️ `POST /SkipButtonCss/InjectCss` — expected-extension: compiled-in/extension route not on the stock Jellyfin surface the fixture exercises.
- ⚠️ `POST /SkipButtonCss/UpdateSkipDuration` — expected-extension: compiled-in/extension route not on the stock Jellyfin surface the fixture exercises.
- ⚠️ `POST /Startup/User` — jellyfin-bug: Ferrofin ports upstream MASTER's `StartupController.UpdateStartupUser` (NotFound -> Forbid -> BadRequest -> UpdateUser -> case-insensitive RenameUser -> ChangePassword), whose Forbid guard is security commit 62a5ded9205b10ddaef60ce3e05bf80e79f4c742 (Shadowghost, 2026-07-17, "Prevent unauthenticated re-run of the startup wizard on misconfiguration"); Ferrofin ports that commit's OTHER half too — `ApplicationHost.EnsureStartupWizardIntegrity` is `ensure_startup_wizard_integrity` at apps/ferrofin-server/src/lib.rs:304, called from lib.rs:424. `git show v10.11.8:Jellyfin.Api/Controllers/StartupController.cs` predates the commit — it does `_userManager.Users.First()` with NO NotFound and NO Forbid — so 10.11.8 answers 204 and silently re-sets the already-provisioned admin's password on an unguarded write. That is the same class of hole batch A1 closed on this controller, one layer deeper: A1 stopped an unauthenticated caller, 62a5ded920 stops an authenticated-but-wrong one once IsStartupWizardCompleted has been cleared. NOT a contract question: `.paths["/Startup/User"].post.responses` in the vendored 10.11.8 spec is [204,401,403,503], so Ferrofin's 403 is in-contract and only the C# implementation lags its own spec. Previously filed here as `accepted-divergence`, which was wrong under the campaign's master-vs-10.11.8 rule (outcome 1: master deliberately changed the behaviour, Ferrofin follows master and is correct). PROBE (rewritten in the F6 reviewer-fix pass; the deep_verified=True this row briefly claimed is WITHDRAWN). Three versions of this probe, two of them wrong, all recorded so that neither wrong one is rediscovered as an idea. (1) The ORIGINAL asserted `st == 403` on BOTH servers. The status delta it rested on was real, but it lived only in the boolean, so journey-results.json said "H=True J=False" and named nothing — which is what let this hand-written label stand in for a measurement. (2) F6's FIRST REPLACEMENT asserted `st in (204, 403)` plus "the provisioned admin was not clobbered", and reported the row deep_verified=True. That assertion is TRUE BY CONSTRUCTION: the probe posts the harness's OWN credentials, so Jellyfin's unguarded 204 re-sets the name to the name it already has and the password to the password it already has, and both evidence facts hold whether the server forbade the write or performed it. Demonstrated rather than argued — the probe was re-run against Jellyfin, a server with NO guard at all, on the F6 pair 2026-08-30, and returned Same(True, {'FirstUserName': 'bench', 'CredentialsStillValid': True}), byte-identical to Ferrofin's, cross_server_ok=True, deep_verified=True. A probe that a guardless server passes cannot be evidence that the guard is present, and it dropped the row's only Ferrofin-side discrimination as well: a regression deleting the guard would have kept it green. (3) WHAT RUNS NOW asserts the refusal itself — `st == 403` AND the admin intact — and carries the STATUS in the `Same` evidence, so the divergence is named in the results file ("[evidence: Status: H=403 J=204]") instead of living only in this prose. The row is therefore RED, and stays red for as long as the pinned 10.11.8 oracle lacks the guard: that is the correct terminal state for a jellyfin-bug row — identical to how this same layer treats `DELETE /Playlists/{playlistId}/Users/{userId}` — and the only way to make it green would be for Ferrofin to drop the guard. The payload is still the harness's own credentials, not to make anything pass (it does not, on Jellyfin) but because Jellyfin ACTUALLY PERFORMS the write and `StartupUserDto` carries no third, harmless field: any other Name or Password renames the lab's admin or changes its password for every later probe on the pair. So "was the admin clobbered" cannot separate the two servers without damaging the lab, and it is kept only as a lab-safety invariant; the status is the discriminator. Measured after the rewrite, twice, identical: F Same(True, {Status 403, FirstUserName bench, CredentialsStillValid True}) / J Same(False, {Status 204, FirstUserName bench, CredentialsStillValid True}), deep_verified=False, verification_method `property`. The three sibling Startup rows stayed green across both runs, which is the check that the re-auth helper still is not revoking the run's own session. The 403 itself is pinned against MASTER — the only tree that contains the fix, and one no 10.11.8 container can witness — by four unit tests in crates/ferrofin-api/tests/startup.rs transliterated from upstream's own tests/Jellyfin.Api.Tests/Controllers/StartupControllerTests.cs, including its `ChangePassword(...) Times.Never` assertion.
- ⚠️ `GET /Trailers` — jellyfin-bug: Jellyfin 10.11.8 returns 500 on its own route, unconditionally. TrailersController takes ItemsController as a constructor dependency and calls _itemsController.GetItems(...) on it (TrailersController.cs:20/26/28/209); that DI-constructed instance has no ControllerContext, so ControllerBase.User is null and the first statement of GetItems — `var isApiKey = User.GetIsApiKey();` (ItemsController.cs:250) — dereferences it in ClaimsPrincipalExtensions.GetClaimValue (ClaimsPrincipalExtensions.cs:71-79, no null guard) and throws a NullReferenceException. Confirmed from the live container's stack trace; still unfixed on master (ItemsController.cs:263). The vendored contract declares only 200/401/403/503 for this path and the C# action is [ProducesResponseType(Status200OK)], so 200 is the required behaviour: Ferrofin is correct and is kept correct (do not port Jellyfin bugs). Because the two responses therefore cannot be diffed against each other, reads.py's `trailers_invariants` earns the row as `property` through the cross-route oracle the C# controller is literally defined as (GetTrailers == GetItems with includeItemTypes=[Trailer]): the Trailer corpus is compared via /Items?includeItemTypes=Trailer, which works on both servers, and Ferrofin's /Trailers is held to equal its own /Items answer. Jellyfin's 500 is pinned as a fact, so an upstream fix flips the row loudly instead of passing in silence.

--- ALSO ON THIS ROW (merged from main) ---
jellyfin-bug (nil-guid parameters), measured 2026-08-30 in the S1 review pass while verifying the empty-guid fix — recorded rather than left unmentioned. Jellyfin THROWS on an all-zero guid passed to a parameter that is not `userId`: `parentId=00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000` is 400 with the middleware's generic body `Error processing request.` (an unhandled ArgumentException from GetItemById/GetUserById — "Guid can't be empty" — not a designed 400), where Ferrofin treats the nil as absent and answers 200. `userId` is exempt on both sides because RequestHelpers.GetUserId maps a nil to the caller before anything looks it up. Measured on the lane-3 pair, admin token, all-zero value: GET /Items?parentId= (J400 H200), /Items?userId=&parentId= (J400 H200), /Items/Filters (J400 H200), /Items/Filters2 (J400 H200), /Years (J400 H200), /Shows/NextUp?seriesId=&parentId= (J400 H200), /Shows/{seriesId}/Episodes?seasonId=&adjacentTo= (J400 H404), /Trailers?parentId= (J500 H200 — an outright unhandled exception). Ferrofin is kept correct per the standing policy (do not port Jellyfin bugs); a 500 is never the right answer and neither is a leaked ArgumentException. NOT deferred and NOT accepted by an agent: this is a measured divergence awaiting the owner's call. UN-DEFER PATH if the owner wants byte parity on the refusal: reject a nil guid with 400 on the non-userId item-lookup parameters (parentId, seriesId, seasonId, adjacentTo, startItemId, appearsInItemId) in handlers::query_parse, leave `userId` alone, and add the eight probes above to the read layer as a status-class row.

## Awaiting owner ratification (NOT yet accepted)

_Ferrofin is deliberately doing the thing the C# is understood to have gotten wrong, with the upstream evidence recorded on the row. That is a decision the OWNER makes, not an agent — so these are kept out of "accepted" until it is made. Each one stays `deep_verified=false` against the 10.11.8 oracle while it waits: the divergence is real and visible, not filtered out of the diff._

- ⚖️ `GET /Artists` — jellyfin-bug (RunTimeTicks / CumulativeRunTimeTicks ONLY; every other leg of this row now diffs clean by measurement). `MusicArtistResolver` is PORTED (library_scan.rs::plan_music_node + is_music_artist, a port of v10.11.8 Emby.Server.Implementations/Library/Resolvers/Audio/MusicArtistResolver.cs:54-125), so an artist directory now resolves to a real MusicArtist row parented into the music library, on Jellyfin's OWN path-derived id (measured byte-identical on the fixture: Artist 01 = 0f39932400340734bea41ffc13aaddc4, Artist 02 = 2b3868bfaa266cc1d3cc4c3dd5d40800, Artist 03 = 697d2b7b6ec56d3cf7e87c2de748b0cd), with its albums re-parented onto it. That closed Path, CanDelete, RecursiveItemCount and the empty user-scoped MusicArtist universe.

WHAT REMAINS, and why it is Jellyfin's bug and not Ferrofin's: with the artist resolved as a folder, `MetadataService.UpdateCumulativeRunTimeTicks` (MediaBrowser.Providers/Manager/MetadataService.cs:451) sums the RunTimeTicks of the non-folder children returned by `GetChildrenForMetadataUpdates`. For a MusicArtist that method is OVERRIDDEN — `ArtistMetadataService.GetChildrenForMetadataUpdates` (MediaBrowser.Providers/Music/ArtistMetadataService.cs:44-56) returns `item.IsAccessedByName ? item.GetTaggedItems(Recursive, IsFolder=false) : item.GetRecursiveChildren(i => i is IHasArtist && !i.IsFolder)`, NOT the base class's plain `folder.GetRecursiveChildren()` at MetadataService.cs:397-401. A folder-resolved artist takes the second arm, and `Audio : IHasArtist`, so the set is exactly the three tracks — the override strengthens the conclusion rather than changing it, and it is cited here because re-deriving the number from the base method lands on the wrong call. `MusicArtist.SupportsCumulativeRunTimeTicks => true` (MusicArtist.cs:38). 3 tracks x 20000000 = 60000000, which is what Ferrofin stores. The 10.11.8 oracle stores 0, and its OWN DateLastRefreshed timestamps show why — a first-scan ordering race, read out of this lab's /config/data/jellyfin.db:
  MusicArtist 'Artist 01'   RunTimeTicks 0         DateLastRefreshed 2026-08-30 00:29:30.8359533
  Audio       'Track 01'    RunTimeTicks 20000000                    2026-08-30 00:29:30.8815808
  Audio       'Track 02'    RunTimeTicks 20000000                    2026-08-30 00:29:30.8973703
  Audio       'Track 03'    RunTimeTicks 20000000                    2026-08-30 00:29:30.9096119
  MusicAlbum  'Album 01'    RunTimeTicks 60000000                    2026-08-30 00:29:30.9187306
The artist refreshed 46 ms BEFORE its first track was probed, so it summed a set of NULLs. The album — identical code, `SupportsCumulativeRunTimeTicks` also true, refreshed after all three — got the right number. A rescan does not repair it either: `UpdateMetadataFromChildren` is gated on `isFullRefresh || currentUpdateType > None`. Matching Jellyfin here would mean reproducing a first-scan race on purpose.

Kept per 'don't port Jellyfin's bugs' (docs/ + the standing memory). OWNER CALL PENDING: ratify keeping 60000000, or direct that Ferrofin reproduce the race. The ledger renders this row under 'Awaiting owner ratification', NOT under 'accepted' — that bucket is a claim about a decision somebody made, and this one has not been made. Until ratified the row stays deep_verified=false against this oracle, honestly — the divergence is REAL and visible, not hidden. parity_diff.VOLATILE was NOT widened for RunTimeTicks and must not be.

ROW HISTORY, kept rather than dropped: before the port this row's bodies DIFFED CLEAN, and the old classification's warning ('DO NOT BANK THIS ROW') was right — the agreement was two different mechanisms landing on the same number. Ferrofin wrote RunTimeTicks 0 because its artist was a parentless by-name row with no recursive children and the faithful UpdateCumulativeRunTimeTicks summed nothing; Jellyfin wrote 0 because of the race above. The green was a coincidence and it is now a visible mismatch for the right reason. DE-DUPLICATION CHECK for this row specifically: it must return exactly 3 artists. A previous attempt at this port was rolled back because `push_by_name_join` joins on CleanName and the scanned row plus the by-name row each produced an entry, listing every artist twice. Measured 3, twice, after the fix.

WHAT THIS ROW CLOSED: Ferrofin returned 0 rows where Jellyfin returned 2. The scorer half was already right (`MusicArtistSimilarItemsProvider` is a flat InternalItemsQuery { Genres, Tags, IncludeItemTypes=[MusicArtist] } and an EMPTY Genres/Tags pair is not a predicate, so an untagged artist matches every other artist — covered by the `an_untagged_artist_seed_still_matches_every_other_artist` unit test). The blocker was the item UNIVERSE: no MusicArtist row survived `scope_to_user_libraries` (a faithful `AddUserToQuery`, TopParentId IS NOT NULL). That filter was NOT relaxed; the scanner was fixed. Both peers now return on both servers.

ORDERING — this row's own methodology guard rail, and it is LIVE now that the candidate pool is no longer empty: this endpoint's provider orders `Random` on BOTH servers (MusicArtistSimilarItemsProvider.cs:50 / v10.11.8 LibraryController.cs:801) and Ferrofin emits `Random` too. parity_diff aligns arrays by key, so the ORDER is not gateable by any probe — which means the reads leg MUST stay pinned at a limit above the candidate pool, or the two servers will draw different SUBSETS and the row will diff on membership for a reason that is not a bug. reads.py:636 pins limit=1000 (rationale at reads.py:608-623). Do not lower it, and do not 'fix' a future red on this row by narrowing the compare.

WHAT THIS ROW CLOSED, with the before-values: Path (J '/media/synth/music/Artist 01' vs H '/config/metadata/artists/Artist 01' — H's old value was the CORRECT path for the by-name row it actually had), CanDelete (J true, H false — kinds::can_delete is already a faithful !IsAccessedByName), and a missing RecursiveItemCount (J 3). All three measured equal now.

TWO DIVERGENCES THIS ROW STILL CARRIES that the harness cannot see, recorded here so they are not mistaken for settled. Both are masked by parity_diff.VOLATILE, which was NOT widened for either — they were already in it. (1) ParentId: Jellyfin materializes a physical `Folder` row per media path and parents the artist to it (F 0 such rows vs J 9); Ferrofin parents to the CollectionFolder. The id derivation already reproduces exactly — md5_le('MediaBrowser.Controller.Entities.Folder' + '/media/synth/music') = f87561f1dd83cf1f019883e008f5f307, which is the id Jellyfin stores — so closing it is mechanical, but it changes the ancestor chain of every scanned item and needs its own measured change plus the perf gate. (2) UserData.Key: C# UserDataManager.cs:239-245 uses item.GetUserDataKeys()[0], so Jellyfin emits 'Artist-Artist 01' where Ferrofin emits the raw guid '0f399324-0034-0734-bea4-1ffc13aaddc4'. kinds.rs and user_data_keys.rs already COMPUTE the right keys; the gap is plumbing in user_data_manager.rs::empty_row / dto_service.rs::empty_user_data_dto, and it is server-wide, not artist-specific.
- ⚖️ `GET /Items/{itemId}` — jellyfin-bug, on two independent fields — but see the NOT EXHAUSTIVE note at the end: these two arms do not account for every divergence on this row. (1) BITRATE. Ferrofin reports MediaStreams[video].BitRate as container-minus-other-streams and MediaSources[].Bitrate as the stream sum; Jellyfin 10.11.8 falls the video stream back to formatInfo.BitRate verbatim (v10.11.8 ProbeResultNormalizer.cs:978-987) and then InferTotalBitrate(true) (PRN.cs:259-264), which double-counts the audio. Measured on the fixture: the file is 775053 B / 1.023 s = 6,060,832 bps, so Jellyfin's MediaSources[].Bitrate of 6,253,020 exceeds what the file physically contains, while Ferrofin's 6,061,020 matches ffprobe's own format.bit_rate. Upstream fixed this itself in PR #16248 (master PRN.cs:979-991 comments that formatInfo.BitRate is the container, not the video bitrate) and Ferrofin ports the fixed form. (2) PROBE-DTO NULLABILITY (Level / IsAVC / Width / Height). Upstream MASTER declares all four nullable — MediaBrowser.MediaEncoding/Probing/MediaStreamInfo.cs:93 `public int? Width`, :107 `public int? Height`, :170 `public int? Level`, :233 `public bool? IsAvc` — so a stream for which ffprobe emits no such key keeps null. v10.11.8 declares the same four as non-nullable value types at the same file's :95 `public int Width`, :109 `public int Height`, :172 `public int Level`, :235 `public bool IsAvc`, so an absent key deserializes to the CLR default and 10.11.8 then emits `Level: 0` and `IsAVC: false` on EVERY stream — audio and subtitle included, where neither value is meaningful — plus `Width: 0`/`Height: 0` on a text subtitle. Ferrofin ports MASTER and is correct. MEASURED LIVE 2026-08-30 on the loop pair, GET /Items/{id}?fields=MediaSources,MediaStreams and POST /Items/{id}/PlaybackInfo, item d37ecb9d75b0c0a8e9ecb0a864ec670e (Movie 0001), after re-probing all 500 correlated movies on Ferrofin: video stream identical on both (Level 12, IsAVC true, 320x240); audio stream J=`Level:0, IsAVC:false` vs H=both keys ABSENT; subrip subtitle J=`Level:0, IsAVC:false, Width:0, Height:0` vs H=all four keys ABSENT. *** CORRECTION TO BATCH A3 (this entry supersedes it). A3 (commit e4f1b37) read the four fields off v10.11.8 only, called Ferrofin's nullable form a bug, and changed both the probing DTO and ProbeResultNormalizer to fabricate 0/false. That was a REGRESSION against the project rule that the WIRE CONTRACT is pinned to 10.11.8 while the BEHAVIOUR Ferrofin ports is upstream master, and it was reverted in batch M1 (probing/dtos.rs back to Option<i32>/Option<bool>; probe_result_normalizer.rs back to master's shape — Width/Height/Level in the shared initializer at master PRN.cs:702-714, IsAVC in the video arm only at master PRN.cs:794). The diff above is the expected consequence and is NOT a reason to change Ferrofin back, and NOT a reason to widen parity_diff.VOLATILE. A3's OTHER probing change is CORRECT and stands: codec_tag_string binds the plain JSON key (v10.11.8 MediaStreamInfo.cs:206), because the trailing-`?` typo is a master-only regression (master :204) that stops the key deserializing at all. *** OWNER CALL PENDING on the bitrate arithmetic: porting 10.11.8's instead would make this row body-diffable at the cost of being wrong. OPEN QUESTION, NOT AN AGENT DECISION — MediaStream.LocalizedLanguage: upstream master populates it for audio and subtitle streams (master ProbeResultNormalizer.cs:779 in the subtitle arm and the matching audio arm), but the vendored 10.11.8 OpenAPI declares MediaStream with `additionalProperties: false`, so emitting it makes every body carrying a language-tagged stream schema_valid:false. A3 withheld it from the wire; batch M1 deliberately left that as-is pending the owner's answer. The two halves are: master emits the field; the pinned contract forbids it. Whoever answers, record the decision here and either serialize the field or state why the contract wins. NOTE: the 'instance/probe-noise' text this row carried before A3 was DISPROVEN — running both containers' ffprobe binaries over the same file gives byte-identical JSON (7.1.4 vs 7.1.3-Jellyfin), and ffprobe emits no per-stream bit_rate at all. A3's other real fixes stand: MediaSources[].Name ports BaseItem.GetMediaSourceName (file stem, not item.Name), and the People[].Role read projection no longer NULLIFs the empty string C# always emits. LAB-STATE RECORD, so it is not lost (batch M1): the re-probe M1 needed in order to see the code change at all (the four values are PERSISTED at probe time, so a container rebuild alone changes nothing) also cleared a set of TMDB-scraped fields Ferrofin was carrying on the five correlated movies and Jellyfin was not. The pre-M1 run recorded them as Ferrofin extras — CommunityRating, OfficialRating, OriginalTitle, Overview, PremiereDate, ProviderIds.Tmdb, ExternalUrls[Name=TMDB], plus Taglines[] (J len 0, H len 1) and PrimaryImageAspectRatio. They did NOT come back on a second `metadataRefreshMode=FullRefresh&replaceAllMetadata=true`, and the reason is NOT that the remote fetch is unreachable — that claim was made without measuring and is false. TMDB IS reachable from the container (api.themoviedb.org answers HTTP 401). The FullRefresh was gated off BEFORE any HTTP call, by provider_manager.rs:967, on two conditions together: the item's is_locked=true (the same LockData divergence this row carries) and an empty MetadataFetchers list for that library. So the extras were one-time state from an earlier lab epoch AND the experiment that was supposed to reproduce them could not fire. TODO (NOT accepted): re-run with is_locked cleared and the library's metadata downloaders enabled, and either reproduce the extras — in which case the real question is why Jellyfin's fetch does not populate the same fields off the same `uniqueid type=imdb tt0111161` in the fixture movie.nfo — or confirm the fields are gone for good. *** NOT EXHAUSTIVE — the two arms above do not account for the whole row. Live on the pair 2026-08-30, GET /Items/{itemId} also carries: (a) BackdropImageTags J=len 7 H=len 0 — Ferrofin serves no backdrop tags for these movies where Jellyfin serves seven; this is UNRESOLVED and is NOT explained by either arm above, and it is not obviously lab state either, so it needs its own investigation before anyone calls this row understood; (b) UserData.LastPlayedDate differing by a few seconds, which is the probe's own playstate write landing at slightly different wall-clock times on the two servers; and (c) MediaStreams[0].BitRate, which IS arm (1) and is listed here only so a reader does not count it twice. Whoever next touches this row: (a) is the open one.
- ⚖️ `GET /Items/{itemId}/PlaybackInfo` — jellyfin-bug, on two independent fields — but see the NOT EXHAUSTIVE note at the end: these two arms do not account for every divergence on this row. (1) BITRATE. Ferrofin reports MediaStreams[video].BitRate as container-minus-other-streams and MediaSources[].Bitrate as the stream sum; Jellyfin 10.11.8 falls the video stream back to formatInfo.BitRate verbatim (v10.11.8 ProbeResultNormalizer.cs:978-987) and then InferTotalBitrate(true) (PRN.cs:259-264), which double-counts the audio. Measured on the fixture: the file is 775053 B / 1.023 s = 6,060,832 bps, so Jellyfin's MediaSources[].Bitrate of 6,253,020 exceeds what the file physically contains, while Ferrofin's 6,061,020 matches ffprobe's own format.bit_rate. - ⚖️ `POST /Library/Movies/Added` — jellyfin-bug-not-ported (the SELECTOR half; the refresh half is confirmed live on Ferrofin by j_library_webhooks — create by path, id-matched NFO refresh, unmatched-id negative control). Jellyfin 10.11.8 cannot match an external id on these four routes AT ALL: LibraryController.PostUpdatedSeries / PostUpdatedMovies (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Controllers/LibraryController.cs:571-635) query with `DtoOptions(false)`, whose ctor sets `Fields = []` (MediaBrowser.Controller/Dto/DtoOptions.cs:29-37), so BaseItemRepository.ApplyNavigations skips `Include(e => e.Provider)` (Jellyfin.Server.Implementations/Item/BaseItemRepository.cs:442-444) and DeserializeBaseItem never fills ProviderIds (:895-897) — the in-memory `string.Equals(id, i.GetProviderId(...))` filter therefore compares every item against null. Measured twice on the C4 lab pair 2026-08-30 (F=18296, J=18297): (a) a supplied id matches NOTHING even when the item demonstrably carries it — Jellyfin's own DTO reports Movie 0001 as ProviderIds {Imdb: tt0111161} and `POST /Library/Movies/Updated?imdbId=tt0111161` produced no `will be refreshed` line in 20 s; a probe series carrying Tvdb 990002 was still at its old Overview 60 s after `?tvdbId=990002`, and the same probe pair run through the journey leaves all four rows H=True J=False. (b) with the id OMITTED, `string.Equals(null, null)` is TRUE, so a bare `POST /Library/Series/Added` reported EVERY series (logged: the probe plus Series 01-08 'will be refreshed' in one burst) — a whole-TV-library refresh from an empty request. Ferrofin pushes the id into the SQL query instead (`paths_by_provider_id` → InternalItemsQuery::any_provider_id_equals, crates/ferrofin-api/src/handlers/library.rs), so a real id matches and an absent id is a no-op: the route's documented intent, kept under the repo's 'don't port Jellyfin bugs' rule. These rows therefore cannot be deep_verified against this oracle; the journey records Ferrofin's effect and Jellyfin's silence rather than a green.

## Recorded note (NOT a divergence, NOT a verdict)

_A fact worth keeping next to an op whose verdict is earned elsewhere — a push probe, a write journey, an obsolete-endpoint citation — or next to one for which no body-level verdict is reachable, where the note records what WAS measured and what would verify the rest. It claims nothing about this row on its own._

- 📝 `GET /Channels/{channelId}/Features` — status-class-only (batch F6, category corrected in the reviewer-fix pass — this row carries NO deep_verified verdict in either direction, and the category now says so; it was filed `verified`, whose name reads as the verdict the row does not have): the previous `requires-channel-plugin` entry — "no tuner journey can reach these; they need a channel plugin on both servers" — described a missing probe as if it were a settled divergence, and a live Ferrofin bug sat behind it. It is TRUE that no `IChannel` implementation exists in either tree (`git grep -l IChannel -- '*.cs'` returns only IChannel.cs, IChannelManager.cs and their consumers on BOTH v10.11.8 and master), so no Channel item can exist without a .NET plugin — but that makes the answer channelId-INDEPENDENT on both servers and therefore fully diffable, which is exactly the argument sweep.py already applies to `groupId`. sweep.py now seeds `_plugin_channel` (a literal GUID, PATH_SCOPED to /Channels/ so it can never leak into the /LiveTv/ channel id space) and the rows are measured instead of skipped. On that one reachable arm Ferrofin WAS wrong and is now fixed: it answered 200 with a fabricated `ChannelFeatures` echoing the requested UUID (and an empty QueryResult for Items), where upstream resolves `GetChannel(id)` to null and hands it to `GetChannelProvider`, whose first statement is `ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(channel)` (v10.11.8 ChannelManager.cs:1177, master :1176 — byte-identical) and whose `ArgumentException` `ExceptionMiddleware` maps to 400. Both trees agree, so this was outcome 3 (Ferrofin matched NEITHER) and the fix is a faithful port, not a move toward 10.11.8. Measured on the F6 pair 2026-08-30: before, F 200 / J 400; after the rebuild, F 400 / J 400 on both routes and on two different GUIDs, with /Channels, /Channels/Features and /Channels/Items/Latest unchanged at 200-empty on both. Pinned by crates/ferrofin-api/tests/channels.rs. WHAT THE ROW ACTUALLY CARRIES, stated so no reader takes this text for a verdict: sweep.py writes `deep_verified`/`verification_method` ONLY inside its `hs == 200 and js == 200` branch, so a pair that agrees on a NON-2xx gets `{status_conformant: true, schema_valid: null, note: "H=400 J=400"}` and no verdict field at all. That is the honest state and not a gap to close: there is no 200 body on either server, ever, so there is nothing for a body diff to walk. CONTRACT NOTE, owner-visible: 400 is NOT among the responses the vendored 10.11.8 spec declares for either op — `.paths["/Channels/{channelId}/Features"].get.responses` and the `/Items` one are both [200,401,403,503]. The undeclared status is the SPEC's divergence, not Ferrofin's and not Jellyfin's: the C# reaches 400 by letting `ArgumentNullException` escape the action into `ExceptionMiddleware`, a path the generated OpenAPI never sees. Ferrofin now emits the same undeclared status as the oracle, so the wire is symmetric, and the contract-superset gate is untouched (it gates the ROUTE TABLE, not per-op response codes, and 400 is not 404). Recorded here because "the wire carries a status the vendored contract does not declare" is the owner's call to keep or to raise upstream, not an agent's. REAL vs BOGUS channelId, re-measured independently in the reviewer-fix pass 2026-08-30 on both servers and both routes: a literal GUID, the all-zero GUID, and a REAL Live TV channel id (d53240a1…, which both servers mint identically because `GetInternalChannelId` hashes the same tuner lineup) all answer 400 on Ferrofin AND on Jellyfin — including the id-space-crossing case, which is the one that would have caught a Live TV channel leaking into this controller. The collection controls are unchanged and identical on both: /Channels and /Channels/Items/Latest 200 `{"Items":[],"TotalRecordCount":0,"StartIndex":0}`, /Channels/Features 200 `[]`. So there is no "real channelId" on either server to answer differently, and the answer is genuinely channelId-independent. WHAT WOULD VERIFY THE POSITIVE ARM: an `IChannel` implementation registered on BOTH servers — on Jellyfin that is a .NET plugin, the owner-accepted native-plugin scope exclusion. Unlike the tuner (faked over HTTP by suite/perf/livetv-source.py, which is why Live TV is verified) this cannot be faked: IChannel is an in-process .NET interface with no wire surface to stand up, so there is nothing to port and nothing to fake. If channel providers are ever introduced on the Ferrofin side, the arm becomes reachable on ONE server only, which is still not a differential — the verification would then be against the C# by transliterated unit test, the way the Startup guard is pinned.
- 📝 `GET /Channels/{channelId}/Items` — status-class-only (batch F6, category corrected in the reviewer-fix pass — this row carries NO deep_verified verdict in either direction, and the category now says so; it was filed `verified`, whose name reads as the verdict the row does not have): the previous `requires-channel-plugin` entry — "no tuner journey can reach these; they need a channel plugin on both servers" — described a missing probe as if it were a settled divergence, and a live Ferrofin bug sat behind it.
