--- ALSO ON THIS ROW (merged from main) ---

requires-channel-plugin: the ChannelsController serves IChannel plugin channels, not Live TV — no tuner journey can reach these; they need a channel plugin on both servers. | security-fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1, cross-user authz): C# RequestHelpers.GetUserId (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Helpers/RequestHelpers.cs:67-85) has two halves: an absent/all-zero userId falls back to the caller, and a userId that is NOT the caller is honoured only for an ADMINISTRATOR, else SecurityException -> 403. Ferrofin ported only the first half here. Measured twice on the lane-3 pair (Ferrofin 18396 / Jellyfin 18397) with a second, non-admin account 's1probe' created on both: The route also took ?userId= without the gate; the sibling routes were measured BEFORE at 200 on Ferrofin against 403 on Jellyfin for a non-admin naming the admin's id, and this one is fixed with them. After the fix both servers return 403 for the same request, while an administrator naming another user's id and a non-administrator naming their own id still succeed on both (measured, twice). Regression tests in crates/ferrofin-api/tests/ cover all three cases per route. Fifth instance in this campaign of a C# authorization check Ferrofin served without one (A1 startup, B3 x29 Live TV routes, D2 get_lineups, F1 items); the campaign owes a dedicated audit of every [Authorize(Policy=...)] and RequestHelpers.GetUserId call site in the 10.11.8 controllers against Ferrofin's route guards. | empty-guid binding fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1 review pass, reviewer finding F2): ASP.NET binds an EMPTY or whitespace-only value for a `Guid?` to null (SimpleTypeModelBinder), so upstream answers `?userId=` and `?userId=%20` exactly as it answers a request with no userId at all; serde's `Option<Uuid>` tried to parse "" and made both a 400. S1 spread that pre-existing defect to five more routes when it gave previously parameter-less handlers a `Query<T>`. Re-measured on the lane-3 pair BEFORE the fix: `?userId=` and `?userId=%20` were 400 on Ferrofin against 200 on Jellyfin (404/404 where the probe id does not exist). FIXED AS A CLASS, not as an instance: every `Option<Uuid>` field of a `Query<...>` struct in ferrofin-api now binds through `handlers::query_parse::empty_as_none_uuid`. AFTER, the four spellings (absent / all-zero / empty / whitespace) agree on both servers across /Devices, /Channels, /Channels/Items/Latest, /LiveTv/Recordings/Folders, /LiveTv/Recordings/{recordingId}, /Audio/{itemId}/universal AND the four routes that carried the defect before S1 ever ran (/Items, /UserViews, /Persons, /Years). Request BODIES keep serde's strict parse on purpose: System.Text.Json rejects `"UserId": ""` upstream too.
- 📝 `POST /LiveTv/ChannelMappings` — jellyfin-bug, error path only: an unknown `ProviderId`, or a `TunerChannelId` that is not in the lineup, makes Jellyfin throw from an unguarded `.First(…)` → 500 (ListingsManager.cs:231 and :264 @ v10.11.8). Ferrofin answers 404 to both. The persistence order is ported as-is, not "fixed": upstream saves the configuration at :251 BEFORE the :264 throw, so a mapping keyed on a channel that does not exist is stored on both servers and then reported as not found — a dangling pair the match ladder simply never hits. The success path agrees and is the `effect` row j_livetv_admin measures.
- 📝 `DELETE /LiveTv/TunerHosts` — accepted-divergence, SIDE EFFECT ONLY (the verdict on this row is the configuration read-back, not this): `LiveTvController.DeleteTunerHost` (Jellyfin.Api/Controllers/LiveTvController.cs:1000-1006 @ v10.11.8) rewrites `LiveTvOptions.TunerHosts`, saves, and returns — it queues NO `RefreshGuideScheduledTask`, unlike `ListingsManager.DeleteListingsProvider` (src/Jellyfin.LiveTv/Listings/ListingsManager.cs:83-91) which does. So on Jellyfin the removed tuner's `LiveTvChannel` items LINGER in the library until the next guide refresh prunes them through `GuideManager.CleanDatabase`; on Ferrofin the channel rows are FK children of the tuner host (`crates/ferrofin-db/migrations/0003_live_tv.sql:36-38`, `FK_LiveTvChannels_TunerHosts_TunerHostId … ON DELETE CASCADE`, and the programmes hang off the channels the same way at :60-62, `FK_LiveTvPrograms_Channels_ChannelId`) and go with it in the same statement. Measured on the loop pair 2026-08-30, both servers taking a case-flipped id and answering 204: immediately after the DELETE F reports 0 channels AND 0 programmes (the airings are FK children of the channel rows, so they go too) where it had 2 / 19200, while J still reports 2 / 338. Both servers converge on the SAME end state — Jellyfin's next refresh deletes exactly those rows, because `GuideManager.CleanDatabase` (src/Jellyfin.LiveTv/Guide/GuideManager.cs:147-148, 331-360 @ v10.11.8) removes every `LiveTvChannel`/`LiveTvProgram` the pass did not re-emit, and a channel of a tuner that no longer exists is never re-emitted — and what differs is the window in between, during which Jellyfin serves channels no tuner can stream. Ferrofin keeps the cascade (see the 'don't port Jellyfin bugs' rule): a dangling channel is a 5xx waiting for a client that tries to play it. NOT PORTED, deliberately, and NOT hidden: the journey asserts this op on the configuration, which is what both servers agree the delete means, and this note is the record of what it does not assert.
- 📝 `GET /MusicGenres/{genreName}` — verified 2026-08-31 (batch F2, revised after review): 3/3 legs clean, 34 fields compared, measured twice. This row had NO classification before F2 and one probe leg.

`MusicGenresController.GetMusicGenre` carries the SAME slug branch as the genres controller (`GetItemFromSlugName<MusicGenre>`, '-'->'&'/'/'/'?', three lookups, creating nothing) but NOT the same fallback: it keeps `if (item is null) return NotFound();` on BOTH trees (v10.11.8 :164-167, master :165-168, where the action is additionally `[Obsolete("Use GetGenre instead")]`), where `GenresController` has `item ??= new Genre()`. That asymmetry is upstream's and it is the port. It is also how the wire caught a wrong port: the first cut copied the genres fallback here and answered 200 where a live 10.11.8 answers 404 (`/MusicGenres/R-B` J=404) — no unit test would have found it, because the two controllers' C# is identical until the last five lines.

REAL WIRE DIVERGENCE FOUND AND FIXED IN THE REVIEW PASS (the reason this row is 34 fields and not 28). The two 404 legs used to be scored clean by a harness branch that treated 'both servers, same status, neither body JSON' as agreement — but `reads.py::token_get` discarded the body of EVERY non-200, so 'neither body is JSON' was a property of the probe, not of the responses. Measured directly with curl: J returned `{"type":"https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc9110#section-15.5.5","title":"Not Found","status":404,"traceId":"00-…"}` and H returned `{"error":"not found: music genre R-B"}`. Two different documents, scored as one clean leg.
Fixed on BOTH sides. Harness: `token_probe` keeps the body at any status and the multi legs diff it like any other body, so an error body is now part of the comparison; a leg where neither server sent ANY body is recorded as `status-class` evidence (the ledger's weakest verdict) rather than as a body diff, and two different non-JSON bodies behind the same status are a mismatch. Server: `ApiError::Problem(ProblemStatus::NotFound)` renders ASP.NET's RFC 9110 ProblemDetails, and this handler uses it because its C# `NotFound()` takes no argument — ASP.NET then fills the body from `ApiBehaviorOptions.ClientErrorMapping`.
It is deliberately a PER-CALL-SITE variant and not a global change to how Ferrofin renders errors, because Jellyfin's error bodies are not uniform. Measured on the live 10.11.8 container in one session: `NotFound()` -> ProblemDetails; `GET /Users/{unknown}` (`NotFound("User not found")`) -> the bare JSON string `"User not found"`; a model-binding failure -> a `ValidationProblemDetails` with an `errors` map; an unauthenticated request -> an EMPTY 401 with no content type; a wrong-verb request -> an empty 405. A blanket ProblemDetails would have traded one divergence for four. Those four call-site families are un-ported and NOT owned by this batch — they belong to the rows that probe them, and they are now visible to any multi row that probes one, because the harness no longer throws error bodies away.
`traceId` is the one field on the VOLATILE list for this: it is ASP.NET's per-request W3C trace-context id, which two independent instances cannot agree on and one instance never repeats. Scoped to that exact key; `type`, `title`, `status` and `errors` are all still compared, which is what makes an error-body diff mean anything.
- 📝 `POST /SyncPlay/SetRepeatMode` — verified-by-push-probe: the response body carries no signal here — the observable behaviour is the SyncPlayCommand/SyncPlayGroupUpdate pushed to member sockets. Verified end-to-end over two real WebSockets by `suite/run.sh push` (suite/ws/probe_remote_control.py).
- 📝 `POST /SyncPlay/SetShuffleMode` — verified-by-push-probe: the response body carries no signal here — the observable behaviour is the SyncPlayCommand/SyncPlayGroupUpdate pushed to member sockets. Verified end-to-end over two real WebSockets by `suite/run.sh push` (suite/ws/probe_remote_control.py).

## Lab state (NOT accepted — the servers' DATA differ, not their code)

_The diff is real and is NOT dropped from the compare. Its cause was measured, and it is drift in a long-lived shared lab rather than a Ferrofin behaviour. On the authoritative freshly-wiped pair these must come back clean; one that does not is a bug, not an accepted state._

- 🧪 `GET /Items/Root` — lab-state (NOT a Ferrofin divergence, and NOT an accepted one — this row renders under "Lab state" and is counted there, never in the accepted tally). Re-measured 2026-08-31 on the lane pair: the body diffs 22 leaves and exactly ONE differs, `ChildCount` J=6 H=5. The whole of that difference is a `Recordings` CollectionFolder (`/config/root/default/Recordings`, id 79a2726d…) that Jellyfin grew from an earlier DVR journey and this Ferrofin does not have; Ferrofin currently holds 0 recordings to Jellyfin's 12. On a freshly-wiped pair this row must come back clean, and if it does not it is a bug. PREVIOUS CLASSIFICATION, KEPT AS A RECORD BECAUSE IT WAS REAL AND IS NOW CLOSED: this row used to carry `accepted-divergence (structural: where the playlists plugin folder hangs)` — Ferrofin stored the playlists folder as a real DB child of the UserRootFolder where upstream parents it to the AggregateFolder (`LibraryManager.CreateRootFolder()`, v10.11.8 LibraryManager.cs:838-887, `folder.ParentId = rootFolder.Id`), which showed as the folder sorting INLINE by SortName instead of being appended last (`UserRootFolder.GetEligibleChildrenForRecursiveChildren` = `base.Children` + `list.AddRange(RootFolder.VirtualChildren)`, an APPEND, with `IsPreSorted => true`). That divergence was PORTED OUT by the AggregateFolder work, and the fix is measured, not assumed: on the pair today both servers list Playlists LAST, and the playlists folder's id and Path agree byte for byte (1071671e7bffa0532e930debee501d2e, /config/data/playlists). The old text also asserted "the row's own body diffs CLEAN and this op stays deep-verified", which was false while the row was red — that claim is what put a live red under the accepted heading, and it is the reason this row is now categorised by its measurement instead of by its story.
- 🧪 `GET /LiveTv/Recordings/Groups/{groupId}` — lab-state (NOT accepted): the OBSOLETE-endpoint substance below is intact and still measured, but the row is RED and the label must say why. `recording_seed_present` is J=True H=False — Jellyfin holds 12 recordings left behind by an earlier DVR journey and this Ferrofin holds 0 (re-measured 2026-08-31). That fact is recorded on purpose, so the `recording_id_does_not_resolve` leg can never silently degrade to "there was nothing to try"; it is lab drift, not a behaviour, and on a freshly-wiped pair the row must come back clean. Ferrofin CAN record: `GET`/`DELETE /LiveTv/Recordings/{recordingId}` are both green on both servers in the same run, so the empty inventory is the journey cleaning up after itself, not a gap. ALSO ON THIS ROW — verified 2026-08-30: OBSOLETE endpoint. LiveTvController.GetRecordingGroup (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Controllers/LiveTvController.cs) is [Obsolete] and its whole body is `return NotFound();`; `git grep RecordingGroup v10.11.8 -- '*.cs'` returns only the two controller signatures, so there is no LiveTvManager.GetRecordingGroups, no group-id derivation and no DTO anywhere in the tree. The response is groupId-INDEPENDENT, which makes the old note ('needs a recordings-group probe once a recording exists') work that cannot exist: recordings DO exist on both servers now and the answer is still 404 for those very recording ids. Property-verified live by reads.py (recording_group_invariants), measured twice: the nil GUID, a random GUID, a real Live TV channel id and a real recording id all 404 on both servers; a malformed groupId is a route-binding 400 on both (exact status, not class); an anonymous caller gets 401 on both. It is a property row and not a body diff because there is no 200 body to diff, ever. The 404 BODY differs only by the server-wide error envelope (Jellyfin ProblemDetails vs Ferrofin {"error":…}), an existing project-wide divergence, not an op-level one.

## No probe yet (NOT accepted, NOT verified)

_An absence of evidence: no layer exercises this op, or the one that would needs credentials or a device the lab does not have. These sat under "accepted — not a bug" until this section existed, which read an untested op as a closed question._

- 🕳 `POST /Items/RemoteSearch/Trailer` — remote-opt-in: OMDb is the ONLY provider implementing IRemoteMetadataProvider<Trailer, TrailerInfo> upstream (v10.11.8: `git grep "Trailer, TrailerInfo"` matches only MediaBrowser.Providers/Plugins/Omdb/OmdbItemProvider.cs:31 plus ItemLookupController), and Jellyfin ships a hardcoded key — OmdbProvider.cs:257 `const string Url = "https://www.omdbapi.com?apikey=2c9d9507";`. Ferrofin's OMDb client is inert until FERROFIN_OMDB_KEY / config `omdb_api_key` is set (the owner-listed scope divergence in CLAUDE.md "Current scope"; omdb.rs `enabled()` is `!api_key.is_empty()`), so a keyless Ferrofin answers [] where the oracle answers 6 rows. Measured twice on the C1 lab pair: F 200 [] / J 200 six results for {"Name":"Inception","Year":2010}; the bogus-term case is 200 [] on both. NOT a routing, auth or DTO gap — the same body against /Items/RemoteSearch/Movie returns matching TMDB results on both, and curl from inside the Ferrofin container reaches omdbapi.com fine. The two real port gaps this row would otherwise have surfaced ARE fixed and unit-tested (the index-number echo of OmdbItemProvider.cs:185-192, and the `_libraryManager.ParseName` pass at :110-117 that lifts the year out of the title); they cannot be observed live until the lab's Ferrofin service is given an OMDb key. Un-classify by setting FERROFIN_OMDB_KEY on the Ferrofin service only and re-running the j_remote_search_identify journey, which already probes this op on both servers.
- 🕳 `POST /Items/{itemId}/RemoteSearch/Subtitles/{subtitleId}` — remote-opt-in: needs an opensubtitles.com account and the public internet. With OPENSUBTITLES_USERNAME/PASSWORD/API_KEY set, the parity leg installs the plugin on the oracle, configures both, and the remote-subtitle journey (search -> download -> stream read-back -> provider file) runs live.
- 🕳 `GET /Providers/Subtitles/Subtitles/{subtitleId}` — remote-opt-in: needs an opensubtitles.com account and the public internet. With OPENSUBTITLES_USERNAME/PASSWORD/API_KEY set, the parity leg installs the plugin on the oracle, configures both, and the remote-subtitle journey (search -> download -> stream read-back -> provider file) runs live.

## Open work (NOT accepted — a named divergence still to port)

_Real gaps with an owner and a path, kept OUT of the accepted section: rendering an unfinished port under "accepted" is how a gap quietly becomes a decision._

- ⛏ `GET /Genres` — open-work (NOT accepted) — SUPERSEDES the 2026-08-11 'verified: QueryResult<BaseItemDto>, status+schema conformant' seed (a shape check that never diffed the listing) and the 2026-08-31 revision of it. Measured twice on 2026-08-31 after the by-name ports: H lists [Action, Ambient, Comedy, Drama, Jazz, Rock, SciFi, Thriller], J lists [Action, Comedy, Drama, Jazz, News, SciFi, Thriller]. Two separate axes, one upstream and one a REAL Ferrofin gap that is named here rather than hidden.

AXIS 1 (H's 'extra' Ambient and Rock) — the same mechanism as GET /MusicGenres axis 1, and NOT a missing port: upstream's `GroupBy(PresentationUniqueKey)` collapse IS now implemented in Ferrofin (`item_repository::push_representative_rank`, see that row). Both servers hold Genre rows named Ambient/Jazz/Rock; on Jellyfin all three were lazily created by `CreateItemByName` at 2026-08-31 01:16:57 with a NULL PresentationUniqueKey and a NULL DateLastRefreshed (read out of the container's own DB), so they form one NULL group and surface as the single representative Jazz. Ferrofin's carry Genre-Ambient/-Jazz/-Rock — the value `MetadataService.BeforeSaveInternal` would give them on their first refresh — so all three list. On a freshly provisioned pair neither server holds those rows at all, so this axis should not appear in the end-of-campaign sweep.

AXIS 2 (FERROFIN GAP, open work, NOT accepted) — J lists a genre 'News' that H does not. Root cause measured, not guessed: J's `news` value is carried by 676 `LiveTvProgram` rows plus 15 Movie rows (its EPG), while Ferrofin has NO ItemValues row for `news` at all — because Ferrofin stores EPG programmes in its own `FerrofinLiveTvPrograms` table (ferrofin-livetv/src/manager.rs:542) and never materializes them as `BaseItems`, so they contribute to no by-name aggregate. Upstream persists a programme through `BaseItemRepository.SaveItems`, whose second half writes `ItemValues`/`ItemValuesMap` for it, which is why every EPG genre shows on Jellyfin's /Genres. UN-DEFER PATH: make the guide refresh write a `LiveTvProgram` BaseItems row per programme (or, narrower, project the guide's genres into `ItemValues` alongside the FerrofinLiveTvPrograms row) and re-measure this row plus /Items/Filters2. Still NOT done and not claimed as done — it is a Live TV storage-model change, outside the four by-name rows batch F2 owns.
- ⛏ `GET /Items` — open-work (NOT accepted): the UserRootFolder browse took the WRONG CODE PATH entirely, and the harness could not see it. C# ItemsController.GetItems (ItemsController.cs:307, 525-529) answers a request that is not recursive, names no ids, and whose parent resolves to the UserRootFolder from `folder.GetChildren(user, true)` — not from a query — so no sort, no paging and none of the request's filters apply, and the AggregateFolder's virtual children are APPENDED (UserRootFolder.cs:96-102, `list.AddRange`), not merged into the sort. LibraryManager.GetParentItem(null, userId) resolves an ABSENT parentId to that same folder, so a bare GET /Items is in the branch too. Measured on the lab's 10.11.8 (18097): /Items?userId=…, plus parentId={root} with sortBy=SortName&sortOrder=Descending, with limit=2&startIndex=1, and with includeItemTypes=Movie ALL return the identical seven root rows in the identical order with Playlists LAST, and StartIndex echoes the request. Ferrofin answered the bare form with the WHOLE SERVER (554 rows to Jellyfin's 7), honoured sort/limit/startIndex, and sorted the playlists folder inline (its SortName 'playlists' landing before 'shows (synth)'). Ported 2026-08-30 (InternalItemsQuery.user_root_children, item_repository::user_root_children, and a leading virtual-children-last ORDER BY term in translate_query::append_order_by); all four shapes re-measured live and now agree with Jellyfin on branch, order and StartIndex. THE HARNESS BLIND SPOT WAS REAL AND IS CLOSED: parity_diff aligns arrays of OBJECTS by Path/Name/Id and never compares position, so the misordered row diffed clean; reads.py now runs this op as two legs, both under the existing ADDITIVE with_item_order projection (it adds `_ItemNameOrder`, a list of plain strings, which parity_diff compares POSITIONALLY), and the second leg is the user-root browse itself, carrying the sort/paging/filter parameters upstream ignores. REMAINING RED on this long-lived drifted lab, instance state rather than code, to be re-scored on the fresh-pair sweep: leg 1 TotalRecordCount J=507 H=500 and a UserData.LastPlayedDate/BackdropImageTags difference on Movie 0001 (a write journey's leftovers), leg 2 the different library SETS (Jellyfin carries ParityCRUD + Recordings, Ferrofin a 'Movies (synth) Renamed' rename ghost) — which is also the whole of the _ItemNameOrder mismatch: positions 0-1 agree, positions 2-5 are the drifted libraries, and Playlists is last on BOTH.

open-work — NOT a divergence in this handler, a LAB ARTEFACT plus one closed security gap, both recorded so neither goes quiet. (1) The movie leg of the reads.py `GET /Items` row is red because the lab's Jellyfin carries leftover `…/library/d3/…/backdropN.png` files that assets.py's upload/reorder sequence never removed, so `BackdropImageTags` is non-empty on J and empty on F for Movie 0001. The files are not in the media fixture; the residue accumulates across runs (1 file, then 2). Owner: whichever batch owns assets.py — its write rows must reap what they upload. It will not exist on the freshly-wiped pair the final sweep runs on. Deliberately NOT deleted by hand: that would be a one-sided mutation of the pair. (2) Closed in the F1 review pass, recorded because it was live until then: a non-administrator passing ANOTHER user's `userId` was answered 200 with that user's data on Ferrofin and 403 on Jellyfin. `RequestHelpers.GetUserId` throws `SecurityException("Forbidden")` unless the caller is the named user or an administrator (v10.11.8 RequestHelpers.cs:77-82), mapped to 403 by `ExceptionMiddleware:129`. Measured on the lane-3 pair with a freshly created non-admin account, before: F 200 / J 403 on `/Items?userId={admin}`, `/Users/{admin}/Items` and `/Items/Suggestions?userId={admin}`; after: 403 on both, with the all-zero userId still 200 on both. The probe user was created and deleted symmetrically on both servers. | jellyfin-bug (nil-guid parameters), measured 2026-08-30 in the S1 review pass while verifying the empty-guid fix — recorded rather than left unmentioned. Jellyfin THROWS on an all-zero guid passed to a parameter that is not `userId`: `parentId=00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000` is 400 with the middleware's generic body `Error processing request.` (an unhandled ArgumentException from GetItemById/GetUserById — "Guid can't be empty" — not a designed 400), where Ferrofin treats the nil as absent and answers 200. `userId` is exempt on both sides because RequestHelpers.GetUserId maps a nil to the caller before anything looks it up. Measured on the lane-3 pair, admin token, all-zero value: GET /Items?parentId= (J400 H200), /Items?userId=&parentId= (J400 H200), /Items/Filters (J400 H200), /Items/Filters2 (J400 H200), /Years (J400 H200), /Shows/NextUp?seriesId=&parentId= (J400 H200), /Shows/{seriesId}/Episodes?seasonId=&adjacentTo= (J400 H404), /Trailers?parentId= (J500 H200 — an outright unhandled exception). Ferrofin is kept correct per the standing policy (do not port Jellyfin bugs); a 500 is never the right answer and neither is a leaked ArgumentException. NOT deferred and NOT accepted by an agent: this is a measured divergence awaiting the owner's call. UN-DEFER PATH if the owner wants byte parity on the refusal: reject a nil guid with 400 on the non-userId item-lookup parameters (parentId, seriesId, seasonId, adjacentTo, startItemId, appearsInItemId) in handlers::query_parse, leave `userId` alone, and add the eight probes above to the read layer as a status-class row.

--- ALSO ON THIS ROW (merged from worktree-dv-lane1) ---

open-work (NOT accepted) — the LiveTvProgram half is PORTED here; what is left is named. Ferrofin never wrote a `BaseItems` row for a Live TV airing: `insert_programs` wrote only `FerrofinLiveTvPrograms` and `program_entity()` built the item in memory at read time for `/LiveTv/Programs` alone, so the recursive item universe held zero airings — measured F 0 / J 338 while `/LiveTv/Programs` answered 338 on BOTH, and `GET /Items/{programId}` was F 404 / J 200. Upstream stores an airing exactly as it stores a channel (`CreateItems(newPrograms, currentChannel, …)` / `UpdateItemsAsync`, v10.11.8 src/Jellyfin.LiveTv/Guide/GuideManager.cs:277-291) and prunes it with the `CleanDatabase(newProgramIdList, [LiveTvProgram])` one line below the channel one (:145-148). `sync_program_items` is that port, on batch G5's channel mechanism rather than beside it: the same bounded field-wise merge (`projection::program_item_update`, so a guide refresh cannot revert a metadata edit on the 24h timer) and the same global clean. Verified live on the lane pair after a `RefreshGuide`: LiveTvProgram F 338 / J 338, `GET /Items/{programId}` 200 on both for the SAME id with ChannelName/ChannelNumber, and `/Genres` picked up the guide-only `News` row it was missing. The stored row now matches the oracle column for column (ParentId=channel, TopParentId=Live TV view, IsVirtualItem=1, UnratedType=LiveTvProgram, MediaType=Unknown, Width/Height=0, ShowId, PresentationUniqueKey NULL). A REGRESSION THIS ROW SHIPPED AND THEN CLOSED, recorded so it cannot come back invisibly: porting the bare `GROUP BY "PresentationUniqueKey"` (upstream's own `dbQuery.GroupBy(e => e.PresentationUniqueKey)`, BaseItemRepository.cs:417) made a KEYLESS row an item-loss bug, because SQLite groups NULLs together. Migration 0027's backfill was scoped `"TopParentId" IS NOT NULL`, and an item-by-name row has TopParentId NULL by construction, so it missed exactly the rows that were keyless: `GET /Items?userId=…&ids=<three Person ids>` measured F 1 / J 3 on the lane pair, and a mixed six-id request returned one item. It also wrote the row's OWN ID as the value, where the five overriding kinds spell it `{Type}-{Name.RemoveDiacritics()}` (Genre.cs:37-47, `Artist` for MusicArtist at MusicArtist.cs:152) — the oracle stores `Person-Bob Parity`, `MusicGenre-Jazz`. Both halves are fixed: migration 0028 widens the scope and narrows the value to the kinds whose key really is their own id, and `ferrofin_db::presentation_key::backfill_by_name_presentation_keys` repairs the five by-name kinds in Rust (SQLite cannot express `RemoveDiacritics`) through the SAME helper the inserts call. Re-measured live after the rebuild: F 3 / J 3 on both routes, mixed six-id F 6, and the lane's stored keys read back `Person-Bob Parity` / `MusicGenre-Jazz`. The probe blind spot that let it through is named too: every leg this batch added to GET /Items is scoped to the recursive user universe, which is the one place TopParentId-less rows are already filtered out, so the guard is the unit test `item_repository::presentation_key_backfill_rescues_a_grouped_query`, not a parity leg.

STILL OPEN on this row. Census re-measured live on the recursive universe after the fix (F total 558 / J 630), every difference named: (1) Live TV RECORDINGS as library items — Jellyfin scans `%AppDataPath%/livetv/recordings` into real rows (measured now: Movie F 500 / J 531, Recording F 0 / J 2); Ferrofin keeps them in `FerrofinLiveTvRecordings` and projects none (`projection::recording_entity`'s doc comment names it). Same mechanism as the programme mirror, third half of the guide. (2) PLAYLISTS: Playlist F 0 / J 25 and ManualPlaylistsFolder F 0 / J 1 — the playlists library and its entries are outside Ferrofin's recursive universe. (3) PHYSICAL FOLDERS beneath each virtual library: Folder F 0 / J 10. Jellyfin materializes the physical folder under every library (`/media/synth/movies`, `/media/synth/music`, `/media/synth/tv`, `/config/data/collections`, `/config/data/livetv/recordings` and the five per-recording folders) as a `Folder` row inside the universe; Ferrofin materializes none. (4) CollectionFolder F 1 / J 0 — the SAME subject as (3) seen from the other side, and NOT lab drift: `/config/data/collections` is a `CollectionFolder` row inside Ferrofin's recursive universe, while on Jellyfin that path is a plain `Folder` and the virtual `Collections` library row itself is excluded. Ferrofin is exposing the library row where upstream exposes its physical child. This was NOT named in the previous version of this row; it is named now. (5) MusicArtist F 0 / J 3 and UserView F 0 / J 1 — the same NULL-`TopParentId` family, different write paths. None of the five is accepted; all five are the next ports on this op.
- ⛏ `POST /Items/RemoteSearch/Apply/{itemId}` — open-work (NOT accepted): Apply itself is correct on both servers; what diverges is whether its effect SURVIVES a library scan. Measured on the lab pair, reproduced three times: Apply into a library with every "Metadata downloaders" box cleared leaves Movie 0497 identical on both (ProductionYear null, Genres [], Studios [], ProviderIds {Tmdb: 603}, and Name / RunTimeTicks / ImageTags / People untouched) - the `unlocked` half of the journey's evidence matches field for field. The journey then runs the library scan to completion on each server and reads the item back again: Jellyfin holds the cleared state, Ferrofin reverts to ProductionYear 2020 / Genres [Comedy, SciFi] / Studios [Ferrofin Studios], re-applied from /media/synth/movies/Movie 0497 (2020)/movie.nfo. That is the whole of the row's `unlocked_after_scan` diff. CAUSE: upstream gates every local metadata reader on a change monitor - v10.11.8 MediaBrowser.XbmcMetadata/Providers/BaseNfoProvider.cs `HasChanged` is `nfoLastWriteTimeUtc - item.DateLastSaved > TimeSpan.FromMinutes(1)`, and MetadataService.GetProviders (:613-708) returns an EMPTY provider list when no provider reports a change, so a scan over an item saved more recently than its sidecar runs no providers and merges nothing. Ferrofin's scan re-derives every item from disk on every pass: library_scan.rs calls `fetch_local_nfo` unconditionally for any unlocked row and `save_scanned_items` writes the result over the stored columns. UN-DEFER PATH, and why it is not a ride-along: the port needs three things together - (1) populate `BaseItems.DateLastSaved` on every save (nothing writes it today; the column exists and is bound, but only ever from an entity carrying None), (2) gate `fetch_local_nfo` / `fetch_remote_metadata` on the sidecar-vs-DateLastSaved comparison, and (3) a third scan-upsert variant that PRESERVES the provider-supplied columns when no provider ran, or skipping the read would wipe the very fields it is meant to protect. (1) alone changes `Etag` (dto_service.rs:1260 hashes DateLastSaved) and the `minDateLastSaved` query filters on every DTO in the API, so it is a server-wide change needing its own batch and its own perf run. Recorded here rather than hidden: the journey deliberately forces the scan and waits for it, so this stays a deterministic red instead of a race whose colour depends on when the suite happened to run.
- ⛏ `GET /Items/Suggestions` — open-work (NOT accepted). `SuggestionsController.GetSuggestions` (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Controllers/SuggestionsController.cs:78-96) is ONE recursive user query projected with ALL DtoOptions fields, and Ferrofin's handler is a line-for-line port — the handler was never the bug. Random PERMUTES but does not change the set, so at `limit >= |universe|` the answer is fully determined and the probe's seven full-limit legs body-diff it honestly; only the narrow startIndex leg is projected, and only onto what a Random page genuinely determines. The row was red for FIVE independent causes. THREE ARE FIXED HERE. (a) The item-universe gap on its biggest kind: LiveTvProgram was F 0 / J 338 and is now 338/338 (see `GET /Items`). (b) FOUR DTO fields the all-fields projection is supposed to carry and Ferrofin assigned NOWHERE — `RecursiveItemCount` and the folder `UserData.PlayedPercentage`/`Played` (`Folder.FillUserDataDtoValues`, v10.11.8 MediaBrowser.Controller/Entities/Folder.cs:1798-1838, whose two gates are DIFFERENT: the count is gated on its field alone and the played numbers on `SupportsPlayedStatus`, which is why a MusicAlbum carries the count and no percentage); `CumulativeRunTimeTicks` (Emby.Server.Implementations/Dto/DtoService.cs:492-495, the folder branch, not under EnableUserData); `HasLyrics`, unconditional on every Audio DTO and a plain bool — `false`, not an absent key (DtoService.cs:308-311); and `SeriesStudio` for both Episode and Season (:1228-1234 and :1256-1262), which is `series.Studios.FirstOrDefault()` and so lives on a row the projected item is not. (c) The `PresentationUniqueKey` grouping, which the LiveTvProgram mirror made visible: see `GET /Items`. STILL OPEN, both named: the MusicArtist/UserView/Live TV RECORDINGS half of the universe gap, and the BitRate offset — that last one is NOT a Ferrofin defect and is classified on its own row (`GET /Items/{itemId}`, jellyfin-bug: 10.11.8 reports more bitrate than the file physically contains, master's PR #16248 fixed it and Ferrofin ports the fixed form). The remaining Movie 0401/Inception and UserData.LastPlayedDate rows in the current results are LAB DRIFT from other batches' write journeys on this long-lived pair, not defects; they clear on a freshly wiped pair. Previous text kept for the record: open-work (NOT accepted). The old text ('Random, so the set is non-deterministic') was only half true and it hid two real divergences. Random here PERMUTES but does not change the set: `SuggestionsController` runs ONE query (`OrderBy = [(Random, Descending)]`, Recursive, mediaType/type, IsVirtualItem=false) and Ferrofin's handler is a line-for-line port, so at `limit >= |universe|` the answer is fully determined and parity_diff aligns Items by Path. The probe was the problem: `limit=10` sampled 10 of 552 at random and could never diff. It is now eight legs: seven pinned at a full limit (per type), where Random only permutes and the whole body IS diffed, plus one narrow startIndex/limit leg. That narrow leg is the one place a projection is applied — a page of 3 out of 500 Random-ordered rows draws a different triple per call on BOTH servers (measured: three consecutive calls, three different triples each side), so it compares StartIndex, TotalRecordCount and the page length only. Diffing its Items would pin this row red forever and destroy its ability to signal when the two real divergences below are fixed; the projection is scoped to that leg alone and nothing was added to parity_diff.VOLATILE. Two causes remain, BOTH owned by other rows and deliberately left visible here rather than filtered out of the probe: (1) ITEM UNIVERSE — Ferrofin's recursive user-root universe has no MusicArtist, Folder, TvChannel or UserView rows (F 552 vs J 561 unfiltered; per type F 0 vs J 3/3/2/1), the same parentless-by-name/TopParentId cause as GET /Artists/{itemId}/Similar; detector `/Items?userId=<u>&recursive=true&limit=100000`, which shows the identical 9-row delta, so it is not this handler's bug. (2) PER-ITEM DTO FIELDS — MediaSources[].Name/Bitrate, MediaStreams[].BitRate (a constant 192000 offset), LocalizedLanguage, Level/IsAVC/Width/Height: the same set already classified on GET /Items/{itemId} as independent-probe noise, inherited here because Suggestions returns full item DTOs. Narrowing the compare to hide either would be the dishonest green; the row stays red until they are fixed on their own rows. The multi-leg probe no longer skips a leg whose servers disagree on status: a status divergence (or a body that is not 200-with-JSON on both sides) is recorded as a mismatch, which is the whole point of keeping the type=MusicArtist and type=TvChannel legs. UNIVERSE GAP, re-measured 2026-08-29 and WIDER than first written: LiveTvProgram belongs on the missing-kind list too — `/Items?userId=<u>&recursive=true&includeItemTypes=LiveTvProgram` returns 338 rows on Jellyfin and 0 on Ferrofin, while `/LiveTv/Programs` returns the guide on both (F 19200, J 338). The knock-on is visible on a THIRD row: GET /Genres is red only because Jellyfin's genre list carries `News`, the XMLTV guide's programme genre, which Ferrofin's genre list cannot see for the same reason. One fix (give the by-name/programme rows a TopParentId so `scope_to_user_libraries` stops filtering them out) closes /Artists Similar, these Suggestions legs and the /Genres row together; it changes the recursive item universe server-wide, so it is its own change WITH the perf gate.

--- ALSO ON THIS ROW (merged from worktree-dv-lane2) ---

open-work (NOT accepted) — but the MusicArtist half of this row is CLOSED. `MusicArtistResolver` is now ported, so `/Items/Suggestions?type=MusicArtist` returns the same 3 artists on both servers (measured twice: F 3 ['Artist 01','Artist 02','Artist 03'], J the same) where Ferrofin used to return 0, and the three `Items[Path=/media/synth/music/Artist NN] (whole item)` misses are gone. The artist rows now carry the RunTimeTicks divergence classified on `GET /Artists` (a Jellyfin first-scan race; 60000000 is correct). What is still open on this row belongs to OTHER causes and OTHER rows: the universe kinds Ferrofin does not materialize at all (physical Folder 0/9, TvChannel, UserView, LiveTvProgram), `SeriesStudio` on Episode, the MediaStreams/MediaSources bitrate offsets already classified on `GET /Items/{itemId}`, and the movie/playlist corpus asymmetry between the two long-lived instances. `RecursiveItemCount` and folder `UserData.PlayedPercentage` ARE now ported (dto_service.rs, a port of Folder.cs:1798-1836). Do NOT widen VOLATILE or drop a leg to hide any of the rest.

- ⛏ `GET /Items/{itemId}/Ancestors` — open-work (NOT accepted): the two causes this row was flagged for are PORTED and verified live (2026-08-30, pair 18096/18097, measured twice, identical both passes). (1) The AggregateFolder root is modelled: LibraryManager.CreateRootFolder() (LibraryManager.cs:838-888) is ported in ferrofin-core/src/aggregate_folder.rs — the row at {program data}/root carrying Jellyfin's own derived id f27caa37-e514-2225-cced-ed48f6553502, the playlists folder re-parented onto it (folder.ParentId = rootFolder.Id) and stored under 10.11.8's real class name PlaylistsFolder (id 1071671e-7bff-a053-2e93-0debee501d2e, byte-for-byte Jellyfin's; the old ManualPlaylistsFolder spelling is only PlaylistsFolder.GetClientTypeName() and is now emitted as the DTO Type, never stored), and AddVirtualChild ported as the virtual-children concat in BOTH places it shows — the direct-children predicate (translate_query.rs) and the ChildCount prefetch (item_count_service.rs). GET /Items/{playlistsId}/Ancestors now answers [AggregateFolder root] on both servers, and a playlist's chain is [Playlists, root] on both, diffing clean over 42 fields. reads.py seeds this row with the playlists folder as well as the five movies, because a movie's chain stops at the UserRootFolder and never exercises the aggregate hop. (2) FolderImageProvider is ported (dynamic_images.rs, DynamicImageKind::Folder) behind the IsTopParent guard (BaseItem.cs:733-757), so the UserRootFolder's PrimaryImageAspectRatio is 1.7777777777777777 on BOTH servers and the playlists folder correctly has no image. It is CreateSingleImage — a File.Copy of the first descendant's Primary — not a collage: md5 on the live pair showed Jellyfin's root poster.png is byte-identical to the 'Movies (synth)' library tile. RecursiveItemCount came with it (Folder.cs:701-714 + 1805-1808, gated on SupportsUserDataFromChildren, which BasePluginFolder opts out of via ICollectionFolder). REMAINING on this long-lived drifted lab, all instance state rather than code, to be re-scored on the end-of-campaign fresh-pair sweep: ChildCount J=7 H=6 (the two containers hold different library SETS — Jellyfin carries ParityCRUD + Recordings, Ferrofin carries a 'Movies (synth) Renamed' rename ghost — and each server's ChildCount equals its own /Library/MediaFolders total, so the concat is right on both); RecursiveItemCount 541 vs 540 (different leaf universes: 506 movies on Jellyfin to 500 on Ferrofin); and PrimaryImageAspectRatio 1.3333 vs 0.6666 on the AggregateFolder only (both servers pick the same source row, 'Movie 0001', by the same query — Ferrofin's copy of it is a 2000x3000 poster an image-upload write journey left behind, Jellyfin's is the fixture's 320x240 on-disk poster; same rule, different bytes). Do NOT add PrimaryImageAspectRatio to VOLATILE: it is a real comparable value on every other row, and it is already equal on the UserRootFolder hop here. ADDED 2026-08-30 by the review-fix pass, on top of the above. (3) TranslateParentItem (LibraryController.cs:959-966) is now the C# search rather than a type exemption: it looks a hop under the aggregate up in the user root's children — BOTH groups of them, since GetChildren there is the concatenated set — matching on PhysicalLocations.Contains(item.Path). A CollectionFolder view contributes its library locations; a virtual child's BaseItem.PhysicalLocations is [Path] (BaseItem.cs:450-461), so the search matches such a folder against ITSELF and the walk continues through it. Same answer as the type list it replaces under 10.11.8's single AddVirtualChild call site, but it is the rule, not a special case — and a PATHLESS row now correctly ends the walk, because PhysicalLocations is empty for one. (4) A recursive browse of the aggregate is a browse with no PARENT, not one with no SCOPE: C# SetTopParentIdsOrAncestors (LibraryManager.cs:1673-1698) leaves AncestorIds = [aggregate] behind, and Ferrofin's early return dropped scoping altogether. Measured on the pair before the fix, /Items?parentId={aggregate}&recursive=true&includeItemTypes=Person was Ferrofin 3 / Jellyfin 0 — rows outside the physical root's closure leaking out. Ferrofin keeps a one-level AncestorIds table rather than a transitive closure, so binding the aggregate there would match nothing; the branch now takes exactly the scope the same query with no parent gets. Re-measured: Person 0/0, and the aggregate-recursive total is now identical to Ferrofin's own unparented total (554), where it was 583. (5) RecursiveItemCount is now counted by ONE rule for every folder on a page: the per-folder query was missing IsVirtualItem = 0 while the aggregate's whole-server variant carried it, and upstream sets IsVirtualItem = false in BOTH Folder.GetRecursiveChildCount (Folder.cs:701-714) and the UnplayedItemCount query (Folder.cs:1812-1824). Invisible on this fixture (0 virtual rows) and so unmeasurable here — stated as a code-level correction, not as a measured flip. (6) FolderImageProvider is NOT ported narrower than upstream, as it can look: it is an ICustomMetadataProvider<Folder>, and ProviderManager dispatches by _metadataProviders.OfType<IMetadataProvider<T>>() with T bound to the item's MetadataService type (ProviderManager.cs:440-459), an invariant generic — so Season/Series/BoxSet/Playlist/CollectionFolder/MusicAlbum/MusicArtist/PhotoAlbum/UserView, which all have an exact MetadataService of their own, never reach it. FolderMetadataService (T = Folder) only takes the Folder subclasses with no service: Folder, AggregateFolder, UserRootFolder and BasePluginFolder, and the last is excluded by IsTopParent. Confirmed live: 10.11.8 serves no ImageTags.Primary for any Series, Season, MusicAlbum, BoxSet or plain Folder on this fixture. The three residual field diffs above persist unchanged and are still instance state: re-measured twice on 2026-08-30 after these fixes, ChildCount J=7 H=6, RecursiveItemCount J=542 H=540, PrimaryImageAspectRatio 1.3333 vs 0.6666 on the AggregateFolder only.
- ⛏ `GET /Library/MediaFolders` — open-work (NOT accepted) — measured 2026-08-31 on the lane pair, 66 leaves compared, and the row is RED for THREE distinct reasons that must not be folded together. (1) FIXED IN THIS BATCH, recorded so the defect cannot come back unnoticed: Ferrofin's auto-provisioned `Collections` library carried a NULL `CollectionType` where Jellyfin sends `boxsets`. 10.11.8 keeps that field in the row's `Data` blob and creates the library through `AddVirtualFolder(name, CollectionTypeOptions.boxsets, …)` (v10.11.8 Emby.Server.Implementations/Collections/CollectionManager.cs:81-109); Ferrofin's `ensure_container` wrote no `Data` at all. Now written (only over an EMPTY `Data`, so an adopted database's `PhysicalLocationsList`/`PhysicalFolderIds` survive a swap-back), and re-measured live: the folder answers `boxsets` on `/Library/MediaFolders`, `/UserViews` and `/Items?parentId={root}`, the same as Jellyfin. (2) STILL OPEN — the Collections library's PATH and therefore its ID diverge, which is a real drop-in adoption hazard. Upstream's row is the virtual-folder shortcut `{RootFolderPath}/default/Collections`, so its id is `GetNewItemIdInternal("root\\default\\Collections")` = 9d7ad6afe9afa2dab1a2f6e00ad28fa6; Ferrofin provisions `{data}/collections` directly and gets 6f929a39bd27711ce6208fb0aef66e5b. Both ids read off the live pair. Consequences, both measured: the library is absent from `GET /Library/VirtualFolders` on Ferrofin and present on Jellyfin, and `item_persistence_service::container_at` — which matches BY PATH — misses upstream's row on an adopted database and would provision a SECOND Collections library beside it. The code comment that used to justify the current path asserted Jellyfin derives its id from `%AppDataPath%/collections`; that is false and has been corrected in place. It IS true of the playlists folder, which agrees byte for byte on both servers (1071671e7bffa0532e930debee501d2e, /config/data/playlists). UN-DEFER PATH: provision the collections library through the same virtual-folder path the user libraries already use (shortcut dir + `.mblink` -> `{data}/collections`, registered so it lists in `GET /Library/VirtualFolders`), WITH a migration that re-parents the box sets hanging off the old id — the id change is why this is not a one-line edit — then re-measure `/Library/VirtualFolders`, `/Library/MediaFolders` and `/UserViews` against 18097 and re-run suite/roundtrip.sh. (3) STILL OPEN — `PrimaryImageAspectRatio` is absent on every Ferrofin library row and present on Jellyfin's (J=1.7777777777777777 on `Movies (synth)`). Not caused by this batch and not previously recorded here; the un-defer path is to compute the aspect ratio for the collection-folder image the way the item DTO path already does, and to add a leg that asserts it on a library row. (4) LAB STATE, not a divergence: Jellyfin additionally lists a `Recordings` library from an earlier DVR journey (see `GET /Items/Root`), which is the `TotalRecordCount` J=6 H=5.
- ⛏ `GET /MusicGenres` — open-work (NOT accepted) — the LIST shape was verified 2026-08-11 (QueryResult<BaseItemDto>, status + schema conformant, same by-name shape as /Genres), and that still holds. What a 2026-08-31 id-correlation measured on the lane pair is that the two servers give the SAME genre three different ids: F Ambient 1dcbb1e8…, Jazz 5721323b…, Rock f3bc3ad5…; J Ambient 7566ee57…, Jazz 2140463c…, Rock 56fb6975…. Person rows, by contrast, correlate exactly on both servers (Alice/Bob/Carol share ids), so this is MusicGenre's derivation specifically, not by-name ids in general. Cause, read in the code: `item_persistence_service::music_genre_row` derives the id with `derive_item_id(BaseItemKind::MusicGenre, value)` — the bare NAME, lowercased (`IdDerivation::LegacyLowercase`) — where upstream derives it from the metadata PATH: `GetItemByNameId<MusicGenre>(MusicGenre.GetPath(name))`, and `MusicGenre.GetPath` is `Path.Combine(ApplicationPaths.MusicGenrePath, GetValidFilename(name).Trim().TrimEnd('.'))` (v10.11.8 MediaBrowser.Controller/Entities/Audio/MusicGenre.cs:76-89). Ferrofin already has both halves of the correct derivation — `IdDerivation::Jellyfin { program_data_path }` and the `by_name_item_id`/`person_path` pattern the Person rows use. UN-DEFER PATH: route `music_genre_row` through the path-based derivation, then re-key the existing rows and their `ItemValuesMap`/`UserDatas` references in a migration — the re-key is the part that makes this a port of its own rather than a one-line change, and it is why it was not folded into the batch F4 reviewer-fix pass. Discovered while verifying that pass's `ids=`-scoped grouping fix, which is the query that made the id mismatch visible (F answers 3 of its own MusicGenre ids, J answers 0 for the same ids).

--- ALSO ON THIS ROW (merged from worktree-dv-lane2) ---

open-work (NOT accepted) — SUPERSEDES the 2026-08-31 'jellyfin-bug' record, which was the wrong category and said so with the wrong evidence. That record folded upstream's `GroupBy(PresentationUniqueKey)` collapse under 'jellyfin-bug', which in this campaign means 'master already fixed it'. Master has NOT: master `BaseItemRepository.ByName.cs:213-224` still ships the collapse (`g.Min(e => e.Id)`, with a `TopParentIds`-preference arm for MusicArtist), and v10.11.8 `BaseItemRepository.cs:1313-1316` ships it as `g.FirstOrDefault()`. An upstream behaviour present in BOTH trees and absent from Ferrofin is an un-ported gap, so it has now been PORTED rather than labelled.

WHAT F2 (as revised) FIXED — four things, all measured on the lane-2 pair:
1. `get_genres` passed the music types as `exclude_content_types` and `get_music_genres` passed them as `include_content_types`, restricting which CONTENT items feed the aggregate. C# restricts neither: master ByName.cs:44-52 and v10.11.8 :211-222 are both `GetItemValues(filter, _getGenreValueTypes, <returnType>)`, whose inner query is built from `IncludeItemTypes` ALONE (ByName.cs:154-168); only the outer `.Where(e => e.Type == returnType)` differs. The owner-type split lives solely on the *Names* variants (`GetItemValueNames`, ByName.cs:120-141), which `get_genre_names`/`get_music_genre_names` still carry — that asymmetry IS the port. The compensation was paying for a scanner mis-split already fixed in da43f0a.
2. `ItemCounts` were never computed (every per-kind count 0, matching neither tree). Ported MASTER's per-CleanName `BuildItemCountsByCleanName` (ByName.cs:283-338; the seven `BaseItemKindNames` locals it reads are at :304-310) as seven conditional `SUM(ci."Type" = ?)` aggregates in the same GROUP BY, emitted only when the caller named item types (`if (filter.IncludeItemTypes.Length > 0)`, ByName.cs:249). `ItemCount`/`ProgramCount` stay 0 because neither tree assigns them.
3. The representative collapse itself is now ported — `item_repository::push_representative_rank`, a `ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY bi."PresentationUniqueKey" ORDER BY [MusicArtist TopParentId preference,] bi."Id")` filtered to rank 1, run over the FILTERED row set exactly as upstream groups `masterQuery`, with the page total counting GROUPS (`representativeIds.Count()`, ByName.cs:229-232) instead of rows. Ferrofin followed master's deterministic `Min(Id)` over 10.11.8's `FirstOrDefault`. This is user-visible well beyond genres: upstream's own comment at that site says the grouping is what collapses alternate versions and a MusicArtist with a folder in more than one library.
4. The `PresentationUniqueKey` half. The earlier record claimed 'music_genre_row now writes PresentationUniqueKey … Ferrofin writes a real {Type}-{Name} key on every one'. That was FALSE on the lab it cited: the insert is `INSERT ... WHERE NOT EXISTS`, so it never touches a row that already exists, and `MusicGenre|Ambient/Jazz/Rock` still read back NULL. Fixed properly with a startup repair, `item_persistence_service::backfill_missing_presentation_keys`, scoped to the four by-name kinds whose key is a pure function of the name. It logged `items=3` on the first boot after the change and nothing on the second, and the container's own DB now reads `MusicGenre-Ambient/-Jazz/-Rock`. Without it, porting item 3 would have COLLAPSED those three into one — reproducing upstream's collapse without reproducing upstream's keys.

WHY THE ROW IS STILL RED (measured twice, 2026-08-31, after the ports above):
H lists [Action, Ambient, Comedy, Drama, Jazz, Rock, SciFi, Thriller]; J lists [Action, Ambient, Jazz, Rock]. Two axes, and NEITHER is now an un-ported behaviour.
AXIS 1 — Jellyfin's un-refreshed by-name keys. `CreateItemByName<T>` (master LibraryManager.cs:1289-1329 / v10.11.8 :1052-1092) constructs `new T { Name, Id, DateCreated, DateModified, Path }` and queues no refresh, so the row is written with a NULL `PresentationUniqueKey`; the key is assigned later by `MetadataService.BeforeSaveInternal` (MediaBrowser.Providers/Manager/MetadataService.cs:332-338, identical on both trees), i.e. on the row's FIRST metadata refresh. The lab's own jellyfin.db proves the mechanism exactly — every by-name row with a non-NULL DateLastRefreshed has a key and every row with a NULL one does not:
  MusicGenre Ambient  key MusicGenre-Ambient  DateLastRefreshed 2026-08-30 00:29:31.807812
  MusicGenre Jazz     key MusicGenre-Jazz     DateLastRefreshed 2026-08-30 00:29:31.8121208
  MusicGenre Rock     key MusicGenre-Rock     DateLastRefreshed 2026-08-30 00:29:31.8180006
  MusicGenre Action   key NULL                DateLastRefreshed NULL   (created 01:00:11 by a probe GET)
  MusicGenre Comedy/Drama/SciFi/Thriller  key NULL, DateLastRefreshed NULL (created 02:20-02:21)
So Jellyfin's four unkeyed rows form ONE NULL group and surface as the single representative Action, while its three refreshed ones list individually. Ferrofin writes the key at creation and repairs any legacy row at startup, so it is permanently in the state Jellyfin reaches after a refresh. Reproducing J's list would mean deliberately withholding a column upstream itself fills in. On a freshly provisioned pair the probe-created rows do not exist, so this axis should not appear in the end-of-campaign sweep.
AXIS 2 — the uncorrelated ItemCounts, a genuine jellyfin-bug that MASTER FIXED. J returns SongCount 9 for EVERY music genre and MovieCount 514 for Action, while BOTH databases hold exactly 3 Audio rows per music genre and 200 Movies carrying `action`. v10.11.8 BaseItemRepository.cs:1368-1392 hands every row the same `itemCountQuery.Count(...)` totals and comments 'TODO: This is bad refactor!'; master replaced it with the per-CleanName dictionary Ferrofin ported. Ferrofin returns each database's own truth. Following master here is the campaign rule, not a choice.

NOT ACCEPTED, and nothing here is deferred: axis 1 needs no port (it is upstream transient state, with the mechanism and the evidence above) and axis 2 is master's fix. parity_diff.VOLATILE was not widened for either. deep_verified stays false, which is the honest state while the two listings genuinely differ.
- ⛏ `POST /Plugins/{pluginId}/{version}/Disable` — open-work (PENDING OWNER RATIFICATION — NOT accepted): the text previously here described a DIFFERENT op (the pre-restart window of `POST /Packages/Installed/{name}`, already classified on its own row) and no layer had ever issued this request — sweep hands writes to Layer 2, journeys.py contains no plugin journey, and reads.py covered only Configuration/Manifest/Image. A probe now exists (reads.py `plugin_enable_invariants` / `plugin_disable_invariants`, aliases PluginsEnable / PluginsDisable, one per route). Measured twice on the F6 pair 2026-08-30, the two servers agree on EVERY id-independent contract fact — unknown guid 404/404, unparseable version 400/400, wrong version 404/404, installed-version-minus-a-component 404/404 (.NET `Version` treats an absent component as -1), anonymous 401/401 — and on the resulting state: status_after_disable 'Disabled' on both, status_after_enable 'Active' on both, restored True on both. Exactly ONE fact diverges, and it is kept in the diff on purpose, so the row is RED: the HTTP status of a toggle that SUCCEEDS is Ferrofin 204 / Jellyfin 404. (a) THE 404 IS A JELLYFIN BUG AND FERROFIN MUST KEEP 204. All five of Jellyfin's plugins are bundled in-assembly, so `PluginManager.CreatePluginInstance` builds `new LocalPlugin(instance.AssemblyFilePath, ...)` (v10.11.8 PluginManager.cs:541,559) and `Path` is a .dll FILE. `ChangePluginState` (:513) flips `Manifest.Status` in memory FIRST, then `SaveManifest` (:364) does `File.WriteAllText(Path.Combine(path, "meta.json"))` and catches only `ArgumentException`, so the `DirectoryNotFoundException` escapes the action and `ExceptionMiddleware` maps it to 404 (`DirectoryNotFoundException => Status404NotFound`, identical v10.11.8/master). Confirmed in the container log: "Error processing request: Could not find a part of the path '/jellyfin/MediaBrowser.Providers.dll/meta.json'. URL POST /Plugins/.../Disable". Net effect on Jellyfin: a 204 never changes state and a state change is always reported 404. `git diff v10.11.8 master -- PluginManager.cs` is ONE hunk (line 567, ImageResourceName) — master does not fix it. (b) THE OWNER QUESTION, not to be resolved by an agent. Jellyfin's toggle takes effect at the NEXT BOOT: `EnablePlugin`/`DisablePlugin` call `ProcessAlternative`, whose last two lines are `plugin.Manifest.Status = PluginStatus.Restart; plugin.Manifest.AutoUpdate = false;` (:882, commented "This value is memory only - so that the web will show restart required"). Ferrofin's toggle takes effect IMMEDIATELY — every consumer asks the PluginManager at use time (ferrofin-extensions file_transformation.rs:244,313,333; merge_versions.rs:179,212,246; ferrofin-wasm/src/lib.rs:558-575 behind a TTL cache), never only at boot — so it reports Active/Disabled and reporting `Restart` would be a false statement about Ferrofin's own behaviour. Both values are schema-valid (`PluginStatus` in the vendored contract is [Active,Restart,Deleted,Superseded,Superceded,Malfunctioned,NotSupported,Disabled]), and jellyfin-web computes `isEnabled = Status !== 'Disabled'`, so Jellyfin's own value leaves the dashboard toggle reading ENABLED right after a disable. The question: do we (i) keep immediate-effect + truthful Active/Disabled [today], (ii) keep immediate-effect but ALSO emit Restart for jellyfin-web's restart banner, or (iii) port Jellyfin's defer-until-restart semantics? If (ii)/(iii): crates/ferrofin-api/src/handlers/plugins.rs `to_plugin_info`/`to_plugin_manifest` plus a toggled-since-boot set in ferrofin-core/src/plugin_manager.rs `set_enabled`; no query/repository/DTO path is involved. NOTE for whoever edits the probe: on Jellyfin `ChangePluginState` early-returns when `Status == state`, and that early return is the ONLY path reaching `ProcessAlternative` — so a NO-OP toggle pins the plugin at `Restart` in memory until the container restarts, poisoning `plugin_manifest_invariants`' status_value and every later GET /Plugins diff. Neither probe issues one. Do not add an "idempotent toggle" fact. The mutate/restore pair is wrapped in try/finally (reviewer-fix pass) and restores through `restore_plugin_state`, which READS the status first and issues nothing when it already matches — so an interrupted or timed-out run cannot leave a Jellyfin plugin Disabled for the rest of the container's life, and the restore itself can never become the no-op toggle it exists to avoid. Verified after the change: one full Active→Disabled→Active cycle per route per server, all 5 Jellyfin plugins and the 1 Ferrofin plugin back at `Active`, none at `Restart`. The `Disable` probe measures its OWN route; the sibling verb appears only as setup or restore.

--- ALSO ON THIS ROW (merged from main) ---

accepted-divergence: same `ProcessAlternative` memory-only `Restart` status as the Enable route — see that row for the C# citation and why Ferrofin stays truthful (its disable takes effect immediately, upstream's needs a restart). Note the in-tree providers take an extra upstream quirk on THIS route: their dummy `LocalPlugin.Path` is a DLL file, so `SaveManifest` fails, `ChangePluginState` returns false and `ProcessAlternative` never runs — a stock Jellyfin therefore reports `Disabled`, not `Restart`, for exactly those five. The pre-existing note stands: a freshly repository-installed WASM plugin 404s until its activating restart. Admin-only. The path version is now honoured (wrong -> 404, unparseable -> 400).

--- ALSO ON THIS ROW (merged from main) ---

accepted-divergence: after a successful toggle Jellyfin reports the plugin's `Status` as `Restart` and flips `AutoUpdate` to false, and Ferrofin reports `Active`/`Disabled`. This is `ProcessAlternative` (v10.11.8 PluginManager.cs:881-909), whose tail is unconditionally `plugin.Manifest.Status = PluginStatus.Restart; plugin.Manifest.AutoUpdate = false;` with the comment "This value is memory only - so that the web will show restart required". It exists because a .NET assembly cannot be swapped in a running process: upstream's toggle does not take effect until the next boot, and the dashboard has to say so. Ferrofin's toggle takes effect IMMEDIATELY — every consumer reads the enabled flag through `PluginManager::get_plugin` at call time (see `ferrofin-extensions`) — so reporting `Restart` would be a false statement to the client, and per the repo's 'don't port Jellyfin bugs' rule Ferrofin stays truthful. Verified live on the lane-3 pair 2026-08-30: `POST /Plugins/{tmdb}/{v}/Enable` is 204 on both, after which Jellyfin's `GET /Plugins` reports TMDb `Status: "Restart"` and its manifest `autoUpdate: false`, while Ferrofin reports `Active`/`true`. The pre-existing note also stands: a freshly repository-installed WASM plugin 404s until its activating restart while Jellyfin already lists it restart-pending. Admin-only. The version in the path is now honoured on this route too (a wrong version is 404, an unparseable one 400).
- ⛏ `GET /UserViews` — open-work (NOT accepted) — found 2026-08-30 by batch S1 while re-running the read layer, and recorded rather than left as an unreviewed detector flag. `POST /Library/VirtualFolders/Name` leaks a user view on Ferrofin: renaming a virtual folder and renaming it BACK leaves an orphan entry under the intermediate name in `GET /UserViews`. Measured on the lane-3 pair, same request sequence against both servers (`Music (synth)` -> `Music (synth) Tmp` -> `Music (synth)`, both legs 204 on both servers): Ferrofin's view list grew by one ('Music (synth) Tmp') and Jellyfin's was unchanged. It is not a cache — an earlier orphan ('Movies (synth) Renamed', left by the parity write layer's own rename journey) survived BOTH a container restart and a `POST /Library/Refresh`, while Jellyfin's equivalent transient view rows clear on restart. A user sees a phantom library in the sidebar. UN-DEFER PATH: make the rename path move the existing user-view row instead of creating a second one (C# `LibraryManager.RenameVirtualFolder` renames the folder on disk and the view follows it), add a journey leg that asserts the view COUNT is unchanged across a rename round-trip, and re-provision the lane pair (the two orphans above are on it now). ALSO ON THIS ROW — a SECOND open-work gap, measured 2026-08-31 on the lane pair and previously unrecorded anywhere: Ferrofin serves NO `Playlists` user view. Jellyfin answers 7 views to Ferrofin's 5; two of those are accounted for (`Recordings` is the lab-state library above), and the third is real. `UserViewManager.GetUserViews` (v10.11.8 Emby.Server.Implementations/Library/UserViewManager.cs:44-95) walks `GetUserRootFolder().GetChildren(user, true)`, and `UserView.IsUserSpecific(folder)` is TRUE for the playlists folder (UserView.cs:155-169: `collectionFolder.CollectionType == CollectionType.playlists`), so upstream materialises a PER-USER `UserView` for it via `GetNamedView(user, folder.Name, folder.Id, folderViewType, null)` — id = `GetNewItemId("38_namedview_" + name + userIdN + parentIdN + viewType, typeof(UserView))` (LibraryManager.cs:2462-2496), Path `{InternalMetadataPath}/views/{idN}`, `Data` = `{"ViewType":"playlists","DisplayParentId":<playlists folder>,"UserId":<user>}`. Read off the lab's 10.11.8: id 134ea3dcf722741c8892ad56d425dd3c, CollectionType `playlists`, ChildCount 6, with a Primary image. Ferrofin's `get_user_views` selects rows by KIND (`CollectionFolder` + `UserView`) and has no per-user named-view provisioning at all, so the view can never appear. UN-DEFER PATH, and it is three parts, not one — which is why it is a named work item and not a drive-by: (1) provision the row, reusing the `ensure_live_tv_view` shape in crates/ferrofin-core/src/user_view_manager.rs with the `38_namedview_` id key above; (2) SCOPE IT — `get_user_views` currently returns every `UserView` row to every user, so a per-user view added without a `Data.UserId` filter would leak one user's view into another's sidebar, a NEW divergence worse than the missing one; (3) the DTO must carry `ChildCount` and the Primary image, or the row swaps a missing view for a wrong one. Each part needs its own live re-measure against 18097.

- 🔍 `GET /Items/Latest` — flagged: read diff vs Jellyfin (verify)

## Recorded side-path divergence (the row's own verdict does not cover it)

_A real, evidenced difference on a path this op's verdict says nothing about — an error input the contract never sends, or a side effect the probe deliberately does not assert. Kept OUT of both the accepted count and the verdict: a note here can never make a row green, and a row going red here is an unexplained failure, not a settled decision._

- 📎 ✅ `GET /LiveTv/ChannelMappingOptions` — jellyfin-bug, error path only: with NO `providerId` (which is what the breadth sweep sends) Jellyfin runs `ListingProviders.First(info => providerId == info.Id)` unguarded and throws InvalidOperationException → 500 (`Sequence contains no matching element` in the container log; src/Jellyfin.LiveTv/Listings/ListingsManager.cs:207-210 @ v10.11.8). Ferrofin answers 404 — the same `ResourceNotFoundException` Jellyfin's own `GetProvider` (ListingsManager.cs:461-463) raises for an unresolvable provider — so the sweep's bare-URL row reads H=404 J=500 by design. The CONTRACT path (`?providerId=<configured provider>`) agrees and is body-diffed in reads.py.
- 📎 ⚠️ `POST /LiveTv/ChannelMappings` — jellyfin-bug, error path only: an unknown `ProviderId`, or a `TunerChannelId` that is not in the lineup, makes Jellyfin throw from an unguarded `.First(…)` → 500 (ListingsManager.cs:231 and :264 @ v10.11.8). Ferrofin answers 404 to both. The persistence order is ported as-is, not "fixed": upstream saves the configuration at :251 BEFORE the :264 throw, so a mapping keyed on a channel that does not exist is stored on both servers and then reported as not found — a dangling pair the match ladder simply never hits. The success path agrees and is the `effect` row j_livetv_admin measures.
- 📎 ⊙ `POST /LiveTv/ListingProviders` — open-work (NOT accepted): the SCHEDULES DIRECT listings backend is unported. Jellyfin registers two `IListingsProvider`s — `XmlTvListingsProvider` and `SchedulesDirect` (src/Jellyfin.LiveTv/Listings/SchedulesDirect.cs @ v10.11.8: token auth against json.schedulesdirect.org, `GetHeadends` → lineups, `GetLineups`/`AddLineup` and the schedules ingest) — and Ferrofin registers only XMLTV; `ferrofin-livetv/src/schedules_direct.rs` is the country list and nothing else. WHAT THAT COSTS, op by op: this route accepts and SAVES a provider whose `Type` is `SchedulesDirect` instead of rejecting it the way C# `GetProvider` does (ResourceNotFoundException → 404), so the row is stored and every later read of it resolves nothing; `GET /LiveTv/ListingProviders/Lineups?type=SchedulesDirect` answers 404 where a credentialed Jellyfin lists that account's headends; and `GET /LiveTv/ListingProviders/SchedulesDirect/Countries` is real but is the ONLY piece of the backend that is. NOT PROBEABLE ON THIS PAIR, which is why it needs this row: the fixture configures one XMLTV provider and no Schedules Direct account, so both servers agree on every request the lab can make and no live layer can ever go red on it. The `effect` verdict this op carries is the XMLTV path only — hence scope side-path. Un-defer path: port `SchedulesDirect` (token cache, `GetHeadends`, `GetLineups`, `AddLineup`, `GetProgramsAsync`) as a second `IListingsProvider` behind the same seam the XMLTV one uses, then make `save_listing_provider` resolve `Type` through the registry so an unknown type 404s like the C#. Needs its own batch (credentials + a recorded-response fixture); it is an open work item, not an accepted divergence.
- 📎 ✅ `GET /LiveTv/ListingProviders/Lineups` — jellyfin-bug, error path only: `?type=xmltv` with no `id` makes Jellyfin call `GetProvider(type).GetLineups(null, …)` (ListingsManager.cs:104-107), and `XmlTvListingsProvider.GetLineups` dereferences the null `info` inside `GetXml` → NullReferenceException → 500 (XmlTvListingsProvider.cs:239-249 @ v10.11.8). Ferrofin returns 404 there, which is what Jellyfin itself returns for every other unresolvable provider. The two contract paths agree: `?id=<configured provider>` is 200 and body-diffed in reads.py, and no id/unknown id is 404 on both.
- 📎 ⚠️ `DELETE /LiveTv/TunerHosts` — accepted-divergence, SIDE EFFECT ONLY (the verdict on this row is the configuration read-back, not this): `LiveTvController.DeleteTunerHost` (Jellyfin.Api/Controllers/LiveTvController.cs:1000-1006 @ v10.11.8) rewrites `LiveTvOptions.TunerHosts`, saves, and returns — it queues NO `RefreshGuideScheduledTask`, unlike `ListingsManager.DeleteListingsProvider` (src/Jellyfin.LiveTv/Listings/ListingsManager.cs:83-91) which does. So on Jellyfin the removed tuner's `LiveTvChannel` items LINGER in the library until the next guide refresh prunes them through `GuideManager.CleanDatabase`; on Ferrofin the channel rows are FK children of the tuner host (`crates/ferrofin-db/migrations/0003_live_tv.sql:36-38`, `FK_LiveTvChannels_TunerHosts_TunerHostId … ON DELETE CASCADE`, and the programmes hang off the channels the same way at :60-62, `FK_LiveTvPrograms_Channels_ChannelId`) and go with it in the same statement. Measured on the loop pair 2026-08-30, both servers taking a case-flipped id and answering 204: immediately after the DELETE F reports 0 channels AND 0 programmes (the airings are FK children of the channel rows, so they go too) where it had 2 / 19200, while J still reports 2 / 338. Both servers converge on the SAME end state — Jellyfin's next refresh deletes exactly those rows, because `GuideManager.CleanDatabase` (src/Jellyfin.LiveTv/Guide/GuideManager.cs:147-148, 331-360 @ v10.11.8) removes every `LiveTvChannel`/`LiveTvProgram` the pass did not re-emit, and a channel of a tuner that no longer exists is never re-emitted — and what differs is the window in between, during which Jellyfin serves channels no tuner can stream. Ferrofin keeps the cascade (see the 'don't port Jellyfin bugs' rule): a dangling channel is a 5xx waiting for a client that tries to play it. NOT PORTED, deliberately, and NOT hidden: the journey asserts this op on the configuration, which is what both servers agree the delete means, and this note is the record of what it does not assert.
- 📎 ▫ `POST /LiveTv/Tuners/{tunerId}/Reset` — jellyfin-bug, error path only: a `tunerId` whose prefix DOES name the registered service but carries no `_` makes Jellyfin index `parts[1]` of a one-element `Split('_', 2)` → IndexOutOfRangeException → 500 (src/Jellyfin.LiveTv/LiveTvManager.cs:1233-1245 @ v10.11.8; measured J=500 on `af999c25a00715699361240d4c6c7a53`). Ferrofin no-ops with 204. The two contract-relevant statuses agree and are the status-pair j_livetv_admin measures: a registered service prefix → 204, an unknown prefix → 400 (`ArgumentException("Service not found.")`, mapped by Jellyfin.Api/Middleware/ExceptionMiddleware.cs:127).
