- ❌ `GET /MusicGenres/{genreName}` — verified 2026-08-31 (batch F2, revised after review): 3/3 legs clean, 34 fields compared, measured twice. This row had NO classification before F2 and one probe leg.

`MusicGenresController.GetMusicGenre` carries the SAME slug branch as the genres controller (`GetItemFromSlugName<MusicGenre>`, '-'->'&'/'/'/'?', three lookups, creating nothing) but NOT the same fallback: it keeps `if (item is null) return NotFound();` on BOTH trees (v10.11.8 :164-167, master :165-168, where the action is additionally `[Obsolete("Use GetGenre instead")]`), where `GenresController` has `item ??= new Genre()`. That asymmetry is upstream's and it is the port. It is also how the wire caught a wrong port: the first cut copied the genres fallback here and answered 200 where a live 10.11.8 answers 404 (`/MusicGenres/R-B` J=404) — no unit test would have found it, because the two controllers' C# is identical until the last five lines.

REAL WIRE DIVERGENCE FOUND AND FIXED IN THE REVIEW PASS (the reason this row is 34 fields and not 28). The two 404 legs used to be scored clean by a harness branch that treated 'both servers, same status, neither body JSON' as agreement — but `reads.py::token_get` discarded the body of EVERY non-200, so 'neither body is JSON' was a property of the probe, not of the responses. Measured directly with curl: J returned `{"type":"https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc9110#section-15.5.5","title":"Not Found","status":404,"traceId":"00-…"}` and H returned `{"error":"not found: music genre R-B"}`. Two different documents, scored as one clean leg.
Fixed on BOTH sides. Harness: `token_probe` keeps the body at any status and the multi legs diff it like any other body, so an error body is now part of the comparison; a leg where neither server sent ANY body is recorded as `status-class` evidence (the ledger's weakest verdict) rather than as a body diff, and two different non-JSON bodies behind the same status are a mismatch. Server: `ApiError::Problem(ProblemStatus::NotFound)` renders ASP.NET's RFC 9110 ProblemDetails, and this handler uses it because its C# `NotFound()` takes no argument — ASP.NET then fills the body from `ApiBehaviorOptions.ClientErrorMapping`.
It is deliberately a PER-CALL-SITE variant and not a global change to how Ferrofin renders errors, because Jellyfin's error bodies are not uniform. Measured on the live 10.11.8 container in one session: `NotFound()` -> ProblemDetails; `GET /Users/{unknown}` (`NotFound("User not found")`) -> the bare JSON string `"User not found"`; a model-binding failure -> a `ValidationProblemDetails` with an `errors` map; an unauthenticated request -> an EMPTY 401 with no content type; a wrong-verb request -> an empty 405. A blanket ProblemDetails would have traded one divergence for four. Those four call-site families are un-ported and NOT owned by this batch — they belong to the rows that probe them, and they are now visible to any multi row that probes one, because the harness no longer throws error bodies away.
`traceId` is the one field on the VOLATILE list for this: it is ASP.NET's per-request W3C trace-context id, which two independent instances cannot agree on and one instance never repeats. Scoped to that exact key; `type`, `title`, `status` and `errors` are all still compared, which is what makes an error-body diff mean anything.

## Verified WITH a recorded caveat

_These rows diffed clean, and something about them is still worth knowing: a field the probe did not compare, or an accepted divergence underneath a green verdict. A green row is not the same as a row with nothing left to say._

- ∅ `GET /Albums/{itemId}/Similar`
  - classification: flat-provider methodology (SET-GATED, order not gateable). Seeds of this kind take the C# FLAT provider: one `InternalItemsQuery { Genres = item.Genres, Tags = item.Tags, IncludeItemTypes = [<kind>], EnableGroupByMetadataKey = false, EnableTotalRecordCount = true, OrderBy = [(ItemSortBy.Random, SortOrder.Ascending)] }` — identical in v10.11.8 (built inline at LibraryController.cs:791-801) and in master (SeriesSimilarItemsProvider.cs:49, MusicAlbumSimilarItemsProvider.cs:50, MusicArtistSimilarItemsProvider.cs:50, AudioSimilarItemsProvider.cs:50, LiveTvProgramSimilarItemsProvider.cs:89). Ferrofin runs the same query and, as of this pass, the same `Random` order. IT BRIEFLY DID NOT: batch A2 first shipped `SortName` here, which is not cosmetic — at limit < |candidates| a fixed order returns a DIFFERENT ITEM, measured on the loop pair as `/Items/{audio}/Similar?limit=1` answering 'Artist 01 Album 01 Track 02' on five consecutive Ferrofin calls while Jellyfin alternated Track 02 / Track 03. That divergence is now closed in the server (`similar_items_manager.rs::flat_similar`, unit test `the_flat_provider_orders_random_not_by_name`), not papered over. WHAT THIS LEDGER ROW DOES AND DOES NOT GATE: `Random` on both sides means the ORDER can never be diffed by any probe, and neither can a page narrower than the candidate pool — parity_diff aligns arrays by Path>Name>Id, so a reordered Items array returns 0 diffs and always will. The reads.py leg therefore pins `limit=1000`, far above the fixture's pool, where the answer is the whole candidate set and so fully determined; what is gated there is the SET, TotalRecordCount and every per-item DTO field. Anything order-sensitive is covered by unit tests instead, never by this row. GREEN BUT VACUOUS ON THE CURRENT FIXTURE — read this row as 'no divergence detected', not as 'verified'. `suite/perf/gen-fixtures.sh` gave each of the 3 albums a UNIQUE music genre, so the candidate universe is empty by construction on BOTH servers and the leg diffs `{"Items": [], "TotalRecordCount": 0}` against itself. The fixture is changed (two of the three artists now share a genre) but that only takes effect at the campaign's final wipe-and-provision; until that sweep runs, the album matching itself is covered by the `an_album_seed_matches_albums_sharing_a_genre_only` unit test (two albums sharing a genre, a same-genre track that must NOT come back because IncludeItemTypes is [MusicAlbum] alone, and the ExcludeArtistIds leg with a positive control). A real Ferrofin bug found behind this row IS fixed and verified live: album Genres/Studios/PremiereDate are inherited from the album's Audio children (C# AlbumMetadataService + MetadataService.UpdateGenres/UpdateStudios/UpdatePremiereDate); Ferrofin aggregated only album-artist and year, so every album had `Genres: []` and was unreachable by the Genres= query this endpoint issues. After the fix, F album Genres/PremiereDate/ProductionYear match J exactly (Jazz/Rock/Ambient, 2022-01-01T00:00:00.0000000Z, 2022).
- ✅ `GET /Audio/{itemId}/universal`
  - classification: security-fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1, cross-user authz): C# RequestHelpers.GetUserId (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Helpers/RequestHelpers.cs:67-85) has two halves: an absent/all-zero userId falls back to the caller, and a userId that is NOT the caller is honoured only for an ADMINISTRATOR, else SecurityException -> 403. Ferrofin ported only the first half here. Found by the S1 sweep. The handler took no userId at all; it is the audio playback path (v10.11.8 UniversalAudioController.cs:120). Measured: 200 on Ferrofin against 403 on Jellyfin. Re-measured independently in the S1 review pass with a fresh non-admin account ('s1fixprobe', created and deleted on both servers): a non-administrator naming the administrator's id is 403 on BOTH servers, while the administrator naming another user and a non-administrator naming their own id still succeed on both. Recorded in the S1 review pass: the batch claimed these five as flipped but left no row for them, so the fix existed only in the code and the claim. | empty-guid binding fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1 review pass, reviewer finding F2): ASP.NET binds an EMPTY or whitespace-only value for a `Guid?` to null (SimpleTypeModelBinder), so upstream answers `?userId=` and `?userId=%20` exactly as it answers a request with no userId at all; serde's `Option<Uuid>` tried to parse "" and made both a 400. S1 spread that pre-existing defect to five more routes when it gave previously parameter-less handlers a `Query<T>`. Re-measured on the lane-3 pair BEFORE the fix: `?userId=` and `?userId=%20` were 400 on Ferrofin against 200 on Jellyfin (404/404 where the probe id does not exist). FIXED AS A CLASS, not as an instance: every `Option<Uuid>` field of a `Query<...>` struct in ferrofin-api now binds through `handlers::query_parse::empty_as_none_uuid`. AFTER, the four spellings (absent / all-zero / empty / whitespace) agree on both servers across /Devices, /Channels, /Channels/Items/Latest, /LiveTv/Recordings/Folders, /LiveTv/Recordings/{recordingId}, /Audio/{itemId}/universal AND the four routes that carried the defect before S1 ever ran (/Items, /UserViews, /Persons, /Years). Request BODIES keep serde's strict parse on purpose: System.Text.Json rejects `"UserId": ""` upstream too.

- ✅ `GET /Devices`
  - classification: security-parity 2026-08-30 (batch S1 review pass, reviewer finding F1 — a claim refuted by measurement, then fixed). GET /Devices sits behind [Authorize(Policy = Policies.RequiresElevation)] (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Controllers/DevicesController.cs:21), which neutralises only the SECOND half of RequestHelpers.GetUserId (the administrator gate). S1 concluded the whole helper was therefore a no-op here and left the hand-rolled `query.user_id.unwrap_or(caller)` in place — the fourteenth site of the very pattern the batch says it eliminated. The FIRST half was still unported: `userId.IsNullOrEmpty()` is true for Guid.Empty upstream, so an all-zero id falls back to the caller, where Ferrofin looked it up literally. MEASURED AS THE ADMINISTRATOR on the lane-3 pair: `GET /Devices?userId=00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000` was 404 on Ferrofin against 200 with the caller's 13 devices on Jellyfin 10.11.8. The route now goes through the crate's one port of the rule (handlers::items::effective_user_id). AFTER: absent / all-zero / empty / whitespace are all 200 on both servers, and a non-administrator naming another user is 403 on both (the elevation policy). The regression tests in crates/ferrofin-api/tests/devices.rs assert the id the handler RESOLVED and handed the manager, not just the status — a stub that ignores the argument would otherwise pass. | empty-guid binding fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1 review pass, reviewer finding F2): ASP.NET binds an EMPTY or whitespace-only value for a `Guid?` to null (SimpleTypeModelBinder), so upstream answers `?userId=` and `?userId=%20` exactly as it answers a request with no userId at all; serde's `Option<Uuid>` tried to parse "" and made both a 400.   - classification: security-fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1, cross-user authz): C# RequestHelpers.GetUserId (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Helpers/RequestHelpers.cs:67-85) has two halves: an absent/all-zero userId falls back to the caller, and a userId that is NOT the caller is honoured only for an ADMINISTRATOR, else SecurityException -> 403. Ferrofin ported only the first half here. Measured twice on the lane-3 pair (Ferrofin 18396 / Jellyfin 18397) with a second, non-admin account 's1probe' created on both: BEFORE, a non-admin reading the admin's row (GET ?userId=<admin>) got 200 with the admin's preferences on Ferrofin against 403 on Jellyfin. After the fix both servers return 403 for the same request, while an administrator naming another user's id and a non-administrator naming their own id still succeed on both (measured, twice). The reads-results row committed at cec34ea is `flagged` / deep_verified FALSE on one field — CustomPrefs.enableNextVideoInfoOverlay (J="False" H="True") — and S1's own cleanup then rewrote the administrator's Ferrofin row to match Jellyfin's byte for byte, i.e. it edited lab state on exactly the field that was flagged, and reported the row as "unchanged at ok/deep_verified". No real divergence was destroyed, and this is the evidence rather than the assertion: a BRAND-NEW user was created on both servers in the review pass and GET /DisplayPreferences/usersettings?client=emby for that user is byte-identical on both — the same six CustomPrefs keys (chromecastVersion, dashboardTheme, enableNextVideoInfoOverlay, skipBackLength, skipForwardLength, tvhome), zero value differences, zero top-level differences, enableNextVideoInfoOverlay "False" on both. The flag was fixture drift on the long-lived bench admin's row, not a code divergence. The S1 security fix itself re-verified end to end in the review pass: a non-admin GET and POST of the administrator's row are 403 on both servers and the administrator's read-back carries no injected key on either.

--- ALSO ON THIS ROW (merged from worktree-dv-lane1) ---

verified 2026-08-11: the auto-flag was a harness artifact (literal unresolved {displayPreferencesId}); with a real client key (e.g. usersettings?client=emby) Ferrofin returns 200 DisplayPreferencesDto (Id/Client/SortBy/CustomPrefs) — the by-client resolution Jellyfin uses.
- ⊙ `POST /DisplayPreferences/{displayPreferencesId}`
  - classification: security-fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1, cross-user authz): C# RequestHelpers.GetUserId (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Helpers/RequestHelpers.cs:67-85) has two halves: an absent/all-zero userId falls back to the caller, and a userId that is NOT the caller is honoured only for an ADMINISTRATOR, else SecurityException -> 403. Ferrofin ported only the first half here. Measured twice on the lane-3 pair (Ferrofin 18396 / Jellyfin 18397) with a second, non-admin account 's1probe' created on both: BEFORE, a non-admin OVERWRITING the admin's row (POST ?userId=<admin>) got 204 on Ferrofin against 403 on Jellyfin, and the write landed - reading the admin's own row back AS the admin showed the injected CustomPrefs key ('s1probe'='PWNED'); the same read on Jellyfin showed the key absent. This is the exploitable half: cross-user data corruption by any authenticated account. After the fix both servers return 403 for the same request, while an administrator naming another user's id and a non-administrator naming their own id still succeed on both (measured, twice). Regression tests in crates/ferrofin-api/tests/ cover all three cases per route. Fifth instance in this campaign of a C# authorization check Ferrofin served without one (A1 startup, B3 x29 Live TV routes, D2 get_lineups, F1 items); the campaign owes a dedicated audit of every [Authorize(Policy=...)] and RequestHelpers.GetUserId call site in the 10.11.8 controllers against Ferrofin's route guards.
- ✅ `GET /Environment/Drives`
  - classification: instance: per-host filesystem drives.
- ✅ `GET /Genres/{genreName}`
  - classification: verified 2026-08-31 (batch F2, revised after review): 4/4 legs clean, 108 fields compared, measured twice. SUPERSEDES both the batch-B1 record (true about the lazy create, blind to everything else — it probed ONE name) and the 2026-08-31 'accepted-divergence' record, whose accepted divergence has been REMOVED from the code rather than argued for.

`GenresController.GetGenre` has THREE behaviours on both trees (v10.11.8 :159-208; master's only file delta is dropping `.AddClientFields(User)` and adding `[Tags("Genre")]`):
  (a) a plain name -> `_libraryManager.GetGenre(name)` = `CreateItemByName<Genre>` (ported by B1);
  (b) a name containing `BaseItem.SlugChar` ('-', BaseItem.cs:98 on v10.11.8, :100 on master) -> `GetItemFromSlugName<T>`: EXACTLY three plain `GetItemList` lookups substituting '-'->'&', then '/', then '?', which create NOTHING;
  (c) no match -> `item ??= new Genre()`, a default-constructed row — the route cannot 404.
Ferrofin had only (a), so a hyphenated name MINTED a bogus genre: measured before the fix, `GET /Genres/Diag-Slug-F2x3` returned H {Name, Path /config/metadata/Genre/Diag-Slug-F2x3, SortName, LocationType FileSystem} against J's all-zero DTO. Ported as `by_name::resolve_by_name_or_slug` + the non-creating `LibraryManager::find_named_item` (core: `resolve_named_rows`, the resolve half of `get_named_items` with the CreateItemByName write left off) + `LibraryManager::empty_by_name_item`. Verified live that it creates nothing: after five reads.py runs across two builds, the only `Diag`-named genre in Ferrofin's BaseItems is the `Diag-Slug-F2x3` row minted by the OLD build, and no `Diag-No-Such-F2` row exists on either side.

Fixing (c) exposed a THIRD, wider defect this row is the only one that could see: `DtoService` derived `LocationType` from `IsVirtualItem`, where C# `BaseItem.LocationType` (v10.11.8 BaseItem.cs:319-336, master :327-344, identical) derives it from the PATH — empty path -> Virtual, a path with a scheme other than `file://` -> Remote (`ManagedFileSystem.IsPathFile`, :526-535), otherwise FileSystem. Ferrofin therefore said FileSystem for every pathless row. Ported as `dto_service::location_type_of`; measured on the empty item J=Virtual H=Virtual.

RECORDED UPSTREAM QUIRK (Ferrofin now reproduces it, deliberately). Because branch (b) reads a hyphen as a slug and nothing else, a genre whose real name CONTAINS a hyphen is unreachable through this route on both trees: `/Genres/Sci-Fi` answers with the empty DTO of (c) even when a genre named exactly `Sci-Fi` exists. The first cut of this port added a fourth, non-creating lookup on the literal name to reach it, and recorded that as an accepted divergence justified by 'don't port Jellyfin's bugs' plus the campaign rule against deleting a capability Ferrofin ships. Both justifications were wrong and the fourth lookup has been DROPPED: `GetItemFromSlugName` is byte-identical on v10.11.8 and master and stops after '?', so a fourth candidate matched NEITHER tree — the campaign's own definition of the bug — and the pre-existing behaviour the 'capability' argument pointed at was the create-on-GET defect this batch removed, not a shipped feature. No probe leg could observe the difference either (the fixture has no hyphenated genre), which is exactly why it had to be settled by the C# rather than by the wire. Pinned by the unit test `by_name_browse::a_hyphenated_name_is_only_ever_read_as_a_slug`, whose fake resolves a genre literally named `Sci-Fi` so a returning fourth candidate fails it.

FIXTURE: `suite/perf/gen-fixtures.sh` seeds a genre literally named `R&B` so the '&' substitution is probed on the wire (reads.py legs `/Genres/R-B` and `/Genres/Diag-No-Such-F2`). It is ADDED to every 50th movie rather than folded into the genre rotation: widening the rotation from 5 to 6 repartitions the whole shared corpus (500/5 -> 500/6 movies per genre) at the next wipe-and-provision, which would stale every count other batches recorded — including this campaign's own '200 movies carry action' — and make suite/perf-baseline.json non-comparable for the mandatory end-of-campaign perf gate. Verified by simulation that all five original genres keep exactly 200 movies and `R&B` gains 10. Until the fixture is re-provisioned both slug legs exercise the (c) empty-item path, which is a real agreement but not the substitution.
- ✅ `GET /Items/Filters`
  - classification: jellyfin-bug (nil-guid parameters), measured 2026-08-30 in the S1 review pass while verifying the empty-guid fix — recorded rather than left unmentioned. Jellyfin THROWS on an all-zero guid passed to a parameter that is not `userId`: `parentId=00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000` is 400 with the middleware's generic body `Error processing request.` (an unhandled ArgumentException from GetItemById/GetUserById — "Guid can't be empty" — not a designed 400), where Ferrofin treats the nil as absent and answers 200. `userId` is exempt on both sides because RequestHelpers.GetUserId maps a nil to the caller before anything looks it up. Measured on the lane-3 pair, admin token, all-zero value: GET /Items?parentId= (J400 H200), /Items?userId=&parentId= (J400 H200), /Items/Filters (J400 H200), /Items/Filters2 (J400 H200), /Years (J400 H200), /Shows/NextUp?seriesId=&parentId= (J400 H200), /Shows/{seriesId}/Episodes?seasonId=&adjacentTo= (J400 H404), /Trailers?parentId= (J500 H200 — an outright unhandled exception). Ferrofin is kept correct per the standing policy (do not port Jellyfin bugs); a 500 is never the right answer and neither is a leaked ArgumentException. NOT deferred and NOT accepted by an agent: this is a measured divergence awaiting the owner's call. UN-DEFER PATH if the owner wants byte parity on the refusal: reject a nil guid with 400 on the non-userId item-lookup parameters (parentId, seriesId, seasonId, adjacentTo, startItemId, appearsInItemId) in handlers::query_parse, leave `userId` alone, and add the eight probes above to the read layer as a status-class row.

- ✅ `POST /Items/RemoteSearch/BoxSet`
  - classification: jellyfin-bug-not-ported (a NARROW input class; the op itself is deep-verified — j_remote_search_identify diffs the FULL response body, key for key, on every other case). A NON-NUMERIC `Tmdb` provider id crashes the oracle's provider: v10.11.8 TmdbBoxSetProvider.cs:44 `Convert.ToInt32(searchInfo.GetProviderId(MetadataProvider.Tmdb), CultureInfo.InvariantCulture)` parses the stored id unguarded, so "notanumber" throws a FormatException, ProviderManager.GetRemoteSearchResults' catch-all swallows it, and Jellyfin answers [] — the name search the user actually typed never runs. Ferrofin declines to port the crash: a value that is not a number is not an id, so the remaining branches (and finally the name search) run. Per the repo's "don't port Jellyfin bugs" practice, Ferrofin is the correct side. Measured twice on the C1 lab pair with {"ProviderIds":{"Tmdb":"notanumber"},"Name":"<a real title>"}: Ferrofin 200 with 3 /search/collection rows for Name="The Lord of the Rings Collection", Jellyfin 200 []. NOT to be confused with a WHITESPACE-PADDED id: .NET's `int.Parse`/`Convert.ToInt32` default to NumberStyles.Integer (AllowLeadingWhite|AllowTrailingWhite), so " 603 " DOES pin the title upstream. That one was a real Ferrofin bug (`str::parse` rejects the padding, so the id branch was skipped and Identify answered with the name search); it is fixed — provider_manager.rs `parse_numeric_provider_id` trims — and is now probed live by the `*_padded` cases in j_remote_search_identify. Un-classify only if upstream starts guarding the parse.
- ✅ `POST /Items/RemoteSearch/Movie`
  - classification: jellyfin-bug-not-ported (a NARROW input class; the op itself is deep-verified — j_remote_search_identify diffs the FULL response body, key for key, on every other case). A NON-NUMERIC `Tmdb` provider id crashes the oracle's provider: v10.11.8 TmdbMovieProvider.cs:59 `int.Parse(id, CultureInfo.InvariantCulture)` parses the stored id unguarded, so "notanumber" throws a FormatException, ProviderManager.GetRemoteSearchResults' catch-all swallows it, and Jellyfin answers [] — the name search the user actually typed never runs. Ferrofin declines to port the crash: a value that is not a number is not an id, so the remaining branches (and finally the name search) run. Per the repo's "don't port Jellyfin bugs" practice, Ferrofin is the correct side. Measured twice on the C1 lab pair with {"ProviderIds":{"Tmdb":"notanumber"},"Name":"<a real title>"}: Ferrofin 200 with 20 /search/movie rows for Name="The Matrix", Jellyfin 200 []. NOT to be confused with a WHITESPACE-PADDED id: .NET's `int.Parse`/`Convert.ToInt32` default to NumberStyles.Integer (AllowLeadingWhite|AllowTrailingWhite), so " 603 " DOES pin the title upstream. That one was a real Ferrofin bug (`str::parse` rejects the padding, so the id branch was skipped and Identify answered with the name search); it is fixed — provider_manager.rs `parse_numeric_provider_id` trims — and is now probed live by the `*_padded` cases in j_remote_search_identify. Un-classify only if upstream starts guarding the parse.
- ◐ `POST /Items/RemoteSearch/MusicArtist`
  - classification: The library metadata-fetcher gate is now PORTED on this path: `LocalProviderManager::remote_search` resolves the `ItemId` reference item, reads ITS library's TypeOptions and drops every remote fetcher the "Metadata downloaders" list leaves unticked, honouring `IncludeDisabledProviders` as the short-circuit and the library's `MetadataFetcherOrder` for ordering — a port of MediaBrowser.Providers/Manager/ProviderManager.cs:787-830 -> :440-460 -> :462-491 -> MediaBrowser.Controller/BaseItemManager/BaseItemManager.cs:26-46. It only became REACHABLE for MusicArtist once `MusicArtistResolver` landed, because the gate resolves a library through `TopParentId` and an accessed-by-name artist has none. Measured live on the lab pair, both servers: scoped search with `ItemId` -> 200 with 0 results on BOTH (the fixture library ticks no MusicArtist fetcher; Ferrofin used to return 25), and `IncludeDisabledProviders=true` puts MusicBrainz back. The row also gained its first probe — `j_remote_search_identify` now sends `ItemId` and `IncludeDisabledProviders`, which no case did before, and resolves the artist id PER SERVER by Path so a server with no resolvable artist FAILS the row rather than skipping it. MEASUREMENT CAVEAT, not a divergence: MusicBrainz rate-limits both containers behind one egress IP and answers an over-rate query with an empty list, so the probe paces its legs 6 s apart and retries 5 times; if both servers still come back empty the row goes red with `named_count: 0` on both, which is the honest reading. The unscoped candidate list is compared whole, field for field, with ORDER normalised only — MusicBrainz returns tie-scored artists in an order two independent live queries do not agree on.

--- ALSO ON THIS ROW (merged from main) ---

open-work (NOT accepted): the fetcher gate is ported and correct, but Ferrofin cannot RESOLVE a MusicArtist's library, so the gate is inert for that one kind. `LocalProviderManager::remote_search` now runs the C# `CanRefreshMetadata` chain (v10.11.8 ProviderManager.cs:801-830 -> :462-491 -> BaseItemManager.cs:26-46) and resolves the owning library through the reference item's `TopParentId`. Ferrofin's MusicArtist rows are materialized BY NAME out of `ItemValues` (library_scan.rs `item_values_of`, Artist=0/AlbumArtist=1), so they carry TopParentId='', ParentId='', Path=NULL and no `AncestorIds` row — verified directly in the lab DB for all three fixture artists. With no library resolvable the gate falls through permissive, so the GATED leg of the reads probe (ItemId=<own Artist>, IncludeDisabledProviders=false, against a Music library whose TypeOptions[MusicArtist].MetadataFetchers is []) answers with the MusicBrainz hit where Jellyfin answers []. Jellyfin's MusicArtist IS the artist DIRECTORY (measured: Path=/media/synth/music/Artist 01, ParentId=<Music library>), which is what makes `LibraryManager.GetCollectionFolders` (LibraryManager.cs:2262-2303) find the collection folder by path. The UNGATED leg of the same row diffs byte-identical, so the search itself is right; only the reference-item resolution is missing. Fix, in order of faithfulness: (1) give the materialized MusicArtist row its artist directory as `Path` (plus the owning library's TopParentId/ParentId) so the existing resolution works and `/Artists` stops serving Path:null — a library_scan change on the perf-gated path, needing its own batch and its own perf run; or (2) add a path-prefix fallback to `LocalProviderManager::library_options_for`, which only helps once (1) supplies the path. Same root cause as the separately observed `GET /Users/{userId}/Items?IncludeItemTypes=MusicArtist&Recursive=true` returning [] on Ferrofin and 3 on Jellyfin.

- ◐ `GET /Items/{itemId}/Similar`
  - classification: instance/methodology (PROPERTY-VERIFIED, NOT body-diffed — the ledger counts and lists this row separately from the deep-verified ones; status codes, the empty short-circuits, the count invariant and the candidate rule are what is gated, by the reads.py `similar_invariants` probe, and each of the three property rows now issues its OWN alias rather than all three probing /Movies). Two independent reasons a movie seed's item array cannot diff, both measured on the loop pair. (1) ORDER: Jellyfin 10.11.8 sets `OrderBy = [(ItemSortBy.Random, Ascending)]` (v10.11.8 LibraryController.cs:801); five identical calls at limit=12 returned five different SETS, because limit < |universe|. (2) ALGORITHM: 10.11.8 builds ONE flat `InternalItemsQuery { Genres = item.Genres, Tags = item.Tags }`, while Ferrofin ports upstream master's weighted `MovieSimilarItemsProvider` (genre 10 / tag 5 / studio 5 / director 50 / actor+guest-star 15, MovieSimilarItemsProvider.cs:27-31) plus its phase-2 `IsMovie = true, IsPlayed = false` + ConfigureUserAccess pass. Measured at limit=1000: Jellyfin 299 items, Ferrofin 432, and Jellyfin's set is a STRICT SUBSET of Ferrofin's (onlyJ=0); the 133 extras each share a studio and/or a person with the seed and no genre — master's dimensions exactly. *** OWNER DECISION PENDING (NOT an agent-granted acceptance): running master's scorer against a 10.11.8 oracle is a deliberate ALGORITHM divergence on three ops, and CLAUDE.md reserves additions to the accepted-divergence list to the owner. The engineering case for keeping master is the repo's 'do not port Jellyfin bugs' rule — 10.11.8 additionally over-returns by 4 at every limit (measured 1/3/5/12/50 -> 5/7/9/16/54) because `BaseItemRepository.PrepareFilterQuery` (v10.11.8:1427-1430) adds 4 when `EnableGroupByMetadataKey` and never trims back, while Ferrofin honours limit exactly as master's `.Take(requestedLimit)` does — but the call is the owner's, and reverting to 10.11.8's flat query would make these three rows body-diffable at a pinned wide limit. *** WHAT IS VERIFIED LIVE instead of a body diff (every fact computed per server and then compared): status 200, StartIndex 0, TotalRecordCount == len(Items), the seed is never returned, every Type is in the C# IncludeItemTypes, the page is full, len(Items) <= limit+4 (the named Jellyfin quirk is the ONLY slack), an unknown seed is 404 on both, a nil seed is 200-empty on both, an Episode seed short-circuits empty on both, the five aliases agree as sets at limit=1000, and `obeys_own_candidate_rule` — each server's answer is re-derived independently from the DTO fields under ITS OWN documented algorithm (Jellyfin: every row shares a genre or tag with the seed, which 200 of the 500 fixture movies violate; Ferrofin: the answer equals master's weighted model's top-N IN ORDER by score desc, then Ferrofin's OWN SortName/Id tiebreak — master has no tiebreak at all, `MovieSimilarItemsProvider.cs:189-195` is `OrderByDescending(score)` alone, so that clause pins Ferrofin's rule, which is strictly more determined than the C#'s). The person dimension is matched as (Name, PersonType) pairs because that is what a `PeopleId` IS — 'the Peoples table has one row per (Name, PersonType)' (PeopleRepository.cs:47) — which is exactly what both master's `PeopleId` join and Ferrofin's SQL compute. Negative controls run 2026-08-29: the Ferrofin clause fails on a reversed order and on 12 arbitrary movies, the Jellyfin clause fails on 12 movies outside its universe. NOT REACHED BY ANY PARITY LAYER, and unit-covered instead: the phase-2 filters — the lab has zero played movies and one all-libraries user, so `IsPlayed = false` and ConfigureUserAccess are exercised only by `a_played_movie_is_not_suggested_to_the_user_who_played_it`, `a_library_the_user_cannot_access_contributes_nothing`, `a_blocked_media_folder_hides_its_rows` and `the_access_filter_does_not_starve_the_page` in similar_items_manager.rs. Three real Ferrofin bugs this row used to hide were fixed in the same pass: an unknown seed returned 200-empty instead of 404, a nil seed returned 400 instead of the C# root-folder fallback, and the no-limit default was 10 instead of C#'s `limit ?? 50` (SimilarItemsManager.cs:93).
- ⊙ `POST /LiveStreams/Open`
  - classification: security-fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1, cross-user authz): C# RequestHelpers.GetUserId (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Helpers/RequestHelpers.cs:67-85) has two halves: an absent/all-zero userId falls back to the caller, and a userId that is NOT the caller is honoured only for an ADMINISTRATOR, else SecurityException -> 403. Ferrofin ported only the first half here. Found by the S1 sweep, not by the plan. The route takes the id from BOTH the query and the body (query wins — v10.11.8 MediaInfoController.cs:287), and the resolved id is what the stream, and for Live TV the tuner, is opened as. Ferrofin folded both raw ids into the LiveStreamRequest with no gate. Re-measured independently in the S1 review pass with a fresh non-admin account ('s1fixprobe', created and deleted on both servers): a non-administrator naming the administrator's id is 403 on BOTH servers, while the administrator naming another user and a non-administrator naming their own id still succeed on both. Recorded in the S1 review pass: the batch claimed these five as flipped but left no row for them, so the fix existed only in the code and the claim. | empty-guid binding fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1 review pass, reviewer finding F2): ASP.NET binds an EMPTY or whitespace-only value for a `Guid?` to null (SimpleTypeModelBinder), so upstream answers `?userId=` and `?userId=%20` exactly as it answers a request with no userId at all; serde's `Option<Uuid>` tried to parse "" and made both a 400. S1 spread that pre-existing defect to five more routes when it gave previously parameter-less handlers a `Query<T>`. Re-measured on the lane-3 pair BEFORE the fix: `?userId=` and `?userId=%20` were 400 on Ferrofin against 200 on Jellyfin (404/404 where the probe id does not exist). FIXED AS A CLASS, not as an instance: every `Option<Uuid>` field of a `Query<...>` struct in ferrofin-api now binds through `handlers::query_parse::empty_as_none_uuid`. AFTER, the four spellings (absent / all-zero / empty / whitespace) agree on both servers across /Devices, /Channels, /Channels/Items/Latest, /LiveTv/Recordings/Folders, /LiveTv/Recordings/{recordingId}, /Audio/{itemId}/universal AND the four routes that carried the defect before S1 ever ran (/Items, /UserViews, /Persons, /Years). Request BODIES keep serde's strict parse on purpose: System.Text.Json rejects `"UserId": ""` upstream too.
- ✅ `GET /LiveTv/ChannelMappingOptions`
  - classification: jellyfin-bug, error path only: with NO `providerId` (which is what the breadth sweep sends) Jellyfin runs `ListingProviders.First(info => providerId == info.Id)` unguarded and throws InvalidOperationException → 500 (`Sequence contains no matching element` in the container log; src/Jellyfin.LiveTv/Listings/ListingsManager.cs:207-210 @ v10.11.8). Ferrofin answers 404 — the same `ResourceNotFoundException` Jellyfin's own `GetProvider` (ListingsManager.cs:461-463) raises for an unresolvable provider — so the sweep's bare-URL row reads H=404 J=500 by design. The CONTRACT path (`?providerId=<configured provider>`) agrees and is body-diffed in reads.py.
- ◐ `GET /LiveTv/GuideInfo`
  - classification: instance: property-verified by reads.py (guide_info_invariants), not asserted. The old note claimed the 'now .. now+7d' window matched Jellyfin; it did not — GuideManager.GetGuideInfo is now .. now + GetGuideDays() (LiveTvOptions.GuideDays clamped 1..14, default 7) and Ferrofin's handler took no state, so it answered a hardcoded 7 days whatever the dashboard was set to (measured: GuideDays=14 gave J +14d, F +7d). Ported. What is compared now: window length in seconds, whole-day-ness, now-relativity, UTC tick wire format, and that both bounds come from ONE UtcNow. Excluded: the instant itself, and the fraction's DIGIT COUNT — Utf8JsonWriter's FFFFFFF trims trailing zeros, so both servers emit 5, 6 or 7 digits depending on the instant (sampled 60x each).
- ✅ `GET /LiveTv/Info`
  - NOT compared: `EnabledUsers` is NOT compared as it is served: each raw user GUID is replaced by that server's own username before the diff, because the two `bench` accounts were minted independently. Count, membership and duplicates are still compared; the GUIDs themselves are not.
  - NOT compared: …and the substituted list is SORTED, so `EnabledUsers` ORDER is not compared either. Upstream emits `_userManager.Users.Where(IsLiveTvEnabled)` in store order (v10.11.8 LiveTvManager.cs:1207-1210), so order is a real upstream signal — it is dropped only because the two stores were populated in separate transactions. Pin the provisioning order on both servers and the `sorted()` can go, restoring the check.
- ⊙ `DELETE /LiveTv/ListingProviders`
  - classification: requires-livetv-tuner: the fixture tuner (M3U + XMLTV, two channels) exists, but no probe exercises this op yet — needs a journey that removes the fixture's listings provider and observes the guide emptying.
- ⊙ `POST /LiveTv/ListingProviders`
  - classification: open-work (NOT accepted): the SCHEDULES DIRECT listings backend is unported. Jellyfin registers two `IListingsProvider`s — `XmlTvListingsProvider` and `SchedulesDirect` (src/Jellyfin.LiveTv/Listings/SchedulesDirect.cs @ v10.11.8: token auth against json.schedulesdirect.org, `GetHeadends` → lineups, `GetLineups`/`AddLineup` and the schedules ingest) — and Ferrofin registers only XMLTV; `ferrofin-livetv/src/schedules_direct.rs` is the country list and nothing else. WHAT THAT COSTS, op by op: this route accepts and SAVES a provider whose `Type` is `SchedulesDirect` instead of rejecting it the way C# `GetProvider` does (ResourceNotFoundException → 404), so the row is stored and every later read of it resolves nothing; `GET /LiveTv/ListingProviders/Lineups?type=SchedulesDirect` answers 404 where a credentialed Jellyfin lists that account's headends; and `GET /LiveTv/ListingProviders/SchedulesDirect/Countries` is real but is the ONLY piece of the backend that is. NOT PROBEABLE ON THIS PAIR, which is why it needs this row: the fixture configures one XMLTV provider and no Schedules Direct account, so both servers agree on every request the lab can make and no live layer can ever go red on it. The `effect` verdict this op carries is the XMLTV path only — hence scope side-path. Un-defer path: port `SchedulesDirect` (token cache, `GetHeadends`, `GetLineups`, `AddLineup`, `GetProgramsAsync`) as a second `IListingsProvider` behind the same seam the XMLTV one uses, then make `save_listing_provider` resolve `Type` through the registry so an unknown type 404s like the C#. Needs its own batch (credentials + a recorded-response fixture); it is an open work item, not an accepted divergence.
- ✅ `GET /LiveTv/ListingProviders/Lineups`
  - classification: jellyfin-bug, error path only: `?type=xmltv` with no `id` makes Jellyfin call `GetProvider(type).GetLineups(null, …)` (ListingsManager.cs:104-107), and `XmlTvListingsProvider.GetLineups` dereferences the null `info` inside `GetXml` → NullReferenceException → 500 (XmlTvListingsProvider.cs:239-249 @ v10.11.8). Ferrofin returns 404 there, which is what Jellyfin itself returns for every other unresolvable provider. The two contract paths agree: `?id=<configured provider>` is 200 and body-diffed in reads.py, and no id/unknown id is 404 on both.
- ✅ `POST /LiveTv/Programs`
  - classification: lab-state (NOT a Ferrofin divergence, and NOT an accepted one — this row renders under "Lab state" and is counted there, never in the accepted tally). BOTH binder defects this row carried are now FIXED and pinned. (a) THE DELIMITED-STRING FORM: Ferrofin bound all seven of GetProgramsDto's collection properties as plain arrays and answered 422 to the delimited-STRING spelling upstream accepts — v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Models/LiveTvDtos/GetProgramsDto.cs decorates ChannelIds/SortBy/SortOrder/GenreIds/EnableImageTypes/Fields with JsonCommaDelimitedCollectionConverterFactory and Genres with the PIPE factory, and JsonDelimitedCollectionConverter.Read splits with RemoveEmptyEntries + Trim() and DROPS an entry the TypeConverter refuses ('catch (FormatException) { /* Ignore unconvertible inputs */ }') while the array arm stays a strict JsonSerializer.Deserialize<T[]>. reads.py legs 5-7 pin exactly that, drop semantics included. (b) THE BINDER'S REJECTION STATUS — fixed in batch G5 as a CLASS, not on this route: every Ferrofin route bound its body with axum::Json, whose rejection is 422 with a text/plain body, where every Jellyfin controller derives from BaseJellyfinApiController's [ApiController] (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/BaseJellyfinApiController.cs:12-18) and answers 400 with a ValidationProblemDetails document as application/json; charset=utf-8 — the untouched ASP.NET default (`git grep -n "InvalidModelStateResponseFactory|SuppressModelStateInvalidFilter|ValidationProblemDetails" v10.11.8 -- '*.cs'` returns nothing, and master's GetProgramsDto is byte-identical, so this was outcome 3: Ferrofin matched NEITHER tree). The opposite half of the same defect was that serde's derived Deserialize binds a JSON SEQUENCE to a struct positionally, so `[]` and `["StartDate"]` came back 200 with the whole 338-programme guide where System.Text.Json 400s — a malformed body silently accepted as a valid one. Both are closed by crates/ferrofin-api/src/extract.rs (JsonBody/JsonSeqBody/JsonValueBody, 75 extractor positions across 22 handler files; Json stays the RESPONSE type), tested as a class in crates/ferrofin-api/tests/body_binder.rs, and pinned live by reads.py legs 9-10. Only the STATUS is asserted there: the errors dictionary's keys and messages carry .NET type names ("Jellyfin.Data.Enums.ItemSortBy") and traceId is a per-request ASP.NET activity id — neither is reproducible, and neither was put in parity_diff.VOLATILE. RESIDUAL, the only thing left on this row and the reason it is still here: leg 2 (the whole guide inside the window BOTH servers hold, ~4058 leaves) reports MISSING keys with 0 mismatch and 0 extra, and every one of them is Status/TimerId/SeriesTimerId on a programme Jellyfin holds a timer for. That is DVR STATE, not code — drift from sibling batches' journeys on a lab that has been up for days. Ferrofin implements the enrichment (LiveTvManager.AddRecordingInfo is ported as add_recording_info in crates/ferrofin-livetv/src/manager.rs, and manager::tests::program_dtos_link_their_recording_timer asserts all three fields on a programme whose timer exists); it simply has no timers to attach. sweep.sh runs reads.py BEFORE journeys.py, so on the authoritative freshly-wiped pair neither server holds a timer when this leg runs. The three fields are deliberately NOT dropped from the compare: dropping them would hide a real enrichment regression, which is the one thing this leg is uniquely placed to catch. Delete this entry when the row comes back clean on a fresh sweep.

- ◐ `POST /LiveTv/SeriesTimers`
  - classification: jellyfin-bug (unknown ProgramId only): the op is deep-verified live on the fixture tuner by the j_livetv_series_timers journey (re-measured 2026-08-31, GREEN on both servers, twice); this row records the ONE input that journey deliberately does not diff a status on, so it is not lost. Posting a series timer whose ProgramId the guide does not hold makes Jellyfin throw InvalidOperationException("SeriesId for program not found") (v10.11.8 DefaultLiveTvService.cs:270-274), which surfaces as 500. Ferrofin ports the rejection but answers 400 and stores nothing. Rejecting is the ported behaviour; the 500 is the bug, and it is deliberately not reproduced (see 'Don't port Jellyfin bugs'). ALSO ON THIS ROW — PROBE DEFECT, FOUND AND FIXED 2026-08-31, kept because the row was red on it for a whole batch and the wrong conclusion was drawn. The journey used to overwrite `defaults["Name"]` on the same series timer it then fan-out-checked. `GetTimersForSeries` (v10.11.8 DefaultLiveTvService.cs:803-821) sets `query.Name = seriesTimer.Name` whenever `SeriesId` is empty — which is every timer in this XMLTV fixture — so a renamed series timer matches NO programme and schedules nothing. `posted_name_kept` and `fan_out` were therefore mutually exclusive and the row could not pass on ANY server, Jellyfin included. The batch report read that double red as 'behaviour upstream does not have'; it is not. Measured on the pair, identically on both servers: unmodified defaults schedule 7 showings (1 New = earliest, 6 Cancelled), renamed defaults schedule 0. The probe now splits the two roles across different series timers and asserts the renamed one schedules nothing as a POSITIVE leg, so the rule is measured from both sides. Three legs that were VACUOUS while the fan-out found nothing now really run: `its_timers_are_gone` on delete, and both arms of the `IsManual` hand-cancel contract.

  - classification: requires-livetv-tuner: the fixture tuner (M3U + XMLTV, two channels) exists, but no probe exercises this op yet — needs a series-timer journey (create from a programme → list → delete).

  - classification: requires-livetv-tuner: the fixture tuner (M3U + XMLTV, two channels) exists, but no probe exercises this op yet — needs a series-timer journey (create from a programme → list → delete).
- ✅ `POST /LiveTv/SeriesTimers/{timerId}`
  - classification: jellyfin-bug (unknown id only): the op itself is deep-verified body-diff live; this row records the ONE input whose STATUS the journey does not compare. UpdateSeriesTimerAsync no-ops when info.Id matches no series timer (v10.11.8 DefaultLiveTvService.cs:312-340), so 204 is the intended answer and Ferrofin gives it; Jellyfin reaches LiveTvManager.UpdateSeriesTimer's GetSeriesTimerInfo/GetService (LiveTvManager.cs:1177-1184) before that check and answers 500 (measured on the lab pair 2026-08-30: H=204, J=500). The journey compares only the half that IS comparable across the two: no row was created. Also recorded here: Ferrofin keys the update on the BODY's id ONLY, as the C# does — an earlier route-id fallback was strictly more lenient than upstream and has been removed, with a journey leg (foreign_body_id_changed_nothing) pinning it.

  - classification: verified 2026-08-11: SeriesTimerInfoDto defaults (ChannelId/Days/padding flags/…), 200 + schema conformant.

  - classification: jellyfin-bug (unknown id only): the op itself is deep-verified live; this row records the ONE input whose STATUS the journey does not compare. UpdateTimerAsync INTENDS ResourceNotFoundException on an unknown id (v10.11.8 DefaultLiveTvService.cs:346-349) and Ferrofin answers 404, but LiveTvDtoService.GetTimerInfo dereferences a null series timer first (LiveTvDtoService.cs:453-458), so Jellyfin really answers 500 (measured 2026-08-30: H=404, J=500). - ▫ `POST /LiveTv/Tuners/{tunerId}/Reset`
  - classification: jellyfin-bug, error path only: a `tunerId` whose prefix DOES name the registered service but carries no `_` makes Jellyfin index `parts[1]` of a one-element `Split('_', 2)` → IndexOutOfRangeException → 500 (src/Jellyfin.LiveTv/LiveTvManager.cs:1233-1245 @ v10.11.8; measured J=500 on `af999c25a00715699361240d4c6c7a53`). Ferrofin no-ops with 204. The two contract-relevant statuses agree and are the status-pair j_livetv_admin measures: a registered service prefix → 204, an unknown prefix → 400 (`ArgumentException("Service not found.")`, mapped by Jellyfin.Api/Middleware/ExceptionMiddleware.cs:127).
- ◐ `GET /Movies/{itemId}/Similar`
  - classification: instance/methodology (PROPERTY-VERIFIED, NOT body-diffed — the ledger counts and lists this row separately from the deep-verified ones; status codes, the empty short-circuits, the count invariant and the candidate rule are what is gated, by the reads.py `similar_invariants` probe, and each of the three property rows now issues its OWN alias rather than all three probing /Movies). Two independent reasons a movie seed's item array cannot diff, both measured on the loop pair. (1) ORDER: Jellyfin 10.11.8 sets `OrderBy = [(ItemSortBy.Random, Ascending)]` (v10.11.8 LibraryController.cs:801); five identical calls at limit=12 returned five different SETS, because limit < |universe|. (2) ALGORITHM: 10.11.8 builds ONE flat `InternalItemsQuery { Genres = item.Genres, Tags = item.Tags }`, while Ferrofin ports upstream master's weighted `MovieSimilarItemsProvider` (genre 10 / tag 5 / studio 5 / director 50 / actor+guest-star 15, MovieSimilarItemsProvider.cs:27-31) plus its phase-2 `IsMovie = true, IsPlayed = false` + ConfigureUserAccess pass. Measured at limit=1000: Jellyfin 299 items, Ferrofin 432, and Jellyfin's set is a STRICT SUBSET of Ferrofin's (onlyJ=0); the 133 extras each share a studio and/or a person with the seed and no genre — master's dimensions exactly. *** OWNER DECISION PENDING (NOT an agent-granted acceptance): running master's scorer against a 10.11.8 oracle is a deliberate ALGORITHM divergence on three ops, and CLAUDE.md reserves additions to the accepted-divergence list to the owner. The engineering case for keeping master is the repo's 'do not port Jellyfin bugs' rule — 10.11.8 additionally over-returns by 4 at every limit (measured 1/3/5/12/50 -> 5/7/9/16/54) because `BaseItemRepository.PrepareFilterQuery` (v10.11.8:1427-1430) adds 4 when `EnableGroupByMetadataKey` and never trims back, while Ferrofin honours limit exactly as master's `.Take(requestedLimit)` does — but the call is the owner's, and reverting to 10.11.8's flat query would make these three rows body-diffable at a pinned wide limit. *** WHAT IS VERIFIED LIVE instead of a body diff (every fact computed per server and then compared): status 200, StartIndex 0, TotalRecordCount == len(Items), the seed is never returned, every Type is in the C# IncludeItemTypes, the page is full, len(Items) <= limit+4 (the named Jellyfin quirk is the ONLY slack), an unknown seed is 404 on both, a nil seed is 200-empty on both, an Episode seed short-circuits empty on both, the five aliases agree as sets at limit=1000, and `obeys_own_candidate_rule` — each server's answer is re-derived independently from the DTO fields under ITS OWN documented algorithm (Jellyfin: every row shares a genre or tag with the seed, which 200 of the 500 fixture movies violate; Ferrofin: the answer equals master's weighted model's top-N IN ORDER by score desc, then Ferrofin's OWN SortName/Id tiebreak — master has no tiebreak at all, `MovieSimilarItemsProvider.cs:189-195` is `OrderByDescending(score)` alone, so that clause pins Ferrofin's rule, which is strictly more determined than the C#'s). The person dimension is matched as (Name, PersonType) pairs because that is what a `PeopleId` IS — 'the Peoples table has one row per (Name, PersonType)' (PeopleRepository.cs:47) — which is exactly what both master's `PeopleId` join and Ferrofin's SQL compute. Negative controls run 2026-08-29: the Ferrofin clause fails on a reversed order and on 12 arbitrary movies, the Jellyfin clause fails on 12 movies outside its universe. NOT REACHED BY ANY PARITY LAYER, and unit-covered instead: the phase-2 filters — the lab has zero played movies and one all-libraries user, so `IsPlayed = false` and ConfigureUserAccess are exercised only by `a_played_movie_is_not_suggested_to_the_user_who_played_it`, `a_library_the_user_cannot_access_contributes_nothing`, `a_blocked_media_folder_hides_its_rows` and `the_access_filter_does_not_starve_the_page` in similar_items_manager.rs. Three real Ferrofin bugs this row used to hide were fixed in the same pass: an unknown seed returned 200-empty instead of 404, a nil seed returned 400 instead of the C# root-folder fallback, and the no-limit default was 10 instead of C#'s `limit ?? 50` (SimilarItemsManager.cs:93).
- ✅ `GET /Packages`
  - classification: instance: aggregates the enabled repositories' manifests (PackageInfo[]) plus synthesized compiled-in entries; repositoryName/Url stamped from the repository actually fetched (manifest claims are untrusted); manifest fetch is size-capped + timed out. Catalog content is repository-config-specific — harness instances configure none — so nothing to diff. Verified 2026-08-14 by unit + server HTTP tests.
- ◐ `GET /Packages/{name}`
  - classification: property-verified: the two catalogues are each server's own repository content, so the single-package BODY is not comparable across instances, but every branch of InstallationManager.FilterPackages is. reads.py's `packages_by_name_invariants` derives 17 facts from each server's OWN /Packages[0] and diffs the fact dicts (a fact that is False on Ferrofin fails the row even when Jellyfin agrees): exact + case-insensitive name, guid-beats-name (`if (!id.IsEmpty()) ... else if (name is not null) ...` — the two predicates are alternatives, guid wins), an empty and an all-zeros guid falling through to the name (`IsEmpty()`), an unknown name as 404, the single-package body equalling that catalogue entry, and the WHOLE `Guid?` model-binding format set. THE FORMAT SET WAS THE HOLE, and it was a real live divergence, not a documentation slip: the fact list enumerated N, D and B and stopped, while `uuid::Uuid::parse_str` — what the handler used — is NOT `Guid.TryParse`'s set. Measured on the loop pair 2026-08-30, before the fix: `(guid)` (P) was 400 on Ferrofin and 200 on Jellyfin; `{0x...,{0x..}}` (X) was 400 and 200; a value padded with whitespace was 400 and 200 (.NET trims both ends); and `urn:uuid:guid`, which .NET refuses and `parse_str` accepts, was 200 and 400. Fixed in Rust, not in the probe: ferrofin_util::guid_extensions::parse_dotnet_guid ports Guid.TryParseGuid's dispatch (trim, then '(' -> ExactP, '{' -> ExactB when it contains a hyphen else ExactX, otherwise ExactD/ExactN) including TryParseExactX's two documented compat quirks — it eats whitespace anywhere, and its components are read as 32-bit so `0x1031b` is an accepted spelling of the short component `0x031b`, while a byte component above 0xFF is rejected. Both guid-bound plugin routes (GET /Packages/{name} and POST /Packages/Installed/{name}) bind through it. Re-measured after the fix: 27 spellings, all agreeing — the 12 basic ones and 15 exotic X/whitespace/overflow cases (truncating short-component overflow, 9-significant-digit overflow, a doubled 0x prefix, a missing 0x prefix, an empty component, 7 and 9 byte components, trailing junk, a leading '+', interior whitespace in D and P). NOT FIXED, recorded: the same `Uuid::parse_str` narrowness remains on every OTHER guid-bound route (axum's `Path<Uuid>`/`Query` extractors and handlers::query_parse), so P and X spellings are still refused there and `urn:uuid:` still accepted; no client sends either, and widening it is a whole-surface change this batch did not measure. Superseded the Layer-1 row, whose {name} was filled from a GENRE (sweep.py resolve_fixtures) so the live probe was GET /Packages/Action — 404/404 with the lookup never issued.
- ✅ `GET /Persons/{name}`
  - classification: verified 2026-08-31 (batch F2, revised after review): 2/2 legs clean, 56 fields compared, measured twice. REPLACES a pre-F2 'accepted-divergence: no lazy create on GET' record that was wrong on both counts — `LibraryManager.GetPerson` is a plain `GetItemList` lookup on both trees and creates nothing (unlike `GetGenre`/`GetStudio`, which are `CreateItemByName`), and Ferrofin already matched it. That upstream asymmetry is recorded HERE so it does not become invisible: `/Persons/{unknown}` must NOT materialize a Person row, while `/Genres/{unknown}` and `/Studios/{unknown}` must materialize theirs.

The row was red for two ported-wrong COLUMNS, both now fixed. (1) `SortName`: J="Alice Parity" H="alice parity". `BaseItem.CreateSortName` is gated on `EnableAlphaNumericSorting`, which `Person` overrides to false, so a Person's sort name is the name verbatim. The rule now has one home, `kinds::sort_name_for`, which `upsert_item`'s fallback, `backfill_missing_sort_names` and the people repository's inserts all route through. (2) `Path` and `PresentationUniqueKey` were never written: `save_people`'s Person insert now writes `Person.GetPath` (the first-letter subfolder form) and `Person-{Name}`, and creates the metadata directory after commit — `/config/metadata/People/{A,B,C}/{Name}` now exists on disk exactly as it does in the Jellyfin container.

STARTUP REPAIR, and the bug the review caught in it. Rows an older build wrote are repaired by `people_repository::repair_person_items` (it reported 3 on the first boot and 0 on the second). As first written that pass rewrote `SortName` to the verbatim name for EVERY Person row whose sort name differed, without reading `ForcedSortName` — so a person carrying a user sort-title override would have had it destroyed on every startup, and the pass would never converge on that row. It also falsified the pass's own 'no-op on a database adopted from Jellyfin' guarantee, since Jellyfin stores `ModifySortChunks(ForcedSortName).ToLowerInvariant()` there (BaseItem.cs:544 on master, :536 on v10.11.8). Fixed: the SELECT now reads `coalesce("ForcedSortName",'')` and a row carrying one keeps its `SortName` untouched (the `Path`/`PresentationUniqueKey` halves still run — those are derived columns no user owns), which is the same guard migration `0012_hermit_episode_sort_names.sql` and `upsert_item` already applied. The lab could not have caught it: all three of its people have an empty ForcedSortName. Pinned by `repair_person_items_never_overwrites_a_forced_sort_name`, which asserts both the survival of the override and that the second pass reports 0.
- ⊙ `POST /Playlists`
  - classification: security-fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1, cross-user authz): C# RequestHelpers.GetUserId (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Helpers/RequestHelpers.cs:67-85) has two halves: an absent/all-zero userId falls back to the caller, and a userId that is NOT the caller is honoured only for an ADMINISTRATOR, else SecurityException -> 403. Ferrofin ported only the first half here. Measured twice on the lane-3 pair (Ferrofin 18396 / Jellyfin 18397) with a second, non-admin account 's1probe' created on both: BEFORE, a non-admin passing ?userId=<admin> got 200 on Ferrofin - creating a playlist OWNED by the admin - against 403 on Jellyfin. The body's UserId is the same parameter by another route and is folded in before the gate, exactly as C# does. After the fix both servers return 403 for the same request, while an administrator naming another user's id and a non-administrator naming their own id still succeed on both (measured, twice). Regression tests in crates/ferrofin-api/tests/ cover all three cases per route. Fifth instance in this campaign of a C# authorization check Ferrofin served without one (A1 startup, B3 x29 Live TV routes, D2 get_lineups, F1 items); the campaign owes a dedicated audit of every [Authorize(Policy=...)] and RequestHelpers.GetUserId call site in the 10.11.8 controllers against Ferrofin's route guards.
- ◐ `GET /Playlists/{itemId}/InstantMix`
  - classification: expected-extension: Ferrofin generates an instant mix for a playlist (H=200); stock Jellyfin returns 404 (playlists are not an instant-mix source). A harmless superset.
- ⊙ `GET /Playlists/{playlistId}/Items`
  - classification: jellyfin-bug (nil-guid userId), measured 2026-08-30 in the S1 review pass (reviewer finding F6). This route is deliberately NOT gated on either server — v10.11.8 PlaylistsController.cs:521 is `var callingUserId = userId ?? User.GetUserId();`, no RequestHelpers.GetUserId call, followed by a playlist-access check — and S1's ruling to leave it alone is confirmed live: a non-administrator naming the owner is 200 on BOTH servers, so gating it would have diverged FROM Jellyfin. The one unrecorded difference is the all-zero guid: because the controller skips the helper, `Guid.Empty` reaches PlaylistManager.GetPlaylistForUser -> GetPlaylists -> UserManager.GetUserById(Guid.Empty), which throws ArgumentException and surfaces as 400 with the generic `Error processing request.` body; Ferrofin resolves no playlist for the nil user and answers 404. Measured: ?userId=00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000 is H404 J400, while empty, whitespace and absent are H404 J404 on the same probe id. Both servers refuse; only the status differs, and Jellyfin's is a leaked exception. Same family as the nil-guid cluster recorded on GET /Items — the owner's call, not an agent's.
- ⊙ `POST /Playlists/{playlistId}/Items`
  - classification: security-fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1, cross-user authz): C# RequestHelpers.GetUserId (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Helpers/RequestHelpers.cs:67-85) has two halves: an absent/all-zero userId falls back to the caller, and a userId that is NOT the caller is honoured only for an ADMINISTRATOR, else SecurityException -> 403. Ferrofin ported only the first half here. Measured twice on the lane-3 pair (Ferrofin 18396 / Jellyfin 18397) with a second, non-admin account 's1probe' created on both: The edit-permission check is made against the resolved userId, so an ungated one let a caller borrow another user's share on the playlist; measured BEFORE alongside the sibling routes at 200/204 on Ferrofin against 403 on Jellyfin for a non-admin naming another user. After the fix both servers return 403 for the same request, while an administrator naming another user's id and a non-administrator naming their own id still succeed on both (measured, twice). Regression tests in crates/ferrofin-api/tests/ cover all three cases per route. Fifth instance in this campaign of a C# authorization check Ferrofin served without one (A1 startup, B3 x29 Live TV routes, D2 get_lineups, F1 items); the campaign owes a dedicated audit of every [Authorize(Policy=...)] and RequestHelpers.GetUserId call site in the 10.11.8 controllers against Ferrofin's route guards.
- ⊙ `DELETE /Plugins/{pluginId}`
  - classification: accepted-divergence (NARROWED — the shared ids now behave identically). The previous note said 'a compiled-in extension honestly rejects with 400 where Jellyfin can uninstall any plugin'. The second half was false: `InstallationManager.UninstallPlugin` starts with `if (plugin.Instance?.CanUninstall == false) { LogWarning("Attempt to delete non removable plugin {PluginName}, ignoring request"); return; }` and `PluginsController` answers 204 anyway (v10.11.8). `BasePlugin.CanUninstall` is false for anything whose assembly sits in the server's own directory (BasePlugin.cs:54-55), which is every plugin compiled into Jellyfin's tree — so Jellyfin CANNOT uninstall its five in-tree providers either; it just says 204 and does nothing. Measured on the lane-3 pair before the fix: `DELETE /Plugins/{omdb}` F 400 / J 204. Ported in the F1 review pass and now verified by the journeys.py `j_plugin_uninstall` row (204 + still installed on each server's own read-back, `effect`), together with the version gate (`GetPlugin(id, version)`: 10.11.8.0 -> 204, 9.9.9.9 -> 404, 10.11.8 -> 404 because `Version.Equals` compares four components, notaversion -> 400 from the model binder). WHAT REMAINS DIVERGENT: a Ferrofin compiled-in EXTENSION reports `CanUninstall: true` — deliberately, because jellyfin-web gates its enable/disable toggle on that same flag — and then rejects the uninstall with 400, where upstream's true-flagged plugins really are deletable folders. A staged WASM plugin IS really removed (file + config + enabled override, restart-required). Admin-only.
- ◐ `GET /Plugins/{pluginId}/Configuration`
  - classification: expected-extension (SCOPE ONLY — the shared ids now diff clean). The previous note read 'instance: the two servers share no plugin id … so there is no config to fetch on both sides'. That was true when it was written and stopped being true in batch F1, which registered Jellyfin's five IN-TREE provider plugins (TMDb b8715ed1-…, Studio Images 872a7849-…, OMDb a628c0da-…, MusicBrainz 8c95c4d2-…, AudioDB a629c0da-… — the `Id` overrides on `MediaBrowser.Providers/Plugins/*/Plugin.cs`, identical on any Jellyfin). All five are now diffed live by the reads.py `GET /Plugins/{pluginId}/Configuration` row (5/5 clean, body-diff), and the configurations are no longer inert: the composition root injects the plugin manager into TmdbClient/OmdbClient/MusicBrainzClient/AudioDbClient/StudiosClient and each reads its settings at call time (see the table in `crates/ferrofin-providers/src/builtin_plugins.rs`). What REMAINS divergent is only scope: Ferrofin also serves this route for its own compiled-in extensions and staged WASM plugins, ids no stock Jellyfin has. Two TMDb settings are named OPEN WORK in that module doc (`ExcludeTags*`, `ImportSeasonName`) because the behaviour they gate — TMDB keywords as Tags, and a season named from TMDB — has never been ported at all.

--- ALSO ON THIS ROW (merged from worktree-dv-lane1) ---

instance (the body only; the row is now MEASURED by property). The old text claimed 'stock Jellyfin ships neither [extensions nor WASM plugins]', which is false and was hiding two defects: a live 10.11.8 ships FIVE bundled provider plugins with hardcoded GUIDs (TMDb b8715ed1-6c47-4528-9ad3-f72deb539cd4, MusicBrainz 8c95c4d2-e50c-4fb0-a4f3-6c06ff0f9a1a, OMDb a628c0da-fac5-4c7e-9d1a-7134223f14c8, AudioDB a629c0da-fac5-4c7e-931a-7174223f14c8, Studio Images 872a7849-1171-458d-a6fb-3de3d442ad30), each answering this route with a real BasePluginConfiguration. What remains true is that Ferrofin registers NONE of those ids (it registers compiled-in extensions and staged WASM components), so no shared subject exists and the 200 body cannot be id-correlated. FIXED HERE: the route was `RequireAuth`, so any authenticated account — a guest profile, a stolen playback token — could read a plugin's stored {ApiKey, Username, Password}. `PluginsController` carries [Authorize(Policy = Policies.RequiresElevation)] at CLASS level (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Controllers/PluginsController.cs:25, byte-identical on master) with exactly one [AllowAnonymous] override, GetPluginImage (:221) — so this read is admin-only and now is. reads.py `plugin_configuration_invariants` pins the object shape, the unknown/malformed-id statuses and the anonymous refusal on BOTH servers. WHAT WOULD EARN A BODY DIFF: the same plugin installed on both instances, which on the Jellyfin side needs a .NET assembly — the loading model Ferrofin rejects by design.
- ◐ `POST /Plugins/{pluginId}/Configuration`
  - classification: expected-extension (SCOPE ONLY). Same correction as the GET: the 'no shared plugin id' premise is false since batch F1 registered the five in-tree provider plugins. The write is real (JSON-validated, persisted, admin-only — a WASM plugin's config is guest input) and it is now LOAD-BEARING rather than merely stored: a saved TMDb `MaxCastMembers`, OMDb `CastAndCrew`, MusicBrainz `Server`/`RateLimit`/`ReplaceArtistName`, AudioDB `ReplaceAlbumName` or Studio Images `RepositoryUrl` changes the next metadata lookup, with no restart, the way `Plugin.Instance.Configuration` does upstream. Remaining divergence is scope: Ferrofin also accepts writes for its own extension/WASM plugin ids. Verified by unit + handler tests incl. 403, and the read-back is diffed cross-server by the GET row.

--- ALSO ON THIS ROW (merged from worktree-dv-lane1) ---

instance (the body only; the WRITE SEMANTICS are now measured by property, and three were wrong). 'No shared plugin id' is true for identity and false as a reason not to check. `UpdatePluginConfiguration` (v10.11.8 PluginsController.cs:186-201, unchanged on master) deserializes into `configPlugin.ConfigurationType` and then `if (configuration is not null) configPlugin.UpdateConfiguration(configuration)`. Ferrofin stored the raw request body, so measured live: (a) POST `null` STORED the literal null and the next GET answered the bare token `null` — an admin-reachable way to destroy a plugin's configuration and a violation of the contract's object shape, where Jellyfin's null body is a documented no-op; (b) `{"Bogus":"zzz"}` round-tripped for ever, where the typed deserializer drops it; (c) `{"Username":123}` was stored and served back as an integer to a plugin whose config type declares a string. All three are fixed in `FerrofinPluginManager::set_plugin_configuration` + `project_config`. ACCEPTED DIVERGENCE that remains: on a malformed/empty/array body Jellyfin answers HTTP 500 'Error processing request.' (an unhandled JsonException; unchanged on master) while Ferrofin answers 400 with a JSON error — Ferrofin is more correct and keeps it, per 'don't port Jellyfin bugs'. That leg is deliberately excluded from the probe's facts.
- ◐ `POST /Plugins/{pluginId}/Manifest`
  - classification: instance (no shared plugin id) — but the SHAPE is diffable, and it was wrong in five ways. `MediaBrowser.Common/Plugins/PluginManifest.cs` (v10.11.8; master changes nothing here) gives every property an explicit lowercase [JsonPropertyName], so this one response is camelCase against the server's PascalCase policy, and the id is `[JsonPropertyName("guid")] public Guid Id` written dashless by JsonGuidConverter. Measured: Jellyfin returns 13 camelCase keys, Ferrofin returned 5 PascalCase ones and a hyphenated `Id` — ZERO keys in common. The stale evidence ('verified 2026-08-14 by handler tests') was a test asserting `"Name":"Demo"`, which cemented the defect. Now a real `ferrofin_model::plugins::PluginManifest` DTO, projected with upstream's own bundled-plugin rule (`PluginManager.cs:562` builds `new PluginManifest { Id, Status, Name, Version }` and leaves the rest at ctor defaults) — which is exactly Ferrofin's case for both plugin tiers, neither of which ships a manifest document. The read is admin-only now too (class-level RequiresElevation). reads.py `plugin_manifest_invariants` pins the key set, the dashless `guid`, and the unknown-id/anonymous statuses on both servers.
