expected-extension (SCOPE ONLY — the shared ids now diff clean). The previous note ('no shared plugin id … so there is no manifest to fetch on both sides') hid a real wire bug: with the five ids shared, this route was 200 on BOTH servers with UNRELATED bodies. Jellyfin returns `PluginManifest`, the one camelCase DTO on the surface — every property carries an explicit `[JsonPropertyName]` and `Id` is spelled `guid` — with thirteen keys (assemblies/autoUpdate/category/changelog/description/guid/name/overview/owner/status/targetAbi/timestamp/version); Ferrofin answered a five-key PascalCase projection of its own ({Description,Id,Name,Status,Version}) that no Jellyfin client could parse. Ported in the F1 review pass, including the values upstream fills for a plugin with no `meta.json` on disk — the dummy record `PluginManager.CreatePluginInstance` builds (v10.11.8 PluginManager.cs:560-575) sets only Id/Name/Version/Status, so the descriptive strings stay empty, `assemblies` is [], `timestamp` is `0001-01-01T00:00:00.0000000Z`, `autoUpdate` keeps its `true` initializer and `imagePath` is omitted. Now diffed live by the reads.py `POST /Plugins/{pluginId}/Manifest` row over all five ids. Remaining divergence is scope only (Ferrofin's own extension/WASM ids). CURRENT LAB STATE: the row reads 4/5 clean, and the fifth leg is TMDb — whose Jellyfin-side `status`/`autoUpdate` were changed to `Restart`/`false` by an earlier reviewer's `POST /Plugins/{tmdb}/{v}/Enable` probe on this pair (they disclosed it). That is `ProcessAlternative`'s memory-only pair, exactly as described on the Enable row, and it survives until Jellyfin restarts. The other four ids are byte-identical on both servers, and Ferrofin's TMDb values (`Active`/`true`) match a PRISTINE Jellyfin's — verified against the four unperturbed ids on this same pair. Deliberately NOT worked around: dropping the TMDb leg would hide the one field that can diverge here, and restarting the Jellyfin container to clear it would be a one-sided mutation of the pair. The final sweep runs on a freshly wiped pair, where the leg is clean.
- ⊙ `DELETE /Plugins/{pluginId}/{version}`
  - classification: accepted-divergence (NARROWED — the shared ids now behave identically). The previous note said 'a compiled-in extension honestly rejects with 400 where Jellyfin can uninstall any plugin'. The second half was false: `InstallationManager.UninstallPlugin` starts with `if (plugin.Instance?.CanUninstall == false) { LogWarning("Attempt to delete non removable plugin {PluginName}, ignoring request"); return; }` and `PluginsController` answers 204 anyway (v10.11.8). `BasePlugin.CanUninstall` is false for anything whose assembly sits in the server's own directory (BasePlugin.cs:54-55), which is every plugin compiled into Jellyfin's tree — so Jellyfin CANNOT uninstall its five in-tree providers either; it just says 204 and does nothing. Measured on the lane-3 pair before the fix: `DELETE /Plugins/{omdb}` F 400 / J 204. Ported in the F1 review pass and now verified by the journeys.py `j_plugin_uninstall` row (204 + still installed on each server's own read-back, `effect`), together with the version gate (`GetPlugin(id, version)`: 10.11.8.0 -> 204, 9.9.9.9 -> 404, 10.11.8 -> 404 because `Version.Equals` compares four components, notaversion -> 400 from the model binder). WHAT REMAINS DIVERGENT: a Ferrofin compiled-in EXTENSION reports `CanUninstall: true` — deliberately, because jellyfin-web gates its enable/disable toggle on that same flag — and then rejects the uninstall with 400, where upstream's true-flagged plugins really are deletable folders. A staged WASM plugin IS really removed (file + config + enabled override, restart-required). Admin-only.
- ▫ `GET /Plugins/{pluginId}/{version}/Image`
  - classification: expected-extension (SCOPE ONLY). The previous note ('no shared plugin id across the two servers, so no image to fetch on both sides') is false since batch F1. Measured on the shared ids: 404 on BOTH, for the upstream reason — an in-tree plugin's manifest has `ImagePath = null`, so `PluginsController.GetPluginImage` returns `NotFound()` (v10.11.8 PluginsController.cs:212-215). The assets.py `GET /Plugins/{pluginId}/{version}/Image` row now probes all five plus an unknown guid and is stamped `status-class`, NOT the body-diff headline: nothing is served, so the only thing that agreed is the refusal. The row would compare bytes if either server ever shipped one. Also fixed here: the path version is now honoured (`GetPlugin(id, version)`), so a wrong version is a 404 rather than the plugin's image.

--- ALSO ON THIS ROW (merged from worktree-dv-lane1) ---

instance (the 200 BODY only). No plugin on EITHER server reports HasImage — Ferrofin's registrations all pass `None`, and 10.11.8's five bundled plugins carry no Manifest.ImagePath — so the 200 branch is unreachable on both, and a shared image subject would need the SAME external plugin installed on both, which on the Jellyfin side needs .NET assembly loading. The STATUS paths are fully diffable and one of them diverged: the C# binds `[FromRoute, Required] Version version` and calls `GetPlugin(id, version)`, which matches with `p.Version.Equals(version)` (v10.11.8 Emby.Server.Implementations/Plugins/PluginManager.cs:293-311); Ferrofin bound the segment and DISCARDED it, so a malformed version was a 404 where Jellyfin gives 400, and — on the three sibling `{version}` routes — `9.9.9.9` and even `notaversion` returned 204 and really did enable/disable/uninstall the plugin. Fixed for all four routes via `plugin_at_version`, including .NET's absent-component-is-minus-one rule (a live 10.11.8 404s `10.11.8` against its installed `10.11.8.0`). The 200 path also gained the `Content-Disposition: attachment` header both trees set. Anonymous access is correct and stays: this is the ONE action upstream marks [AllowAnonymous] (PluginsController.cs:221).
- ▫ `POST /QuickConnect/Authorize`
  - classification: security-fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1, cross-user authz): C# RequestHelpers.GetUserId (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Helpers/RequestHelpers.cs:67-85) has two halves: an absent/all-zero userId falls back to the caller, and a userId that is NOT the caller is honoured only for an ADMINISTRATOR, else SecurityException -> 403. Ferrofin ported only the first half here. Measured twice on the lane-3 pair (Ferrofin 18396 / Jellyfin 18397) with a second, non-admin account 's1probe' created on both: BEFORE, a non-admin passing ?userId=<admin> reached the manager on Ferrofin (404 for the unknown probe code - i.e. the user gate never ran) against 403 on Jellyfin. This one is a privilege escalation, not only a parity divergence: authorizing a pending request mints a session for the NAMED user on the pairing device, so any authenticated account could hand a device an administrator session. After the fix both servers return 403 for the same request, while an administrator naming another user's id and a non-administrator naming their own id still succeed on both (measured, twice). Regression tests in crates/ferrofin-api/tests/ cover all three cases per route. Fifth instance in this campaign of a C# authorization check Ferrofin served without one (A1 startup, B3 x29 Live TV routes, D2 get_lineups, F1 items); the campaign owes a dedicated audit of every [Authorize(Policy=...)] and RequestHelpers.GetUserId call site in the 10.11.8 controllers against Ferrofin's route guards.
- ✅ `GET /Repositories`
  - classification: instance: returns the admin-configured package repositories — instance-local state (harness instances configure none), nothing to diff. Verified 2026-08-14 by unit + handler tests.
- ⊙ `POST /Repositories`
  - classification: instance: replaces the configured package repositories (admin-only, RequiresElevation port) — instance-local state, nothing to diff. Verified 2026-08-14 by unit + handler tests incl. 403 for non-admin.
- ✅ `GET /ScheduledTasks`
  - classification: instance: LastExecutionResult — which tasks have run on this instance and when — is the ONE per-run field, and reads.py's `tasks_projection` drops it. Everything else is diffed across five legs, one per isHidden/isEnabled filter combination: the task set, the wire ORDER, each task's Name/Description/Category/IsHidden/State/CurrentProgressPercentage/Triggers, and — as the explicit `WireId` alias, because `Id` is in the global VOLATILE denylist — the task Id itself. PREVIOUSLY MIS-CLASSIFIED, NOW FIXED IN THE SERVER: this entry used to call the Id divergence 'a pre-existing accepted divergence', which it never was. `ScheduledTaskWorker.Id` (v10.11.8:219) is `ScheduledTask.GetType().FullName.GetMD5().ToString("N")` — MD5 over the UTF-16LE type name wrapped in a .NET Guid (`BaseExtensions.GetMD5`) — a PORTABLE value every Jellyfin install agrees on, so Ferrofin emitting the task key there made the two servers' task URLs mutually incompatible: `GET /ScheduledTasks/b461ef918ab28520928183e794350e3c` was 200 on Jellyfin and 404 on Ferrofin, and `GET /ScheduledTasks/CleanActivityLog` the reverse, so a stored dashboard link broke. No C# evidence said Jellyfin was wrong, because there is none. `ferrofin_traits::tasks::task_id_for_key` now reproduces the derivation from a table of the twenty C# type FullNames (plus the two ported plugins'), unit-pinned against the twenty ids a live 10.11.8 returns, and the registry resolves `/ScheduledTasks/{taskId}` by Id case-insensitively exactly as `FirstOrDefault(i => string.Equals(i.Id, taskId, OrdinalIgnoreCase))` does — a KEY is deliberately 404, as it is upstream. ONE NAMED OPEN DIFFERENCE remains and is NOT accepted: `list_filtered` sorts by name with Rust's ordinal `String::cmp`, where C# `OrderBy(o => o.Name)` is `Comparer<string>.Default` (CurrentCulture). The two agree on all twenty 10.11.8 task names — the live wire order is byte-identical on both servers — because every name is ASCII and capitalised; they would diverge on a lower-case initial or on punctuation/diacritics. Porting it needs an ICU collation dependency.
- ▫ `DELETE /ScheduledTasks/Running/{taskId}`
  - classification: jellyfin-bug: stopping a task that is no longer running is 500 on Jellyfin and 204 on Ferrofin. `ScheduledTaskWorker.Cancel()` (v10.11.8:377-385) opens with `if (State != TaskState.Running) throw new InvalidOperationException("Cannot cancel a Task unless it is in the Running state.")`, and `ScheduledTasksController.StopTask` calls `_taskManager.Cancel(task)` with no catch, so the exception reaches the error middleware. The action declares only 204 and 404 (`[ProducesResponseType(Status204NoContent)]`, `Status404NotFound`) and the vendored contract declares no 500 for this path, so the throw is unintended, not a documented behaviour — Ferrofin's `FerrofinTaskManager::stop_run` returns Ok for an already-idle task (404 only for an unknown id) and is kept correct (do not port Jellyfin bugs). MEASURED 2026-08-30 on the loop pair, deterministic, not a race: POST-then-DELETE on `ec2f221fd8e7706b3d3afd2c4591b4d7` (Audio Normalization) is (204, 500) four times running on Jellyfin and (204, 204) four times on Ferrofin, and a DELETE with nothing running at all is 500 vs 204. The journeys row therefore reads H=True J=False and only goes red on a WARM lab: the first Audio Normalization run does real work and is still Running when the DELETE lands (which is why an earlier journeys record has it green), while every later run finishes in well under a millisecond. Ferrofin's own leg passes on both.
- ✅ `GET /Sessions`
  - classification: security-fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1, cross-user authz): C# RequestHelpers.GetUserId (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Helpers/RequestHelpers.cs:67-85) has two halves: an absent/all-zero userId falls back to the caller, and a userId that is NOT the caller is honoured only for an ADMINISTRATOR, else SecurityException -> 403. Ferrofin ported only the first half here. The gate is on `controllableByUserId`. Measured: a non-admin passing ?controllableByUserId=<admin> was 200 on Ferrofin against 403 on Jellyfin. Re-measured independently in the S1 review pass with a fresh non-admin account ('s1fixprobe', created and deleted on both servers): a non-administrator naming the administrator's id is 403 on BOTH servers, while the administrator naming another user and a non-administrator naming their own id still succeed on both. Recorded in the S1 review pass: the batch claimed these five as flipped but left no row for them, so the fix existed only in the code and the claim. | empty-guid binding fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1 review pass, reviewer finding F2): ASP.NET binds an EMPTY or whitespace-only value for a `Guid?` to null (SimpleTypeModelBinder), so upstream answers `?userId=` and `?userId=%20` exactly as it answers a request with no userId at all; serde's `Option<Uuid>` tried to parse "" and made both a 400. S1 spread that pre-existing defect to five more routes when it gave previously parameter-less handlers a `Query<T>`. Re-measured on the lane-3 pair BEFORE the fix: `?userId=` and `?userId=%20` were 400 on Ferrofin against 200 on Jellyfin (404/404 where the probe id does not exist). FIXED AS A CLASS, not as an instance: every `Option<Uuid>` field of a `Query<...>` struct in ferrofin-api now binds through `handlers::query_parse::empty_as_none_uuid`. AFTER, the four spellings (absent / all-zero / empty / whitespace) agree on both servers across /Devices, /Channels, /Channels/Items/Latest, /LiveTv/Recordings/Folders, /LiveTv/Recordings/{recordingId}, /Audio/{itemId}/universal AND the four routes that carried the defect before S1 ever ran (/Items, /UserViews, /Persons, /Years). Request BODIES keep serde's strict parse on purpose: System.Text.Json rejects `"UserId": ""` upstream too.
- ∅ `GET /Shows/NextUp`
  - classification: jellyfin-bug (nil-guid parameters), measured 2026-08-30 in the S1 review pass while verifying the empty-guid fix — recorded rather than left unmentioned. Jellyfin THROWS on an all-zero guid passed to a parameter that is not `userId`: `parentId=00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000` is 400 with the middleware's generic body `Error processing request.` (an unhandled ArgumentException from GetItemById/GetUserById — "Guid can't be empty" — not a designed 400), where Ferrofin treats the nil as absent and answers 200. `userId` is exempt on both sides because RequestHelpers.GetUserId maps a nil to the caller before anything looks it up. Measured on the lane-3 pair, admin token, all-zero value: GET /Items?parentId= (J400 H200), /Items?userId=&parentId= (J400 H200), /Items/Filters (J400 H200), /Items/Filters2 (J400 H200), /Years (J400 H200), /Shows/NextUp?seriesId=&parentId= (J400 H200), /Shows/{seriesId}/Episodes?seasonId=&adjacentTo= (J400 H404), /Trailers?parentId= (J500 H200 — an outright unhandled exception). Ferrofin is kept correct per the standing policy (do not port Jellyfin bugs); a 500 is never the right answer and neither is a leaked ArgumentException. NOT deferred and NOT accepted by an agent: this is a measured divergence awaiting the owner's call. UN-DEFER PATH if the owner wants byte parity on the refusal: reject a nil guid with 400 on the non-userId item-lookup parameters (parentId, seriesId, seasonId, adjacentTo, startItemId, appearsInItemId) in handlers::query_parse, leave `userId` alone, and add the eight probes above to the read layer as a status-class row.
- ✅ `GET /Shows/{itemId}/Similar`
  - classification: flat-provider methodology (SET-GATED, order not gateable). Seeds of this kind take the C# FLAT provider: one `InternalItemsQuery { Genres = item.Genres, Tags = item.Tags, IncludeItemTypes = [<kind>], EnableGroupByMetadataKey = false, EnableTotalRecordCount = true, OrderBy = [(ItemSortBy.Random, SortOrder.Ascending)] }` — identical in v10.11.8 (built inline at LibraryController.cs:791-801) and in master (SeriesSimilarItemsProvider.cs:49, MusicAlbumSimilarItemsProvider.cs:50, MusicArtistSimilarItemsProvider.cs:50, AudioSimilarItemsProvider.cs:50, LiveTvProgramSimilarItemsProvider.cs:89). Ferrofin runs the same query and, as of this pass, the same `Random` order. IT BRIEFLY DID NOT: batch A2 first shipped `SortName` here, which is not cosmetic — at limit < |candidates| a fixed order returns a DIFFERENT ITEM, measured on the loop pair as `/Items/{audio}/Similar?limit=1` answering 'Artist 01 Album 01 Track 02' on five consecutive Ferrofin calls while Jellyfin alternated Track 02 / Track 03. That divergence is now closed in the server (`similar_items_manager.rs::flat_similar`, unit test `the_flat_provider_orders_random_not_by_name`), not papered over. WHAT THIS LEDGER ROW DOES AND DOES NOT GATE: `Random` on both sides means the ORDER can never be diffed by any probe, and neither can a page narrower than the candidate pool — parity_diff aligns arrays by Path>Name>Id, so a reordered Items array returns 0 diffs and always will. The reads.py leg therefore pins `limit=1000`, far above the fixture's pool, where the answer is the whole candidate set and so fully determined; what is gated there is the SET, TotalRecordCount and every per-item DTO field. Anything order-sensitive is covered by unit tests instead, never by this row. SERIES SEED: the pre-A2 server ran the weighted MOVIE scorer for every kind, which credits a shared studio (+5) and shared people and pulled genre-unrelated series in (F ['Series 06','Series 03','Series 05','Series 07'] vs J ['Series 06']); `SeriesSimilarItemsProvider` filters on Genres/Tags alone, and porting it made the set match. The probe also exposed a DTO gap since fixed: Ferrofin omitted `AirDays` on Series where 10.11.8 always sends `[]` (v10.11.8 DtoService.cs:1243 assigns `series.AirDays`, and v10.11.8 Series.cs:31 initialises it to `Array.Empty<DayOfWeek>()` while no column persists it) — the null-where-Jellyfin-sends-non-null shape strict clients crash on.

- ✅ `GET /Studios/{name}`
  - classification: open-work (NOT accepted) — SUPERSEDES the batch-B1 record. B1's two claims still hold and were re-measured on the current tree: the `CreateItemByName<Studio>` lazy create is live (`/Studios/<never-seen name>` is 200 on both, with the Jellyfin-shaped Path), and the per-kind ItemValue count fix holds. Its closing sentence, 'the REAL studios still count 254/250/4 on both servers', was a hand measurement no layer reproduced; reads.py now carries a THIRD leg over the LAST shared studio by SortName, so the claim is probed. Current state: 2/3 legs clean.

WHAT F2 FIXED (a real server bug the third leg exists to keep honest): a provider refresh updated `BaseItems.Studios/Genres/Tags` but never re-derived `ItemValues`/`ItemValuesMap`, so the by-name counts this route returns went stale against Ferrofin's OWN corpus. `ProviderManager::persist_refreshed` called only `save_items`; C# does both halves in one call — `BaseItemRepository.SaveItems` saves the row and then rewrites the by-name index for it (v10.11.8 `BaseItemRepository.cs:674-735`, unchanged on master), reached from a refresh via `MetadataService.SaveItemAsync` → `UpdateToRepositoryAsync` → `LibraryManager.UpdateItemAsync`. Ferrofin's editor path (`LibraryManager::update_items`) already did this; the refresh path was the hole. Fixed by calling `save_item_values(id, &item_values_of(entity))` there; `item_values_of` moved to `ferrofin-db` so `ferrofin-providers` can use it without depending on `ferrofin-core`. Damage already on disk is still visible on this lab (H's Movie 0001 row says Studios='Castle Rock Entertainment' while its ItemValuesMap still says 'Ferrofin Studios') and is repaired by the next library scan, which re-derives the index per item — unlike the Person columns, this index has a self-heal path, so no startup repair was added.

WHY THE ROW IS STILL RED, and it is NOT this fix: the remaining leg diffs ChildCount J=253 H=254 / MovieCount J=249 H=250 on 'Parity Pictures', and that is measured CORPUS SKEW from an earlier lane's write journey, not a query defect. Each server's answer is exactly consistent with its own database (H 250 movies carry 'parity pictures', J 249). The single divergent item is Movie 0002: its on-disk NFO says `<studio>Parity Pictures</studio>`, H's row carries Studios='Parity Pictures', Genres='Comedy|SciFi', Tags='parity-test', and J's row carries NONE of the three — Jellyfin lost them. Cleared by the campaign's wipe-and-provision sweep; NOT chased here, because resetting one side of a shared pair is exactly what the lab-hygiene rule forbids.

PROBE NOTE (review pass): the third leg is the LAST shared studio by SortName. When fewer than two names are shared it is now SKIPPED, not silently re-pointed at the first shared name — the old fallback made the extra leg a byte-identical duplicate and doubled the `field(s) compared` depth the ledger publishes for no extra coverage. It does not fire on this lab (both studios are shared), but a corpus that narrows should shrink the evidence, not inflate it.

- ⊙ `GET /System/ActivityLog/Entries`
  - classification: instance: activity-log entries differ per instance/run.
- ◐ `GET /System/Info/Storage`
  - classification: instance: exactly three things here are not comparable, and they are named. (1) The Path strings are the container layout, not code: Ferrofin's /config/cache vs Jellyfin's JELLYFIN_CACHE_DIR=/cache, /config/web vs /jellyfin/jellyfin-web. (2) FreeSpace/UsedSpace are sampled at two different instants. (3) The Libraries array ORDER is inherited verbatim from GET /Library/VirtualFolders and belongs to that row. Everything else IS comparable because both containers stat the same host filesystem, and reads.py's `storage_invariants` now asserts it as `property`: the exact FolderStorageDto key set, DeviceId == Path (on Unix DriveInfo.Name is the ctor argument verbatim), FreeSpace+UsedSpace == the filesystem's TotalSize byte-for-byte, the StorageType DriveType word, and the libraries compared as a SET. The missing-folder branch (FreeSpace/UsedSpace -1 with StorageType/DeviceId null, StorageHelper.GetFreeSpaceOf's catch arm) cannot be forced symmetrically from a probe and is covered by a unit test in crates/ferrofin-core/src/system_manager.rs instead.
- ◐ `GET /System/Logs`
  - classification: instance: `LogFile` has four fields and two (DateCreated/DateModified) are already VOLATILE, so a diff could only compare Name and Size — and both are the host's own. Jellyfin's Serilog sink writes `log_YYYYMMDD.log` (Jellyfin.Server/Resources/Configuration/logging.json: `%JELLYFIN_LOG_DIR%//log_.log`, rollingInterval Day) while Ferrofin's tracing-appender writes `log.YYYY-MM-DD.log` (apps/ferrofin-server/src/bootstrap.rs); Size is each server's own log volume. Name is also parity_diff's array-align key, so the sweep can only ever report '(whole item)'. Body-diff is structurally impossible; the row is earned as `property` by assets.py. WHAT THE SHAPE ASSERTIONS ALONE MISSED, AND WHY THE ROW NOW ASSERTS CONTENTS TOO: the listing's shape, extension filter, bare filenames and ordering can all pass while a whole CLASS of file Jellyfin produces is absent. It was. `TranscodeManager.StartFfMpeg` (MediaBrowser.MediaEncoding/Transcoding/TranscodeManager.cs:450-467 at v10.11.8) writes each transcode's ffmpeg stderr into `ApplicationPaths.LogDirectoryPath` as `FFmpeg.Transcode-`/`FFmpeg.Remux-`/`FFmpeg.DirectStream-{yyyy-MM-dd_HH-mm-ss}_{mediaSourceId}_{guid8}.log` — deliberately into the directory this route serves ('let's put it in the log directory'), so an operator can download a failed transcode's log from the dashboard. Ferrofin wrote it beside the playlist in the transcode temp dir instead (`playlist_path.with_extension("log")`), so its listing held no ffmpeg log at all while Jellyfin's held several. FIXED IN THE SERVER: `StartFfMpegRequest` now carries the LOG DIRECTORY and `TranscodeManagerImpl::start_ffmpeg` names the file with the ported `ffmpeg_log_file_name`. assets.py starts a real transcode on each server and then asserts the listing contains a matching name — a fact both servers must satisfy, and the one a Name/Size diff could never reach. The rest of the row is unchanged: the exact 4-key `LogFile` contract, the .txt/.log extension filter (`ManagedFileSystem.GetFiles` — the Jellyfin container's extension-less `.jellyfin-log` must be absent), bare filenames, and the C# ordering rule OrderByDescending(DateModified).ThenByDescending(DateCreated).ThenBy(Name), unit-tested in crates/ferrofin-api/tests/system.rs. `sig_match` now fails this row when either server answers False on any of those facts, or when the fact could not be established at all — agreeing on a broken invariant is not parity.
- ✅ `GET /System/Ping`
  - classification: instance: returns the server name string (ferrofin vs the Jellyfin instance name).
- ◐ `GET /Trailers/{itemId}/Similar`
  - classification: instance/methodology (PROPERTY-VERIFIED, NOT body-diffed — the ledger counts and lists this row separately from the deep-verified ones; status codes, the empty short-circuits, the count invariant and the candidate rule are what is gated, by the reads.py `similar_invariants` probe, and each of the three property rows now issues its OWN alias rather than all three probing /Movies). Two independent reasons a movie seed's item array cannot diff, both measured on the loop pair. (1) ORDER: Jellyfin 10.11.8 sets `OrderBy = [(ItemSortBy.Random, Ascending)]` (v10.11.8 LibraryController.cs:801); five identical calls at limit=12 returned five different SETS, because limit < |universe|. (2) ALGORITHM: 10.11.8 builds ONE flat `InternalItemsQuery { Genres = item.Genres, Tags = item.Tags }`, while Ferrofin ports upstream master's weighted `MovieSimilarItemsProvider` (genre 10 / tag 5 / studio 5 / director 50 / actor+guest-star 15, MovieSimilarItemsProvider.cs:27-31) plus its phase-2 `IsMovie = true, IsPlayed = false` + ConfigureUserAccess pass. Measured at limit=1000: Jellyfin 299 items, Ferrofin 432, and Jellyfin's set is a STRICT SUBSET of Ferrofin's (onlyJ=0); the 133 extras each share a studio and/or a person with the seed and no genre — master's dimensions exactly. *** OWNER DECISION PENDING (NOT an agent-granted acceptance): running master's scorer against a 10.11.8 oracle is a deliberate ALGORITHM divergence on three ops, and CLAUDE.md reserves additions to the accepted-divergence list to the owner. The engineering case for keeping master is the repo's 'do not port Jellyfin bugs' rule — 10.11.8 additionally over-returns by 4 at every limit (measured 1/3/5/12/50 -> 5/7/9/16/54) because `BaseItemRepository.PrepareFilterQuery` (v10.11.8:1427-1430) adds 4 when `EnableGroupByMetadataKey` and never trims back, while Ferrofin honours limit exactly as master's `.Take(requestedLimit)` does — but the call is the owner's, and reverting to 10.11.8's flat query would make these three rows body-diffable at a pinned wide limit. *** WHAT IS VERIFIED LIVE instead of a body diff (every fact computed per server and then compared): status 200, StartIndex 0, TotalRecordCount == len(Items), the seed is never returned, every Type is in the C# IncludeItemTypes, the page is full, len(Items) <= limit+4 (the named Jellyfin quirk is the ONLY slack), an unknown seed is 404 on both, a nil seed is 200-empty on both, an Episode seed short-circuits empty on both, the five aliases agree as sets at limit=1000, and `obeys_own_candidate_rule` — each server's answer is re-derived independently from the DTO fields under ITS OWN documented algorithm (Jellyfin: every row shares a genre or tag with the seed, which 200 of the 500 fixture movies violate; Ferrofin: the answer equals master's weighted model's top-N IN ORDER by score desc, then Ferrofin's OWN SortName/Id tiebreak — master has no tiebreak at all, `MovieSimilarItemsProvider.cs:189-195` is `OrderByDescending(score)` alone, so that clause pins Ferrofin's rule, which is strictly more determined than the C#'s). The person dimension is matched as (Name, PersonType) pairs because that is what a `PeopleId` IS — 'the Peoples table has one row per (Name, PersonType)' (PeopleRepository.cs:47) — which is exactly what both master's `PeopleId` join and Ferrofin's SQL compute. Negative controls run 2026-08-29: the Ferrofin clause fails on a reversed order and on 12 arbitrary movies, the Jellyfin clause fails on 12 movies outside its universe. NOT REACHED BY ANY PARITY LAYER, and unit-covered instead: the phase-2 filters — the lab has zero played movies and one all-libraries user, so `IsPlayed = false` and ConfigureUserAccess are exercised only by `a_played_movie_is_not_suggested_to_the_user_who_played_it`, `a_library_the_user_cannot_access_contributes_nothing`, `a_blocked_media_folder_hides_its_rows` and `the_access_filter_does_not_starve_the_page` in similar_items_manager.rs. Three real Ferrofin bugs this row used to hide were fixed in the same pass: an unknown seed returned 200-empty instead of 404, a nil seed returned 400 instead of the C# root-folder fallback, and the no-limit default was 10 instead of C#'s `limit ?? 50` (SimilarItemsManager.cs:93).
- ✅ `GET /Years`
  - classification: jellyfin-bug (ONE FIELD ONLY — TotalRecordCount; everything else on this row is diffed strictly by the reads.py `GET /Years` probe). Jellyfin's `/Years` reports a paging total that has nothing to do with the page: `YearsController.GetYears` finishes with `new QueryResult(startIndex, totalCount == -1 ? ibnItemsArray.Count : totalCount, dtos)`, where `totalCount` is the out-param of `folder.GetRecursiveChildren(user, query, out totalCount)` (v10.11.8 Folder.cs:1450-1458) — the count of the underlying MEDIA items. `RequestHelpers.GetUserId` always resolves a non-empty user, so that branch always runs and the `ibnItemsArray.Count` fallback is dead code. Measured on the lab pair (twice): the 3-year fixture returns J TotalRecordCount=559 with 3 Items, and the value tracks the filter (includeItemTypes=Series → 8, mediaTypes=Audio → 9, parentId=Movies → 500). Ferrofin reports the distinct-year count (3), captured BEFORE Skip/Take, which is what the field means and what pages the list; per the repo's 'do not port Jellyfin bugs' rule Ferrofin stays correct. Gated per-server instead of cross-server: the `items_root_ancestors_and_years_over_real_http` integration test asserts Ferrofin's total equals the distinct-year count and does not move with limit/startIndex. The PREVIOUS classification on this row ('expected-extension: Ferrofin does not materialize Year items (documented out-of-scope)') was measurably false — Ferrofin has materialized Year items since `FerrofinLibraryManager::get_named_items` gained `create_missing_years`, with ids byte-identical to Jellyfin's — and it masked five real unported behaviours behind the two routes, all fixed in batch F1: the ignored sortBy/sortOrder/includeItemTypes/excludeItemTypes/mediaTypes/recursive parameters, a total computed AFTER paging, all-zero item counts, a guid UserData.Key, and a per-item DisplayPreferencesId. NOT compared: the item ORDER on the ONE no-sortBy leg (the set and every DTO field ARE compared there; only the sequence is normalised, and the reads.py row declares it in `caveats`). The earlier wording blamed `GetOrderBy` returning `Array.Empty` and `LibraryManager.Sort` being a no-op, which is only half of it and reads as if the two servers agreed: the no-op LEAVES an order in place, it does not produce a sorted one. The order it leaves is `GetAllItems`'s — `items.Select(i => i.ProductionYear ?? 0).Where(i => i > 0).Distinct()` (v10.11.8 YearsController.cs:220-227) — and LINQ `Distinct()` preserves FIRST-OCCURRENCE order, so Jellyfin's resting order is the order years first appear in `Folder.GetRecursiveChildren`, its in-memory BaseItem tree walk (measured stable at 2020,2022,2021 over three repeats). Ferrofin has no such tree by design (CLAUDE.md, 'There is no domain-object hierarchy'), so it sorts ascending and that is a deliberate divergence, not parity. The default call shape IS now issued — batch F1's nine legs all pinned `sortBy=SortName`, i.e. the one axis where the two agree — and every other leg still compares order strictly, including under paging. | jellyfin-bug (nil-guid parameters), measured 2026-08-30 in the S1 review pass while verifying the empty-guid fix — recorded rather than left unmentioned. Jellyfin THROWS on an all-zero guid passed to a parameter that is not `userId`: `parentId=00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000` is 400 with the middleware's generic body `Error processing request.` (an unhandled ArgumentException from GetItemById/GetUserById — "Guid can't be empty" — not a designed 400), where Ferrofin treats the nil as absent and answers 200. `userId` is exempt on both sides because RequestHelpers.GetUserId maps a nil to the caller before anything looks it up. Measured on the lane-3 pair, admin token, all-zero value: GET /Items?parentId= (J400 H200), /Items?userId=&parentId= (J400 H200), /Items/Filters (J400 H200), /Items/Filters2 (J400 H200), /Years (J400 H200), /Shows/NextUp?seriesId=&parentId= (J400 H200), /Shows/{seriesId}/Episodes?seasonId=&adjacentTo= (J400 H404), /Trailers?parentId= (J500 H200 — an outright unhandled exception). Ferrofin is kept correct per the standing policy (do not port Jellyfin bugs); a 500 is never the right answer and neither is a leaked ArgumentException. NOT deferred and NOT accepted by an agent: this is a measured divergence awaiting the owner's call. UN-DEFER PATH if the owner wants byte parity on the refusal: reject a nil guid with 400 on the non-userId item-lookup parameters (parentId, seriesId, seasonId, adjacentTo, startItemId, appearsInItemId) in handlers::query_parse, leave `userId` alone, and add the eight probes above to the read layer as a status-class row.

--- ALSO ON THIS ROW (merged from worktree-dv-lane2) ---

instance/methodology — Ferrofin is the more-correct side. SUPERSEDES the earlier "expected-extension: Ferrofin does not materialize Year items (documented out-of-scope)" record, which is FALSE on the current tree (`YearStore` materializes a Year item per distinct ProductionYear and /Years returns them) and which used wording this project does not accept. The divergence that actually remains is Jellyfin's TotalRecordCount, not the items: `YearsController.GetYears` takes `totalCount` from `folder.GetRecursiveChildren(user, query, out totalCount)` (YearsController.cs:122) — the number of ITEMS scanned — and returns it beside the year DTOs (YearsController.cs:156-158). Measured on the lane-2 pair: J TotalRecordCount=559 with 3 Items; Ferrofin returns 3 with the same 3 Items (2020/2021/2022, identical bodies). Kept correct per the project's don't-port-Jellyfin-bugs rule.
  - NOT compared: TotalRecordCount — dropped from every leg. Jellyfin reports the recursive MEDIA-child count out-param of `folder.GetRecursiveChildren(user, query, out totalCount)` (v10.11.8 Folder.cs:1450-1458): 559 next to 3 Items on this fixture, tracking the filter (Series -> 8, Audio -> 9, parentId=Movies -> 500). Ferrofin reports the distinct-year count, which is what pages the list; recorded as a jellyfin-bug and gated per-server by `items_root_ancestors_and_years_over_real_http`.
  - NOT compared: Item ORDER on the no-sortBy leg only — the SET and every DTO field are compared, the sequence is sorted on both sides first. `GetAllItems` is `items.Select(ProductionYear).Where(> 0).Distinct()` (v10.11.8 YearsController.cs:220-227) and LINQ `Distinct()` preserves FIRST-OCCURRENCE order, so Jellyfin's resting order is the order years appear in its in-memory `Folder.GetRecursiveChildren` walk (measured 2020,2022,2021); Ferrofin sorts ascending. Reproducing it needs the BaseItem tree Ferrofin deliberately does not have. Every OTHER leg pins `sortBy=SortName`, where the two agree exactly, including under paging.
- ✅ `GET /Years/{year}`
  - classification: verified 2026-08-29 (batch B1, reviewer round 2). SUPERSEDES "expected-extension: Ferrofin does not materialize Year items (documented out-of-scope)" — false on the current tree, and not an answer this project accepts. Year items ARE materialized (`YearStore`) and the route 200s. A SECOND defect was found and fixed in this pass, and it is recorded rather than silently closed: `ItemCountService.cs:170-181` counts a Year by `e.ProductionYear == year` and never touches ItemValues, while Ferrofin routed Year through the clean-value aggregate — so every year reported ChildCount 0. Measured before: /Years/2020 H 0 vs J 500. After the port: /Years/2020 500/500, /Years/2021 8 series, /Years/2022 12, /Years/2001 0, diffs=0 on all four. Now probed by reads.py as its own row (`GET /Years/{year}`), which it never was.

| `GET /Albums/{itemId}/InstantMix` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ∅ |  |
| `GET /Albums/{itemId}/Similar` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ∅ | flat-provider methodology (SET-GATED, order not gateable). Seeds of this kind take the C# FLAT provider: one `InternalItemsQuery { Genres = item.Genres, Tags = item.Tags, IncludeItemTypes = [<kind>], EnableGroupByMetadataKey = false, EnableTotalRecordCount = true, OrderBy = [(ItemSortBy.Random, SortOrder.Ascending)] }` — identical in v10.11.8 (built inline at LibraryController.cs:791-801) and in master (SeriesSimilarItemsProvider.cs:49, MusicAlbumSimilarItemsProvider.cs:50, MusicArtistSimilarItemsProvider.cs:50, AudioSimilarItemsProvider.cs:50, LiveTvProgramSimilarItemsProvider.cs:89). Ferrofin runs the same query and, as of this pass, the same `Random` order. IT BRIEFLY DID NOT: batch A2 first shipped `SortName` here, which is not cosmetic — at limit < |candidates| a fixed order returns a DIFFERENT ITEM, measured on the loop pair as `/Items/{audio}/Similar?limit=1` answering 'Artist 01 Album 01 Track 02' on five consecutive Ferrofin calls while Jellyfin alternated Track 02 / Track 03. That divergence is now closed in the server (`similar_items_manager.rs::flat_similar`, unit test `the_flat_provider_orders_random_not_by_name`), not papered over. WHAT THIS LEDGER ROW DOES AND DOES NOT GATE: `Random` on both sides means the ORDER can never be diffed by any probe, and neither can a page narrower than the candidate pool — parity_diff aligns arrays by Path>Name>Id, so a reordered Items array returns 0 diffs and always will. The reads.py leg therefore pins `limit=1000`, far above the fixture's pool, where the answer is the whole candidate set and so fully determined; what is gated there is the SET, TotalRecordCount and every per-item DTO field. Anything order-sensitive is covered by unit tests instead, never by this row. GREEN BUT VACUOUS ON THE CURRENT FIXTURE — read this row as 'no divergence detected', not as 'verified'. `suite/perf/gen-fixtures.sh` gave each of the 3 albums a UNIQUE music genre, so the candidate universe is empty by construction on BOTH servers and the leg diffs `{"Items": [], "TotalRecordCount": 0}` against itself. The fixture is changed (two of the three artists now share a genre) but that only takes effect at the campaign's final wipe-and-provision; until that sweep runs, the album matching itself is covered by the `an_album_seed_matches_albums_sharing_a_genre_only` unit test (two albums sharing a genre, a same-genre track that must NOT come back because IncludeItemTypes is [MusicAlbum] alone, and the ExcludeArtistIds leg with a positive control). A real Ferrofin bug found behind this row IS fixed and verified live: album Genres/Studios/PremiereDate are inherited from the album's Audio children (C# AlbumMetadataService + MetadataService.UpdateGenres/UpdateStudios/UpdatePremiereDate); Ferrofin aggregated only album-artist and year, so every album had `Genres: []` and was unreachable by the Genres= query this endpoint issues. After the fix, F album Genres/PremiereDate/ProductionYear match J exactly (Jazz/Rock/Ambient, 2022-01-01T00:00:00.0000000Z, 2022). |
| `GET /Artists` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ⚠️ | jellyfin-bug (RunTimeTicks / CumulativeRunTimeTicks ONLY; every other leg of this row now diffs clean by measurement). `MusicArtistResolver` is PORTED (library_scan.rs::plan_music_node + is_music_artist, a port of v10.11.8 Emby.Server.Implementations/Library/Resolvers/Audio/MusicArtistResolver.cs:54-125), so an artist directory now resolves to a real MusicArtist row parented into the music library, on Jellyfin's OWN path-derived id (measured byte-identical on the fixture: Artist 01 = 0f39932400340734bea41ffc13aaddc4, Artist 02 = 2b3868bfaa266cc1d3cc4c3dd5d40800, Artist 03 = 697d2b7b6ec56d3cf7e87c2de748b0cd), with its albums re-parented onto it. That closed Path, CanDelete, RecursiveItemCount and the empty user-scoped MusicArtist universe.

WHAT REMAINS, and why it is Jellyfin's bug and not Ferrofin's: with the artist resolved as a folder, `MetadataService.UpdateCumulativeRunTimeTicks` (MediaBrowser.Providers/Manager/MetadataService.cs:451) sums the RunTimeTicks of the non-folder children returned by `GetChildrenForMetadataUpdates`. For a MusicArtist that method is OVERRIDDEN — `ArtistMetadataService.GetChildrenForMetadataUpdates` (MediaBrowser.Providers/Music/ArtistMetadataService.cs:44-56) returns `item.IsAccessedByName ? item.GetTaggedItems(Recursive, IsFolder=false) : item.GetRecursiveChildren(i => i is IHasArtist && !i.IsFolder)`, NOT the base class's plain `folder.GetRecursiveChildren()` at MetadataService.cs:397-401. A folder-resolved artist takes the second arm, and `Audio : IHasArtist`, so the set is exactly the three tracks — the override strengthens the conclusion rather than changing it, and it is cited here because re-deriving the number from the base method lands on the wrong call. `MusicArtist.SupportsCumulativeRunTimeTicks => true` (MusicArtist.cs:38). 3 tracks x 20000000 = 60000000, which is what Ferrofin stores. The 10.11.8 oracle stores 0, and its OWN DateLastRefreshed timestamps show why — a first-scan ordering race, read out of this lab's /config/data/jellyfin.db:
  MusicArtist 'Artist 01'   RunTimeTicks 0         DateLastRefreshed 2026-08-30 00:29:30.8359533
  Audio       'Track 01'    RunTimeTicks 20000000                    2026-08-30 00:29:30.8815808
  Audio       'Track 02'    RunTimeTicks 20000000                    2026-08-30 00:29:30.8973703
  Audio       'Track 03'    RunTimeTicks 20000000                    2026-08-30 00:29:30.9096119
  MusicAlbum  'Album 01'    RunTimeTicks 60000000                    2026-08-30 00:29:30.9187306
The artist refreshed 46 ms BEFORE its first track was probed, so it summed a set of NULLs. The album — identical code, `SupportsCumulativeRunTimeTicks` also true, refreshed after all three — got the right number. A rescan does not repair it either: `UpdateMetadataFromChildren` is gated on `isFullRefresh || currentUpdateType > None`. Matching Jellyfin here would mean reproducing a first-scan race on purpose.

Kept per 'don't port Jellyfin's bugs' (docs/ + the standing memory). OWNER CALL PENDING: ratify keeping 60000000, or direct that Ferrofin reproduce the race. The ledger renders this row under 'Awaiting owner ratification', NOT under 'accepted' — that bucket is a claim about a decision somebody made, and this one has not been made. Until ratified the row stays deep_verified=false against this oracle, honestly — the divergence is REAL and visible, not hidden. parity_diff.VOLATILE was NOT widened for RunTimeTicks and must not be.

ROW HISTORY, kept rather than dropped: before the port this row's bodies DIFFED CLEAN, and the old classification's warning ('DO NOT BANK THIS ROW') was right — the agreement was two different mechanisms landing on the same number. Ferrofin wrote RunTimeTicks 0 because its artist was a parentless by-name row with no recursive children and the faithful UpdateCumulativeRunTimeTicks summed nothing; Jellyfin wrote 0 because of the race above. The green was a coincidence and it is now a visible mismatch for the right reason. DE-DUPLICATION CHECK for this row specifically: it must return exactly 3 artists. A previous attempt at this port was rolled back because `push_by_name_join` joins on CleanName and the scanned row plus the by-name row each produced an entry, listing every artist twice. Measured 3, twice, after the fix. |

WHAT REMAINS, and why it is Jellyfin's bug and not Ferrofin's: with the artist resolved as a folder, `MetadataService.UpdateCumulativeRunTimeTicks` (MediaBrowser.Providers/Manager/MetadataService.cs:451) sums the RunTimeTicks of the non-folder children returned by `GetChildrenForMetadataUpdates`. For a MusicArtist that method is OVERRIDDEN — `ArtistMetadataService.GetChildrenForMetadataUpdates` (MediaBrowser.Providers/Music/ArtistMetadataService.cs:44-56) returns `item.IsAccessedByName ? item.GetTaggedItems(Recursive, IsFolder=false) : item.GetRecursiveChildren(i => i is IHasArtist && !i.IsFolder)`, NOT the base class's plain `folder.GetRecursiveChildren()` at MetadataService.cs:397-401. A folder-resolved artist takes the second arm, and `Audio : IHasArtist`, so the set is exactly the three tracks — the override strengthens the conclusion rather than changing it, and it is cited here because re-deriving the number from the base method lands on the wrong call. `MusicArtist.SupportsCumulativeRunTimeTicks => true` (MusicArtist.cs:38). 3 tracks x 20000000 = 60000000, which is what Ferrofin stores. The 10.11.8 oracle stores 0, and its OWN DateLastRefreshed timestamps show why — a first-scan ordering race, read out of this lab's /config/data/jellyfin.db:
  MusicArtist 'Artist 01'   RunTimeTicks 0         DateLastRefreshed 2026-08-30 00:29:30.8359533
  Audio       'Track 01'    RunTimeTicks 20000000                    2026-08-30 00:29:30.8815808
  Audio       'Track 02'    RunTimeTicks 20000000                    2026-08-30 00:29:30.8973703
  Audio       'Track 03'    RunTimeTicks 20000000                    2026-08-30 00:29:30.9096119
  MusicAlbum  'Album 01'    RunTimeTicks 60000000                    2026-08-30 00:29:30.9187306
The artist refreshed 46 ms BEFORE its first track was probed, so it summed a set of NULLs. The album — identical code, `SupportsCumulativeRunTimeTicks` also true, refreshed after all three — got the right number. A rescan does not repair it either: `UpdateMetadataFromChildren` is gated on `isFullRefresh || currentUpdateType > None`. Matching Jellyfin here would mean reproducing a first-scan race on purpose.

Kept per 'don't port Jellyfin's bugs' (docs/ + the standing memory). OWNER CALL PENDING: ratify keeping 60000000, or direct that Ferrofin reproduce the race. The ledger renders this row under 'Awaiting owner ratification', NOT under 'accepted' — that bucket is a claim about a decision somebody made, and this one has not been made. Until ratified the row stays deep_verified=false against this oracle, honestly — the divergence is REAL and visible, not hidden. parity_diff.VOLATILE was NOT widened for RunTimeTicks and must not be.

WHAT THIS ROW CLOSED: Ferrofin returned 0 rows where Jellyfin returned 2. The scorer half was already right (`MusicArtistSimilarItemsProvider` is a flat InternalItemsQuery { Genres, Tags, IncludeItemTypes=[MusicArtist] } and an EMPTY Genres/Tags pair is not a predicate, so an untagged artist matches every other artist — covered by the `an_untagged_artist_seed_still_matches_every_other_artist` unit test). The blocker was the item UNIVERSE: no MusicArtist row survived `scope_to_user_libraries` (a faithful `AddUserToQuery`, TopParentId IS NOT NULL). That filter was NOT relaxed; the scanner was fixed. Both peers now return on both servers.

ORDERING — this row's own methodology guard rail, and it is LIVE now that the candidate pool is no longer empty: this endpoint's provider orders `Random` on BOTH servers (MusicArtistSimilarItemsProvider.cs:50 / v10.11.8 LibraryController.cs:801) and Ferrofin emits `Random` too. parity_diff aligns arrays by key, so the ORDER is not gateable by any probe — which means the reads leg MUST stay pinned at a limit above the candidate pool, or the two servers will draw different SUBSETS and the row will diff on membership for a reason that is not a bug. reads.py:636 pins limit=1000 (rationale at reads.py:608-623). Do not lower it, and do not 'fix' a future red on this row by narrowing the compare. |

WHAT THIS ROW CLOSED, with the before-values: Path (J '/media/synth/music/Artist 01' vs H '/config/metadata/artists/Artist 01' — H's old value was the CORRECT path for the by-name row it actually had), CanDelete (J true, H false — kinds::can_delete is already a faithful !IsAccessedByName), and a missing RecursiveItemCount (J 3). All three measured equal now.

TWO DIVERGENCES THIS ROW STILL CARRIES that the harness cannot see, recorded here so they are not mistaken for settled. Both are masked by parity_diff.VOLATILE, which was NOT widened for either — they were already in it. (1) ParentId: Jellyfin materializes a physical `Folder` row per media path and parents the artist to it (F 0 such rows vs J 9); Ferrofin parents to the CollectionFolder. The id derivation already reproduces exactly — md5_le('MediaBrowser.Controller.Entities.Folder' + '/media/synth/music') = f87561f1dd83cf1f019883e008f5f307, which is the id Jellyfin stores — so closing it is mechanical, but it changes the ancestor chain of every scanned item and needs its own measured change plus the perf gate. (2) UserData.Key: C# UserDataManager.cs:239-245 uses item.GetUserDataKeys()[0], so Jellyfin emits 'Artist-Artist 01' where Ferrofin emits the raw guid '0f399324-0034-0734-bea4-1ffc13aaddc4'. kinds.rs and user_data_keys.rs already COMPUTE the right keys; the gap is plumbing in user_data_manager.rs::empty_row / dto_service.rs::empty_user_data_dto, and it is server-wide, not artist-specific. |

| `GET /Audio/{itemId}/universal` | registered | REAL | ✅ | · | ✅ | security-fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1, cross-user authz): C# RequestHelpers.GetUserId (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Helpers/RequestHelpers.cs:67-85) has two halves: an absent/all-zero userId falls back to the caller, and a userId that is NOT the caller is honoured only for an ADMINISTRATOR, else SecurityException -> 403. Ferrofin ported only the first half here. Found by the S1 sweep. The handler took no userId at all; it is the audio playback path (v10.11.8 UniversalAudioController.cs:120). Measured: 200 on Ferrofin against 403 on Jellyfin. Re-measured independently in the S1 review pass with a fresh non-admin account ('s1fixprobe', created and deleted on both servers): a non-administrator naming the administrator's id is 403 on BOTH servers, while the administrator naming another user and a non-administrator naming their own id still succeed on both. Recorded in the S1 review pass: the batch claimed these five as flipped but left no row for them, so the fix existed only in the code and the claim. | empty-guid binding fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1 review pass, reviewer finding F2): ASP.NET binds an EMPTY or whitespace-only value for a `Guid?` to null (SimpleTypeModelBinder), so upstream answers `?userId=` and `?userId=%20` exactly as it answers a request with no userId at all; serde's `Option<Uuid>` tried to parse "" and made both a 400. S1 spread that pre-existing defect to five more routes when it gave previously parameter-less handlers a `Query<T>`. Re-measured on the lane-3 pair BEFORE the fix: `?userId=` and `?userId=%20` were 400 on Ferrofin against 200 on Jellyfin (404/404 where the probe id does not exist). FIXED AS A CLASS, not as an instance: every `Option<Uuid>` field of a `Query<...>` struct in ferrofin-api now binds through `handlers::query_parse::empty_as_none_uuid`. AFTER, the four spellings (absent / all-zero / empty / whitespace) agree on both servers across /Devices, /Channels, /Channels/Items/Latest, /LiveTv/Recordings/Folders, /LiveTv/Recordings/{recordingId}, /Audio/{itemId}/universal AND the four routes that carried the defect before S1 ever ran (/Items, /UserViews, /Persons, /Years). Request BODIES keep serde's strict parse on purpose: System.Text.Json rejects `"UserId": ""` upstream too. |

| `GET /Channels/{channelId}/Features` | registered | REAL | ✅ | · | · | status-class-only (batch F6, category corrected in the reviewer-fix pass — this row carries NO deep_verified verdict in either direction, and the category now says so; it was filed `verified`, whose name reads as the verdict the row does not have): the previous `requires-channel-plugin` entry — "no tuner journey can reach these; they need a channel plugin on both servers" — described a missing probe as if it were a settled divergence, and a live Ferrofin bug sat behind it. It is TRUE that no `IChannel` implementation exists in either tree (`git grep -l IChannel -- '*.cs'` returns only IChannel.cs, IChannelManager.cs and their consumers on BOTH v10.11.8 and master), so no Channel item can exist without a .NET plugin — but that makes the answer channelId-INDEPENDENT on both servers and therefore fully diffable, which is exactly the argument sweep.py already applies to `groupId`. sweep.py now seeds `_plugin_channel` (a literal GUID, PATH_SCOPED to /Channels/ so it can never leak into the /LiveTv/ channel id space) and the rows are measured instead of skipped. On that one reachable arm Ferrofin WAS wrong and is now fixed: it answered 200 with a fabricated `ChannelFeatures` echoing the requested UUID (and an empty QueryResult for Items), where upstream resolves `GetChannel(id)` to null and hands it to `GetChannelProvider`, whose first statement is `ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(channel)` (v10.11.8 ChannelManager.cs:1177, master :1176 — byte-identical) and whose `ArgumentException` `ExceptionMiddleware` maps to 400. Both trees agree, so this was outcome 3 (Ferrofin matched NEITHER) and the fix is a faithful port, not a move toward 10.11.8. Measured on the F6 pair 2026-08-30: before, F 200 / J 400; after the rebuild, F 400 / J 400 on both routes and on two different GUIDs, with /Channels, /Channels/Features and /Channels/Items/Latest unchanged at 200-empty on both. Pinned by crates/ferrofin-api/tests/channels.rs. WHAT THE ROW ACTUALLY CARRIES, stated so no reader takes this text for a verdict: sweep.py writes `deep_verified`/`verification_method` ONLY inside its `hs == 200 and js == 200` branch, so a pair that agrees on a NON-2xx gets `{status_conformant: true, schema_valid: null, note: "H=400 J=400"}` and no verdict field at all. That is the honest state and not a gap to close: there is no 200 body on either server, ever, so there is nothing for a body diff to walk. CONTRACT NOTE, owner-visible: 400 is NOT among the responses the vendored 10.11.8 spec declares for either op — `.paths["/Channels/{channelId}/Features"].get.responses` and the `/Items` one are both [200,401,403,503]. The undeclared status is the SPEC's divergence, not Ferrofin's and not Jellyfin's: the C# reaches 400 by letting `ArgumentNullException` escape the action into `ExceptionMiddleware`, a path the generated OpenAPI never sees. Ferrofin now emits the same undeclared status as the oracle, so the wire is symmetric, and the contract-superset gate is untouched (it gates the ROUTE TABLE, not per-op response codes, and 400 is not 404). Recorded here because "the wire carries a status the vendored contract does not declare" is the owner's call to keep or to raise upstream, not an agent's. REAL vs BOGUS channelId, re-measured independently in the reviewer-fix pass 2026-08-30 on both servers and both routes: a literal GUID, the all-zero GUID, and a REAL Live TV channel id (d53240a1…, which both servers mint identically because `GetInternalChannelId` hashes the same tuner lineup) all answer 400 on Ferrofin AND on Jellyfin — including the id-space-crossing case, which is the one that would have caught a Live TV channel leaking into this controller. The collection controls are unchanged and identical on both: /Channels and /Channels/Items/Latest 200 `{"Items":[],"TotalRecordCount":0,"StartIndex":0}`, /Channels/Features 200 `[]`. So there is no "real channelId" on either server to answer differently, and the answer is genuinely channelId-independent. WHAT WOULD VERIFY THE POSITIVE ARM: an `IChannel` implementation registered on BOTH servers — on Jellyfin that is a .NET plugin, the owner-accepted native-plugin scope exclusion. Unlike the tuner (faked over HTTP by suite/perf/livetv-source.py, which is why Live TV is verified) this cannot be faked: IChannel is an in-process .NET interface with no wire surface to stand up, so there is nothing to port and nothing to fake. If channel providers are ever introduced on the Ferrofin side, the arm becomes reachable on ONE server only, which is still not a differential — the verification would then be against the C# by transliterated unit test, the way the Startup guard is pinned. |

Measured on the F6 pair 2026-08-30: before, F 200 / J 400; after the rebuild, F 400 / J 400 on both routes and on two different GUIDs, with /Channels, /Channels/Features and /Channels/Items/Latest unchanged at 200-empty on both. Pinned by crates/ferrofin-api/tests/channels.rs. WHAT THE ROW ACTUALLY CARRIES, stated so no reader takes this text for a verdict: sweep.py writes `deep_verified`/`verification_method` ONLY inside its `hs == 200 and js == 200` branch, so a pair that agrees on a NON-2xx gets `{status_conformant: true, schema_valid: null, note: "H=400 J=400"}` and no verdict field at all. That is the honest state and not a gap to close: there is no 200 body on either server, ever, so there is nothing for a body diff to walk. CONTRACT NOTE, owner-visible: 400 is NOT among the responses the vendored 10.11.8 spec declares for either op — `.paths["/Channels/{channelId}/Features"].get.responses` and the `/Items` one are both [200,401,403,503]. The undeclared status is the SPEC's divergence, not Ferrofin's and not Jellyfin's: the C# reaches 400 by letting `ArgumentNullException` escape the action into `ExceptionMiddleware`, a path the generated OpenAPI never sees. Ferrofin now emits the same undeclared status as the oracle, so the wire is symmetric, and the contract-superset gate is untouched (it gates the ROUTE TABLE, not per-op response codes, and 400 is not 404). Recorded here because "the wire carries a status the vendored contract does not declare" is the owner's call to keep or to raise upstream, not an agent's. REAL vs BOGUS channelId, re-measured independently in the reviewer-fix pass 2026-08-30 on both servers and both routes: a literal GUID, the all-zero GUID, and a REAL Live TV channel id (d53240a1…, which both servers mint identically because `GetInternalChannelId` hashes the same tuner lineup) all answer 400 on Ferrofin AND on Jellyfin — including the id-space-crossing case, which is the one that would have caught a Live TV channel leaking into this controller. The collection controls are unchanged and identical on both: /Channels and /Channels/Items/Latest 200 `{"Items":[],"TotalRecordCount":0,"StartIndex":0}`, /Channels/Features 200 `[]`. So there is no "real channelId" on either server to answer differently, and the answer is genuinely channelId-independent. WHAT WOULD VERIFY THE POSITIVE ARM: an `IChannel` implementation registered on BOTH servers — on Jellyfin that is a .NET plugin, the owner-accepted native-plugin scope exclusion. Unlike the tuner (faked over HTTP by suite/perf/livetv-source.py, which is why Live TV is verified) this cannot be faked: IChannel is an in-process .NET interface with no wire surface to stand up, so there is nothing to port and nothing to fake. If channel providers are ever introduced on the Ferrofin side, the arm becomes reachable on ONE server only, which is still not a differential — the verification would then be against the C# by transliterated unit test, the way the Startup guard is pinned.

requires-channel-plugin: the ChannelsController serves IChannel plugin channels, not Live TV — no tuner journey can reach these; they need a channel plugin on both servers. | security-fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1, cross-user authz): C# RequestHelpers.GetUserId (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Helpers/RequestHelpers.cs:67-85) has two halves: an absent/all-zero userId falls back to the caller, and a userId that is NOT the caller is honoured only for an ADMINISTRATOR, else SecurityException -> 403. Ferrofin ported only the first half here. Measured twice on the lane-3 pair (Ferrofin 18396 / Jellyfin 18397) with a second, non-admin account 's1probe' created on both: The route also took ?userId= without the gate; the sibling routes were measured BEFORE at 200 on Ferrofin against 403 on Jellyfin for a non-admin naming the admin's id, and this one is fixed with them. After the fix both servers return 403 for the same request, while an administrator naming another user's id and a non-administrator naming their own id still succeed on both (measured, twice). Regression tests in crates/ferrofin-api/tests/ cover all three cases per route. Fifth instance in this campaign of a C# authorization check Ferrofin served without one (A1 startup, B3 x29 Live TV routes, D2 get_lineups, F1 items); the campaign owes a dedicated audit of every [Authorize(Policy=...)] and RequestHelpers.GetUserId call site in the 10.11.8 controllers against Ferrofin's route guards. | empty-guid binding fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1 review pass, reviewer finding F2): ASP.NET binds an EMPTY or whitespace-only value for a `Guid?` to null (SimpleTypeModelBinder), so upstream answers `?userId=` and `?userId=%20` exactly as it answers a request with no userId at all; serde's `Option<Uuid>` tried to parse "" and made both a 400. S1 spread that pre-existing defect to five more routes when it gave previously parameter-less handlers a `Query<T>`. Re-measured on the lane-3 pair BEFORE the fix: `?userId=` and `?userId=%20` were 400 on Ferrofin against 200 on Jellyfin (404/404 where the probe id does not exist). FIXED AS A CLASS, not as an instance: every `Option<Uuid>` field of a `Query<...>` struct in ferrofin-api now binds through `handlers::query_parse::empty_as_none_uuid`. AFTER, the four spellings (absent / all-zero / empty / whitespace) agree on both servers across /Devices, /Channels, /Channels/Items/Latest, /LiveTv/Recordings/Folders, /LiveTv/Recordings/{recordingId}, /Audio/{itemId}/universal AND the four routes that carried the defect before S1 ever ran (/Items, /UserViews, /Persons, /Years). Request BODIES keep serde's strict parse on purpose: System.Text.Json rejects `"UserId": ""` upstream too. |

| `GET /Genres` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ⚠️ | open-work (NOT accepted) — SUPERSEDES the 2026-08-11 'verified: QueryResult<BaseItemDto>, status+schema conformant' seed (a shape check that never diffed the listing) and the 2026-08-31 revision of it. Measured twice on 2026-08-31 after the by-name ports: H lists [Action, Ambient, Comedy, Drama, Jazz, Rock, SciFi, Thriller], J lists [Action, Comedy, Drama, Jazz, News, SciFi, Thriller]. Two separate axes, one upstream and one a REAL Ferrofin gap that is named here rather than hidden.

AXIS 1 (H's 'extra' Ambient and Rock) — the same mechanism as GET /MusicGenres axis 1, and NOT a missing port: upstream's `GroupBy(PresentationUniqueKey)` collapse IS now implemented in Ferrofin (`item_repository::push_representative_rank`, see that row). Both servers hold Genre rows named Ambient/Jazz/Rock; on Jellyfin all three were lazily created by `CreateItemByName` at 2026-08-31 01:16:57 with a NULL PresentationUniqueKey and a NULL DateLastRefreshed (read out of the container's own DB), so they form one NULL group and surface as the single representative Jazz. Ferrofin's carry Genre-Ambient/-Jazz/-Rock — the value `MetadataService.BeforeSaveInternal` would give them on their first refresh — so all three list. On a freshly provisioned pair neither server holds those rows at all, so this axis should not appear in the end-of-campaign sweep.

AXIS 2 (FERROFIN GAP, open work, NOT accepted) — J lists a genre 'News' that H does not. Root cause measured, not guessed: J's `news` value is carried by 676 `LiveTvProgram` rows plus 15 Movie rows (its EPG), while Ferrofin has NO ItemValues row for `news` at all — because Ferrofin stores EPG programmes in its own `FerrofinLiveTvPrograms` table (ferrofin-livetv/src/manager.rs:542) and never materializes them as `BaseItems`, so they contribute to no by-name aggregate. Upstream persists a programme through `BaseItemRepository.SaveItems`, whose second half writes `ItemValues`/`ItemValuesMap` for it, which is why every EPG genre shows on Jellyfin's /Genres. UN-DEFER PATH: make the guide refresh write a `LiveTvProgram` BaseItems row per programme (or, narrower, project the guide's genres into `ItemValues` alongside the FerrofinLiveTvPrograms row) and re-measure this row plus /Items/Filters2. Still NOT done and not claimed as done — it is a Live TV storage-model change, outside the four by-name rows batch F2 owns. |
| `GET /Genres/{genreName}` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | verified 2026-08-31 (batch F2, revised after review): 4/4 legs clean, 108 fields compared, measured twice. SUPERSEDES both the batch-B1 record (true about the lazy create, blind to everything else — it probed ONE name) and the 2026-08-31 'accepted-divergence' record, whose accepted divergence has been REMOVED from the code rather than argued for.

`GenresController.GetGenre` has THREE behaviours on both trees (v10.11.8 :159-208; master's only file delta is dropping `.AddClientFields(User)` and adding `[Tags("Genre")]`):
  (a) a plain name -> `_libraryManager.GetGenre(name)` = `CreateItemByName<Genre>` (ported by B1);
  (b) a name containing `BaseItem.SlugChar` ('-', BaseItem.cs:98 on v10.11.8, :100 on master) -> `GetItemFromSlugName<T>`: EXACTLY three plain `GetItemList` lookups substituting '-'->'&', then '/', then '?', which create NOTHING;
  (c) no match -> `item ??= new Genre()`, a default-constructed row — the route cannot 404.
Ferrofin had only (a), so a hyphenated name MINTED a bogus genre: measured before the fix, `GET /Genres/Diag-Slug-F2x3` returned H {Name, Path /config/metadata/Genre/Diag-Slug-F2x3, SortName, LocationType FileSystem} against J's all-zero DTO. Ported as `by_name::resolve_by_name_or_slug` + the non-creating `LibraryManager::find_named_item` (core: `resolve_named_rows`, the resolve half of `get_named_items` with the CreateItemByName write left off) + `LibraryManager::empty_by_name_item`. Verified live that it creates nothing: after five reads.py runs across two builds, the only `Diag`-named genre in Ferrofin's BaseItems is the `Diag-Slug-F2x3` row minted by the OLD build, and no `Diag-No-Such-F2` row exists on either side.

Fixing (c) exposed a THIRD, wider defect this row is the only one that could see: `DtoService` derived `LocationType` from `IsVirtualItem`, where C# `BaseItem.LocationType` (v10.11.8 BaseItem.cs:319-336, master :327-344, identical) derives it from the PATH — empty path -> Virtual, a path with a scheme other than `file://` -> Remote (`ManagedFileSystem.IsPathFile`, :526-535), otherwise FileSystem. Ferrofin therefore said FileSystem for every pathless row. Ported as `dto_service::location_type_of`; measured on the empty item J=Virtual H=Virtual.

RECORDED UPSTREAM QUIRK (Ferrofin now reproduces it, deliberately). Because branch (b) reads a hyphen as a slug and nothing else, a genre whose real name CONTAINS a hyphen is unreachable through this route on both trees: `/Genres/Sci-Fi` answers with the empty DTO of (c) even when a genre named exactly `Sci-Fi` exists. The first cut of this port added a fourth, non-creating lookup on the literal name to reach it, and recorded that as an accepted divergence justified by 'don't port Jellyfin's bugs' plus the campaign rule against deleting a capability Ferrofin ships. Both justifications were wrong and the fourth lookup has been DROPPED: `GetItemFromSlugName` is byte-identical on v10.11.8 and master and stops after '?', so a fourth candidate matched NEITHER tree — the campaign's own definition of the bug — and the pre-existing behaviour the 'capability' argument pointed at was the create-on-GET defect this batch removed, not a shipped feature. No probe leg could observe the difference either (the fixture has no hyphenated genre), which is exactly why it had to be settled by the C# rather than by the wire. Pinned by the unit test `by_name_browse::a_hyphenated_name_is_only_ever_read_as_a_slug`, whose fake resolves a genre literally named `Sci-Fi` so a returning fourth candidate fails it.

FIXTURE: `suite/perf/gen-fixtures.sh` seeds a genre literally named `R&B` so the '&' substitution is probed on the wire (reads.py legs `/Genres/R-B` and `/Genres/Diag-No-Such-F2`). It is ADDED to every 50th movie rather than folded into the genre rotation: widening the rotation from 5 to 6 repartitions the whole shared corpus (500/5 -> 500/6 movies per genre) at the next wipe-and-provision, which would stale every count other batches recorded — including this campaign's own '200 movies carry action' — and make suite/perf-baseline.json non-comparable for the mandatory end-of-campaign perf gate. Verified by simulation that all five original genres keep exactly 200 movies and `R&B` gains 10. Until the fixture is re-provisioned both slug legs exercise the (c) empty-item path, which is a real agreement but not the substitution. |

| `GET /Items` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ⚠️ | open-work (NOT accepted): the UserRootFolder browse took the WRONG CODE PATH entirely, and the harness could not see it. C# ItemsController.GetItems (ItemsController.cs:307, 525-529) answers a request that is not recursive, names no ids, and whose parent resolves to the UserRootFolder from `folder.GetChildren(user, true)` — not from a query — so no sort, no paging and none of the request's filters apply, and the AggregateFolder's virtual children are APPENDED (UserRootFolder.cs:96-102, `list.AddRange`), not merged into the sort. LibraryManager.GetParentItem(null, userId) resolves an ABSENT parentId to that same folder, so a bare GET /Items is in the branch too. Measured on the lab's 10.11.8 (18097): /Items?userId=…, plus parentId={root} with sortBy=SortName&sortOrder=Descending, with limit=2&startIndex=1, and with includeItemTypes=Movie ALL return the identical seven root rows in the identical order with Playlists LAST, and StartIndex echoes the request. Ferrofin answered the bare form with the WHOLE SERVER (554 rows to Jellyfin's 7), honoured sort/limit/startIndex, and sorted the playlists folder inline (its SortName 'playlists' landing before 'shows (synth)'). Ported 2026-08-30 (InternalItemsQuery.user_root_children, item_repository::user_root_children, and a leading virtual-children-last ORDER BY term in translate_query::append_order_by); all four shapes re-measured live and now agree with Jellyfin on branch, order and StartIndex. THE HARNESS BLIND SPOT WAS REAL AND IS CLOSED: parity_diff aligns arrays of OBJECTS by Path/Name/Id and never compares position, so the misordered row diffed clean; reads.py now runs this op as two legs, both under the existing ADDITIVE with_item_order projection (it adds `_ItemNameOrder`, a list of plain strings, which parity_diff compares POSITIONALLY), and the second leg is the user-root browse itself, carrying the sort/paging/filter parameters upstream ignores. REMAINING RED on this long-lived drifted lab, instance state rather than code, to be re-scored on the fresh-pair sweep: leg 1 TotalRecordCount J=507 H=500 and a UserData.LastPlayedDate/BackdropImageTags difference on Movie 0001 (a write journey's leftovers), leg 2 the different library SETS (Jellyfin carries ParityCRUD + Recordings, Ferrofin a 'Movies (synth) Renamed' rename ghost) — which is also the whole of the _ItemNameOrder mismatch: positions 0-1 agree, positions 2-5 are the drifted libraries, and Playlists is last on BOTH.

--- ALSO ON THIS ROW (merged from lane 3) ---
open-work — NOT a divergence in this handler, a LAB ARTEFACT plus one closed security gap, both recorded so neither goes quiet. (1) The movie leg of the reads.py `GET /Items` row is red because the lab's Jellyfin carries leftover `…/library/d3/…/backdropN.png` files that assets.py's upload/reorder sequence never removed, so `BackdropImageTags` is non-empty on J and empty on F for Movie 0001. The files are not in the media fixture; the residue accumulates across runs (1 file, then 2). Owner: whichever batch owns assets.py — its write rows must reap what they upload. It will not exist on the freshly-wiped pair the final sweep runs on. Deliberately NOT deleted by hand: that would be a one-sided mutation of the pair. (2) Closed in the F1 review pass, recorded because it was live until then: a non-administrator passing ANOTHER user's `userId` was answered 200 with that user's data on Ferrofin and 403 on Jellyfin. `RequestHelpers.GetUserId` throws `SecurityException("Forbidden")` unless the caller is the named user or an administrator (v10.11.8 RequestHelpers.cs:77-82), mapped to 403 by `ExceptionMiddleware:129`. Measured on the lane-3 pair with a freshly created non-admin account, before: F 200 / J 403 on `/Items?userId={admin}`, `/Users/{admin}/Items` and `/Items/Suggestions?userId={admin}`; after: 403 on both, with the all-zero userId still 200 on both. The probe user was created and deleted symmetrically on both servers. | jellyfin-bug (nil-guid parameters), measured 2026-08-30 in the S1 review pass while verifying the empty-guid fix — recorded rather than left unmentioned. Jellyfin THROWS on an all-zero guid passed to a parameter that is not `userId`: `parentId=00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000` is 400 with the middleware's generic body `Error processing request.` (an unhandled ArgumentException from GetItemById/GetUserById — "Guid can't be empty" — not a designed 400), where Ferrofin treats the nil as absent and answers 200. `userId` is exempt on both sides because RequestHelpers.GetUserId maps a nil to the caller before anything looks it up. Measured on the lane-3 pair, admin token, all-zero value: GET /Items?parentId= (J400 H200), /Items?userId=&parentId= (J400 H200), /Items/Filters (J400 H200), /Items/Filters2 (J400 H200), /Years (J400 H200), /Shows/NextUp?seriesId=&parentId= (J400 H200), /Shows/{seriesId}/Episodes?seasonId=&adjacentTo= (J400 H404), /Trailers?parentId= (J500 H200 — an outright unhandled exception). Ferrofin is kept correct per the standing policy (do not port Jellyfin bugs); a 500 is never the right answer and neither is a leaked ArgumentException. NOT deferred and NOT accepted by an agent: this is a measured divergence awaiting the owner's call. UN-DEFER PATH if the owner wants byte parity on the refusal: reject a nil guid with 400 on the non-userId item-lookup parameters (parentId, seriesId, seasonId, adjacentTo, startItemId, appearsInItemId) in handlers::query_parse, leave `userId` alone, and add the eight probes above to the read layer as a status-class row.

open-work (NOT accepted) — the LiveTvProgram half is PORTED here; what is left is named. Ferrofin never wrote a `BaseItems` row for a Live TV airing: `insert_programs` wrote only `FerrofinLiveTvPrograms` and `program_entity()` built the item in memory at read time for `/LiveTv/Programs` alone, so the recursive item universe held zero airings — measured F 0 / J 338 while `/LiveTv/Programs` answered 338 on BOTH, and `GET /Items/{programId}` was F 404 / J 200. Upstream stores an airing exactly as it stores a channel (`CreateItems(newPrograms, currentChannel, …)` / `UpdateItemsAsync`, v10.11.8 src/Jellyfin.LiveTv/Guide/GuideManager.cs:277-291) and prunes it with the `CleanDatabase(newProgramIdList, [LiveTvProgram])` one line below the channel one (:145-148). `sync_program_items` is that port, on batch G5's channel mechanism rather than beside it: the same bounded field-wise merge (`projection::program_item_update`, so a guide refresh cannot revert a metadata edit on the 24h timer) and the same global clean. Verified live on the lane pair after a `RefreshGuide`: LiveTvProgram F 338 / J 338, `GET /Items/{programId}` 200 on both for the SAME id with ChannelName/ChannelNumber, and `/Genres` picked up the guide-only `News` row it was missing. The stored row now matches the oracle column for column (ParentId=channel, TopParentId=Live TV view, IsVirtualItem=1, UnratedType=LiveTvProgram, MediaType=Unknown, Width/Height=0, ShowId, PresentationUniqueKey NULL). A REGRESSION THIS ROW SHIPPED AND THEN CLOSED, recorded so it cannot come back invisibly: porting the bare `GROUP BY "PresentationUniqueKey"` (upstream's own `dbQuery.GroupBy(e => e.PresentationUniqueKey)`, BaseItemRepository.cs:417) made a KEYLESS row an item-loss bug, because SQLite groups NULLs together. Migration 0027's backfill was scoped `"TopParentId" IS NOT NULL`, and an item-by-name row has TopParentId NULL by construction, so it missed exactly the rows that were keyless: `GET /Items?userId=…&ids=<three Person ids>` measured F 1 / J 3 on the lane pair, and a mixed six-id request returned one item. It also wrote the row's OWN ID as the value, where the five overriding kinds spell it `{Type}-{Name.RemoveDiacritics()}` (Genre.cs:37-47, `Artist` for MusicArtist at MusicArtist.cs:152) — the oracle stores `Person-Bob Parity`, `MusicGenre-Jazz`. Both halves are fixed: migration 0028 widens the scope and narrows the value to the kinds whose key really is their own id, and `ferrofin_db::presentation_key::backfill_by_name_presentation_keys` repairs the five by-name kinds in Rust (SQLite cannot express `RemoveDiacritics`) through the SAME helper the inserts call. Re-measured live after the rebuild: F 3 / J 3 on both routes, mixed six-id F 6, and the lane's stored keys read back `Person-Bob Parity` / `MusicGenre-Jazz`. The probe blind spot that let it through is named too: every leg this batch added to GET /Items is scoped to the recursive user universe, which is the one place TopParentId-less rows are already filtered out, so the guard is the unit test `item_repository::presentation_key_backfill_rescues_a_grouped_query`, not a parity leg.

STILL OPEN on this row. Census re-measured live on the recursive universe after the fix (F total 558 / J 630), every difference named: (1) Live TV RECORDINGS as library items — Jellyfin scans `%AppDataPath%/livetv/recordings` into real rows (measured now: Movie F 500 / J 531, Recording F 0 / J 2); Ferrofin keeps them in `FerrofinLiveTvRecordings` and projects none (`projection::recording_entity`'s doc comment names it). Same mechanism as the programme mirror, third half of the guide. (2) PLAYLISTS: Playlist F 0 / J 25 and ManualPlaylistsFolder F 0 / J 1 — the playlists library and its entries are outside Ferrofin's recursive universe. (3) PHYSICAL FOLDERS beneath each virtual library: Folder F 0 / J 10. Jellyfin materializes the physical folder under every library (`/media/synth/movies`, `/media/synth/music`, `/media/synth/tv`, `/config/data/collections`, `/config/data/livetv/recordings` and the five per-recording folders) as a `Folder` row inside the universe; Ferrofin materializes none. (4) CollectionFolder F 1 / J 0 — the SAME subject as (3) seen from the other side, and NOT lab drift: `/config/data/collections` is a `CollectionFolder` row inside Ferrofin's recursive universe, while on Jellyfin that path is a plain `Folder` and the virtual `Collections` library row itself is excluded. Ferrofin is exposing the library row where upstream exposes its physical child. This was NOT named in the previous version of this row; it is named now. (5) MusicArtist F 0 / J 3 and UserView F 0 / J 1 — the same NULL-`TopParentId` family, different write paths. None of the five is accepted; all five are the next ports on this op. |
