| `POST /Items/RemoteSearch/Apply/{itemId}` | registered | REAL | · | · | ⚠️ | open-work (NOT accepted): Apply itself is correct on both servers; what diverges is whether its effect SURVIVES a library scan. Measured on the lab pair, reproduced three times: Apply into a library with every "Metadata downloaders" box cleared leaves Movie 0497 identical on both (ProductionYear null, Genres [], Studios [], ProviderIds {Tmdb: 603}, and Name / RunTimeTicks / ImageTags / People untouched) - the `unlocked` half of the journey's evidence matches field for field. The journey then runs the library scan to completion on each server and reads the item back again: Jellyfin holds the cleared state, Ferrofin reverts to ProductionYear 2020 / Genres [Comedy, SciFi] / Studios [Ferrofin Studios], re-applied from /media/synth/movies/Movie 0497 (2020)/movie.nfo. That is the whole of the row's `unlocked_after_scan` diff. CAUSE: upstream gates every local metadata reader on a change monitor - v10.11.8 MediaBrowser.XbmcMetadata/Providers/BaseNfoProvider.cs `HasChanged` is `nfoLastWriteTimeUtc - item.DateLastSaved > TimeSpan.FromMinutes(1)`, and MetadataService.GetProviders (:613-708) returns an EMPTY provider list when no provider reports a change, so a scan over an item saved more recently than its sidecar runs no providers and merges nothing. Ferrofin's scan re-derives every item from disk on every pass: library_scan.rs calls `fetch_local_nfo` unconditionally for any unlocked row and `save_scanned_items` writes the result over the stored columns. UN-DEFER PATH, and why it is not a ride-along: the port needs three things together - (1) populate `BaseItems.DateLastSaved` on every save (nothing writes it today; the column exists and is bound, but only ever from an entity carrying None), (2) gate `fetch_local_nfo` / `fetch_remote_metadata` on the sidecar-vs-DateLastSaved comparison, and (3) a third scan-upsert variant that PRESERVES the provider-supplied columns when no provider ran, or skipping the read would wipe the very fields it is meant to protect. (1) alone changes `Etag` (dto_service.rs:1260 hashes DateLastSaved) and the `minDateLastSaved` query filters on every DTO in the API, so it is a server-wide change needing its own batch and its own perf run. Recorded here rather than hidden: the journey deliberately forces the scan and waits for it, so this stays a deterministic red instead of a race whose colour depends on when the suite happened to run. |

| `POST /Items/RemoteSearch/BoxSet` | registered | REAL | · | · | ✅ | jellyfin-bug-not-ported (a NARROW input class; the op itself is deep-verified — j_remote_search_identify diffs the FULL response body, key for key, on every other case). A NON-NUMERIC `Tmdb` provider id crashes the oracle's provider: v10.11.8 TmdbBoxSetProvider.cs:44 `Convert.ToInt32(searchInfo.GetProviderId(MetadataProvider.Tmdb), CultureInfo.InvariantCulture)` parses the stored id unguarded, so "notanumber" throws a FormatException, ProviderManager.GetRemoteSearchResults' catch-all swallows it, and Jellyfin answers [] — the name search the user actually typed never runs. Ferrofin declines to port the crash: a value that is not a number is not an id, so the remaining branches (and finally the name search) run. Per the repo's "don't port Jellyfin bugs" practice, Ferrofin is the correct side. Measured twice on the C1 lab pair with {"ProviderIds":{"Tmdb":"notanumber"},"Name":"<a real title>"}: Ferrofin 200 with 3 /search/collection rows for Name="The Lord of the Rings Collection", Jellyfin 200 []. NOT to be confused with a WHITESPACE-PADDED id: .NET's `int.Parse`/`Convert.ToInt32` default to NumberStyles.Integer (AllowLeadingWhite|AllowTrailingWhite), so " 603 " DOES pin the title upstream. That one was a real Ferrofin bug (`str::parse` rejects the padding, so the id branch was skipped and Identify answered with the name search); it is fixed — provider_manager.rs `parse_numeric_provider_id` trims — and is now probed live by the `*_padded` cases in j_remote_search_identify. Un-classify only if upstream starts guarding the parse. |

| `POST /Items/RemoteSearch/MusicArtist` | registered | REAL | · | · | ◐ | The library metadata-fetcher gate is now PORTED on this path: `LocalProviderManager::remote_search` resolves the `ItemId` reference item, reads ITS library's TypeOptions and drops every remote fetcher the "Metadata downloaders" list leaves unticked, honouring `IncludeDisabledProviders` as the short-circuit and the library's `MetadataFetcherOrder` for ordering — a port of MediaBrowser.Providers/Manager/ProviderManager.cs:787-830 -> :440-460 -> :462-491 -> MediaBrowser.Controller/BaseItemManager/BaseItemManager.cs:26-46. It only became REACHABLE for MusicArtist once `MusicArtistResolver` landed, because the gate resolves a library through `TopParentId` and an accessed-by-name artist has none. Measured live on the lab pair, both servers: scoped search with `ItemId` -> 200 with 0 results on BOTH (the fixture library ticks no MusicArtist fetcher; Ferrofin used to return 25), and `IncludeDisabledProviders=true` puts MusicBrainz back. The row also gained its first probe — `j_remote_search_identify` now sends `ItemId` and `IncludeDisabledProviders`, which no case did before, and resolves the artist id PER SERVER by Path so a server with no resolvable artist FAILS the row rather than skipping it. MEASUREMENT CAVEAT, not a divergence: MusicBrainz rate-limits both containers behind one egress IP and answers an over-rate query with an empty list, so the probe paces its legs 6 s apart and retries 5 times; if both servers still come back empty the row goes red with `named_count: 0` on both, which is the honest reading. The unscoped candidate list is compared whole, field for field, with ORDER normalised only — MusicBrainz returns tie-scored artists in an order two independent live queries do not agree on.

open-work (NOT accepted): the fetcher gate is ported and correct, but Ferrofin cannot RESOLVE a MusicArtist's library, so the gate is inert for that one kind. `LocalProviderManager::remote_search` now runs the C# `CanRefreshMetadata` chain (v10.11.8 ProviderManager.cs:801-830 -> :462-491 -> BaseItemManager.cs:26-46) and resolves the owning library through the reference item's `TopParentId`. Ferrofin's MusicArtist rows are materialized BY NAME out of `ItemValues` (library_scan.rs `item_values_of`, Artist=0/AlbumArtist=1), so they carry TopParentId='', ParentId='', Path=NULL and no `AncestorIds` row — verified directly in the lab DB for all three fixture artists. With no library resolvable the gate falls through permissive, so the GATED leg of the reads probe (ItemId=<own Artist>, IncludeDisabledProviders=false, against a Music library whose TypeOptions[MusicArtist].MetadataFetchers is []) answers with the MusicBrainz hit where Jellyfin answers []. Jellyfin's MusicArtist IS the artist DIRECTORY (measured: Path=/media/synth/music/Artist 01, ParentId=<Music library>), which is what makes `LibraryManager.GetCollectionFolders` (LibraryManager.cs:2262-2303) find the collection folder by path. The UNGATED leg of the same row diffs byte-identical, so the search itself is right; only the reference-item resolution is missing. Fix, in order of faithfulness: (1) give the materialized MusicArtist row its artist directory as `Path` (plus the owning library's TopParentId/ParentId) so the existing resolution works and `/Artists` stops serving Path:null — a library_scan change on the perf-gated path, needing its own batch and its own perf run; or (2) add a path-prefix fallback to `LocalProviderManager::library_options_for`, which only helps once (1) supplies the path. Same root cause as the separately observed `GET /Users/{userId}/Items?IncludeItemTypes=MusicArtist&Recursive=true` returning [] on Ferrofin and 3 on Jellyfin. |

| `GET /Items/Root` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ⚠️ | lab-state (NOT a Ferrofin divergence, and NOT an accepted one — this row renders under "Lab state" and is counted there, never in the accepted tally). Re-measured 2026-08-31 on the lane pair: the body diffs 22 leaves and exactly ONE differs, `ChildCount` J=6 H=5. The whole of that difference is a `Recordings` CollectionFolder (`/config/root/default/Recordings`, id 79a2726d…) that Jellyfin grew from an earlier DVR journey and this Ferrofin does not have; Ferrofin currently holds 0 recordings to Jellyfin's 12. On a freshly-wiped pair this row must come back clean, and if it does not it is a bug. PREVIOUS CLASSIFICATION, KEPT AS A RECORD BECAUSE IT WAS REAL AND IS NOW CLOSED: this row used to carry `accepted-divergence (structural: where the playlists plugin folder hangs)` — Ferrofin stored the playlists folder as a real DB child of the UserRootFolder where upstream parents it to the AggregateFolder (`LibraryManager.CreateRootFolder()`, v10.11.8 LibraryManager.cs:838-887, `folder.ParentId = rootFolder.Id`), which showed as the folder sorting INLINE by SortName instead of being appended last (`UserRootFolder.GetEligibleChildrenForRecursiveChildren` = `base.Children` + `list.AddRange(RootFolder.VirtualChildren)`, an APPEND, with `IsPreSorted => true`). That divergence was PORTED OUT by the AggregateFolder work, and the fix is measured, not assumed: on the pair today both servers list Playlists LAST, and the playlists folder's id and Path agree byte for byte (1071671e7bffa0532e930debee501d2e, /config/data/playlists). The old text also asserted "the row's own body diffs CLEAN and this op stays deep-verified", which was false while the row was red — that claim is what put a live red under the accepted heading, and it is the reason this row is now categorised by its measurement instead of by its story. |
| `GET /Items/Suggestions` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ⚠️ | open-work (NOT accepted). `SuggestionsController.GetSuggestions` (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Controllers/SuggestionsController.cs:78-96) is ONE recursive user query projected with ALL DtoOptions fields, and Ferrofin's handler is a line-for-line port — the handler was never the bug. Random PERMUTES but does not change the set, so at `limit >= |universe|` the answer is fully determined and the probe's seven full-limit legs body-diff it honestly; only the narrow startIndex leg is projected, and only onto what a Random page genuinely determines. The row was red for FIVE independent causes. THREE ARE FIXED HERE. (a) The item-universe gap on its biggest kind: LiveTvProgram was F 0 / J 338 and is now 338/338 (see `GET /Items`). (b) FOUR DTO fields the all-fields projection is supposed to carry and Ferrofin assigned NOWHERE — `RecursiveItemCount` and the folder `UserData.PlayedPercentage`/`Played` (`Folder.FillUserDataDtoValues`, v10.11.8 MediaBrowser.Controller/Entities/Folder.cs:1798-1838, whose two gates are DIFFERENT: the count is gated on its field alone and the played numbers on `SupportsPlayedStatus`, which is why a MusicAlbum carries the count and no percentage); `CumulativeRunTimeTicks` (Emby.Server.Implementations/Dto/DtoService.cs:492-495, the folder branch, not under EnableUserData); `HasLyrics`, unconditional on every Audio DTO and a plain bool — `false`, not an absent key (DtoService.cs:308-311); and `SeriesStudio` for both Episode and Season (:1228-1234 and :1256-1262), which is `series.Studios.FirstOrDefault()` and so lives on a row the projected item is not. (c) The `PresentationUniqueKey` grouping, which the LiveTvProgram mirror made visible: see `GET /Items`. STILL OPEN, both named: the MusicArtist/UserView/Live TV RECORDINGS half of the universe gap, and the BitRate offset — that last one is NOT a Ferrofin defect and is classified on its own row (`GET /Items/{itemId}`, jellyfin-bug: 10.11.8 reports more bitrate than the file physically contains, master's PR #16248 fixed it and Ferrofin ports the fixed form). The remaining Movie 0401/Inception and UserData.LastPlayedDate rows in the current results are LAB DRIFT from other batches' write journeys on this long-lived pair, not defects; they clear on a freshly wiped pair. Previous text kept for the record: open-work (NOT accepted). The old text ('Random, so the set is non-deterministic') was only half true and it hid two real divergences. Random here PERMUTES but does not change the set: `SuggestionsController` runs ONE query (`OrderBy = [(Random, Descending)]`, Recursive, mediaType/type, IsVirtualItem=false) and Ferrofin's handler is a line-for-line port, so at `limit >= |universe|` the answer is fully determined and parity_diff aligns Items by Path. The probe was the problem: `limit=10` sampled 10 of 552 at random and could never diff. It is now eight legs: seven pinned at a full limit (per type), where Random only permutes and the whole body IS diffed, plus one narrow startIndex/limit leg. That narrow leg is the one place a projection is applied — a page of 3 out of 500 Random-ordered rows draws a different triple per call on BOTH servers (measured: three consecutive calls, three different triples each side), so it compares StartIndex, TotalRecordCount and the page length only. Diffing its Items would pin this row red forever and destroy its ability to signal when the two real divergences below are fixed; the projection is scoped to that leg alone and nothing was added to parity_diff.VOLATILE. Two causes remain, BOTH owned by other rows and deliberately left visible here rather than filtered out of the probe: (1) ITEM UNIVERSE — Ferrofin's recursive user-root universe has no MusicArtist, Folder, TvChannel or UserView rows (F 552 vs J 561 unfiltered; per type F 0 vs J 3/3/2/1), the same parentless-by-name/TopParentId cause as GET /Artists/{itemId}/Similar; detector `/Items?userId=<u>&recursive=true&limit=100000`, which shows the identical 9-row delta, so it is not this handler's bug. (2) PER-ITEM DTO FIELDS — MediaSources[].Name/Bitrate, MediaStreams[].BitRate (a constant 192000 offset), LocalizedLanguage, Level/IsAVC/Width/Height: the same set already classified on GET /Items/{itemId} as independent-probe noise, inherited here because Suggestions returns full item DTOs. Narrowing the compare to hide either would be the dishonest green; the row stays red until they are fixed on their own rows. The multi-leg probe no longer skips a leg whose servers disagree on status: a status divergence (or a body that is not 200-with-JSON on both sides) is recorded as a mismatch, which is the whole point of keeping the type=MusicArtist and type=TvChannel legs. UNIVERSE GAP, re-measured 2026-08-29 and WIDER than first written: LiveTvProgram belongs on the missing-kind list too — `/Items?userId=<u>&recursive=true&includeItemTypes=LiveTvProgram` returns 338 rows on Jellyfin and 0 on Ferrofin, while `/LiveTv/Programs` returns the guide on both (F 19200, J 338). The knock-on is visible on a THIRD row: GET /Genres is red only because Jellyfin's genre list carries `News`, the XMLTV guide's programme genre, which Ferrofin's genre list cannot see for the same reason. One fix (give the by-name/programme rows a TopParentId so `scope_to_user_libraries` stops filtering them out) closes /Artists Similar, these Suggestions legs and the /Genres row together; it changes the recursive item universe server-wide, so it is its own change WITH the perf gate.

--- ALSO ON THIS ROW (merged from worktree-dv-lane2) ---

open-work (NOT accepted) — but the MusicArtist half of this row is CLOSED. `MusicArtistResolver` is now ported, so `/Items/Suggestions?type=MusicArtist` returns the same 3 artists on both servers (measured twice: F 3 ['Artist 01','Artist 02','Artist 03'], J the same) where Ferrofin used to return 0, and the three `Items[Path=/media/synth/music/Artist NN] (whole item)` misses are gone. The artist rows now carry the RunTimeTicks divergence classified on `GET /Artists` (a Jellyfin first-scan race; 60000000 is correct). What is still open on this row belongs to OTHER causes and OTHER rows: the universe kinds Ferrofin does not materialize at all (physical Folder 0/9, TvChannel, UserView, LiveTvProgram), `SeriesStudio` on Episode, the MediaStreams/MediaSources bitrate offsets already classified on `GET /Items/{itemId}`, and the movie/playlist corpus asymmetry between the two long-lived instances. `RecursiveItemCount` and folder `UserData.PlayedPercentage` ARE now ported (dto_service.rs, a port of Folder.cs:1798-1836). Do NOT widen VOLATILE or drop a leg to hide any of the rest.

| `DELETE /Items/{itemId}` | registered | REAL | · | · | ⊙ | ok |
| `GET /Items/{itemId}` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ⚠️ | jellyfin-bug, on two independent fields — but see the NOT EXHAUSTIVE note at the end: these two arms do not account for every divergence on this row. (1) BITRATE. Ferrofin reports MediaStreams[video].BitRate as container-minus-other-streams and MediaSources[].Bitrate as the stream sum; Jellyfin 10.11.8 falls the video stream back to formatInfo.BitRate verbatim (v10.11.8 ProbeResultNormalizer.cs:978-987) and then InferTotalBitrate(true) (PRN.cs:259-264), which double-counts the audio. Measured on the fixture: the file is 775053 B / 1.023 s = 6,060,832 bps, so Jellyfin's MediaSources[].Bitrate of 6,253,020 exceeds what the file physically contains, while Ferrofin's 6,061,020 matches ffprobe's own format.bit_rate. Upstream fixed this itself in PR #16248 (master PRN.cs:979-991 comments that formatInfo.BitRate is the container, not the video bitrate) and Ferrofin ports the fixed form. (2) PROBE-DTO NULLABILITY (Level / IsAVC / Width / Height). Upstream MASTER declares all four nullable — MediaBrowser.MediaEncoding/Probing/MediaStreamInfo.cs:93 `public int? Width`, :107 `public int? Height`, :170 `public int? Level`, :233 `public bool? IsAvc` — so a stream for which ffprobe emits no such key keeps null. v10.11.8 declares the same four as non-nullable value types at the same file's :95 `public int Width`, :109 `public int Height`, :172 `public int Level`, :235 `public bool IsAvc`, so an absent key deserializes to the CLR default and 10.11.8 then emits `Level: 0` and `IsAVC: false` on EVERY stream — audio and subtitle included, where neither value is meaningful — plus `Width: 0`/`Height: 0` on a text subtitle. Ferrofin ports MASTER and is correct. MEASURED LIVE 2026-08-30 on the loop pair, GET /Items/{id}?fields=MediaSources,MediaStreams and POST /Items/{id}/PlaybackInfo, item d37ecb9d75b0c0a8e9ecb0a864ec670e (Movie 0001), after re-probing all 500 correlated movies on Ferrofin: video stream identical on both (Level 12, IsAVC true, 320x240); audio stream J=`Level:0, IsAVC:false` vs H=both keys ABSENT; subrip subtitle J=`Level:0, IsAVC:false, Width:0, Height:0` vs H=all four keys ABSENT. *** CORRECTION TO BATCH A3 (this entry supersedes it). A3 (commit e4f1b37) read the four fields off v10.11.8 only, called Ferrofin's nullable form a bug, and changed both the probing DTO and ProbeResultNormalizer to fabricate 0/false. That was a REGRESSION against the project rule that the WIRE CONTRACT is pinned to 10.11.8 while the BEHAVIOUR Ferrofin ports is upstream master, and it was reverted in batch M1 (probing/dtos.rs back to Option<i32>/Option<bool>; probe_result_normalizer.rs back to master's shape — Width/Height/Level in the shared initializer at master PRN.cs:702-714, IsAVC in the video arm only at master PRN.cs:794). The diff above is the expected consequence and is NOT a reason to change Ferrofin back, and NOT a reason to widen parity_diff.VOLATILE. A3's OTHER probing change is CORRECT and stands: codec_tag_string binds the plain JSON key (v10.11.8 MediaStreamInfo.cs:206), because the trailing-`?` typo is a master-only regression (master :204) that stops the key deserializing at all. *** OWNER CALL PENDING on the bitrate arithmetic: porting 10.11.8's instead would make this row body-diffable at the cost of being wrong. OPEN QUESTION, NOT AN AGENT DECISION — MediaStream.LocalizedLanguage: upstream master populates it for audio and subtitle streams (master ProbeResultNormalizer.cs:779 in the subtitle arm and the matching audio arm), but the vendored 10.11.8 OpenAPI declares MediaStream with `additionalProperties: false`, so emitting it makes every body carrying a language-tagged stream schema_valid:false. A3 withheld it from the wire; batch M1 deliberately left that as-is pending the owner's answer. The two halves are: master emits the field; the pinned contract forbids it. Whoever answers, record the decision here and either serialize the field or state why the contract wins. NOTE: the 'instance/probe-noise' text this row carried before A3 was DISPROVEN — running both containers' ffprobe binaries over the same file gives byte-identical JSON (7.1.4 vs 7.1.3-Jellyfin), and ffprobe emits no per-stream bit_rate at all. A3's other real fixes stand: MediaSources[].Name ports BaseItem.GetMediaSourceName (file stem, not item.Name), and the People[].Role read projection no longer NULLIFs the empty string C# always emits. LAB-STATE RECORD, so it is not lost (batch M1): the re-probe M1 needed in order to see the code change at all (the four values are PERSISTED at probe time, so a container rebuild alone changes nothing) also cleared a set of TMDB-scraped fields Ferrofin was carrying on the five correlated movies and Jellyfin was not. The pre-M1 run recorded them as Ferrofin extras — CommunityRating, OfficialRating, OriginalTitle, Overview, PremiereDate, ProviderIds.Tmdb, ExternalUrls[Name=TMDB], plus Taglines[] (J len 0, H len 1) and PrimaryImageAspectRatio. They did NOT come back on a second `metadataRefreshMode=FullRefresh&replaceAllMetadata=true`, and the reason is NOT that the remote fetch is unreachable — that claim was made without measuring and is false. TMDB IS reachable from the container (api.themoviedb.org answers HTTP 401). The FullRefresh was gated off BEFORE any HTTP call, by provider_manager.rs:967, on two conditions together: the item's is_locked=true (the same LockData divergence this row carries) and an empty MetadataFetchers list for that library. So the extras were one-time state from an earlier lab epoch AND the experiment that was supposed to reproduce them could not fire. TODO (NOT accepted): re-run with is_locked cleared and the library's metadata downloaders enabled, and either reproduce the extras — in which case the real question is why Jellyfin's fetch does not populate the same fields off the same `uniqueid type=imdb tt0111161` in the fixture movie.nfo — or confirm the fields are gone for good. *** NOT EXHAUSTIVE — the two arms above do not account for the whole row. Live on the pair 2026-08-30, GET /Items/{itemId} also carries: (a) BackdropImageTags J=len 7 H=len 0 — Ferrofin serves no backdrop tags for these movies where Jellyfin serves seven; this is UNRESOLVED and is NOT explained by either arm above, and it is not obviously lab state either, so it needs its own investigation before anyone calls this row understood; (b) UserData.LastPlayedDate differing by a few seconds, which is the probe's own playstate write landing at slightly different wall-clock times on the two servers; and (c) MediaStreams[0].BitRate, which IS arm (1) and is listed here only so a reader does not count it twice. Whoever next touches this row: (a) is the open one. |
| `POST /Items/{itemId}` | registered | REAL | · | · | ⊙ | ok |
| `GET /Items/{itemId}/Ancestors` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ⚠️ | open-work (NOT accepted): the two causes this row was flagged for are PORTED and verified live (2026-08-30, pair 18096/18097, measured twice, identical both passes). (1) The AggregateFolder root is modelled: LibraryManager.CreateRootFolder() (LibraryManager.cs:838-888) is ported in ferrofin-core/src/aggregate_folder.rs — the row at {program data}/root carrying Jellyfin's own derived id f27caa37-e514-2225-cced-ed48f6553502, the playlists folder re-parented onto it (folder.ParentId = rootFolder.Id) and stored under 10.11.8's real class name PlaylistsFolder (id 1071671e-7bff-a053-2e93-0debee501d2e, byte-for-byte Jellyfin's; the old ManualPlaylistsFolder spelling is only PlaylistsFolder.GetClientTypeName() and is now emitted as the DTO Type, never stored), and AddVirtualChild ported as the virtual-children concat in BOTH places it shows — the direct-children predicate (translate_query.rs) and the ChildCount prefetch (item_count_service.rs). GET /Items/{playlistsId}/Ancestors now answers [AggregateFolder root] on both servers, and a playlist's chain is [Playlists, root] on both, diffing clean over 42 fields. reads.py seeds this row with the playlists folder as well as the five movies, because a movie's chain stops at the UserRootFolder and never exercises the aggregate hop. (2) FolderImageProvider is ported (dynamic_images.rs, DynamicImageKind::Folder) behind the IsTopParent guard (BaseItem.cs:733-757), so the UserRootFolder's PrimaryImageAspectRatio is 1.7777777777777777 on BOTH servers and the playlists folder correctly has no image. It is CreateSingleImage — a File.Copy of the first descendant's Primary — not a collage: md5 on the live pair showed Jellyfin's root poster.png is byte-identical to the 'Movies (synth)' library tile. RecursiveItemCount came with it (Folder.cs:701-714 + 1805-1808, gated on SupportsUserDataFromChildren, which BasePluginFolder opts out of via ICollectionFolder). REMAINING on this long-lived drifted lab, all instance state rather than code, to be re-scored on the end-of-campaign fresh-pair sweep: ChildCount J=7 H=6 (the two containers hold different library SETS — Jellyfin carries ParityCRUD + Recordings, Ferrofin carries a 'Movies (synth) Renamed' rename ghost — and each server's ChildCount equals its own /Library/MediaFolders total, so the concat is right on both); RecursiveItemCount 541 vs 540 (different leaf universes: 506 movies on Jellyfin to 500 on Ferrofin); and PrimaryImageAspectRatio 1.3333 vs 0.6666 on the AggregateFolder only (both servers pick the same source row, 'Movie 0001', by the same query — Ferrofin's copy of it is a 2000x3000 poster an image-upload write journey left behind, Jellyfin's is the fixture's 320x240 on-disk poster; same rule, different bytes). Do NOT add PrimaryImageAspectRatio to VOLATILE: it is a real comparable value on every other row, and it is already equal on the UserRootFolder hop here. ADDED 2026-08-30 by the review-fix pass, on top of the above. (3) TranslateParentItem (LibraryController.cs:959-966) is now the C# search rather than a type exemption: it looks a hop under the aggregate up in the user root's children — BOTH groups of them, since GetChildren there is the concatenated set — matching on PhysicalLocations.Contains(item.Path). A CollectionFolder view contributes its library locations; a virtual child's BaseItem.PhysicalLocations is [Path] (BaseItem.cs:450-461), so the search matches such a folder against ITSELF and the walk continues through it. Same answer as the type list it replaces under 10.11.8's single AddVirtualChild call site, but it is the rule, not a special case — and a PATHLESS row now correctly ends the walk, because PhysicalLocations is empty for one. (4) A recursive browse of the aggregate is a browse with no PARENT, not one with no SCOPE: C# SetTopParentIdsOrAncestors (LibraryManager.cs:1673-1698) leaves AncestorIds = [aggregate] behind, and Ferrofin's early return dropped scoping altogether. Measured on the pair before the fix, /Items?parentId={aggregate}&recursive=true&includeItemTypes=Person was Ferrofin 3 / Jellyfin 0 — rows outside the physical root's closure leaking out. Ferrofin keeps a one-level AncestorIds table rather than a transitive closure, so binding the aggregate there would match nothing; the branch now takes exactly the scope the same query with no parent gets. Re-measured: Person 0/0, and the aggregate-recursive total is now identical to Ferrofin's own unparented total (554), where it was 583. (5) RecursiveItemCount is now counted by ONE rule for every folder on a page: the per-folder query was missing IsVirtualItem = 0 while the aggregate's whole-server variant carried it, and upstream sets IsVirtualItem = false in BOTH Folder.GetRecursiveChildCount (Folder.cs:701-714) and the UnplayedItemCount query (Folder.cs:1812-1824). Invisible on this fixture (0 virtual rows) and so unmeasurable here — stated as a code-level correction, not as a measured flip. (6) FolderImageProvider is NOT ported narrower than upstream, as it can look: it is an ICustomMetadataProvider<Folder>, and ProviderManager dispatches by _metadataProviders.OfType<IMetadataProvider<T>>() with T bound to the item's MetadataService type (ProviderManager.cs:440-459), an invariant generic — so Season/Series/BoxSet/Playlist/CollectionFolder/MusicAlbum/MusicArtist/PhotoAlbum/UserView, which all have an exact MetadataService of their own, never reach it. FolderMetadataService (T = Folder) only takes the Folder subclasses with no service: Folder, AggregateFolder, UserRootFolder and BasePluginFolder, and the last is excluded by IsTopParent. Confirmed live: 10.11.8 serves no ImageTags.Primary for any Series, Season, MusicAlbum, BoxSet or plain Folder on this fixture. The three residual field diffs above persist unchanged and are still instance state: re-measured twice on 2026-08-30 after these fixes, ChildCount J=7 H=6, RecursiveItemCount J=542 H=540, PrimaryImageAspectRatio 1.3333 vs 0.6666 on the AggregateFolder only. |

The Season/Episode/Movie/Person TVDB fields Ferrofin used to advertise were HOLLOW — nothing read them — and were deleted in this batch rather than classified. `Comic Vine`/`ComicVine`/`Person` is a Ferrofin addition: 10.11.8 has no ComicVine IExternalId (same `git grep` evidence as above). It is backed — `external_ids.rs::book_urls` renders a Comic Vine "Links" entry for `Book | Person` items carrying the id — and Ferrofin ships a books provider (`ferrofin-providers/src/books.rs`, which also writes GoogleBooks/ISBN from OPF metadata). The Book-kind fields (ComicVine/GoogleBooks/ISBN) are the same divergence but are NOT measurable in this fixture, which has no Book items — see the residual note for batch A4. | `GET /Items/{itemId}/PlaybackInfo` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ⚠️ | jellyfin-bug, on two independent fields — but see the NOT EXHAUSTIVE note at the end: these two arms do not account for every divergence on this row. (1) BITRATE. Ferrofin reports MediaStreams[video].BitRate as container-minus-other-streams and MediaSources[].Bitrate as the stream sum; Jellyfin 10.11.8 falls the video stream back to formatInfo.BitRate verbatim (v10.11.8 ProbeResultNormalizer.cs:978-987) and then InferTotalBitrate(true) (PRN.cs:259-264), which double-counts the audio. Measured on the fixture: the file is 775053 B / 1.023 s = 6,060,832 bps, so Jellyfin's MediaSources[].Bitrate of 6,253,020 exceeds what the file physically contains, while Ferrofin's 6,061,020 matches ffprobe's own format.bit_rate. Upstream fixed this itself in PR #16248 (master PRN.cs:979-991 comments that formatInfo.BitRate is the container, not the video bitrate) and Ferrofin ports the fixed form. (2) PROBE-DTO NULLABILITY (Level / IsAVC / Width / Height). Upstream MASTER declares all four nullable — MediaBrowser.MediaEncoding/Probing/MediaStreamInfo.cs:93 `public int? Width`, :107 `public int? Height`, :170 `public int? Level`, :233 `public bool? IsAvc` — so a stream for which ffprobe emits no such key keeps null. v10.11.8 declares the same four as non-nullable value types at the same file's :95 `public int Width`, :109 `public int Height`, :172 `public int Level`, :235 `public bool IsAvc`, so an absent key deserializes to the CLR default and 10.11.8 then emits `Level: 0` and `IsAVC: false` on EVERY stream — audio and subtitle included, where neither value is meaningful — plus `Width: 0`/`Height: 0` on a text subtitle. Ferrofin ports MASTER and is correct. MEASURED LIVE 2026-08-30 on the loop pair, GET /Items/{id}?fields=MediaSources,MediaStreams and POST /Items/{id}/PlaybackInfo, item d37ecb9d75b0c0a8e9ecb0a864ec670e (Movie 0001), after re-probing all 500 correlated movies on Ferrofin: video stream identical on both (Level 12, IsAVC true, 320x240); audio stream J=`Level:0, IsAVC:false` vs H=both keys ABSENT; subrip subtitle J=`Level:0, IsAVC:false, Width:0, Height:0` vs H=all four keys ABSENT. *** CORRECTION TO BATCH A3 (this entry supersedes it). A3 (commit e4f1b37) read the four fields off v10.11.8 only, called Ferrofin's nullable form a bug, and changed both the probing DTO and ProbeResultNormalizer to fabricate 0/false. That was a REGRESSION against the project rule that the WIRE CONTRACT is pinned to 10.11.8 while the BEHAVIOUR Ferrofin ports is upstream master, and it was reverted in batch M1 (probing/dtos.rs back to Option<i32>/Option<bool>; probe_result_normalizer.rs back to master's shape — Width/Height/Level in the shared initializer at master PRN.cs:702-714, IsAVC in the video arm only at master PRN.cs:794). The diff above is the expected consequence and is NOT a reason to change Ferrofin back, and NOT a reason to widen parity_diff.VOLATILE. A3's OTHER probing change is CORRECT and stands: codec_tag_string binds the plain JSON key (v10.11.8 MediaStreamInfo.cs:206), because the trailing-`?` typo is a master-only regression (master :204) that stops the key deserializing at all. *** OWNER CALL PENDING on the bitrate arithmetic: porting 10.11.8's instead would make this row body-diffable at the cost of being wrong. OPEN QUESTION, NOT AN AGENT DECISION — MediaStream.LocalizedLanguage: upstream master populates it for audio and subtitle streams (master ProbeResultNormalizer.cs:779 in the subtitle arm and the matching audio arm), but the vendored 10.11.8 OpenAPI declares MediaStream with `additionalProperties: false`, so emitting it makes every body carrying a language-tagged stream schema_valid:false. A3 withheld it from the wire; batch M1 deliberately left that as-is pending the owner's answer. The two halves are: master emits the field; the pinned contract forbids it. Whoever answers, record the decision here and either serialize the field or state why the contract wins. NOTE: the 'instance/probe-noise' text this row carried before A3 was DISPROVEN — running both containers' ffprobe binaries over the same file gives byte-identical JSON (7.1.4 vs 7.1.3-Jellyfin), and ffprobe emits no per-stream bit_rate at all. A3's other real fixes stand: MediaSources[].Name ports BaseItem.GetMediaSourceName (file stem, not item.Name), and the People[].Role read projection no longer NULLIFs the empty string C# always emits. Same root cause, same evidence and the same open question as GET /Items/{itemId}; the PlaybackInfo leg was measured in the same pass. |

| `GET /Items/{itemId}/RemoteImages` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ⚠️ | accepted-divergence (provider lineup): two documented lineup facts, no server bug left. (1) `Providers` is built from `GetRemoteImageProviderInfo(item)` and narrowed ONLY by `type`, never by `providerName` (v10.11.8 RemoteImageController.cs:92-99) — so every leg, scoped or not, carries Ferrofin's extra `FanArt` entry (see `GET /Items/{itemId}/RemoteImages/Providers`). (2) On the UNSCOPED leg, fanart.tv adds its own images and Jellyfin's OMDb adds one Primary that Ferrofin cannot (upstream bakes an OMDb key at OmdbProvider.cs:257; Ferrofin's OMDb is inert without FERROFIN_OMDB_KEY — CLAUDE.md 'Current scope'). What this row now PROVES: on `providerName=TheMovieDb`, `Images` and `TotalRecordCount` are byte-identical INCLUDING ORDER on all four legs measured live on the A4 pair (default 138/138, includeAllLanguages=true 391/391, type=Logo 7/7, series 0/0). That is the fix this batch landed — Ferrofin was serving 497 images where 10.11.8 served 139, unfiltered and unsorted, because `ProviderManager.GetImages`' preferred-language filter and `OrderByLanguageDescending` were never ported (`RemoteImageQuery.include_all_languages` was read by nothing). |

| `GET /Items/{itemId}/RemoteImages/Providers` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ⚠️ | accepted-divergence (provider lineup, 1 entry): Ferrofin compiles fanart.tv in as a remote image provider and stock Jellyfin 10.11.8 does not — its ten concrete `IRemoteImageProvider` implementors are AudioDb{Album,Artist}, Omdb, StudioImages and Tmdb{BoxSet,Movie,Person,Episode,Season,Series} (`git grep -l IRemoteImageProvider v10.11.8`, minus the interface, the two Manager files and the two test files), with no fanart provider in tree — fanart is an out-of-tree plugin, and the only `fanart` strings in 10.11.8 are filename patterns and NFO tags. So a Movie, Series, MusicArtist or MusicAlbum lists one extra `FanArt` entry. Measured live on the A4 pair over seven path-correlated kinds: dropping that single entry makes the arrays equal field-for-field INCLUDING `SupportedImages` and order. The real bug that was hiding under this flag IS FIXED, not classified: Ferrofin had no `TmdbSeasonImageProvider` arm at all (a Season offered NO providers) and no `TmdbEpisodeImageProvider` (an Episode offered only OMDb, `Order = 90`); both are ported, ordered TMDB-first per `IHasOrder`, and Season/Episode/Audio/Person now diff clean. |

| `GET /Items/{itemId}/Similar` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ◐ | instance/methodology (PROPERTY-VERIFIED, NOT body-diffed — the ledger counts and lists this row separately from the deep-verified ones; status codes, the empty short-circuits, the count invariant and the candidate rule are what is gated, by the reads.py `similar_invariants` probe, and each of the three property rows now issues its OWN alias rather than all three probing /Movies). Two independent reasons a movie seed's item array cannot diff, both measured on the loop pair. (1) ORDER: Jellyfin 10.11.8 sets `OrderBy = [(ItemSortBy.Random, Ascending)]` (v10.11.8 LibraryController.cs:801); five identical calls at limit=12 returned five different SETS, because limit < |universe|. (2) ALGORITHM: 10.11.8 builds ONE flat `InternalItemsQuery { Genres = item.Genres, Tags = item.Tags }`, while Ferrofin ports upstream master's weighted `MovieSimilarItemsProvider` (genre 10 / tag 5 / studio 5 / director 50 / actor+guest-star 15, MovieSimilarItemsProvider.cs:27-31) plus its phase-2 `IsMovie = true, IsPlayed = false` + ConfigureUserAccess pass. Measured at limit=1000: Jellyfin 299 items, Ferrofin 432, and Jellyfin's set is a STRICT SUBSET of Ferrofin's (onlyJ=0); the 133 extras each share a studio and/or a person with the seed and no genre — master's dimensions exactly. *** OWNER DECISION PENDING (NOT an agent-granted acceptance): running master's scorer against a 10.11.8 oracle is a deliberate ALGORITHM divergence on three ops, and CLAUDE.md reserves additions to the accepted-divergence list to the owner. The engineering case for keeping master is the repo's 'do not port Jellyfin bugs' rule — 10.11.8 additionally over-returns by 4 at every limit (measured 1/3/5/12/50 -> 5/7/9/16/54) because `BaseItemRepository.PrepareFilterQuery` (v10.11.8:1427-1430) adds 4 when `EnableGroupByMetadataKey` and never trims back, while Ferrofin honours limit exactly as master's `.Take(requestedLimit)` does — but the call is the owner's, and reverting to 10.11.8's flat query would make these three rows body-diffable at a pinned wide limit. *** WHAT IS VERIFIED LIVE instead of a body diff (every fact computed per server and then compared): status 200, StartIndex 0, TotalRecordCount == len(Items), the seed is never returned, every Type is in the C# IncludeItemTypes, the page is full, len(Items) <= limit+4 (the named Jellyfin quirk is the ONLY slack), an unknown seed is 404 on both, a nil seed is 200-empty on both, an Episode seed short-circuits empty on both, the five aliases agree as sets at limit=1000, and `obeys_own_candidate_rule` — each server's answer is re-derived independently from the DTO fields under ITS OWN documented algorithm (Jellyfin: every row shares a genre or tag with the seed, which 200 of the 500 fixture movies violate; Ferrofin: the answer equals master's weighted model's top-N IN ORDER by score desc, then Ferrofin's OWN SortName/Id tiebreak — master has no tiebreak at all, `MovieSimilarItemsProvider.cs:189-195` is `OrderByDescending(score)` alone, so that clause pins Ferrofin's rule, which is strictly more determined than the C#'s). The person dimension is matched as (Name, PersonType) pairs because that is what a `PeopleId` IS — 'the Peoples table has one row per (Name, PersonType)' (PeopleRepository.cs:47) — which is exactly what both master's `PeopleId` join and Ferrofin's SQL compute. Negative controls run 2026-08-29: the Ferrofin clause fails on a reversed order and on 12 arbitrary movies, the Jellyfin clause fails on 12 movies outside its universe. NOT REACHED BY ANY PARITY LAYER, and unit-covered instead: the phase-2 filters — the lab has zero played movies and one all-libraries user, so `IsPlayed = false` and ConfigureUserAccess are exercised only by `a_played_movie_is_not_suggested_to_the_user_who_played_it`, `a_library_the_user_cannot_access_contributes_nothing`, `a_blocked_media_folder_hides_its_rows` and `the_access_filter_does_not_starve_the_page` in similar_items_manager.rs. Three real Ferrofin bugs this row used to hide were fixed in the same pass: an unknown seed returned 200-empty instead of 404, a nil seed returned 400 instead of the C# root-folder fallback, and the no-limit default was 10 instead of C#'s `limit ?? 50` (SimilarItemsManager.cs:93). |

| `GET /Library/MediaFolders` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ⚠️ | open-work (NOT accepted) — measured 2026-08-31 on the lane pair, 66 leaves compared, and the row is RED for THREE distinct reasons that must not be folded together. (1) FIXED IN THIS BATCH, recorded so the defect cannot come back unnoticed: Ferrofin's auto-provisioned `Collections` library carried a NULL `CollectionType` where Jellyfin sends `boxsets`. 10.11.8 keeps that field in the row's `Data` blob and creates the library through `AddVirtualFolder(name, CollectionTypeOptions.boxsets, …)` (v10.11.8 Emby.Server.Implementations/Collections/CollectionManager.cs:81-109); Ferrofin's `ensure_container` wrote no `Data` at all. Now written (only over an EMPTY `Data`, so an adopted database's `PhysicalLocationsList`/`PhysicalFolderIds` survive a swap-back), and re-measured live: the folder answers `boxsets` on `/Library/MediaFolders`, `/UserViews` and `/Items?parentId={root}`, the same as Jellyfin. (2) STILL OPEN — the Collections library's PATH and therefore its ID diverge, which is a real drop-in adoption hazard. Upstream's row is the virtual-folder shortcut `{RootFolderPath}/default/Collections`, so its id is `GetNewItemIdInternal("root\\default\\Collections")` = 9d7ad6afe9afa2dab1a2f6e00ad28fa6; Ferrofin provisions `{data}/collections` directly and gets 6f929a39bd27711ce6208fb0aef66e5b. Both ids read off the live pair. Consequences, both measured: the library is absent from `GET /Library/VirtualFolders` on Ferrofin and present on Jellyfin, and `item_persistence_service::container_at` — which matches BY PATH — misses upstream's row on an adopted database and would provision a SECOND Collections library beside it. The code comment that used to justify the current path asserted Jellyfin derives its id from `%AppDataPath%/collections`; that is false and has been corrected in place. It IS true of the playlists folder, which agrees byte for byte on both servers (1071671e7bffa0532e930debee501d2e, /config/data/playlists). UN-DEFER PATH: provision the collections library through the same virtual-folder path the user libraries already use (shortcut dir + `.mblink` -> `{data}/collections`, registered so it lists in `GET /Library/VirtualFolders`), WITH a migration that re-parents the box sets hanging off the old id — the id change is why this is not a one-line edit — then re-measure `/Library/VirtualFolders`, `/Library/MediaFolders` and `/UserViews` against 18097 and re-run suite/roundtrip.sh. (3) STILL OPEN — `PrimaryImageAspectRatio` is absent on every Ferrofin library row and present on Jellyfin's (J=1.7777777777777777 on `Movies (synth)`). Not caused by this batch and not previously recorded here; the un-defer path is to compute the aspect ratio for the collection-folder image the way the item DTO path already does, and to add a leg that asserts it on a library row. (4) LAB STATE, not a divergence: Jellyfin additionally lists a `Recordings` library from an earlier DVR journey (see `GET /Items/Root`), which is the `TotalRecordCount` J=6 H=5. |
| `POST /Library/Movies/Added` | registered | REAL | · | · | ⚠️ | jellyfin-bug-not-ported (the SELECTOR half; the refresh half is confirmed live on Ferrofin by j_library_webhooks — create by path, id-matched NFO refresh, unmatched-id negative control). Jellyfin 10.11.8 cannot match an external id on these four routes AT ALL: LibraryController.PostUpdatedSeries / PostUpdatedMovies (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Controllers/LibraryController.cs:571-635) query with `DtoOptions(false)`, whose ctor sets `Fields = []` (MediaBrowser.Controller/Dto/DtoOptions.cs:29-37), so BaseItemRepository.ApplyNavigations skips `Include(e => e.Provider)` (Jellyfin.Server.Implementations/Item/BaseItemRepository.cs:442-444) and DeserializeBaseItem never fills ProviderIds (:895-897) — the in-memory `string.Equals(id, i.GetProviderId(...))` filter therefore compares every item against null. Measured twice on the C4 lab pair 2026-08-30 (F=18296, J=18297): (a) a supplied id matches NOTHING even when the item demonstrably carries it — Jellyfin's own DTO reports Movie 0001 as ProviderIds {Imdb: tt0111161} and `POST /Library/Movies/Updated?imdbId=tt0111161` produced no `will be refreshed` line in 20 s; a probe series carrying Tvdb 990002 was still at its old Overview 60 s after `?tvdbId=990002`, and the same probe pair run through the journey leaves all four rows H=True J=False. (b) with the id OMITTED, `string.Equals(null, null)` is TRUE, so a bare `POST /Library/Series/Added` reported EVERY series (logged: the probe plus Series 01-08 'will be refreshed' in one burst) — a whole-TV-library refresh from an empty request. Ferrofin pushes the id into the SQL query instead (`paths_by_provider_id` → InternalItemsQuery::any_provider_id_equals, crates/ferrofin-api/src/handlers/library.rs), so a real id matches and an absent id is a no-op: the route's documented intent, kept under the repo's 'don't port Jellyfin bugs' rule. These rows therefore cannot be deep_verified against this oracle; the journey records Ferrofin's effect and Jellyfin's silence rather than a green. |

| `DELETE /Library/VirtualFolders` | registered | REAL | · | · | ⊙ | ok |
| `GET /Library/VirtualFolders` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ok |

| `POST /Library/VirtualFolders/Name` | registered | REAL | · | · | ⚠️ | accepted-divergence (Ferrofin converges eagerly where upstream defers). The row reads H=True J=False, measured twice on the loop pair 2026-08-30, and the FALSE is the ORACLE'S — but it is upstream's DOCUMENTED behaviour for the request this row actually issues, not a bug, which is why this entry is no longer categorised jellyfin-bug. WHAT THE PROBE ISSUES: `POST /Library/VirtualFolders/Name?name=&newName=` with NO refreshLibrary parameter, i.e. refreshLibrary=false. WHAT UPSTREAM DOES WITH THAT REQUEST: LibraryStructureController.RenameVirtualFolder (v10.11.8 and master byte-identical apart from three added ClearIgnoreRuleCache() calls, plus a [RegularExpression] master added to a DIFFERENT action's name) moves the directory and then, in its finally block, `if (refreshLibrary) { ValidateTopLibraryFolders(ct, true); ... } else { await Task.Delay(1000); _libraryMonitor.Start(); }`. So on refreshLibrary=false Jellyfin deliberately validates nothing and keeps the CollectionFolder row under the OLD name, pointing at a directory that no longer exists, and serves it from /UserViews until some later scan. THE ASSERTION: the journey asserts the ROOT VIEW converges — after the rename no CollectionFolder still carries the old name, the new name is materialized, and the root's CollectionFolder count is unchanged end to end — over /Items + /UserViews. It used to assert only that /Library/VirtualFolders lists the new name, which upstream builds by walking the user-views DIRECTORY (LibraryManager.GetVirtualFolders, LibraryManager.cs:1527-1572, identical in both trees: it matches each directory against GetUserRootFolder().Children by path and leaves ItemId NULL when nothing matches), so it is the one surface a stale DB row cannot corrupt — and both servers passed it while only one was right. WHY FERROFIN MUST NOT COPY IT: Ferrofin derives a CollectionFolder's id from its directory path and get_virtual_folders mints a row for every directory it sees, so leaving the vacated row behind yields TWO rows where upstream has one stale one — measured before the fix as a permanent phantom library ('Movies (synth) Renamed', 5730b9eb5c86a66014cedd6ea527295f) that survived restarts and POST /Library/Refresh and showed up in the client sidebar. FerrofinVirtualFolderManager::rename_virtual_folder now deletes the row the vacated directory backed and runs prune_orphan_collection_folders (the port of ValidateTopLibraryFolders' remove leg — 'If the user has somehow deleted the collection directory, remove the metadata from the database', LibraryManager.cs:1172-1190, identical in both trees — also called from remove_virtual_folder and at LibraryScanner::scan start). CONSEQUENCE, flagged for the owner: the API handler rescans the renamed library UNCONDITIONALLY, not only when refreshLibrary is set, because deleting the old row cascades its children away (FK_BaseItems_BaseItems_ParentId ON DELETE CASCADE) and skipping the refresh would leave the renamed library empty; upstream can skip it only because it keeps the stale row and its children. The rescan is scoped to the renamed library when library_id_by_name resolves its id, which is the normal case (get_virtual_folders mints the row before the lookup); if it does not resolve, the fallback is a FULL library scan — more than upstream queues for this request — and that arm now logs at WARN so an unrequested full pass is attributable. Dashboard-only op. SEPARATE FINDING, NOT WHAT THIS ROW SCORES: with refreshLibrary=TRUE, where the C# above promises ValidateTopLibraryFolders(ct, removeRoot: true), the 10.11.8 oracle still did not converge — renaming 'Music (synth)' to 'Music (synth) DiagRB' left BOTH names in the root for a full 120 s poll at 5 s intervals, i.e. it added the new row and never deleted the vacated one. Only an explicit DELETE /Library/VirtualFolders?refreshLibrary=true pruned it (which is how that measurement was cleaned up, in the same session). That is an upstream bug, recorded here so it is not lost, but it is deliberately NOT this row's category: the probe never sends refreshLibrary=true, and a category must describe the request that was scored. *** HISTORY, so the finding is not lost: batch S1 first measured this as an ORPHAN USER VIEW leak (rename, then rename back, left a phantom library in GET /UserViews that survived a container restart and a POST /Library/Refresh). Batch F3 then found the cause — ValidateTopLibraryFolders' remove leg was never ported, so rows minted from directories on read were never pruned when the directory vanished — and fixed it. The lane-3 branch still carried S1's pre-fix wording at merge time; this row supersedes it. |

| `POST /LiveStreams/Open` | registered | REAL | · | · | ⊙ | security-fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1, cross-user authz): C# RequestHelpers.GetUserId (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Helpers/RequestHelpers.cs:67-85) has two halves: an absent/all-zero userId falls back to the caller, and a userId that is NOT the caller is honoured only for an ADMINISTRATOR, else SecurityException -> 403. Ferrofin ported only the first half here. Found by the S1 sweep, not by the plan. The route takes the id from BOTH the query and the body (query wins — v10.11.8 MediaInfoController.cs:287), and the resolved id is what the stream, and for Live TV the tuner, is opened as. Ferrofin folded both raw ids into the LiveStreamRequest with no gate. Re-measured independently in the S1 review pass with a fresh non-admin account ('s1fixprobe', created and deleted on both servers): a non-administrator naming the administrator's id is 403 on BOTH servers, while the administrator naming another user and a non-administrator naming their own id still succeed on both. Recorded in the S1 review pass: the batch claimed these five as flipped but left no row for them, so the fix existed only in the code and the claim. | empty-guid binding fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1 review pass, reviewer finding F2): ASP.NET binds an EMPTY or whitespace-only value for a `Guid?` to null (SimpleTypeModelBinder), so upstream answers `?userId=` and `?userId=%20` exactly as it answers a request with no userId at all; serde's `Option<Uuid>` tried to parse "" and made both a 400. S1 spread that pre-existing defect to five more routes when it gave previously parameter-less handlers a `Query<T>`. Re-measured on the lane-3 pair BEFORE the fix: `?userId=` and `?userId=%20` were 400 on Ferrofin against 200 on Jellyfin (404/404 where the probe id does not exist). FIXED AS A CLASS, not as an instance: every `Option<Uuid>` field of a `Query<...>` struct in ferrofin-api now binds through `handlers::query_parse::empty_as_none_uuid`. AFTER, the four spellings (absent / all-zero / empty / whitespace) agree on both servers across /Devices, /Channels, /Channels/Items/Latest, /LiveTv/Recordings/Folders, /LiveTv/Recordings/{recordingId}, /Audio/{itemId}/universal AND the four routes that carried the defect before S1 ever ran (/Items, /UserViews, /Persons, /Years). Request BODIES keep serde's strict parse on purpose: System.Text.Json rejects `"UserId": ""` upstream too. |

Ferrofin answers 404 to both. The persistence order is ported as-is, not "fixed": upstream saves the configuration at :251 BEFORE the :264 throw, so a mapping keyed on a channel that does not exist is stored on both servers and then reported as not found — a dangling pair the match ladder simply never hits. | `GET /LiveTv/GuideInfo` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ◐ | instance: property-verified by reads.py (guide_info_invariants), not asserted. The old note claimed the 'now .. now+7d' window matched Jellyfin; it did not — GuideManager.GetGuideInfo is now .. now + GetGuideDays() (LiveTvOptions.GuideDays clamped 1..14, default 7) and Ferrofin's handler took no state, so it answered a hardcoded 7 days whatever the dashboard was set to (measured: GuideDays=14 gave J +14d, F +7d). Ported. What is compared now: window length in seconds, whole-day-ness, now-relativity, UTC tick wire format, and that both bounds come from ONE UtcNow. Excluded: the instant itself, and the fraction's DIGIT COUNT — Utf8JsonWriter's FFFFFFF trims trailing zeros, so both servers emit 5, 6 or 7 digits depending on the instant (sampled 60x each). |

| `POST /LiveTv/ListingProviders` | registered | REAL | · | · | ⊙ | 📎 (side-path) open-work (NOT accepted): the SCHEDULES DIRECT listings backend is unported. Jellyfin registers two `IListingsProvider`s — `XmlTvListingsProvider` and `SchedulesDirect` (src/Jellyfin.LiveTv/Listings/SchedulesDirect.cs @ v10.11.8: token auth against json.schedulesdirect.org, `GetHeadends` → lineups, `GetLineups`/`AddLineup` and the schedules ingest) — and Ferrofin registers only XMLTV; `ferrofin-livetv/src/schedules_direct.rs` is the country list and nothing else. WHAT THAT COSTS, op by op: this route accepts and SAVES a provider whose `Type` is `SchedulesDirect` instead of rejecting it the way C# `GetProvider` does (ResourceNotFoundException → 404), so the row is stored and every later read of it resolves nothing; `GET /LiveTv/ListingProviders/Lineups?type=SchedulesDirect` answers 404 where a credentialed Jellyfin lists that account's headends; and `GET /LiveTv/ListingProviders/SchedulesDirect/Countries` is real but is the ONLY piece of the backend that is. NOT PROBEABLE ON THIS PAIR, which is why it needs this row: the fixture configures one XMLTV provider and no Schedules Direct account, so both servers agree on every request the lab can make and no live layer can ever go red on it. The `effect` verdict this op carries is the XMLTV path only — hence scope side-path. Un-defer path: port `SchedulesDirect` (token cache, `GetHeadends`, `GetLineups`, `AddLineup`, `GetProgramsAsync`) as a second `IListingsProvider` behind the same seam the XMLTV one uses, then make `save_listing_provider` resolve `Type` through the registry so an unknown type 404s like the C#. Needs its own batch (credentials + a recorded-response fixture); it is an open work item, not an accepted divergence. |

Ferrofin returns 404 there, which is what Jellyfin itself returns for every other unresolvable provider. The two contract paths agree: `?id=<configured provider>` is 200 and body-diffed in reads.py, and no id/unknown id is 404 on both. |

| `POST /LiveTv/Programs` | registered | REAL | · | · | ✅ | lab-state (NOT a Ferrofin divergence, and NOT an accepted one — this row renders under "Lab state" and is counted there, never in the accepted tally). BOTH binder defects this row carried are now FIXED and pinned. (a) THE DELIMITED-STRING FORM: Ferrofin bound all seven of GetProgramsDto's collection properties as plain arrays and answered 422 to the delimited-STRING spelling upstream accepts — v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Models/LiveTvDtos/GetProgramsDto.cs decorates ChannelIds/SortBy/SortOrder/GenreIds/EnableImageTypes/Fields with JsonCommaDelimitedCollectionConverterFactory and Genres with the PIPE factory, and JsonDelimitedCollectionConverter.Read splits with RemoveEmptyEntries + Trim() and DROPS an entry the TypeConverter refuses ('catch (FormatException) { /* Ignore unconvertible inputs */ }') while the array arm stays a strict JsonSerializer.Deserialize<T[]>. reads.py legs 5-7 pin exactly that, drop semantics included. (b) THE BINDER'S REJECTION STATUS — fixed in batch G5 as a CLASS, not on this route: every Ferrofin route bound its body with axum::Json, whose rejection is 422 with a text/plain body, where every Jellyfin controller derives from BaseJellyfinApiController's [ApiController] (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/BaseJellyfinApiController.cs:12-18) and answers 400 with a ValidationProblemDetails document as application/json; charset=utf-8 — the untouched ASP.NET default (`git grep -n "InvalidModelStateResponseFactory|SuppressModelStateInvalidFilter|ValidationProblemDetails" v10.11.8 -- '*.cs'` returns nothing, and master's GetProgramsDto is byte-identical, so this was outcome 3: Ferrofin matched NEITHER tree). The opposite half of the same defect was that serde's derived Deserialize binds a JSON SEQUENCE to a struct positionally, so `[]` and `["StartDate"]` came back 200 with the whole 338-programme guide where System.Text.Json 400s — a malformed body silently accepted as a valid one. Both are closed by crates/ferrofin-api/src/extract.rs (JsonBody/JsonSeqBody/JsonValueBody, 75 extractor positions across 22 handler files; Json stays the RESPONSE type), tested as a class in crates/ferrofin-api/tests/body_binder.rs, and pinned live by reads.py legs 9-10. Only the STATUS is asserted there: the errors dictionary's keys and messages carry .NET type names ("Jellyfin.Data.Enums.ItemSortBy") and traceId is a per-request ASP.NET activity id — neither is reproducible, and neither was put in parity_diff.VOLATILE. RESIDUAL, the only thing left on this row and the reason it is still here: leg 2 (the whole guide inside the window BOTH servers hold, ~4058 leaves) reports MISSING keys with 0 mismatch and 0 extra, and every one of them is Status/TimerId/SeriesTimerId on a programme Jellyfin holds a timer for. That is DVR STATE, not code — drift from sibling batches' journeys on a lab that has been up for days. Ferrofin implements the enrichment (LiveTvManager.AddRecordingInfo is ported as add_recording_info in crates/ferrofin-livetv/src/manager.rs, and manager::tests::program_dtos_link_their_recording_timer asserts all three fields on a programme whose timer exists); it simply has no timers to attach. sweep.sh runs reads.py BEFORE journeys.py, so on the authoritative freshly-wiped pair neither server holds a timer when this leg runs. The three fields are deliberately NOT dropped from the compare: dropping them would hide a real enrichment regression, which is the one thing this leg is uniquely placed to catch. Delete this entry when the row comes back clean on a fresh sweep. |

| `POST /LiveTv/SeriesTimers` | registered | REAL | · | · | ◐ | jellyfin-bug (unknown ProgramId only): the op is deep-verified live on the fixture tuner by the j_livetv_series_timers journey (re-measured 2026-08-31, GREEN on both servers, twice); this row records the ONE input that journey deliberately does not diff a status on, so it is not lost. Posting a series timer whose ProgramId the guide does not hold makes Jellyfin throw InvalidOperationException("SeriesId for program not found") (v10.11.8 DefaultLiveTvService.cs:270-274), which surfaces as 500. Ferrofin ports the rejection but answers 400 and stores nothing. Rejecting is the ported behaviour; the 500 is the bug, and it is deliberately not reproduced (see 'Don't port Jellyfin bugs'). ALSO ON THIS ROW — PROBE DEFECT, FOUND AND FIXED 2026-08-31, kept because the row was red on it for a whole batch and the wrong conclusion was drawn. The journey used to overwrite `defaults["Name"]` on the same series timer it then fan-out-checked. `GetTimersForSeries` (v10.11.8 DefaultLiveTvService.cs:803-821) sets `query.Name = seriesTimer.Name` whenever `SeriesId` is empty — which is every timer in this XMLTV fixture — so a renamed series timer matches NO programme and schedules nothing. `posted_name_kept` and `fan_out` were therefore mutually exclusive and the row could not pass on ANY server, Jellyfin included. The batch report read that double red as 'behaviour upstream does not have'; it is not. Measured on the pair, identically on both servers: unmodified defaults schedule 7 showings (1 New = earliest, 6 Cancelled), renamed defaults schedule 0. The probe now splits the two roles across different series timers and asserts the renamed one schedules nothing as a POSITIVE leg, so the rule is measured from both sides. Three legs that were VACUOUS while the fan-out found nothing now really run: `its_timers_are_gone` on delete, and both arms of the `IsManual` hand-cancel contract. |

So on Jellyfin the removed tuner's `LiveTvChannel` items LINGER in the library until the next guide refresh prunes them through `GuideManager.CleanDatabase`; on Ferrofin the channel rows are FK children of the tuner host (`crates/ferrofin-db/migrations/0003_live_tv.sql:36-38`, `FK_LiveTvChannels_TunerHosts_TunerHostId … ON DELETE CASCADE`, and the programmes hang off the channels the same way at :60-62, `FK_LiveTvPrograms_Channels_ChannelId`) and go with it in the same statement. Measured on the loop pair 2026-08-30, both servers taking a case-flipped id and answering 204: immediately after the DELETE F reports 0 channels AND 0 programmes (the airings are FK children of the channel rows, so they go too) where it had 2 / 19200, while J still reports 2 / 338. Both servers converge on the SAME end state — Jellyfin's next refresh deletes exactly those rows, because `GuideManager.CleanDatabase` (src/Jellyfin.LiveTv/Guide/GuideManager.cs:147-148, 331-360 @ v10.11.8) removes every `LiveTvChannel`/`LiveTvProgram` the pass did not re-emit, and a channel of a tuner that no longer exists is never re-emitted — and what differs is the window in between, during which Jellyfin serves channels no tuner can stream. Ferrofin keeps the cascade (see the 'don't port Jellyfin bugs' rule): a dangling channel is a 5xx waiting for a client that tries to play it. NOT PORTED, deliberately, and NOT hidden: the journey asserts this op on the configuration, which is what both servers agree the delete means, and this note is the record of what it does not assert. |

| `GET /Movies/{itemId}/Similar` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ◐ | instance/methodology (PROPERTY-VERIFIED, NOT body-diffed — the ledger counts and lists this row separately from the deep-verified ones; status codes, the empty short-circuits, the count invariant and the candidate rule are what is gated, by the reads.py `similar_invariants` probe, and each of the three property rows now issues its OWN alias rather than all three probing /Movies). Two independent reasons a movie seed's item array cannot diff, both measured on the loop pair. (1) ORDER: Jellyfin 10.11.8 sets `OrderBy = [(ItemSortBy.Random, Ascending)]` (v10.11.8 LibraryController.cs:801); five identical calls at limit=12 returned five different SETS, because limit < |universe|. (2) ALGORITHM: 10.11.8 builds ONE flat `InternalItemsQuery { Genres = item.Genres, Tags = item.Tags }`, while Ferrofin ports upstream master's weighted `MovieSimilarItemsProvider` (genre 10 / tag 5 / studio 5 / director 50 / actor+guest-star 15, MovieSimilarItemsProvider.cs:27-31) plus its phase-2 `IsMovie = true, IsPlayed = false` + ConfigureUserAccess pass. Measured at limit=1000: Jellyfin 299 items, Ferrofin 432, and Jellyfin's set is a STRICT SUBSET of Ferrofin's (onlyJ=0); the 133 extras each share a studio and/or a person with the seed and no genre — master's dimensions exactly. *** OWNER DECISION PENDING (NOT an agent-granted acceptance): running master's scorer against a 10.11.8 oracle is a deliberate ALGORITHM divergence on three ops, and CLAUDE.md reserves additions to the accepted-divergence list to the owner. The engineering case for keeping master is the repo's 'do not port Jellyfin bugs' rule — 10.11.8 additionally over-returns by 4 at every limit (measured 1/3/5/12/50 -> 5/7/9/16/54) because `BaseItemRepository.PrepareFilterQuery` (v10.11.8:1427-1430) adds 4 when `EnableGroupByMetadataKey` and never trims back, while Ferrofin honours limit exactly as master's `.Take(requestedLimit)` does — but the call is the owner's, and reverting to 10.11.8's flat query would make these three rows body-diffable at a pinned wide limit. *** WHAT IS VERIFIED LIVE instead of a body diff (every fact computed per server and then compared): status 200, StartIndex 0, TotalRecordCount == len(Items), the seed is never returned, every Type is in the C# IncludeItemTypes, the page is full, len(Items) <= limit+4 (the named Jellyfin quirk is the ONLY slack), an unknown seed is 404 on both, a nil seed is 200-empty on both, an Episode seed short-circuits empty on both, the five aliases agree as sets at limit=1000, and `obeys_own_candidate_rule` — each server's answer is re-derived independently from the DTO fields under ITS OWN documented algorithm (Jellyfin: every row shares a genre or tag with the seed, which 200 of the 500 fixture movies violate; Ferrofin: the answer equals master's weighted model's top-N IN ORDER by score desc, then Ferrofin's OWN SortName/Id tiebreak — master has no tiebreak at all, `MovieSimilarItemsProvider.cs:189-195` is `OrderByDescending(score)` alone, so that clause pins Ferrofin's rule, which is strictly more determined than the C#'s). The person dimension is matched as (Name, PersonType) pairs because that is what a `PeopleId` IS — 'the Peoples table has one row per (Name, PersonType)' (PeopleRepository.cs:47) — which is exactly what both master's `PeopleId` join and Ferrofin's SQL compute. Negative controls run 2026-08-29: the Ferrofin clause fails on a reversed order and on 12 arbitrary movies, the Jellyfin clause fails on 12 movies outside its universe. NOT REACHED BY ANY PARITY LAYER, and unit-covered instead: the phase-2 filters — the lab has zero played movies and one all-libraries user, so `IsPlayed = false` and ConfigureUserAccess are exercised only by `a_played_movie_is_not_suggested_to_the_user_who_played_it`, `a_library_the_user_cannot_access_contributes_nothing`, `a_blocked_media_folder_hides_its_rows` and `the_access_filter_does_not_starve_the_page` in similar_items_manager.rs. Three real Ferrofin bugs this row used to hide were fixed in the same pass: an unknown seed returned 200-empty instead of 404, a nil seed returned 400 instead of the C# root-folder fallback, and the no-limit default was 10 instead of C#'s `limit ?? 50` (SimilarItemsManager.cs:93). |
| `GET /MusicGenres` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ⚠️ | open-work (NOT accepted) — the LIST shape was verified 2026-08-11 (QueryResult<BaseItemDto>, status + schema conformant, same by-name shape as /Genres), and that still holds. What a 2026-08-31 id-correlation measured on the lane pair is that the two servers give the SAME genre three different ids: F Ambient 1dcbb1e8…, Jazz 5721323b…, Rock f3bc3ad5…; J Ambient 7566ee57…, Jazz 2140463c…, Rock 56fb6975…. Person rows, by contrast, correlate exactly on both servers (Alice/Bob/Carol share ids), so this is MusicGenre's derivation specifically, not by-name ids in general. Cause, read in the code: `item_persistence_service::music_genre_row` derives the id with `derive_item_id(BaseItemKind::MusicGenre, value)` — the bare NAME, lowercased (`IdDerivation::LegacyLowercase`) — where upstream derives it from the metadata PATH: `GetItemByNameId<MusicGenre>(MusicGenre.GetPath(name))`, and `MusicGenre.GetPath` is `Path.Combine(ApplicationPaths.MusicGenrePath, GetValidFilename(name).Trim().TrimEnd('.'))` (v10.11.8 MediaBrowser.Controller/Entities/Audio/MusicGenre.cs:76-89). Ferrofin already has both halves of the correct derivation — `IdDerivation::Jellyfin { program_data_path }` and the `by_name_item_id`/`person_path` pattern the Person rows use. UN-DEFER PATH: route `music_genre_row` through the path-based derivation, then re-key the existing rows and their `ItemValuesMap`/`UserDatas` references in a migration — the re-key is the part that makes this a port of its own rather than a one-line change, and it is why it was not folded into the batch F4 reviewer-fix pass. Discovered while verifying that pass's `ids=`-scoped grouping fix, which is the query that made the id mismatch visible (F answers 3 of its own MusicGenre ids, J answers 0 for the same ids).

--- ALSO ON THIS ROW (merged from worktree-dv-lane2) ---

open-work (NOT accepted) — SUPERSEDES the 2026-08-31 'jellyfin-bug' record, which was the wrong category and said so with the wrong evidence. That record folded upstream's `GroupBy(PresentationUniqueKey)` collapse under 'jellyfin-bug', which in this campaign means 'master already fixed it'. Master has NOT: master `BaseItemRepository.ByName.cs:213-224` still ships the collapse (`g.Min(e => e.Id)`, with a `TopParentIds`-preference arm for MusicArtist), and v10.11.8 `BaseItemRepository.cs:1313-1316` ships it as `g.FirstOrDefault()`. An upstream behaviour present in BOTH trees and absent from Ferrofin is an un-ported gap, so it has now been PORTED rather than labelled.

WHAT F2 (as revised) FIXED — four things, all measured on the lane-2 pair:
1. `get_genres` passed the music types as `exclude_content_types` and `get_music_genres` passed them as `include_content_types`, restricting which CONTENT items feed the aggregate. C# restricts neither: master ByName.cs:44-52 and v10.11.8 :211-222 are both `GetItemValues(filter, _getGenreValueTypes, <returnType>)`, whose inner query is built from `IncludeItemTypes` ALONE (ByName.cs:154-168); only the outer `.Where(e => e.Type == returnType)` differs. The owner-type split lives solely on the *Names* variants (`GetItemValueNames`, ByName.cs:120-141), which `get_genre_names`/`get_music_genre_names` still carry — that asymmetry IS the port. The compensation was paying for a scanner mis-split already fixed in da43f0a.
2. `ItemCounts` were never computed (every per-kind count 0, matching neither tree). Ported MASTER's per-CleanName `BuildItemCountsByCleanName` (ByName.cs:283-338; the seven `BaseItemKindNames` locals it reads are at :304-310) as seven conditional `SUM(ci."Type" = ?)` aggregates in the same GROUP BY, emitted only when the caller named item types (`if (filter.IncludeItemTypes.Length > 0)`, ByName.cs:249). `ItemCount`/`ProgramCount` stay 0 because neither tree assigns them.
3. The representative collapse itself is now ported — `item_repository::push_representative_rank`, a `ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY bi."PresentationUniqueKey" ORDER BY [MusicArtist TopParentId preference,] bi."Id")` filtered to rank 1, run over the FILTERED row set exactly as upstream groups `masterQuery`, with the page total counting GROUPS (`representativeIds.Count()`, ByName.cs:229-232) instead of rows. Ferrofin followed master's deterministic `Min(Id)` over 10.11.8's `FirstOrDefault`. This is user-visible well beyond genres: upstream's own comment at that site says the grouping is what collapses alternate versions and a MusicArtist with a folder in more than one library.
4. The `PresentationUniqueKey` half. The earlier record claimed 'music_genre_row now writes PresentationUniqueKey … Ferrofin writes a real {Type}-{Name} key on every one'. That was FALSE on the lab it cited: the insert is `INSERT ... WHERE NOT EXISTS`, so it never touches a row that already exists, and `MusicGenre|Ambient/Jazz/Rock` still read back NULL. Fixed properly with a startup repair, `item_persistence_service::backfill_missing_presentation_keys`, scoped to the four by-name kinds whose key is a pure function of the name. It logged `items=3` on the first boot after the change and nothing on the second, and the container's own DB now reads `MusicGenre-Ambient/-Jazz/-Rock`. Without it, porting item 3 would have COLLAPSED those three into one — reproducing upstream's collapse without reproducing upstream's keys.

WHY THE ROW IS STILL RED (measured twice, 2026-08-31, after the ports above):
H lists [Action, Ambient, Comedy, Drama, Jazz, Rock, SciFi, Thriller]; J lists [Action, Ambient, Jazz, Rock]. Two axes, and NEITHER is now an un-ported behaviour.
AXIS 1 — Jellyfin's un-refreshed by-name keys. `CreateItemByName<T>` (master LibraryManager.cs:1289-1329 / v10.11.8 :1052-1092) constructs `new T { Name, Id, DateCreated, DateModified, Path }` and queues no refresh, so the row is written with a NULL `PresentationUniqueKey`; the key is assigned later by `MetadataService.BeforeSaveInternal` (MediaBrowser.Providers/Manager/MetadataService.cs:332-338, identical on both trees), i.e. on the row's FIRST metadata refresh. The lab's own jellyfin.db proves the mechanism exactly — every by-name row with a non-NULL DateLastRefreshed has a key and every row with a NULL one does not:
  MusicGenre Ambient  key MusicGenre-Ambient  DateLastRefreshed 2026-08-30 00:29:31.807812
  MusicGenre Jazz     key MusicGenre-Jazz     DateLastRefreshed 2026-08-30 00:29:31.8121208
  MusicGenre Rock     key MusicGenre-Rock     DateLastRefreshed 2026-08-30 00:29:31.8180006
  MusicGenre Action   key NULL                DateLastRefreshed NULL   (created 01:00:11 by a probe GET)
  MusicGenre Comedy/Drama/SciFi/Thriller  key NULL, DateLastRefreshed NULL (created 02:20-02:21)
So Jellyfin's four unkeyed rows form ONE NULL group and surface as the single representative Action, while its three refreshed ones list individually. Ferrofin writes the key at creation and repairs any legacy row at startup, so it is permanently in the state Jellyfin reaches after a refresh. Reproducing J's list would mean deliberately withholding a column upstream itself fills in. On a freshly provisioned pair the probe-created rows do not exist, so this axis should not appear in the end-of-campaign sweep.
AXIS 2 — the uncorrelated ItemCounts, a genuine jellyfin-bug that MASTER FIXED. J returns SongCount 9 for EVERY music genre and MovieCount 514 for Action, while BOTH databases hold exactly 3 Audio rows per music genre and 200 Movies carrying `action`. v10.11.8 BaseItemRepository.cs:1368-1392 hands every row the same `itemCountQuery.Count(...)` totals and comments 'TODO: This is bad refactor!'; master replaced it with the per-CleanName dictionary Ferrofin ported. Ferrofin returns each database's own truth. Following master here is the campaign rule, not a choice.

NOT ACCEPTED, and nothing here is deferred: axis 1 needs no port (it is upstream transient state, with the mechanism and the evidence above) and axis 2 is master's fix. parity_diff.VOLATILE was not widened for either. deep_verified stays false, which is the honest state while the two listings genuinely differ. |

`MusicGenresController.GetMusicGenre` carries the SAME slug branch as the genres controller (`GetItemFromSlugName<MusicGenre>`, '-'->'&'/'/'/'?', three lookups, creating nothing) but NOT the same fallback: it keeps `if (item is null) return NotFound();` on BOTH trees (v10.11.8 :164-167, master :165-168, where the action is additionally `[Obsolete("Use GetGenre instead")]`), where `GenresController` has `item ??= new Genre()`. That asymmetry is upstream's and it is the port. It is also how the wire caught a wrong port: the first cut copied the genres fallback here and answered 200 where a live 10.11.8 answers 404 (`/MusicGenres/R-B` J=404) — no unit test would have found it, because the two controllers' C# is identical until the last five lines.

REAL WIRE DIVERGENCE FOUND AND FIXED IN THE REVIEW PASS (the reason this row is 34 fields and not 28). The two 404 legs used to be scored clean by a harness branch that treated 'both servers, same status, neither body JSON' as agreement — but `reads.py::token_get` discarded the body of EVERY non-200, so 'neither body is JSON' was a property of the probe, not of the responses. Measured directly with curl: J returned `{"type":"https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc9110#section-15.5.5","title":"Not Found","status":404,"traceId":"00-…"}` and H returned `{"error":"not found: music genre R-B"}`. Two different documents, scored as one clean leg.
Fixed on BOTH sides. Harness: `token_probe` keeps the body at any status and the multi legs diff it like any other body, so an error body is now part of the comparison; a leg where neither server sent ANY body is recorded as `status-class` evidence (the ledger's weakest verdict) rather than as a body diff, and two different non-JSON bodies behind the same status are a mismatch. Server: `ApiError::Problem(ProblemStatus::NotFound)` renders ASP.NET's RFC 9110 ProblemDetails, and this handler uses it because its C# `NotFound()` takes no argument — ASP.NET then fills the body from `ApiBehaviorOptions.ClientErrorMapping`.
It is deliberately a PER-CALL-SITE variant and not a global change to how Ferrofin renders errors, because Jellyfin's error bodies are not uniform. Measured on the live 10.11.8 container in one session: `NotFound()` -> ProblemDetails; `GET /Users/{unknown}` (`NotFound("User not found")`) -> the bare JSON string `"User not found"`; a model-binding failure -> a `ValidationProblemDetails` with an `errors` map; an unauthenticated request -> an EMPTY 401 with no content type; a wrong-verb request -> an empty 405. A blanket ProblemDetails would have traded one divergence for four. Those four call-site families are un-ported and NOT owned by this batch — they belong to the rows that probe them, and they are now visible to any multi row that probes one, because the harness no longer throws error bodies away.
`traceId` is the one field on the VOLATILE list for this: it is ASP.NET's per-request W3C trace-context id, which two independent instances cannot agree on and one instance never repeats. Scoped to that exact key; `type`, `title`, `status` and `errors` are all still compared, which is what makes an error-body diff mean anything. |

Divergence vs Jellyfin: the install is SYNCHRONOUS — none of the PackageInstalling/Completed/Failed/Cancelled WebSocket messages are emitted (no dashboard progress; a slow repo holds the POST open), and the plugin appears in /Plugins only after the activating restart.
