| `GET /Packages/{name}` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ◐ | property-verified: the two catalogues are each server's own repository content, so the single-package BODY is not comparable across instances, but every branch of InstallationManager.FilterPackages is. reads.py's `packages_by_name_invariants` derives 17 facts from each server's OWN /Packages[0] and diffs the fact dicts (a fact that is False on Ferrofin fails the row even when Jellyfin agrees): exact + case-insensitive name, guid-beats-name (`if (!id.IsEmpty()) ... else if (name is not null) ...` — the two predicates are alternatives, guid wins), an empty and an all-zeros guid falling through to the name (`IsEmpty()`), an unknown name as 404, the single-package body equalling that catalogue entry, and the WHOLE `Guid?` model-binding format set. THE FORMAT SET WAS THE HOLE, and it was a real live divergence, not a documentation slip: the fact list enumerated N, D and B and stopped, while `uuid::Uuid::parse_str` — what the handler used — is NOT `Guid.TryParse`'s set. Measured on the loop pair 2026-08-30, before the fix: `(guid)` (P) was 400 on Ferrofin and 200 on Jellyfin; `{0x...,{0x..}}` (X) was 400 and 200; a value padded with whitespace was 400 and 200 (.NET trims both ends); and `urn:uuid:guid`, which .NET refuses and `parse_str` accepts, was 200 and 400. Fixed in Rust, not in the probe: ferrofin_util::guid_extensions::parse_dotnet_guid ports Guid.TryParseGuid's dispatch (trim, then '(' -> ExactP, '{' -> ExactB when it contains a hyphen else ExactX, otherwise ExactD/ExactN) including TryParseExactX's two documented compat quirks — it eats whitespace anywhere, and its components are read as 32-bit so `0x1031b` is an accepted spelling of the short component `0x031b`, while a byte component above 0xFF is rejected. Both guid-bound plugin routes (GET /Packages/{name} and POST /Packages/Installed/{name}) bind through it. Re-measured after the fix: 27 spellings, all agreeing — the 12 basic ones and 15 exotic X/whitespace/overflow cases (truncating short-component overflow, 9-significant-digit overflow, a doubled 0x prefix, a missing 0x prefix, an empty component, 7 and 9 byte components, trailing junk, a leading '+', interior whitespace in D and P). NOT FIXED, recorded: the same `Uuid::parse_str` narrowness remains on every OTHER guid-bound route (axum's `Path<Uuid>`/`Query` extractors and handlers::query_parse), so P and X spellings are still refused there and `urn:uuid:` still accepted; no client sends either, and widening it is a whole-surface change this batch did not measure. Superseded the Layer-1 row, whose {name} was filled from a GENRE (sweep.py resolve_fixtures) so the live probe was GET /Packages/Action — 404/404 with the lookup never issued. |

| `GET /Persons/{name}` | registered | REAL | ✅ | · | ✅ | verified 2026-08-31 (batch F2, revised after review): 2/2 legs clean, 56 fields compared, measured twice. REPLACES a pre-F2 'accepted-divergence: no lazy create on GET' record that was wrong on both counts — `LibraryManager.GetPerson` is a plain `GetItemList` lookup on both trees and creates nothing (unlike `GetGenre`/`GetStudio`, which are `CreateItemByName`), and Ferrofin already matched it. That upstream asymmetry is recorded HERE so it does not become invisible: `/Persons/{unknown}` must NOT materialize a Person row, while `/Genres/{unknown}` and `/Studios/{unknown}` must materialize theirs.

The row was red for two ported-wrong COLUMNS, both now fixed. (1) `SortName`: J="Alice Parity" H="alice parity". `BaseItem.CreateSortName` is gated on `EnableAlphaNumericSorting`, which `Person` overrides to false, so a Person's sort name is the name verbatim. The rule now has one home, `kinds::sort_name_for`, which `upsert_item`'s fallback, `backfill_missing_sort_names` and the people repository's inserts all route through. (2) `Path` and `PresentationUniqueKey` were never written: `save_people`'s Person insert now writes `Person.GetPath` (the first-letter subfolder form) and `Person-{Name}`, and creates the metadata directory after commit — `/config/metadata/People/{A,B,C}/{Name}` now exists on disk exactly as it does in the Jellyfin container.

STARTUP REPAIR, and the bug the review caught in it. Rows an older build wrote are repaired by `people_repository::repair_person_items` (it reported 3 on the first boot and 0 on the second). As first written that pass rewrote `SortName` to the verbatim name for EVERY Person row whose sort name differed, without reading `ForcedSortName` — so a person carrying a user sort-title override would have had it destroyed on every startup, and the pass would never converge on that row. It also falsified the pass's own 'no-op on a database adopted from Jellyfin' guarantee, since Jellyfin stores `ModifySortChunks(ForcedSortName).ToLowerInvariant()` there (BaseItem.cs:544 on master, :536 on v10.11.8). Fixed: the SELECT now reads `coalesce("ForcedSortName",'')` and a row carrying one keeps its `SortName` untouched (the `Path`/`PresentationUniqueKey` halves still run — those are derived columns no user owns), which is the same guard migration `0012_hermit_episode_sort_names.sql` and `upsert_item` already applied. The lab could not have caught it: all three of its people have an empty ForcedSortName. Pinned by `repair_person_items_never_overwrites_a_forced_sort_name`, which asserts both the survival of the override and that the second pass reports 0. |

| `POST /Playlists` | registered | REAL | · | · | ⊙ | security-fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1, cross-user authz): C# RequestHelpers.GetUserId (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Helpers/RequestHelpers.cs:67-85) has two halves: an absent/all-zero userId falls back to the caller, and a userId that is NOT the caller is honoured only for an ADMINISTRATOR, else SecurityException -> 403. Ferrofin ported only the first half here. Measured twice on the lane-3 pair (Ferrofin 18396 / Jellyfin 18397) with a second, non-admin account 's1probe' created on both: BEFORE, a non-admin passing ?userId=<admin> got 200 on Ferrofin - creating a playlist OWNED by the admin - against 403 on Jellyfin. The body's UserId is the same parameter by another route and is folded in before the gate, exactly as C# does. After the fix both servers return 403 for the same request, while an administrator naming another user's id and a non-administrator naming their own id still succeed on both (measured, twice). Regression tests in crates/ferrofin-api/tests/ cover all three cases per route. Fifth instance in this campaign of a C# authorization check Ferrofin served without one (A1 startup, B3 x29 Live TV routes, D2 get_lineups, F1 items); the campaign owes a dedicated audit of every [Authorize(Policy=...)] and RequestHelpers.GetUserId call site in the 10.11.8 controllers against Ferrofin's route guards. |

| `DELETE /Playlists/{playlistId}/Users/{userId}` | registered | REAL | · | · | ⚠️ | jellyfin-bug: Jellyfin's DELETE is a silent no-op on BOTH v10.11.8 and master. `PlaylistsController.RemoveUserFromPlaylist` (identical in both trees) resolves the share from one Playlist instance, then `PlaylistManager.RemoveUserFromShares` (v10.11.8 L641-648 / master L669-676, byte-identical) re-fetches a DIFFERENT instance via GetPlaylistForUser -> GetPlaylists -> LibraryManager.GetItemList (master L1656-1673, which returns _itemRepository.GetItemList directly, bypassing the BaseItem cache and materialising fresh objects) and calls `shares.Remove(share)`. `MediaBrowser.Model/Entities/PlaylistUserPermissions.cs:8` is a plain class with only UserId/CanEdit auto-properties — no Equals, no IEquatable, not a record — so `EqualityComparer<T>.Default` is REFERENCE equality, Remove returns false, the UNCHANGED list is written back, and the controller returns 204. Corroborated inside the same file: `AddUserToShares` removes an element it took from the SAME list, which is why POST works and DELETE does not. `git log v10.11.8..master` over both paths contains no change to RemoveUserFromShares — MASTER HAS NOT FIXED THIS. Ferrofin deletes by (playlist_id, user_id) in SQL (ferrofin-core/src/collection_manager.rs `remove_user_from_shares`), which is the semantic intent of the C#, so the share actually goes and a repeat DELETE correctly 404s — the controller's own `share is null -> NotFound("User permissions not found")` branch, which on Jellyfin is unreachable. Ferrofin is the correct side. Measured on the F6 pair 2026-08-30, twice: F 204 then GET Users [] then repeat DELETE 404; J 204 then GET Users STILL lists the user then repeat DELETE 204. Discriminating control on the SAME Jellyfin instance: `POST /Playlists/{id}` with `Users: []` (UpdatePlaylist assigns the list wholesale, no List.Remove) DOES clear and persist — so this is not container skew, fixture or a Shares-persistence problem, it is RemoveUserFromShares alone. Previously filed `instance` (instance noise / methodology), which was wrong: a deterministic, twice-reproduced upstream defect is not lab noise. The row cannot reach deep_verified while upstream is broken — journeys.py requires the write's effect on BOTH servers and Jellyfin's legitimately does not hold. That is the correct terminal state for a jellyfin-bug row, not a probe weakness. The second symptom is now recorded rather than asserted: `j_playlist_share` reports the repeat-DELETE status under `notes.playlist_share_repeat_delete_status` in journey-results.json (measured F=404 J=204, twice), deliberately kept OUT of the row's boolean so a future Ferrofin regression to a blind 204 cannot pass by matching Jellyfin. |

instance (the body only; the row is now MEASURED by property). The old text claimed 'stock Jellyfin ships neither [extensions nor WASM plugins]', which is false and was hiding two defects: a live 10.11.8 ships FIVE bundled provider plugins with hardcoded GUIDs (TMDb b8715ed1-6c47-4528-9ad3-f72deb539cd4, MusicBrainz 8c95c4d2-e50c-4fb0-a4f3-6c06ff0f9a1a, OMDb a628c0da-fac5-4c7e-9d1a-7134223f14c8, AudioDB a629c0da-fac5-4c7e-931a-7174223f14c8, Studio Images 872a7849-1171-458d-a6fb-3de3d442ad30), each answering this route with a real BasePluginConfiguration. What remains true is that Ferrofin registers NONE of those ids (it registers compiled-in extensions and staged WASM components), so no shared subject exists and the 200 body cannot be id-correlated. FIXED HERE: the route was `RequireAuth`, so any authenticated account — a guest profile, a stolen playback token — could read a plugin's stored {ApiKey, Username, Password}. `PluginsController` carries [Authorize(Policy = Policies.RequiresElevation)] at CLASS level (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Controllers/PluginsController.cs:25, byte-identical on master) with exactly one [AllowAnonymous] override, GetPluginImage (:221) — so this read is admin-only and now is. reads.py `plugin_configuration_invariants` pins the object shape, the unknown/malformed-id statuses and the anonymous refusal on BOTH servers. WHAT WOULD EARN A BODY DIFF: the same plugin installed on both instances, which on the Jellyfin side needs a .NET assembly — the loading model Ferrofin rejects by design. |

instance (the body only; the WRITE SEMANTICS are now measured by property, and three were wrong). 'No shared plugin id' is true for identity and false as a reason not to check. `UpdatePluginConfiguration` (v10.11.8 PluginsController.cs:186-201, unchanged on master) deserializes into `configPlugin.ConfigurationType` and then `if (configuration is not null) configPlugin.UpdateConfiguration(configuration)`. Ferrofin stored the raw request body, so measured live: (a) POST `null` STORED the literal null and the next GET answered the bare token `null` — an admin-reachable way to destroy a plugin's configuration and a violation of the contract's object shape, where Jellyfin's null body is a documented no-op; (b) `{"Bogus":"zzz"}` round-tripped for ever, where the typed deserializer drops it; (c) `{"Username":123}` was stored and served back as an integer to a plugin whose config type declares a string. All three are fixed in `FerrofinPluginManager::set_plugin_configuration` + `project_config`. ACCEPTED DIVERGENCE that remains: on a malformed/empty/array body Jellyfin answers HTTP 500 'Error processing request.' (an unhandled JsonException; unchanged on master) while Ferrofin answers 400 with a JSON error — Ferrofin is more correct and keeps it, per 'don't port Jellyfin bugs'. That leg is deliberately excluded from the probe's facts. |
| `POST /Plugins/{pluginId}/Manifest` | registered | REAL | · | · | ◐ | instance (no shared plugin id) — but the SHAPE is diffable, and it was wrong in five ways. `MediaBrowser.Common/Plugins/PluginManifest.cs` (v10.11.8; master changes nothing here) gives every property an explicit lowercase [JsonPropertyName], so this one response is camelCase against the server's PascalCase policy, and the id is `[JsonPropertyName("guid")] public Guid Id` written dashless by JsonGuidConverter. Measured: Jellyfin returns 13 camelCase keys, Ferrofin returned 5 PascalCase ones and a hyphenated `Id` — ZERO keys in common. The stale evidence ('verified 2026-08-14 by handler tests') was a test asserting `"Name":"Demo"`, which cemented the defect. Now a real `ferrofin_model::plugins::PluginManifest` DTO, projected with upstream's own bundled-plugin rule (`PluginManager.cs:562` builds `new PluginManifest { Id, Status, Name, Version }` and leaves the rest at ctor defaults) — which is exactly Ferrofin's case for both plugin tiers, neither of which ships a manifest document. The read is admin-only now too (class-level RequiresElevation). reads.py `plugin_manifest_invariants` pins the key set, the dashless `guid`, and the unknown-id/anonymous statuses on both servers.

expected-extension (SCOPE ONLY — the shared ids now diff clean). The previous note ('no shared plugin id … so there is no manifest to fetch on both sides') hid a real wire bug: with the five ids shared, this route was 200 on BOTH servers with UNRELATED bodies. Jellyfin returns `PluginManifest`, the one camelCase DTO on the surface — every property carries an explicit `[JsonPropertyName]` and `Id` is spelled `guid` — with thirteen keys (assemblies/autoUpdate/category/changelog/description/guid/name/overview/owner/status/targetAbi/timestamp/version); Ferrofin answered a five-key PascalCase projection of its own ({Description,Id,Name,Status,Version}) that no Jellyfin client could parse. Ported in the F1 review pass, including the values upstream fills for a plugin with no `meta.json` on disk — the dummy record `PluginManager.CreatePluginInstance` builds (v10.11.8 PluginManager.cs:560-575) sets only Id/Name/Version/Status, so the descriptive strings stay empty, `assemblies` is [], `timestamp` is `0001-01-01T00:00:00.0000000Z`, `autoUpdate` keeps its `true` initializer and `imagePath` is omitted. Now diffed live by the reads.py `POST /Plugins/{pluginId}/Manifest` row over all five ids. Remaining divergence is scope only (Ferrofin's own extension/WASM ids). CURRENT LAB STATE: the row reads 4/5 clean, and the fifth leg is TMDb — whose Jellyfin-side `status`/`autoUpdate` were changed to `Restart`/`false` by an earlier reviewer's `POST /Plugins/{tmdb}/{v}/Enable` probe on this pair (they disclosed it). That is `ProcessAlternative`'s memory-only pair, exactly as described on the Enable row, and it survives until Jellyfin restarts. The other four ids are byte-identical on both servers, and Ferrofin's TMDb values (`Active`/`true`) match a PRISTINE Jellyfin's — verified against the four unperturbed ids on this same pair. Deliberately NOT worked around: dropping the TMDb leg would hide the one field that can diverge here, and restarting the Jellyfin container to clear it would be a one-sided mutation of the pair. The final sweep runs on a freshly wiped pair, where the leg is clean. |

| `POST /Plugins/{pluginId}/{version}/Disable` | registered | REAL | · | · | ⚠️ | open-work (PENDING OWNER RATIFICATION — NOT accepted): the text previously here described a DIFFERENT op (the pre-restart window of `POST /Packages/Installed/{name}`, already classified on its own row) and no layer had ever issued this request — sweep hands writes to Layer 2, journeys.py contains no plugin journey, and reads.py covered only Configuration/Manifest/Image. A probe now exists (reads.py `plugin_enable_invariants` / `plugin_disable_invariants`, aliases PluginsEnable / PluginsDisable, one per route). Measured twice on the F6 pair 2026-08-30, the two servers agree on EVERY id-independent contract fact — unknown guid 404/404, unparseable version 400/400, wrong version 404/404, installed-version-minus-a-component 404/404 (.NET `Version` treats an absent component as -1), anonymous 401/401 — and on the resulting state: status_after_disable 'Disabled' on both, status_after_enable 'Active' on both, restored True on both. Exactly ONE fact diverges, and it is kept in the diff on purpose, so the row is RED: the HTTP status of a toggle that SUCCEEDS is Ferrofin 204 / Jellyfin 404. (a) THE 404 IS A JELLYFIN BUG AND FERROFIN MUST KEEP 204. All five of Jellyfin's plugins are bundled in-assembly, so `PluginManager.CreatePluginInstance` builds `new LocalPlugin(instance.AssemblyFilePath, ...)` (v10.11.8 PluginManager.cs:541,559) and `Path` is a .dll FILE. `ChangePluginState` (:513) flips `Manifest.Status` in memory FIRST, then `SaveManifest` (:364) does `File.WriteAllText(Path.Combine(path, "meta.json"))` and catches only `ArgumentException`, so the `DirectoryNotFoundException` escapes the action and `ExceptionMiddleware` maps it to 404 (`DirectoryNotFoundException => Status404NotFound`, identical v10.11.8/master). Confirmed in the container log: "Error processing request: Could not find a part of the path '/jellyfin/MediaBrowser.Providers.dll/meta.json'. URL POST /Plugins/.../Disable". Net effect on Jellyfin: a 204 never changes state and a state change is always reported 404. `git diff v10.11.8 master -- PluginManager.cs` is ONE hunk (line 567, ImageResourceName) — master does not fix it. (b) THE OWNER QUESTION, not to be resolved by an agent. Jellyfin's toggle takes effect at the NEXT BOOT: `EnablePlugin`/`DisablePlugin` call `ProcessAlternative`, whose last two lines are `plugin.Manifest.Status = PluginStatus.Restart; plugin.Manifest.AutoUpdate = false;` (:882, commented "This value is memory only - so that the web will show restart required"). Ferrofin's toggle takes effect IMMEDIATELY — every consumer asks the PluginManager at use time (ferrofin-extensions file_transformation.rs:244,313,333; merge_versions.rs:179,212,246; ferrofin-wasm/src/lib.rs:558-575 behind a TTL cache), never only at boot — so it reports Active/Disabled and reporting `Restart` would be a false statement about Ferrofin's own behaviour. Both values are schema-valid (`PluginStatus` in the vendored contract is [Active,Restart,Deleted,Superseded,Superceded,Malfunctioned,NotSupported,Disabled]), and jellyfin-web computes `isEnabled = Status !== 'Disabled'`, so Jellyfin's own value leaves the dashboard toggle reading ENABLED right after a disable. The question: do we (i) keep immediate-effect + truthful Active/Disabled [today], (ii) keep immediate-effect but ALSO emit Restart for jellyfin-web's restart banner, or (iii) port Jellyfin's defer-until-restart semantics? If (ii)/(iii): crates/ferrofin-api/src/handlers/plugins.rs `to_plugin_info`/`to_plugin_manifest` plus a toggled-since-boot set in ferrofin-core/src/plugin_manager.rs `set_enabled`; no query/repository/DTO path is involved. NOTE for whoever edits the probe: on Jellyfin `ChangePluginState` early-returns when `Status == state`, and that early return is the ONLY path reaching `ProcessAlternative` — so a NO-OP toggle pins the plugin at `Restart` in memory until the container restarts, poisoning `plugin_manifest_invariants`' status_value and every later GET /Plugins diff. Neither probe issues one. Do not add an "idempotent toggle" fact. The mutate/restore pair is wrapped in try/finally (reviewer-fix pass) and restores through `restore_plugin_state`, which READS the status first and issues nothing when it already matches — so an interrupted or timed-out run cannot leave a Jellyfin plugin Disabled for the rest of the container's life, and the restore itself can never become the no-op toggle it exists to avoid. Verified after the change: one full Active→Disabled→Active cycle per route per server, all 5 Jellyfin plugins and the 1 Ferrofin plugin back at `Active`, none at `Restart`. The `Disable` probe measures its OWN route; the sibling verb appears only as setup or restore.

The pre-existing note stands: a freshly repository-installed WASM plugin 404s until its activating restart. Admin-only. The path version is now honoured (wrong -> 404, unparseable -> 400). |
| `POST /Plugins/{pluginId}/{version}/Enable` | registered | REAL | · | · | ⚠️ | open-work (PENDING OWNER RATIFICATION — NOT accepted): the text previously here described a DIFFERENT op (the pre-restart window of `POST /Packages/Installed/{name}`, already classified on its own row) and no layer had ever issued this request — sweep hands writes to Layer 2, journeys.py contains no plugin journey, and reads.py covered only Configuration/Manifest/Image. A probe now exists (reads.py `plugin_enable_invariants` / `plugin_disable_invariants`, aliases PluginsEnable / PluginsDisable, one per route). Measured twice on the F6 pair 2026-08-30, the two servers agree on EVERY id-independent contract fact — unknown guid 404/404, unparseable version 400/400, wrong version 404/404, installed-version-minus-a-component 404/404 (.NET `Version` treats an absent component as -1), anonymous 401/401 — and on the resulting state: status_after_disable 'Disabled' on both, status_after_enable 'Active' on both, restored True on both. Exactly ONE fact diverges, and it is kept in the diff on purpose, so the row is RED: the HTTP status of a toggle that SUCCEEDS is Ferrofin 204 / Jellyfin 404. (a) THE 404 IS A JELLYFIN BUG AND FERROFIN MUST KEEP 204. All five of Jellyfin's plugins are bundled in-assembly, so `PluginManager.CreatePluginInstance` builds `new LocalPlugin(instance.AssemblyFilePath, ...)` (v10.11.8 PluginManager.cs:541,559) and `Path` is a .dll FILE. `ChangePluginState` (:513) flips `Manifest.Status` in memory FIRST, then `SaveManifest` (:364) does `File.WriteAllText(Path.Combine(path, "meta.json"))` and catches only `ArgumentException`, so the `DirectoryNotFoundException` escapes the action and `ExceptionMiddleware` maps it to 404 (`DirectoryNotFoundException => Status404NotFound`, identical v10.11.8/master). Confirmed in the container log: "Error processing request: Could not find a part of the path '/jellyfin/MediaBrowser.Providers.dll/meta.json'. URL POST /Plugins/.../Disable". Net effect on Jellyfin: a 204 never changes state and a state change is always reported 404. `git diff v10.11.8 master -- PluginManager.cs` is ONE hunk (line 567, ImageResourceName) — master does not fix it. (b) THE OWNER QUESTION, not to be resolved by an agent. Jellyfin's toggle takes effect at the NEXT BOOT: `EnablePlugin`/`DisablePlugin` call `ProcessAlternative`, whose last two lines are `plugin.Manifest.Status = PluginStatus.Restart; plugin.Manifest.AutoUpdate = false;` (:882, commented "This value is memory only - so that the web will show restart required"). Ferrofin's toggle takes effect IMMEDIATELY — every consumer asks the PluginManager at use time (ferrofin-extensions file_transformation.rs:244,313,333; merge_versions.rs:179,212,246; ferrofin-wasm/src/lib.rs:558-575 behind a TTL cache), never only at boot — so it reports Active/Disabled and reporting `Restart` would be a false statement about Ferrofin's own behaviour. Both values are schema-valid (`PluginStatus` in the vendored contract is [Active,Restart,Deleted,Superseded,Superceded,Malfunctioned,NotSupported,Disabled]), and jellyfin-web computes `isEnabled = Status !== 'Disabled'`, so Jellyfin's own value leaves the dashboard toggle reading ENABLED right after a disable. The question: do we (i) keep immediate-effect + truthful Active/Disabled [today], (ii) keep immediate-effect but ALSO emit Restart for jellyfin-web's restart banner, or (iii) port Jellyfin's defer-until-restart semantics? If (ii)/(iii): crates/ferrofin-api/src/handlers/plugins.rs `to_plugin_info`/`to_plugin_manifest` plus a toggled-since-boot set in ferrofin-core/src/plugin_manager.rs `set_enabled`; no query/repository/DTO path is involved. NOTE for whoever edits the probe: on Jellyfin `ChangePluginState` early-returns when `Status == state`, and that early return is the ONLY path reaching `ProcessAlternative` — so a NO-OP toggle pins the plugin at `Restart` in memory until the container restarts, poisoning `plugin_manifest_invariants`' status_value and every later GET /Plugins diff. Neither probe issues one. Do not add an "idempotent toggle" fact. The mutate/restore pair is wrapped in try/finally (reviewer-fix pass) and restores through `restore_plugin_state`, which READS the status first and issues nothing when it already matches — so an interrupted or timed-out run cannot leave a Jellyfin plugin Disabled for the rest of the container's life, and the restore itself can never become the no-op toggle it exists to avoid. Verified after the change: one full Active→Disabled→Active cycle per route per server, all 5 Jellyfin plugins and the 1 Ferrofin plugin back at `Active`, none at `Restart`. The `Enable` probe measures its OWN route; the sibling verb appears only as setup or restore.

accepted-divergence: after a successful toggle Jellyfin reports the plugin's `Status` as `Restart` and flips `AutoUpdate` to false, and Ferrofin reports `Active`/`Disabled`. This is `ProcessAlternative` (v10.11.8 PluginManager.cs:881-909), whose tail is unconditionally `plugin.Manifest.Status = PluginStatus.Restart; plugin.Manifest.AutoUpdate = false;` with the comment "This value is memory only - so that the web will show restart required". It exists because a .NET assembly cannot be swapped in a running process: upstream's toggle does not take effect until the next boot, and the dashboard has to say so. Ferrofin's toggle takes effect IMMEDIATELY — every consumer reads the enabled flag through `PluginManager::get_plugin` at call time (see `ferrofin-extensions`) — so reporting `Restart` would be a false statement to the client, and per the repo's 'don't port Jellyfin bugs' rule Ferrofin stays truthful. Verified live on the lane-3 pair 2026-08-30: `POST /Plugins/{tmdb}/{v}/Enable` is 204 on both, after which Jellyfin's `GET /Plugins` reports TMDb `Status: "Restart"` and its manifest `autoUpdate: false`, while Ferrofin reports `Active`/`true`. The pre-existing note also stands: a freshly repository-installed WASM plugin 404s until its activating restart while Jellyfin already lists it restart-pending. Admin-only. The version in the path is now honoured on this route too (a wrong version is 404, an unparseable one 400). |

| `POST /QuickConnect/Authorize` | registered | REAL | · | · | ▫ | security-fixed 2026-08-30 (batch S1, cross-user authz): C# RequestHelpers.GetUserId (v10.11.8 Jellyfin.Api/Helpers/RequestHelpers.cs:67-85) has two halves: an absent/all-zero userId falls back to the caller, and a userId that is NOT the caller is honoured only for an ADMINISTRATOR, else SecurityException -> 403. Ferrofin ported only the first half here. Measured twice on the lane-3 pair (Ferrofin 18396 / Jellyfin 18397) with a second, non-admin account 's1probe' created on both: BEFORE, a non-admin passing ?userId=<admin> reached the manager on Ferrofin (404 for the unknown probe code - i.e. the user gate never ran) against 403 on Jellyfin. This one is a privilege escalation, not only a parity divergence: authorizing a pending request mints a session for the NAMED user on the pairing device, so any authenticated account could hand a device an administrator session. After the fix both servers return 403 for the same request, while an administrator naming another user's id and a non-administrator naming their own id still succeed on both (measured, twice). Regression tests in crates/ferrofin-api/tests/ cover all three cases per route. Fifth instance in this campaign of a C# authorization check Ferrofin served without one (A1 startup, B3 x29 Live TV routes, D2 get_lineups, F1 items); the campaign owes a dedicated audit of every [Authorize(Policy=...)] and RequestHelpers.GetUserId call site in the 10.11.8 controllers against Ferrofin's route guards. |

| `GET /ScheduledTasks` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | instance: LastExecutionResult — which tasks have run on this instance and when — is the ONE per-run field, and reads.py's `tasks_projection` drops it. Everything else is diffed across five legs, one per isHidden/isEnabled filter combination: the task set, the wire ORDER, each task's Name/Description/Category/IsHidden/State/CurrentProgressPercentage/Triggers, and — as the explicit `WireId` alias, because `Id` is in the global VOLATILE denylist — the task Id itself. PREVIOUSLY MIS-CLASSIFIED, NOW FIXED IN THE SERVER: this entry used to call the Id divergence 'a pre-existing accepted divergence', which it never was. `ScheduledTaskWorker.Id` (v10.11.8:219) is `ScheduledTask.GetType().FullName.GetMD5().ToString("N")` — MD5 over the UTF-16LE type name wrapped in a .NET Guid (`BaseExtensions.GetMD5`) — a PORTABLE value every Jellyfin install agrees on, so Ferrofin emitting the task key there made the two servers' task URLs mutually incompatible: `GET /ScheduledTasks/b461ef918ab28520928183e794350e3c` was 200 on Jellyfin and 404 on Ferrofin, and `GET /ScheduledTasks/CleanActivityLog` the reverse, so a stored dashboard link broke. No C# evidence said Jellyfin was wrong, because there is none. `ferrofin_traits::tasks::task_id_for_key` now reproduces the derivation from a table of the twenty C# type FullNames (plus the two ported plugins'), unit-pinned against the twenty ids a live 10.11.8 returns, and the registry resolves `/ScheduledTasks/{taskId}` by Id case-insensitively exactly as `FirstOrDefault(i => string.Equals(i.Id, taskId, OrdinalIgnoreCase))` does — a KEY is deliberately 404, as it is upstream. ONE NAMED OPEN DIFFERENCE remains and is NOT accepted: `list_filtered` sorts by name with Rust's ordinal `String::cmp`, where C# `OrderBy(o => o.Name)` is `Comparer<string>.Default` (CurrentCulture). The two agree on all twenty 10.11.8 task names — the live wire order is byte-identical on both servers — because every name is ASCII and capitalised; they would diverge on a lower-case initial or on punctuation/diacritics. Porting it needs an ICU collation dependency. |

The action declares only 204 and 404 (`[ProducesResponseType(Status204NoContent)]`, `Status404NotFound`) and the vendored contract declares no 500 for this path, so the throw is unintended, not a documented behaviour — Ferrofin's `FerrofinTaskManager::stop_run` returns Ok for an already-idle task (404 only for an unknown id) and is kept correct (do not port Jellyfin bugs). MEASURED 2026-08-30 on the loop pair, deterministic, not a race: POST-then-DELETE on `ec2f221fd8e7706b3d3afd2c4591b4d7` (Audio Normalization) is (204, 500) four times running on Jellyfin and (204, 204) four times on Ferrofin, and a DELETE with nothing running at all is 500 vs 204. The journeys row therefore reads H=True J=False and only goes red on a WARM lab: the first Audio Normalization run does real work and is still Running when the DELETE lands (which is why an earlier journeys record has it green), while every later run finishes in well under a millisecond. Ferrofin's own leg passes on both. |

| `POST /Startup/User` | registered | REAL | · | · | ⚠️ | jellyfin-bug: Ferrofin ports upstream MASTER's `StartupController.UpdateStartupUser` (NotFound -> Forbid -> BadRequest -> UpdateUser -> case-insensitive RenameUser -> ChangePassword), whose Forbid guard is security commit 62a5ded9205b10ddaef60ce3e05bf80e79f4c742 (Shadowghost, 2026-07-17, "Prevent unauthenticated re-run of the startup wizard on misconfiguration"); Ferrofin ports that commit's OTHER half too — `ApplicationHost.EnsureStartupWizardIntegrity` is `ensure_startup_wizard_integrity` at apps/ferrofin-server/src/lib.rs:304, called from lib.rs:424. `git show v10.11.8:Jellyfin.Api/Controllers/StartupController.cs` predates the commit — it does `_userManager.Users.First()` with NO NotFound and NO Forbid — so 10.11.8 answers 204 and silently re-sets the already-provisioned admin's password on an unguarded write. That is the same class of hole batch A1 closed on this controller, one layer deeper: A1 stopped an unauthenticated caller, 62a5ded920 stops an authenticated-but-wrong one once IsStartupWizardCompleted has been cleared. NOT a contract question: `.paths["/Startup/User"].post.responses` in the vendored 10.11.8 spec is [204,401,403,503], so Ferrofin's 403 is in-contract and only the C# implementation lags its own spec. Previously filed here as `accepted-divergence`, which was wrong under the campaign's master-vs-10.11.8 rule (outcome 1: master deliberately changed the behaviour, Ferrofin follows master and is correct). PROBE (rewritten in the F6 reviewer-fix pass; the deep_verified=True this row briefly claimed is WITHDRAWN). Three versions of this probe, two of them wrong, all recorded so that neither wrong one is rediscovered as an idea. (1) The ORIGINAL asserted `st == 403` on BOTH servers. The status delta it rested on was real, but it lived only in the boolean, so journey-results.json said "H=True J=False" and named nothing — which is what let this hand-written label stand in for a measurement. (2) F6's FIRST REPLACEMENT asserted `st in (204, 403)` plus "the provisioned admin was not clobbered", and reported the row deep_verified=True. That assertion is TRUE BY CONSTRUCTION: the probe posts the harness's OWN credentials, so Jellyfin's unguarded 204 re-sets the name to the name it already has and the password to the password it already has, and both evidence facts hold whether the server forbade the write or performed it. Demonstrated rather than argued — the probe was re-run against Jellyfin, a server with NO guard at all, on the F6 pair 2026-08-30, and returned Same(True, {'FirstUserName': 'bench', 'CredentialsStillValid': True}), byte-identical to Ferrofin's, cross_server_ok=True, deep_verified=True. A probe that a guardless server passes cannot be evidence that the guard is present, and it dropped the row's only Ferrofin-side discrimination as well: a regression deleting the guard would have kept it green. (3) WHAT RUNS NOW asserts the refusal itself — `st == 403` AND the admin intact — and carries the STATUS in the `Same` evidence, so the divergence is named in the results file ("[evidence: Status: H=403 J=204]") instead of living only in this prose. The row is therefore RED, and stays red for as long as the pinned 10.11.8 oracle lacks the guard: that is the correct terminal state for a jellyfin-bug row — identical to how this same layer treats `DELETE /Playlists/{playlistId}/Users/{userId}` — and the only way to make it green would be for Ferrofin to drop the guard. The payload is still the harness's own credentials, not to make anything pass (it does not, on Jellyfin) but because Jellyfin ACTUALLY PERFORMS the write and `StartupUserDto` carries no third, harmless field: any other Name or Password renames the lab's admin or changes its password for every later probe on the pair. So "was the admin clobbered" cannot separate the two servers without damaging the lab, and it is kept only as a lab-safety invariant; the status is the discriminator. Measured after the rewrite, twice, identical: F Same(True, {Status 403, FirstUserName bench, CredentialsStillValid True}) / J Same(False, {Status 204, FirstUserName bench, CredentialsStillValid True}), deep_verified=False, verification_method `property`. The three sibling Startup rows stayed green across both runs, which is the check that the re-auth helper still is not revoking the run's own session. The 403 itself is pinned against MASTER — the only tree that contains the fix, and one no 10.11.8 container can witness — by four unit tests in crates/ferrofin-api/tests/startup.rs transliterated from upstream's own tests/Jellyfin.Api.Tests/Controllers/StartupControllerTests.cs, including its `ChangePassword(...) Times.Never` assertion. |

B1's two claims still hold and were re-measured on the current tree: the `CreateItemByName<Studio>` lazy create is live (`/Studios/<never-seen name>` is 200 on both, with the Jellyfin-shaped Path), and the per-kind ItemValue count fix holds. Its closing sentence, 'the REAL studios still count 254/250/4 on both servers', was a hand measurement no layer reproduced; reads.py now carries a THIRD leg over the LAST shared studio by SortName, so the claim is probed. Current state: 2/3 legs clean.

WHAT F2 FIXED (a real server bug the third leg exists to keep honest): a provider refresh updated `BaseItems.Studios/Genres/Tags` but never re-derived `ItemValues`/`ItemValuesMap`, so the by-name counts this route returns went stale against Ferrofin's OWN corpus. `ProviderManager::persist_refreshed` called only `save_items`; C# does both halves in one call — `BaseItemRepository.SaveItems` saves the row and then rewrites the by-name index for it (v10.11.8 `BaseItemRepository.cs:674-735`, unchanged on master), reached from a refresh via `MetadataService.SaveItemAsync` → `UpdateToRepositoryAsync` → `LibraryManager.UpdateItemAsync`. Ferrofin's editor path (`LibraryManager::update_items`) already did this; the refresh path was the hole. Fixed by calling `save_item_values(id, &item_values_of(entity))` there; `item_values_of` moved to `ferrofin-db` so `ferrofin-providers` can use it without depending on `ferrofin-core`. Damage already on disk is still visible on this lab (H's Movie 0001 row says Studios='Castle Rock Entertainment' while its ItemValuesMap still says 'Ferrofin Studios') and is repaired by the next library scan, which re-derives the index per item — unlike the Person columns, this index has a self-heal path, so no startup repair was added.

WHY THE ROW IS STILL RED, and it is NOT this fix: the remaining leg diffs ChildCount J=253 H=254 / MovieCount J=249 H=250 on 'Parity Pictures', and that is measured CORPUS SKEW from an earlier lane's write journey, not a query defect. Each server's answer is exactly consistent with its own database (H 250 movies carry 'parity pictures', J 249). The single divergent item is Movie 0002: its on-disk NFO says `<studio>Parity Pictures</studio>`, H's row carries Studios='Parity Pictures', Genres='Comedy|SciFi', Tags='parity-test', and J's row carries NONE of the three — Jellyfin lost them. Cleared by the campaign's wipe-and-provision sweep; NOT chased here, because resetting one side of a shared pair is exactly what the lab-hygiene rule forbids.

PROBE NOTE (review pass): the third leg is the LAST shared studio by SortName. When fewer than two names are shared it is now SKIPPED, not silently re-pointed at the first shared name — the old fallback made the extra leg a byte-identical duplicate and doubled the `field(s) compared` depth the ledger publishes for no extra coverage. It does not fire on this lab (both studios are shared), but a corpus that narrows should shrink the evidence, not inflate it. |

| `GET /System/Info/Storage` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ◐ | instance: exactly three things here are not comparable, and they are named. (1) The Path strings are the container layout, not code: Ferrofin's /config/cache vs Jellyfin's JELLYFIN_CACHE_DIR=/cache, /config/web vs /jellyfin/jellyfin-web. (2) FreeSpace/UsedSpace are sampled at two different instants. (3) The Libraries array ORDER is inherited verbatim from GET /Library/VirtualFolders and belongs to that row. Everything else IS comparable because both containers stat the same host filesystem, and reads.py's `storage_invariants` now asserts it as `property`: the exact FolderStorageDto key set, DeviceId == Path (on Unix DriveInfo.Name is the ctor argument verbatim), FreeSpace+UsedSpace == the filesystem's TotalSize byte-for-byte, the StorageType DriveType word, and the libraries compared as a SET. The missing-folder branch (FreeSpace/UsedSpace -1 with StorageType/DeviceId null, StorageHelper.GetFreeSpaceOf's catch arm) cannot be forced symmetrically from a probe and is covered by a unit test in crates/ferrofin-core/src/system_manager.rs instead. |
| `GET /System/Logs` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ◐ | instance: `LogFile` has four fields and two (DateCreated/DateModified) are already VOLATILE, so a diff could only compare Name and Size — and both are the host's own. Jellyfin's Serilog sink writes `log_YYYYMMDD.log` (Jellyfin.Server/Resources/Configuration/logging.json: `%JELLYFIN_LOG_DIR%//log_.log`, rollingInterval Day) while Ferrofin's tracing-appender writes `log.YYYY-MM-DD.log` (apps/ferrofin-server/src/bootstrap.rs); Size is each server's own log volume. Name is also parity_diff's array-align key, so the sweep can only ever report '(whole item)'. Body-diff is structurally impossible; the row is earned as `property` by assets.py. WHAT THE SHAPE ASSERTIONS ALONE MISSED, AND WHY THE ROW NOW ASSERTS CONTENTS TOO: the listing's shape, extension filter, bare filenames and ordering can all pass while a whole CLASS of file Jellyfin produces is absent. It was. `TranscodeManager.StartFfMpeg` (MediaBrowser.MediaEncoding/Transcoding/TranscodeManager.cs:450-467 at v10.11.8) writes each transcode's ffmpeg stderr into `ApplicationPaths.LogDirectoryPath` as `FFmpeg.Transcode-`/`FFmpeg.Remux-`/`FFmpeg.DirectStream-{yyyy-MM-dd_HH-mm-ss}_{mediaSourceId}_{guid8}.log` — deliberately into the directory this route serves ('let's put it in the log directory'), so an operator can download a failed transcode's log from the dashboard. Ferrofin wrote it beside the playlist in the transcode temp dir instead (`playlist_path.with_extension("log")`), so its listing held no ffmpeg log at all while Jellyfin's held several. FIXED IN THE SERVER: `StartFfMpegRequest` now carries the LOG DIRECTORY and `TranscodeManagerImpl::start_ffmpeg` names the file with the ported `ffmpeg_log_file_name`. assets.py starts a real transcode on each server and then asserts the listing contains a matching name — a fact both servers must satisfy, and the one a Name/Size diff could never reach. The rest of the row is unchanged: the exact 4-key `LogFile` contract, the .txt/.log extension filter (`ManagedFileSystem.GetFiles` — the Jellyfin container's extension-less `.jellyfin-log` must be absent), bare filenames, and the C# ordering rule OrderByDescending(DateModified).ThenByDescending(DateCreated).ThenBy(Name), unit-tested in crates/ferrofin-api/tests/system.rs. `sig_match` now fails this row when either server answers False on any of those facts, or when the fact could not be established at all — agreeing on a broken invariant is not parity. |

| `GET /Trailers` | registered | REAL | ⚠️ | ✅ | ⚠️ | jellyfin-bug: Jellyfin 10.11.8 returns 500 on its own route, unconditionally. TrailersController takes ItemsController as a constructor dependency and calls _itemsController.GetItems(...) on it (TrailersController.cs:20/26/28/209); that DI-constructed instance has no ControllerContext, so ControllerBase.User is null and the first statement of GetItems — `var isApiKey = User.GetIsApiKey();` (ItemsController.cs:250) — dereferences it in ClaimsPrincipalExtensions.GetClaimValue (ClaimsPrincipalExtensions.cs:71-79, no null guard) and throws a NullReferenceException. Confirmed from the live container's stack trace; still unfixed on master (ItemsController.cs:263). The vendored contract declares only 200/401/403/503 for this path and the C# action is [ProducesResponseType(Status200OK)], so 200 is the required behaviour: Ferrofin is correct and is kept correct (do not port Jellyfin bugs). Because the two responses therefore cannot be diffed against each other, reads.py's `trailers_invariants` earns the row as `property` through the cross-route oracle the C# controller is literally defined as (GetTrailers == GetItems with includeItemTypes=[Trailer]): the Trailer corpus is compared via /Items?includeItemTypes=Trailer, which works on both servers, and Ferrofin's /Trailers is held to equal its own /Items answer. Jellyfin's 500 is pinned as a fact, so an upstream fix flips the row loudly instead of passing in silence.

--- ALSO ON THIS ROW (merged from main) ---
jellyfin-bug (nil-guid parameters), measured 2026-08-30 in the S1 review pass while verifying the empty-guid fix — recorded rather than left unmentioned. Jellyfin THROWS on an all-zero guid passed to a parameter that is not `userId`: `parentId=00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000` is 400 with the middleware's generic body `Error processing request.` (an unhandled ArgumentException from GetItemById/GetUserById — "Guid can't be empty" — not a designed 400), where Ferrofin treats the nil as absent and answers 200. `userId` is exempt on both sides because RequestHelpers.GetUserId maps a nil to the caller before anything looks it up. Measured on the lane-3 pair, admin token, all-zero value: GET /Items?parentId= (J400 H200), /Items?userId=&parentId= (J400 H200), /Items/Filters (J400 H200), /Items/Filters2 (J400 H200), /Years (J400 H200), /Shows/NextUp?seriesId=&parentId= (J400 H200), /Shows/{seriesId}/Episodes?seasonId=&adjacentTo= (J400 H404), /Trailers?parentId= (J500 H200 — an outright unhandled exception). Ferrofin is kept correct per the standing policy (do not port Jellyfin bugs); a 500 is never the right answer and neither is a leaked ArgumentException. NOT deferred and NOT accepted by an agent: this is a measured divergence awaiting the owner's call. UN-DEFER PATH if the owner wants byte parity on the refusal: reject a nil guid with 400 on the non-userId item-lookup parameters (parentId, seriesId, seasonId, adjacentTo, startItemId, appearsInItemId) in handlers::query_parse, leave `userId` alone, and add the eight probes above to the read layer as a status-class row. |
| `GET /Trailers/{itemId}/Similar` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ◐ | instance/methodology (PROPERTY-VERIFIED, NOT body-diffed — the ledger counts and lists this row separately from the deep-verified ones; status codes, the empty short-circuits, the count invariant and the candidate rule are what is gated, by the reads.py `similar_invariants` probe, and each of the three property rows now issues its OWN alias rather than all three probing /Movies). Two independent reasons a movie seed's item array cannot diff, both measured on the loop pair. (1) ORDER: Jellyfin 10.11.8 sets `OrderBy = [(ItemSortBy.Random, Ascending)]` (v10.11.8 LibraryController.cs:801); five identical calls at limit=12 returned five different SETS, because limit < |universe|. (2) ALGORITHM: 10.11.8 builds ONE flat `InternalItemsQuery { Genres = item.Genres, Tags = item.Tags }`, while Ferrofin ports upstream master's weighted `MovieSimilarItemsProvider` (genre 10 / tag 5 / studio 5 / director 50 / actor+guest-star 15, MovieSimilarItemsProvider.cs:27-31) plus its phase-2 `IsMovie = true, IsPlayed = false` + ConfigureUserAccess pass. Measured at limit=1000: Jellyfin 299 items, Ferrofin 432, and Jellyfin's set is a STRICT SUBSET of Ferrofin's (onlyJ=0); the 133 extras each share a studio and/or a person with the seed and no genre — master's dimensions exactly. *** OWNER DECISION PENDING (NOT an agent-granted acceptance): running master's scorer against a 10.11.8 oracle is a deliberate ALGORITHM divergence on three ops, and CLAUDE.md reserves additions to the accepted-divergence list to the owner. The engineering case for keeping master is the repo's 'do not port Jellyfin bugs' rule — 10.11.8 additionally over-returns by 4 at every limit (measured 1/3/5/12/50 -> 5/7/9/16/54) because `BaseItemRepository.PrepareFilterQuery` (v10.11.8:1427-1430) adds 4 when `EnableGroupByMetadataKey` and never trims back, while Ferrofin honours limit exactly as master's `.Take(requestedLimit)` does — but the call is the owner's, and reverting to 10.11.8's flat query would make these three rows body-diffable at a pinned wide limit. *** WHAT IS VERIFIED LIVE instead of a body diff (every fact computed per server and then compared): status 200, StartIndex 0, TotalRecordCount == len(Items), the seed is never returned, every Type is in the C# IncludeItemTypes, the page is full, len(Items) <= limit+4 (the named Jellyfin quirk is the ONLY slack), an unknown seed is 404 on both, a nil seed is 200-empty on both, an Episode seed short-circuits empty on both, the five aliases agree as sets at limit=1000, and `obeys_own_candidate_rule` — each server's answer is re-derived independently from the DTO fields under ITS OWN documented algorithm (Jellyfin: every row shares a genre or tag with the seed, which 200 of the 500 fixture movies violate; Ferrofin: the answer equals master's weighted model's top-N IN ORDER by score desc, then Ferrofin's OWN SortName/Id tiebreak — master has no tiebreak at all, `MovieSimilarItemsProvider.cs:189-195` is `OrderByDescending(score)` alone, so that clause pins Ferrofin's rule, which is strictly more determined than the C#'s). The person dimension is matched as (Name, PersonType) pairs because that is what a `PeopleId` IS — 'the Peoples table has one row per (Name, PersonType)' (PeopleRepository.cs:47) — which is exactly what both master's `PeopleId` join and Ferrofin's SQL compute. Negative controls run 2026-08-29: the Ferrofin clause fails on a reversed order and on 12 arbitrary movies, the Jellyfin clause fails on 12 movies outside its universe. NOT REACHED BY ANY PARITY LAYER, and unit-covered instead: the phase-2 filters — the lab has zero played movies and one all-libraries user, so `IsPlayed = false` and ConfigureUserAccess are exercised only by `a_played_movie_is_not_suggested_to_the_user_who_played_it`, `a_library_the_user_cannot_access_contributes_nothing`, `a_blocked_media_folder_hides_its_rows` and `the_access_filter_does_not_starve_the_page` in similar_items_manager.rs. Three real Ferrofin bugs this row used to hide were fixed in the same pass: an unknown seed returned 200-empty instead of 404, a nil seed returned 400 instead of the C# root-folder fallback, and the no-limit default was 10 instead of C#'s `limit ?? 50` (SimilarItemsManager.cs:93). |

| `GET /UserViews` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ⚠️ | open-work (NOT accepted) — found 2026-08-30 by batch S1 while re-running the read layer, and recorded rather than left as an unreviewed detector flag. `POST /Library/VirtualFolders/Name` leaks a user view on Ferrofin: renaming a virtual folder and renaming it BACK leaves an orphan entry under the intermediate name in `GET /UserViews`. Measured on the lane-3 pair, same request sequence against both servers (`Music (synth)` -> `Music (synth) Tmp` -> `Music (synth)`, both legs 204 on both servers): Ferrofin's view list grew by one ('Music (synth) Tmp') and Jellyfin's was unchanged. It is not a cache — an earlier orphan ('Movies (synth) Renamed', left by the parity write layer's own rename journey) survived BOTH a container restart and a `POST /Library/Refresh`, while Jellyfin's equivalent transient view rows clear on restart. A user sees a phantom library in the sidebar. UN-DEFER PATH: make the rename path move the existing user-view row instead of creating a second one (C# `LibraryManager.RenameVirtualFolder` renames the folder on disk and the view follows it), add a journey leg that asserts the view COUNT is unchanged across a rename round-trip, and re-provision the lane pair (the two orphans above are on it now). ALSO ON THIS ROW — a SECOND open-work gap, measured 2026-08-31 on the lane pair and previously unrecorded anywhere: Ferrofin serves NO `Playlists` user view. Jellyfin answers 7 views to Ferrofin's 5; two of those are accounted for (`Recordings` is the lab-state library above), and the third is real. `UserViewManager.GetUserViews` (v10.11.8 Emby.Server.Implementations/Library/UserViewManager.cs:44-95) walks `GetUserRootFolder().GetChildren(user, true)`, and `UserView.IsUserSpecific(folder)` is TRUE for the playlists folder (UserView.cs:155-169: `collectionFolder.CollectionType == CollectionType.playlists`), so upstream materialises a PER-USER `UserView` for it via `GetNamedView(user, folder.Name, folder.Id, folderViewType, null)` — id = `GetNewItemId("38_namedview_" + name + userIdN + parentIdN + viewType, typeof(UserView))` (LibraryManager.cs:2462-2496), Path `{InternalMetadataPath}/views/{idN}`, `Data` = `{"ViewType":"playlists","DisplayParentId":<playlists folder>,"UserId":<user>}`. Read off the lab's 10.11.8: id 134ea3dcf722741c8892ad56d425dd3c, CollectionType `playlists`, ChildCount 6, with a Primary image. Ferrofin's `get_user_views` selects rows by KIND (`CollectionFolder` + `UserView`) and has no per-user named-view provisioning at all, so the view can never appear. UN-DEFER PATH, and it is three parts, not one — which is why it is a named work item and not a drive-by: (1) provision the row, reusing the `ensure_live_tv_view` shape in crates/ferrofin-core/src/user_view_manager.rs with the `38_namedview_` id key above; (2) SCOPE IT — `get_user_views` currently returns every `UserView` row to every user, so a per-user view added without a `Data.UserId` filter would leak one user's view into another's sidebar, a NEW divergence worse than the missing one; (3) the DTO must carry `ChildCount` and the Primary image, or the row swaps a missing view for a wrong one. Each part needs its own live re-measure against 18097. |

| `GET /Years` | registered | REAL | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | jellyfin-bug (ONE FIELD ONLY — TotalRecordCount; everything else on this row is diffed strictly by the reads.py `GET /Years` probe). Jellyfin's `/Years` reports a paging total that has nothing to do with the page: `YearsController.GetYears` finishes with `new QueryResult(startIndex, totalCount == -1 ? ibnItemsArray.Count : totalCount, dtos)`, where `totalCount` is the out-param of `folder.GetRecursiveChildren(user, query, out totalCount)` (v10.11.8 Folder.cs:1450-1458) — the count of the underlying MEDIA items. `RequestHelpers.GetUserId` always resolves a non-empty user, so that branch always runs and the `ibnItemsArray.Count` fallback is dead code. Measured on the lab pair (twice): the 3-year fixture returns J TotalRecordCount=559 with 3 Items, and the value tracks the filter (includeItemTypes=Series → 8, mediaTypes=Audio → 9, parentId=Movies → 500). Ferrofin reports the distinct-year count (3), captured BEFORE Skip/Take, which is what the field means and what pages the list; per the repo's 'do not port Jellyfin bugs' rule Ferrofin stays correct. Gated per-server instead of cross-server: the `items_root_ancestors_and_years_over_real_http` integration test asserts Ferrofin's total equals the distinct-year count and does not move with limit/startIndex. The PREVIOUS classification on this row ('expected-extension: Ferrofin does not materialize Year items (documented out-of-scope)') was measurably false — Ferrofin has materialized Year items since `FerrofinLibraryManager::get_named_items` gained `create_missing_years`, with ids byte-identical to Jellyfin's — and it masked five real unported behaviours behind the two routes, all fixed in batch F1: the ignored sortBy/sortOrder/includeItemTypes/excludeItemTypes/mediaTypes/recursive parameters, a total computed AFTER paging, all-zero item counts, a guid UserData.Key, and a per-item DisplayPreferencesId. NOT compared: the item ORDER on the ONE no-sortBy leg (the set and every DTO field ARE compared there; only the sequence is normalised, and the reads.py row declares it in `caveats`). The earlier wording blamed `GetOrderBy` returning `Array.Empty` and `LibraryManager.Sort` being a no-op, which is only half of it and reads as if the two servers agreed: the no-op LEAVES an order in place, it does not produce a sorted one. The order it leaves is `GetAllItems`'s — `items.Select(i => i.ProductionYear ?? 0).Where(i => i > 0).Distinct()` (v10.11.8 YearsController.cs:220-227) — and LINQ `Distinct()` preserves FIRST-OCCURRENCE order, so Jellyfin's resting order is the order years first appear in `Folder.GetRecursiveChildren`, its in-memory BaseItem tree walk (measured stable at 2020,2022,2021 over three repeats). Ferrofin has no such tree by design (CLAUDE.md, 'There is no domain-object hierarchy'), so it sorts ascending and that is a deliberate divergence, not parity. The default call shape IS now issued — batch F1's nine legs all pinned `sortBy=SortName`, i.e. the one axis where the two agree — and every other leg still compares order strictly, including under paging. | jellyfin-bug (nil-guid parameters), measured 2026-08-30 in the S1 review pass while verifying the empty-guid fix — recorded rather than left unmentioned. Jellyfin THROWS on an all-zero guid passed to a parameter that is not `userId`: `parentId=00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000` is 400 with the middleware's generic body `Error processing request.` (an unhandled ArgumentException from GetItemById/GetUserById — "Guid can't be empty" — not a designed 400), where Ferrofin treats the nil as absent and answers 200. `userId` is exempt on both sides because RequestHelpers.GetUserId maps a nil to the caller before anything looks it up. Measured on the lane-3 pair, admin token, all-zero value: GET /Items?parentId= (J400 H200), /Items?userId=&parentId= (J400 H200), /Items/Filters (J400 H200), /Items/Filters2 (J400 H200), /Years (J400 H200), /Shows/NextUp?seriesId=&parentId= (J400 H200), /Shows/{seriesId}/Episodes?seasonId=&adjacentTo= (J400 H404), /Trailers?parentId= (J500 H200 — an outright unhandled exception). Ferrofin is kept correct per the standing policy (do not port Jellyfin bugs); a 500 is never the right answer and neither is a leaked ArgumentException. NOT deferred and NOT accepted by an agent: this is a measured divergence awaiting the owner's call. UN-DEFER PATH if the owner wants byte parity on the refusal: reject a nil guid with 400 on the non-userId item-lookup parameters (parentId, seriesId, seasonId, adjacentTo, startItemId, appearsInItemId) in handlers::query_parse, leave `userId` alone, and add the eight probes above to the read layer as a status-class row.

instance/methodology — Ferrofin is the more-correct side. The divergence that actually remains is Jellyfin's TotalRecordCount, not the items: `YearsController.GetYears` takes `totalCount` from `folder.GetRecursiveChildren(user, query, out totalCount)` (YearsController.cs:122) — the number of ITEMS scanned — and returns it beside the year DTOs (YearsController.cs:156-158). Measured on the lane-2 pair: J TotalRecordCount=559 with 3 Items; Ferrofin returns 3 with the same 3 Items (2020/2021/2022, identical bodies). Kept correct per the project's don't-port-Jellyfin-bugs rule. |
